# Support matrix

This file is the authoritative statement of what CloudBurrow supports.

**Three separate things are tracked separately**, because conflating them is how a tool
over-promises:

| Dimension | Question | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **GCP API compatibility** | Does an official Google SDK call behave correctly? | §Services below |
| **Native Kubernetes portability** | Do ordinary manifests, Helm charts and operators work? | §Native Kubernetes |
| **Knative feature coverage** | Which Cloud Run v2 behaviors does the adapter actually map? | §Cloud Run |

A component being adopted from upstream does not make its operations supported. **Upstream
behavior still has to be demonstrated through an official SDK** before it is promoted here. It is not a roadmap
and not an aspiration. If an operation is not listed as `Verified` here, CloudBurrow does not
support it, regardless of what any other document, README, or commit message says.

## Status values

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Planned` | Not implemented. May return `UNIMPLEMENTED`, or may not be routed at all. |
| `Partial` | Implemented with documented gaps. The gap is named in the Notes column; an unexplained `Partial` is a documentation bug. |
| `Verified` | Implemented **and** exercised by a merged test that drives it through an official Google client library. |
| `UNVERIFIED` (an error code) | The call is Verified, but an error code the test asserts is one **Google does not document**. It is implemented as the most plausible code and marked `// unverified:` in the test, so [docs/coverage](coverage/README.md) lists it and a row that relies on it says `(error code UNVERIFIED)`. It stays so until a recorded observation pins it; see [test/compat/README.md](../test/compat/README.md#unverified-error-codes). |

**The promotion rule.** An operation moves off `Planned` only in a PR that adds a test using
an official Google SDK — `cloud.google.com/go/...`, `google-cloud-*` for Python, and so on.
A curl invocation, a handwritten HTTP request, or an internal unit test is not sufficient
evidence, because those test our understanding of the API rather than the client's.

**Control plane vs data plane.** These are tracked separately because they fail
independently, and conflating them is the standard way an emulator overstates itself.
Creating a Cloud Run service and receiving a well-formed `Service` response says nothing
about whether a container ran. A service is described as supported only when both columns are
`Verified`.

**Backing component** is recorded per service, from the
[upstream reuse audit](upstream-evaluation.md). Inherited limitations are listed with the
service — an upstream gap is still our gap as far as a user is concerned.

**Verified** rows below are backed by `test/compat`, run against a live `cloudburrow up` on
2026-09-20. Everything else remains `Planned`: exercised-by-hand is not evidence, and an
operation a test merely touches in cleanup is not asserted.

### What CI does not run, and why

Every merge runs `test/compat` against a live instance. A test that skips there is not evidence,
so each one that still skips is listed here with its reason (#346). The acceptance workflow
(`test/e2e`) and `test/k8s` run too, on an instance of their own (the
`official SDK compatibility (acceptance)` job), and none of them may skip: the job fails unless
every one passes (#596).

| Tests | Why they skip in the main compat run |
|---|---|
| `TestDatastoreAcrossRestart`, `TestMemorystoreAcrossRestart`, `TestCloudSQLMySQLAcrossRestart`, `TestCloudSQLAcrossRestart`, `TestKMSAcrossRestart`, `TestSecretManagerAcrossRestart`, `TestTasksAcrossRestart`, `TestSchedulerAcrossRestart`, `TestStorageAcrossRestart` | They read what an earlier process wrote. They run, and must pass twice each, in CI's restart probe after `stop`/`up` (persistent mode) and `up --mode ephemeral`, each in the shard that serves it; `TestStorageAcrossRestart` is its own setup too, against the in-cluster server through the instance's tunnel, so it must pass three times (#596), and at setup it also creates a notificationConfig the later runs require present, then absent (#701). `TestCloudSQLAcrossRestart` reads a row `TestCloudSQLRestartSetup` writes alone just before `stop` (#701). |
| `TestGenerateContentThroughTheOfficialSDK`, `TestStreamGenerateContentThroughTheOfficialSDK`, `TestGenerationNeverUsesApplicationDefaultCredentials` | Local AI needs a 2.59 GB model download and minutes of CPU inference. The decision is that it does not run in per-PR CI. The Local AI and Vertex AI generation rows come from runs on a developer machine, dated in [generation.md](generation.md) (2026-09-21) and [local-ai.md](local-ai.md). They and the Functions and source-build rows backed by the three tests below (`TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks`, `TestFunctionsRebuildReusesLayers`, `TestFunctionsBuildBrokenModulePathFails`) are the only Verified rows whose evidence is not re-run on every merge. |
| `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` | It pulls Google's builder and run images and runs two Buildpacks builds, which take minutes, so it is too slow for every merge while runners are the bottleneck; it is in no CI shard and does not run on merges. It runs only with `CLOUDBURROW_TEST_FUNCTIONS=1` against an instance whose cluster nodes are amd64 (the builder's only platform), and needs `pack` and Docker. It runs on demand in [`functions.yml`](../.github/workflows/functions.yml) (Functions build, `workflow_dispatch` only), on an amd64 runner with the run shard's instance and the pinned `pack`, and fails if any selected test skips. **Dated run: passed on 2026-09-27, [run 36357529139](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/actions/runs/36357529139) of `functions.yml` on main, `ubuntu-latest` amd64 runner, pack 0.40.9** (136s; it built `Hello` (`http`) in 58s and `Event` (`cloudevent`) in 39s). That run is the evidence for the Verified Functions Framework and source-build rows; the workflow is dispatch-only, so nothing re-runs it on merge. |
| `TestFunctionsRebuildReusesLayers`, `TestFunctionsBuildBrokenModulePathFails` | Same reason: they pull Google's builder and run Buildpacks builds (the first builds the fixture twice), which take minutes; they are in no CI shard and do not run on merges. `functions.yml` runs them on demand with the test above. They run only with `CLOUDBURROW_TEST_FUNCTIONS=1` and need `pack` and Docker, but no cluster: they build into a local registry and run nothing. **Dated run: both passed on 2026-09-27, macOS/arm64 Docker Desktop, emulated amd64 builder, pack 0.40.9** (first build 1m48s, rebuild 1m32s reusing 6 layers; the broken-module test 50s). **Both passed again on 2026-09-27, [run 36357529139](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/actions/runs/36357529139) of `functions.yml` on main, `ubuntu-latest` amd64 runner, pack 0.40.9**, natively (the rebuild test 74s: first build 38s, rebuild 35s reusing 6 layers; the broken-module test 17s). Those two runs are the evidence for the Verified cache-reuse and failed-build rows; the workflow is dispatch-only, so nothing re-runs them on merge. |

#### Retried once

CI retries five tests once, and no others (#566). When `go test` fails in a compat shard or in
the restart probe and every test it names as failed is on this list, those tests are re-run once,
by name, with the same environment, and the run fails if a retry fails too. A failure of any
other test fails at once, and so does a package-level failure that names no test (#675). A pass
on the second attempt counts as a pass, so the rows that cite these tests say that their evidence
tolerates one retry. Every retry is a `::warning::` annotation on the run page and a line of the
job summary with its outcome (#704).

| Retried test | Where CI runs it | Evidence for |
|---|---|---|
| `TestHostEndpointSurvivesABackendRestart` | Compat shard | Advertised host port after a pod restart |
| `TestMemorystoreAcrossRestart` | Restart probe | Memorystore durability |
| `TestDatastoreAcrossRestart` | Restart probe | Datastore keeps its data in persistent mode |
| `TestDatastoreSurvivesAPodRestart` | Compat shard | Datastore keeps its data in persistent mode |
| `TestSpannerStateDoesNotSurviveARestart` | Compat shard | Spanner state does not survive a restart |

**Removal rule.** An entry is taken off the list once 20 consecutive CI runs on `main` have
passed with no retry of it, counted from the annotations. A test is added only together with a
row in this table: `TestRetryAllowListMatchesTheDocs` (`test/repo`) fails when this table and the
allow-list in `.github/scripts/retry-flaky.sh` differ.

## Native Kubernetes portability

Tested by #29, independently of any GCP API. A cluster that answers GCP calls is not
automatically a cluster your manifests run on, so this is never inferred from the sections
below. CI runs `test/k8s` on every merge, and fails if any of it skips (#596).

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `kubectl apply` of ordinary manifests | **Verified** | `test/k8s`: Namespace, ConfigMap, Secret, multi-replica Deployment and Service applied directly, with `readyReplicas` asserted. |
| Helm chart install | **Verified** | `TestHelmChartInstalls` installs a chart and asserts the release reports `deployed`. |
| Custom resources and operators | **Verified** | `TestCustomResourceDefinition` establishes a CRD and reads back a custom resource — the operator pattern. |
| PersistentVolumeClaims | **Verified** | `TestPersistentVolumeClaimBindsAndPersists` writes from one pod and reads from **another**, which is what proves the volume persisted rather than the data living in one container. |
| Jobs | **Verified** | Used by the PVC test; `batch/v1` Jobs run to completion. |
| **Unmodified GKE manifests** | **Not promised** | Endpoint configuration and local overlays legitimately differ. No universal claim is made, and nothing here tests a GKE-specific API. |

---

## Cloud Storage — JSON API v1

**Backing component:** CloudBurrow's own server (`internal/service/storage`, #485), built to
Google's documentation and the storage v1 discovery document, which it embeds: every method of
that document is either served or answers **501 `notImplemented` naming it**. It is the only
Cloud Storage backend (#519). `up` runs it as one Deployment, from an image built locally from
the binary embedded in the CLI (#514); `cloudburrow storage-server` runs it with no cluster.
Contract: Cloud Storage JSON API v1, and the XML API subset below.
Client endpoint override: `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` (Go, Python; see architecture §4.2). The Node
client needs `apiEndpoint` instead (see [Node.js client libraries](#nodejs-client-libraries)).

The per-method status is generated from the server's own method table in
[docs/coverage/storage.md](coverage/storage.md): a method is **Verified** when an official-SDK
or real-tool compat test annotated for it passes, **Implemented** when it is served but no such
test covers it, and **Unimplemented** when it answers 501. `TestCoverageMatchesCompatibility`
fails if a row below disagrees with that report. The official Go client retries every 5xx, 501
included, until its deadline, so an unimplemented method costs a client its timeout rather than
failing at once.

### Control plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `buckets.insert`, `buckets.get`, `buckets.delete` | **Verified** | `TestStorageBucketLifecycle`. A non-empty bucket delete is 409 (`TestStorageNonEmptyBucketDelete`; UNVERIFIED for the JSON API). |
| `buckets.list` | **Verified** | `TestStorageBucketsList`; buckets are scoped by project. |
| `buckets.patch` | **Verified** | `TestStorageBucketPatchKeepsOmittedFields`, `TestBucketUpdateChangesMetadata`, `TestStorageBucketMetagenerationPreconditions`: a JSON merge patch, with omitted fields kept (#374). |
| `buckets.update` | **Verified** | A full replace: `TestStorageBucketsUpdateReplaces`, through Google's generated JSON API client (`google.golang.org/api/storage/v1`), since the Go client's `Update` is a patch. Labels and versioning the request omits are cleared, the location is kept, and a stale `ifMetagenerationMatch` is 412 (#691). |
| `buckets.lockRetentionPolicy` | **Verified** | `TestStorageLockRetentionPolicy`, `TestStorageLockRetentionPolicyTwice` (a repeat lock is refused, UNVERIFIED; see Retention and holds). |
| `buckets.restore` | **Verified** | `TestStorageBucketsRestore`, through the generated JSON API client: the bucket returns by generation with its soft-deleted objects restorable; a live bucket of the same name is 409 and a generation that is not soft-deleted is 404 (both UNVERIFIED against Google; #691). |
| `buckets.getStorageLayout` | **Verified** | `TestGcloudStorageParallelCompositeUpload`: gcloud reads it before a parallel composite upload. |
| `buckets.getIamPolicy`, `buckets.setIamPolicy`, `buckets.testIamPermissions` | **Verified**, *stored, not enforced* | `TestStorageBucketIAMRoundTrip`, `TestStorageBucketIAMStaleEtag`, under [ADR-0006](adr/0006-iam-policy-surface.md) as amended by #504: a policy round-trips with its etag and version, conditions are refused, and nothing is enforced. From the console's Permissions tab: `TestConsolePermissionsOnABucket` (#793). |
| `projects.hmacKeys.create`, `projects.hmacKeys.get`, `projects.hmacKeys.update`, `projects.hmacKeys.delete`, `projects.hmacKeys.list`, `projects.serviceAccount.get` | **Verified** | `TestStorageHMACKeyLifecycle`, `TestStorageHMACKeyLimit`, `TestGcloudStorageHMAC` (#505). A key's secret is shown once, at creation. |
| `notifications.insert`, `notifications.get`, `notifications.list`, `notifications.delete` | **Verified** | `TestNotificationConfigsCRUDThroughTheSDK`; see the notifications section. |
| `managedFolders.insert`, `managedFolders.get`, `managedFolders.list`, `managedFolders.delete` | **Verified** | `TestStorageManagedFolderLifecycle`, through Google's generated JSON API client, since the official Go client has no managed folder call (#828). A name is returned ending in `/` (one sent without it has it added, UNVERIFIED); a second insert is 409, an absent one 404; list takes `prefix`, `pageSize` and `pageToken`, the parameters the discovery document gives it (it has no delimiter); delete honours `ifMetagenerationMatch` (412 when stale) and refuses a managed folder with objects or managed folders under it unless `allowNonEmpty`, which keeps the objects (409, UNVERIFIED). A bucket needs uniform bucket-level access to hold one (400, status UNVERIFIED), and a bucket that holds one cannot be deleted (409, UNVERIFIED). Managed folders persist with the bucket's metadata: `TestStorageAcrossRestart` finds one, with its policy, after a persistent restart and none after an ephemeral one. gcloud's `managedFolders.list` on `storage rm -r` is `TestGcloudStorageClearLabels`. |
| `managedFolders.getIamPolicy`, `managedFolders.setIamPolicy`, `managedFolders.testIamPermissions` | **Verified**, *stored, not enforced* | `TestStorageManagedFolderIAM`: kept on the managed folder as a bucket's is on its bucket, under [ADR-0006](adr/0006-iam-policy-surface.md) as amended by #504: a policy round-trips, leaves the bucket's alone, a stale etag is 412 and conditions are refused; no object access consults it. Viewed and edited in the console on the managed folder's page (#847, `TestConsolePermissionsOnAManagedFolder`). The same methods on the Storage Control API (`StorageControlClient.GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy`, `TestIamPermissions` with a `managedFolders` resource) are gRPC on the v2 API, which CloudBurrow does not serve. |
| `managedFolders.update` | **Unimplemented** | 501 naming the method: its only settable field is `rapidCacheConfig`, and rapid caches are not built; an insert carrying `rapidCacheConfig` is 501 naming it. |
| `objects.insert`, `objects.get`, `objects.delete` | **Verified** | `TestStorageObjectRoundTrip`. |
| `objects.list` | **Verified** | `TestStorageListWithPrefix`: prefix **and** delimiter, with the synthetic `dir/sub/` prefix. |
| `objects.patch` | **Verified** | `TestObjectMetadataUpdate`; the bytes are re-read afterwards. |
| `objects.update` | **Verified** | A full replace: `TestStorageObjectsUpdateReplaces`, through the generated JSON API client. Omitted metadata and headers are cleared, the bytes and generation kept, and a stale `ifMetagenerationMatch` is 412 (#691). |
| `objects.rewrite` | **Verified** | `TestObjectCopyAndRewrite`, within a bucket and across buckets. |
| `objects.compose` | **Verified** | `TestStorageCompose`, `TestGcloudStorageParallelCompositeUpload`. |
| `objects.copy` | **Verified** | `TestStorageObjectsCopy`, through the generated JSON API client, since the Go client's copier uses `objects.rewrite`: without a body the source's metadata is kept, with one the body's is taken, and `ifGenerationMatch=0` on an existing destination is 412 (#495, #691). |
| `objects.move` | **Verified** | `TestStorageObjectMove`, through the Go client's `ObjectHandle.Move`: a rename within the bucket that leaves nothing soft-deleted (#691). |
| `objects.restore` | **Verified** | `TestStorageSoftDeleteAndRestore`, `TestStorageRestoreNotSoftDeleted412`, `TestStorageRestoreWithoutPolicy412`. |
| `objects.bulkRestore`, `objects.watchAll`, `channels.stop`, bucket and object ACLs, object IAM, `folders`, `anywhereCaches`, `rapidCaches` and `operations` | **Unimplemented** | 501 naming the method (`TestStorageACLNotImplemented`), never an empty policy or list. |

### Data plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Simple upload (`uploadType=media`) | **Verified** | `TestStorageObjectRoundTrip` |
| Multipart upload (`uploadType=multipart`) | **Verified** | `TestMultipartUploadStoresMetadataWithContent`: custom metadata and content type survive alongside the bytes. |
| Resumable upload (`uploadType=resumable`) | **Verified** | `TestStorageResumableUploadAndRangedRead` (8 MiB in 256 KiB chunks) and `TestResumableUploadCompletes`; the resume after an interrupted chunk is `test_resumable_upload_resumes_after_interruption` (#516). |
| Download and ranged download | **Verified** | `TestStorageObjectRoundTrip`; `TestStorageResumableUploadAndRangedRead`, byte-exact; `test_if_generation_match_zero`. |
| Generation and metageneration preconditions | **Verified** | `TestStorageGenerationPreconditions`: `DoesNotExist` on an existing object and a stale `GenerationMatch` are both 412, and a matched precondition advances the generation. |
| CRC32C and MD5 validation | **Verified** | `TestStorageChecksums`: both are returned and checked on read; a mismatched upload is refused. |
| Batch (`/batch/storage/v1`) | **Verified** | `test_batch_deletes_and_patches` (#496, #516). |
| XML API and XML multipart uploads | **Verified** | `TestStorageXML*`, `TestStorageXMLMultipart*`, `test_transfer_manager_xml_multipart_upload` (#507, #508). |
| Signed URLs (V4 HMAC and RSA, V2) | **Verified, failing closed** | `TestStorageSignedURLV4HMAC`, `TestStorageSignedURLV2RegisteredCert`, `test_generate_signed_url_v4` (#509): a bad signature, an unknown key or an expired URL is 403, never 200. RSA signatures verify against a registered public key: `up` registers the ADC fixture's own (#577), so a URL signed with the credentials `cloudburrow env` exports verifies with no setup, and `storage.signingCerts` (or `storage-server --signing-cert`) adds others. The two RSA tests run against both the `up` instance (storage shard, fixture key) and the standalone server (check job, a generated key); `TestUpRegistersTheFixtureKeyForSignedURLs` checks the Deployment's own arguments end to end. |

### What the server does

Built to Google's spec in steps (#485), one issue per feature:

#### Buckets (#490)

Are served: insert, get, list (prefix, pages of at most 1000), patch as a JSON merge patch (an omitted field stays, `null` clears it, a `null` label removes that key), update, delete, and metageneration with `ifMetagenerationMatch`/`NotMatch` (412 `conditionNotMet`; 304 for a read). Every settable field is kept, stored or refused by name with 400, never dropped with 200; see the bucket fields table below. A non-empty bucket delete is 409 (UNVERIFIED for the JSON API).

#### Objects (#491)

Media and multipart uploads (`uploadType=media`, `multipart`; a metadata-only POST is 400 `wrongUrlForUpload`), with `Content-MD5`, `x-goog-hash` and the metadata's `md5Hash`/`crc32c` verified and a mismatch refused; `ifGenerationMatch` (including 0, "only if absent") and the other three preconditions; metadata reads, `alt=media`, `/download/storage/v1/…`, and XML `GET`/`HEAD /{bucket}/{object}` with the `x-goog-*` headers; one byte range (`a-b`, `a-`, `-N`; 206, or 416 with `Content-Range: bytes */size`); and a new generation, metageneration 1, on every finalize. `TestStorageObjectRoundTrip`, `TestStorageChecksums` and `TestMultipartUploadStoresMetadataWithContent` pass against it.

#### Object metadata (#492)

Patch (merge; a `null` metadata key removes it) and update (replace), each advancing the metageneration and keeping the generation and bytes; `customTime` only moves forward and cannot be removed, as documented; A body may restate the object's `storageClass` but not change it, which only a rewrite does (#515); holds and retention follow the retention rules below. Preconditions combine with AND on the live generation unless one is named: `ifGeneration(Not)Match`, `ifMetageneration(Not)Match` (412, or 304 for a failed NotMatch on a read), `If-Match` (412) and `If-None-Match` (304 on reads), and for the XML API `x-goog-if-generation-match`, `x-goog-if-metageneration-match`, `If-Modified-Since` (304) and `If-Unmodified-Since` (412). `TestStorageGenerationPreconditions` and `TestObjectMetadataUpdate` pass against it.

#### Listing (#494)

`prefix`, `delimiter` with synthetic `prefixes` (which count toward `maxResults`), `includeTrailingDelimiter`, `startOffset`/`endOffset`, `matchGlob` (`**`, `*`, `?`, `[…]`, `[!…]`, `{a,b}`), and pages of at most 1000 in byte order. `TestStorageListWithPrefix` passes against it.

#### Resumable uploads (#493)

A session from `uploadType=resumable` (200 with `Location`; the `upload_id` is random), chunks by PUT or POST with `Content-Range: bytes a-b/TOTAL|*` (every chunk but the last a multiple of 256 KiB; persisted bytes immutable, so a re-sent range is skipped), and an incomplete chunk answered **308 with `Range: bytes=0-N`**, or, with `X-GUploader-No-308: yes` as the Go client sends it, **200 with `X-Http-Status-Code-Override: 308`**. Both handshakes are undocumented and required by the clients. `bytes */TOTAL` and `bytes */*` query the status; the final chunk verifies `x-goog-hash` and applies `X-Goog-Meta-*`; DELETE cancels with 499. A session lasts a week, then answers 410 for a week, then 404. Sessions and their chunks survive a restart in persistent mode. `TestStorageResumableUploadAndRangedRead` and `TestResumableUploadCompletes` pass against it.

#### Compose, copy, rewrite and move (#495)

Compose takes 1 to 32 sources of one storage class, with per-source `generation` and `ifGenerationMatch` and `deleteSourceObjects`; the result has **no MD5**, a CRC32C combined from the components, and `componentCount` (counting a composite's components). Copy and rewrite share the source's bytes, cross buckets and honour `ifSource*`; rewrite changes the storage class, and with `maxBytesRewrittenPerCall` (a multiple of 1 MiB) proceeds by tokens, each good for a week and once. Move renames atomically within a bucket. `kmsKeyName`, `dropContextGroups` and the destination ACL and key parameters are refused by name. `TestStorageCompose`, `TestStorageCopy` and `TestObjectCopyAndRewrite` pass against it; the Go client's copier sends `objects.rewrite`, so `TestStorageObjectsCopy` drives `objects.copy` through Google's generated JSON API client, and `TestStorageObjectMove` drives `objects.move` through the Go client's `ObjectHandle.Move`.

#### Batch (#496)

`POST /batch/storage/v1` takes a `multipart/mixed` body of `application/http` parts, dispatches each through the same handler as the direct call, and answers each in order with `Content-ID: <response-…>`; an upload or a download inside a batch is refused per part, and more than 100 calls refuse the whole batch (the refusal is UNVERIFIED). A part's body needs no `Content-Length`: the Python client sends none, and the body is the rest of the part.

#### Object versioning (#498)

On a bucket with `versioning.enabled`, replacing or deleting the live version keeps it as a **noncurrent** version with its generation and a `timeDeleted`; noncurrent versions appear only with `versions=true` (each name's oldest first, paged within a name) or when named by `generation=`, and can be read, patched and used as a copy source. A delete that names a generation removes that version permanently. Disabling versioning stops new noncurrent versions and keeps the existing ones, `ifGenerationMatch=0` succeeds when only noncurrent versions exist, and a bucket holding only noncurrent versions is not empty (409). Versions survive a restart in persistent mode. `TestStorageVersioningKeepsNoncurrent`, `TestStorageVersioningDeleteKeepsNoncurrent`, `TestStorageVersioningDisableKeepsExisting`, `TestStorageDoesNotExistWithOnlyNoncurrent` and the restart probe `TestStorageVersioningPersistentMode` pass against it.

#### Soft delete (#499)

Every bucket has a soft delete policy, 7 days unless set (7 to 90 days, or 0 for none; `effectiveTime` moves when the retention changes). Under it, a version that leaves the bucket (deleted, overwritten, or a deleted noncurrent version) is kept **soft-deleted** with `softDeleteTime` and `hardDeleteTime`: hidden from reads and listings, listed with `softDeleted=true`, its metadata readable by generation (never its bytes), and `objects.restore` makes it live again as a new generation with metageneration 1, retiring any live version it replaces. A restore answers **412 `softDeletePolicyNotSet`** on a bucket without a policy and **412 `objectNotSoftDeleted`** for a live or noncurrent generation, as the status-codes page lists. A bucket whose only objects are soft-deleted can be deleted; it is then soft-deleted itself under its own policy, readable and listable with `softDeleted=true`, and `buckets.restore` brings it back empty, with its soft-deleted objects restorable again (a live bucket of the same name refuses the restore with 409, UNVERIFIED). A policy change applies only to later deletions. Reads treat anything past its `hardDeleteTime` as gone, and a sweep removes it. A move is a rename and leaves nothing soft-deleted (UNVERIFIED). `bulkRestore` is 501. `TestStorageSoftDeleteAndRestore`, `TestStorageRestoreNotSoftDeleted412`, `TestStorageRestoreWithoutPolicy412`, `TestStorageBucketDeleteIgnoresSoftDeleted` and `TestStorageBucketsRestore` pass against it.

#### Retention and holds (#500)

With the status-codes page's reasons: a bucket `retentionPolicy` (1 s to 3,155,760,000 s) applies to existing and new objects, which report `retentionExpirationTime`; an object younger than the period cannot be deleted or replaced (**403 `retentionPolicyNotMet`**), though a versioned bucket may make it noncurrent, and its metadata stays editable. `lockRetentionPolicy` needs `ifMetagenerationMatch` (required in the pinned discovery document; 400 without, 412 stale) and a policy (400 `badRequest`). **A second lock of a locked policy is refused 400 `badRequest`** "The retention policy on bucket … is already locked." and changes nothing, metageneration included (#829). This is UNVERIFIED: the lockRetentionPolicy reference, the bucket-lock and using-bucket-lock pages and the status-codes page say what a lock needs and that it is irreversible, but not what a repeat lock answers, and no observation of Google has been recorded; 400 `badRequest` is the status-codes page's reason for a lock the bucket's settings do not allow, the conservative choice over accepting it. The metageneration is checked first, so a stale one is 412 whether or not the policy is locked; a locked period can grow but not shrink or be removed (400 `badRequestException`; the discovery document says PERMISSION_DENIED, the status-codes page 400). `temporaryHold` and `eventBasedHold` block delete and replace (**403 `objectUnderActiveHold`**) but not metadata changes; releasing an event-based hold restarts the retention clock, and no `retentionExpirationTime` is given while it is held; `defaultEventBasedHold` holds each new object. Object retention `{mode, retainUntilTime}` needs a bucket created with `enableObjectRetention=true` (400 `invalid` otherwise, and with an event-based hold); removing, shortening or locking an Unlocked one needs `overrideUnlockedRetention=true` (403 `forbidden`), and a Locked one only extends. A copy, rewrite or restore does not inherit holds or retention. Holds also block a live version becoming noncurrent (UNVERIFIED). `TestStorageRetentionBlocksDelete`, `TestStorageLockRetentionPolicy`, `TestStorageLockRetentionPolicyTwice`, `TestStorageHoldsBlockDelete` and `TestStorageObjectRetentionRequiresEnabledBucket` pass against it.

#### Lifecycle (#501)

`lifecycle.rule` is validated when set, and an unknown action or condition is 400 naming it, with the old configuration kept. Actions are Delete, SetStorageClass and AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload (#508; with only `age`, `matchesPrefix` and `matchesSuffix`, as documented). Conditions are `age`, `createdBefore`, `customTimeBefore`, `daysSinceCustomTime`, `daysSinceNoncurrentTime`, `isLive`, `matchesPrefix`/`matchesSuffix` (at most 1000 across all rules), `matchesStorageClass`, `noncurrentTimeBefore`, `numNewerVersions` and `sizeAboveBytes`/`sizeBelowBytes` (at most 5 TiB). The rules run at least hourly on the server's clock, and on demand with `POST /_cloudburrow/lifecycle`. A rule's conditions combine with AND; `age` 0 is met at midnight UTC after creation, and age N N days after it. Delete takes precedence over SetStorageClass, and the cheapest target class wins; a class only moves to a cheaper one. A hold or an unmet retention period keeps Delete from taking effect, and a deleted live version becomes noncurrent or soft-deleted as any delete does. No limit on the number of rules is imposed, since none is documented. `TestStorageLifecycleConfigRoundTrip` and `TestStorageLifecycleUnknownCondition400` pass against it.

#### CORS (#502)

**CloudBurrow checks the browser's origin first** (#677, [ADR-0004](adr/0004-local-access-and-no-authentication.md)). Google can allow any origin because every call carries an OAuth token; CloudBurrow checks none, so a page on any site you have open could otherwise read and change every bucket. A request with an `Origin` header is served only when the origin is loopback (`http://` or `https://` at `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, `localhost` or `*.localhost`, any port), the server's own, or named with `up --cors-allow-origin` ([configuration.md](configuration.md#browser-access-to-cloud-storage)). Any other origin, `https://evil.example` say, gets **403 with no CORS headers**, a preflight included, however the bucket's `cors` is set. A request with no `Origin` (the SDKs, `gcloud`, `curl`) is not checked. This is a deliberate difference from Google. `TestStorageCORSRefusesForeignOrigin`, `TestStorageCORSLoopbackOriginWorks` and `TestStorageCORSAllowlistedOriginWorks` pass against it, the last two with a resumable upload started and finished from the browser's origin.

For an origin that passes, Google's behaviour applies. The bucket's `cors` (`origin`, with `*`; `method`; `responseHeader`; `maxAgeSeconds`, 3600 by default) is kept and validated. **XML endpoints** follow it: a preflight is answered from the first rule that matches its origin, its method and every requested header, with that rule's methods and max age; a preflight that matches none gets **200 with no CORS headers**, as documented; an actual request that matches gets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` and the rule's `responseHeader` as `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`. **JSON endpoints** (`/storage/v1`, uploads, downloads, batch) always allow, whatever the bucket says: the Origin echoed, `DELETE, GET, HEAD, PATCH, POST, PUT`, a Max-Age of 3600, and the requested headers echoed (UNVERIFIED). **`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` is not sent.** It was sent on simple requests until #677, but no recorded observation of Google shows it: the storage oracle compares against storage-testbench, which records no CORS behaviour, and Google was never called. CloudBurrow reads no cookies, so dropping it loses nothing a page could use. A resumable session URI answers with the Origin that started the session, and is itself behind the origin check. `TestStorageCORSRoundTrip`, `TestStorageXMLPreflightMatches`, `TestStorageXMLPreflightMismatch200NoHeaders` and `TestStorageJSONAlwaysAllowsCORS` pass against it.

#### XML API subset (#507)

Path-style or virtual-hosted (`{bucket}.{host}` with `storage-server --host`): object `PUT` (with `x-goog-meta-*`, `Cache-Control` and the other content headers, `x-goog-storage-class`, `x-goog-if-generation-match`/`-metageneration-match` and `Content-MD5`; 200 with the ETag, `x-goog-generation` and `x-goog-hash`, no body), `GET`/`HEAD` (#491) and `DELETE` (204); bucket `GET` (a `ListBucketResult` with `prefix`, `delimiter` and its `CommonPrefixes`, `marker`, `max-keys`, `IsTruncated` and `NextMarker`) and `DELETE`; and resumable uploads, where `POST` with `x-goog-resumable: start` is **201 with the session URI**, chunks are `PUT` to it with `Content-Range` (308 while incomplete), and a `DELETE` cancels with **204**. Errors are `<Error>` documents: `NoSuchBucket`, `NoSuchKey`, `PreconditionFailed`, `BucketNotEmpty` (409), `InvalidArgument`, `InvalidRange`, `AccessDenied`. `x-goog-copy-source`, `x-goog-acl` and customer encryption headers are refused by name; bucket `PUT`, service `GET` and form `POST` are 501. Signed URLs (#509) are verified; see the data plane. `TestStorageXMLPutGetDelete`, `TestStorageXMLListBucket`, `TestStorageXMLResumable201And204`, `TestStorageXMLErrorCodes` and `TestStorageXMLHostStyle` pass against it.

#### XML multipart uploads (#508)

`POST ?uploads` (an `InitiateMultipartUploadResult` in the S3 namespace the clients parse), `PUT ?partNumber=N&uploadId=` for N 1 to 10,000 (the ETag, and `x-goog-hash` the Python client validates; the same number again replaces the part), `POST ?uploadId=` with `CompleteMultipartUpload` (parts in ascending order with their ETags; every part but the last at least 5 MiB; **no MD5** on the object, as documented, and a CRC32C), `DELETE ?uploadId=` (**204**), list parts (`max-parts`, `part-number-marker`, at most 1000) and `GET /{bucket}?uploads`. **Preconditions are refused with 400 `NotImplemented`**, as the docs say they are not supported. The codes for a part out of order, a bad ETag or a small part are S3's `InvalidPartOrder`, `InvalidPart` and `EntityTooSmall`, and the completed upload's ETag is S3's `"md5-of-md5s-N"`; all UNVERIFIED. An incomplete upload stays until aborted, or until a lifecycle `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` rule (age, `matchesPrefix`, `matchesSuffix`) removes it. Google's Python `transfer_manager.upload_chunks_concurrently` uploads a 20 MiB file in 5 MiB parts against it: `test_transfer_manager_xml_multipart_upload` runs in CI (#516). `TestStorageXMLMultipartAbort204`, `TestStorageXMLMultipartListParts` and `TestStorageXMLMultipartPreconditionRefused` pass against it.

#### Persistence by mode (#512; the #481 lesson)

With `--data-dir`, `storage-server --mode persistent` (the default) keeps buckets, every object version's bytes and metadata, and in-progress resumable sessions across a restart. `--mode ephemeral` deletes the store an earlier run left in that directory before serving, so nothing survives whatever is on disk: the choice is made by the mode, never by whether a store exists. CI's restart probe `TestStorageAcrossRestart` runs at setup, after a persistent restart (a bucket, a two-version object and a 256 KiB-in session: **present**; with a Pub/Sub emulator, a notificationConfig too, #701) and after an ephemeral restart on the same directory (**absent**); unit `TestStorageEphemeralClearsLeftoverStore` does the same in-process. `up` passes the instance's mode to the in-cluster server (#514), and the compat job's restart probe measures it on every merge (#596): the same `TestStorageAcrossRestart` runs against the in-cluster server through the instance's tunnel at setup, after `stop` and `up` (**present**, from the Deployment's PVC) and after `up --mode ephemeral` (**absent**; the Deployment has no volume). The session is resumed at the restarted instance's endpoint, whose port is OS-assigned and so new.

#### In the cluster (#514)

`up` runs **one** Deployment for the host and the cluster, with no notification front, no second Deployment and no Host-binding workaround (#268), because the server builds `mediaLink` from each request's Host. Its image is built at `up` from the Linux `cloudburrow-storage` binary embedded in the CLI, on a distroless base pinned by digest (`build.storageServerBase`), and `kind load`ed; nothing is published or downloaded but the base. Readiness is a real request (`GET /storage/v1/b?project=_`). A NetworkPolicy allows egress only to DNS and the Pub/Sub emulator; kind's default CNI does not enforce NetworkPolicy, so `TestStorageNoEgress` proves in-process that a whole workload resolves no name at all. Measured against a real instance: 58 builtin storage and notification compat tests passed through the tunnel, the e2e `TestAcceptanceWorkflow` passed with the worker's `STORAGE_ENDPOINT` on the single Deployment, and the CLI scrapes the server's calls into `/admin/events` and `/metrics`. Measured for #514: a pod could **not** reach the CLI's loopback-bound ports through the kind gateway, but **could** through `host.docker.internal` on Docker Desktop, admin API included (#553). **The official Go client retries every 5xx, 501 included, until its deadline**, so a method not built yet costs a client its timeout rather than failing at once. CI runs the whole compat suite against it twice: through the tunnel on an instance (the `official SDK compatibility (storage)` shard, #565), and against a standalone `cloudburrow storage-server` with the Python suite, the restart probes and a registered signing certificate (`check` job).

#### Official clients and tools (#516, #517)

**The official Python client** (google-cloud-storage 3.14.1, #516) runs against the storage server in CI: CRUD with a resumable upload, a resume after an interrupted chunk (`recover()`'s `bytes */*` answered 308 with the `Range` held), `mediaLink` on the emulator's host, `if_generation_match=0` and a ranged download, batch, `transfer_manager` XML multipart, and a V4 signed URL. **gcloud storage** (586.0.0, #517; CI installs that release, checksum-pinned, #694), configured by `gcloud-setup` alone, with every non-loopback request refused by a test proxy: `storage ls`, `buckets create`, `buckets update --update-labels` then `--clear-labels`, `objects update`, `hmac create/list/update/delete`, and `cp` of 200 MiB by the parallel composite path. That path reads `buckets.getStorageLayout` and composes with `deleteSourceObjects` in the request body, which deletes the components, and a download over the sliced threshold reads it back in four ranged slices. `gcloud storage rm -r` lists `managedFolders`, which is empty since none can be created (every other managedFolders method is 501). On macOS, gcloud's `gcloud-crc32c` helper under Gatekeeper quarantine hangs on any file, CloudBurrow or not; the test checks the sliced download by SHA-256 instead. **gsutil** (5.37, the one Google Cloud CLI 586.0.0 carries) requires HTTPS on both APIs (`gcs_json_api.py` hard-codes `https://`; `is_secure = False` is refused), and CloudBurrow serves plain HTTP, so it works only behind a TLS front; `TestGsutilJSONAndHMACXML` supplies one. Through it, cp, ls and rm work over JSON with no credentials and over XML with HMAC-only credentials. gsutil cannot download over XML from any port but 443 or 80, whatever the server (its `_AddCustomEndpointToKey` sets the port as a string, and boto formats it with `%d`). apitools sent **no** `X-GUploader-No-308` (0 of 15 requests).

### Bucket fields

Every settable Bucket property of the discovery document (#503). *Kept* fields are stored, echoed and act as documented. *Stored, no effect* fields are stored and echoed as sent, but nothing here acts on them. *Refused* fields get 400 naming the field, never 200 and a silent drop. `TestStorageBucketFieldsNeverSilentlyDropped` checks this list against the schema.

| Field | Behaviour | Issue |
|---|---|---|
| `location`, `storageClass`, `labels` (a `null` key removes it) | Kept; the location is fixed at creation | #490, #503 |
| `versioning` | Kept | #498 |
| `softDeletePolicy` | Kept | #499 |
| `retentionPolicy`, `defaultEventBasedHold`, `objectRetention` (with `enableObjectRetention=true`) | Kept | #500 |
| `lifecycle` | Kept | #501 |
| `cors` | Kept | #502 |
| `website` | Stored, no effect: no website serving | #503 |
| `logging` | Stored, no effect: no usage logs are written | #503 |
| `encryption` (`defaultKmsKeyName`) | Stored, no effect: objects are not encrypted with the key | #503 |
| `billing` (`requesterPays`) | Stored, no effect: nothing is billed, and `userProject` is accepted and ignored | #503 |
| `iamConfiguration` (`uniformBucketLevelAccess`, `publicAccessPrevention`) | Stored. There are no ACLs, so access is uniform anyway; `uniformBucketLevelAccess.enabled` (or the legacy `bucketPolicyOnly.enabled`) is what `managedFolders.insert` requires, as Google does (`TestStorageManagedFolderLifecycle`, #828) | #503 |
| `rpo` (`DEFAULT` or `ASYNC_TURBO`) | Stored, no effect: there is no replication | #503 |
| `autoclass` | Stored, no effect: classes move only by rewrite or lifecycle | #503 |
| `customPlacementConfig` | Stored, no effect: there is one location | #503 |
| `hierarchicalNamespace` | Stored, no effect: no folders API; names stay flat | #503 |
| `ipFilter` | Refused, 400 naming it | — |
| `acl`, `defaultObjectAcl` | Refused, 400 naming them: ACL methods are not implemented | — |

### UNVERIFIED choices

Where no Google page states a behaviour, the server makes a choice and marks it UNVERIFIED in the
test that pins it; the method-level codes are also listed in [docs/coverage/storage.md](coverage/storage.md).

- `buckets.delete` of a non-empty bucket answers 409, as the XML API documents `BucketNotEmpty`; no page states the JSON API's status.
- `buckets.setIamPolicy` with a stale etag answers 412, the JSON API's precondition code.
- A live bucket of the same name refuses `buckets.restore` with 409.
- A move is a rename and leaves nothing soft-deleted; holds block a live version becoming noncurrent.
- More than 100 calls refuse a whole batch.
- The JSON API's CORS defaults beyond `Allow-Origin`, `Allow-Methods` and `Max-Age`: the requested headers echoed. (`Allow-Credentials` is no longer sent, #677.)
- XML multipart part order, ETag and size errors use S3's `InvalidPartOrder`, `InvalidPart` and `EntityTooSmall`, and the completed upload's ETag is S3's `"md5-of-md5s-N"`.
- A signed URL's `X-Goog-Expires` is at most 604800 seconds, from the docs rather than a measured refusal.

## Cloud Storage — gRPC (`google.storage.v2`)

Deferred. Not part of the first release; see architecture §10. Requires a separate port from
the JSON API (`STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST_GRPC`).

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| All | Planned | Deferred until the JSON API surface is `Verified`. |

---

## Pub/Sub — `google.pubsub.v1`

**Backing component:** Google `cloud-pubsub-emulator` 0.8.35 (integrate).
Contract: `google/pubsub/v1/pubsub.proto`. Client endpoint override: `PUBSUB_EMULATOR_HOST`.

**Inherited limitations**, measured in #24:

- **State is not persisted.** A topic was `NotFound` after restart even with `--data-dir`.
  Pub/Sub is effectively memory-only, and CloudBurrow will not imply otherwise.
- **IAM methods return `Unimplemented`.** The emulator announces this at startup.
- The emulator describes itself as a "fake" that "may be incomplete or differ from the real
  system."

**CloudBurrow's front** (#873). The emulator stores a subscription's `expiration_policy` and never
acts on it, so the Pub/Sub pod runs `cloudburrow-storage pubsub-front` (`internal/pubsubfront`)
beside it, on the Service's port, from the locally built storage image; the emulator listens on
the pod's loopback alone, port 8086, which the Service does not publish and no other pod reaches
(#1114, `TestEmulatorsBehindAFrontRefuseOtherPods`). Every client goes through the front, the
host tunnel, workloads in the cluster and the storage server's notifications alike, and it passes
every call through unchanged except the ones the Subscription expiration row names, and a
`labels` update of a topic or subscription, which the emulator refuses and the front applies
(#949, the `Publisher.UpdateTopic` and `Subscriber.UpdateSubscription` rows). **It serves
the emulator's REST API too**, as the emulator does, on the same port: each request is gRPC when it
is HTTP/2 with an `application/grpc` content type, and REST otherwise, over HTTP/1.1 or plain-text
HTTP/2 with prior knowledge (h2c, #909), proxied to the emulator's REST API with the
same rules (checks and the 31-day default on `PUT .../subscriptions/{s}`, the retention check on
`PATCH`, and every call on `.../subscriptions/{s}` or `{s}:verb`, and a snapshot's create, as
activity), so gcloud and Terraform's google provider, which use REST, work as before
(`TestGcloudPubSub`, `TestPubSubRESTSubscriptionExpiresWhenIdle`, `TestGcloudSetupConfiguration`,
`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`). **A connection is routed by its first request**
(#950): one that does not open with the HTTP/2 preface is HTTP/1.1, served by net/http; an
HTTP/2 connection whose first request is gRPC, as every official client's is, goes to grpc-go's
own transport; any other HTTP/2 connection goes to net/http, which routes each of its requests by
its content type, gRPC through `grpc.Server.ServeHTTP`. To read the first request the front sends
an empty SETTINGS frame itself (a gRPC client waits for one) and hands the chosen server every
byte the client sent but its acknowledgement of that frame (unit `TestClassifyByTheFirstRequest`).
So an h2c REST client is served, and so is a proxy that carries REST and then gRPC on one HTTP/2
connection (unit `TestRESTOverH2CPriorKnowledge`, `TestOneHTTP2ConnectionCarriesGRPCAndREST`); a
connection gRPC opened no longer answers a later REST request on it with grpc-go's 415 (#963):
the front reads every request on such a connection as it passes to grpc-go, and the first that is
not gRPC is never given to grpc-go; the client is sent a GOAWAY (NO_ERROR) whose last stream is
the last gRPC call, which RFC 9113 lets it retry on a new connection, the calls already open run
to their end, and that client's later HTTP/2 connections (by remote address) are routed per
request by net/http. Unit `TestRESTOnAConnectionGRPCOpenedIsServed`: Go's HTTP/2 client, after two
gRPC calls on a connection, gets the REST answer, retried on a second connection, which then
carries both; `TestAMixedConnectionIsRefusedWithGoAway` checks the frames (the GOAWAY's last
stream, a WINDOW_UPDATE giving back the refused DATA's window, nothing answered on a refused
stream, and the next connection served per request). Not checked through the host tunnel, which
never sends such a connection: it keeps gRPC and h2c REST on upstream connections of their own
(unit `TestGuardKeepsGRPCAndH2CRESTApart`, `internal/netfwd`), since every tunnel connection comes
from one address and one mixed connection would move them all to per-request routing. Client
trailers (which gRPC clients do not send, and which grpc-go's transport takes as a protocol error
that closes the connection) are given to grpc-go as an empty DATA frame ending their stream, and
once the front has kept any header block from grpc-go (a refused request, client trailers), each
later block grpc-go is given is re-encoded so that grpc-go's HPACK decoder, which never saw the
kept blocks, can read it (#981). Unit `TestHPACKStaysInStepOnAMixedConnection`: on one connection,
gRPC calls that use the HPACK dynamic table, one ended by client trailers that index an entry, a
call whose headers name it, a refused REST request that indexes another, client trailers naming
that one on a call still open, and a call after the GOAWAY, retried on a new connection; every
call succeeds and the connection stays open.
`TestPubSubRESTOverH2CPriorKnowledge` drives `curl --http2-prior-knowledge` through the tunnel,
between official gRPC client calls, and a ttl Google refuses is refused 400 over it as over
HTTP/1.1. Why not ServeHTTP for everything, as #909 had it: measured with
`go test ./internal/pubsubfront -run '^$' -bench Transport -benchtime 3s -count 5` on an Apple M4
Max (Go 1.27.1), the front before a stub emulator that answers at once, medians of five runs,
ServeHTTP gave 224k published messages/s against grpc-go's transport's 513k (ten 1 KiB messages
a call, 64 callers), 1.38M streaming-pulled messages/s against 2.12M (one stream, batches of a
hundred 1 KiB messages), and a unary GetTopic round trip of 126 µs mean, 118 µs p50 and 350 µs
p99 against 99, 92 and 269 µs. The front measured as grpc-go's transport does: 520k, 2.16M, and
99, 92 and 270 µs (#950); the per-request watch #963 added is measured in its PR, within 5% of
those. **An HTTP/1.1 `Upgrade: h2c`** (`Connection: Upgrade,
HTTP2-Settings`, as `curl --http2` sends on an http:// URL) is answered 101 and the connection
served as HTTP/2 from then on, the request as its stream 1, with every later request routed by
its content type (unit `TestUpgradeToH2C`); one with a malformed `HTTP2-Settings` is answered
over HTTP/1.1 (`TestAMalformedUpgradeIsServedOverHTTP1`). **Through the host tunnel too**
(#964): the tunnel's Host check passes the upgrade request on whole, `HTTP2-Settings` included,
and the connection after the front's 101 is copied both ways, its HTTP/2 requests not checked
one by one (no browser can ask for the switch: it cannot set those headers, and speaks HTTP/2
only over TLS); a foreign Host is still refused 421, and a port that does not take the upgrade
answers over HTTP/1.1 as before (unit `TestGuardPassesAnH2CUpgrade`,
`TestGuardChecksAnH2CUpgradeAndLeavesItToThePort`). `TestPubSubRESTOverH2CUpgrade` runs `curl
--http2` through the tunnel and gets 101, then HTTP/2 200 with the topic the official gRPC client
made. The
[subscription properties page](https://cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/subscription-properties) and
`Subscription.expiration_policy` in `google/pubsub/v1/pubsub.proto` are the rules it follows: a
subscription is active while a subscriber is "issuing operations on the subscription", "Examples of
subscriber activities include open connections, active pulls, or successful pushes", the default
ttl is 31 days, the minimum 1 day, a policy without a ttl never expires, and a ttl "must be at
least as long as the message retention duration". Neither source names a maximum, so none is
enforced. A second method on the same port, `cloudburrow.pubsub.v1.EmulatorClock/Advance`
(a `google.protobuf.Duration` in, the front's time out), moves the front's clock forward, so tests
pass a day in a moment instead of using a ttl Google refuses. It is not part of the Pub/Sub API,
and it grants nothing a caller could not already do, since the emulator has no authentication and
anyone who reaches the port can delete a subscription. Because the front runs from the storage
image, a Pub/Sub instance now needs a CLI with the embedded Linux binaries (`make build` or a
release), as Cloud Storage already did.

Since #880 the front also relays the emulator's pushes, refuses exactly-once delivery on a push or
export subscription (see the Exactly-once delivery row), and answers three more CloudBurrow
methods, for `cloudburrow state save` and `load`: `cloudburrow.pubsub.v1.SubscriptionActivity/Export`
and `/Import` read and set how long each subscription has been idle (a `google.protobuf.Struct` of
seconds by subscription name), and `cloudburrow.pubsub.v1.Projects/List` names every project a call
through the front has named (a `google.protobuf.ListValue`), since the emulator serves any project
and cannot list them. **The push relay:** the front hands the emulator, for every push
subscription, an endpoint of its own on the pod's loopback (port 8087) that carries the
subscription and the real endpoint, and puts the real endpoint back in every subscription the
emulator returns (`CreateSubscription`, `GetSubscription`, `UpdateSubscription`,
`ListSubscriptions`), so no client sees the relay's. Each push is forwarded to the real endpoint
with its method, headers and body, and the endpoint's status, headers and body are the emulator's
answer; one Google counts as a success (102, 200, 201, 202 or 204, from
[the push page](https://cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/push)) is activity on the subscription. An
endpoint the relay cannot reach is a 502 to the emulator, which retries it as it would any failed
push.

Since #891 the front also applies an `UpdateSubscription` of `expiration_policy`, which the
emulator refuses ("Updating the expiration_policy field is currently unsupported in the Pub/Sub
Emulator"). It takes the path out of the mask the emulator sees and forwards the rest; an update of
that path alone never reaches the emulator. The new ttl is checked by the same rules as on create
(at least 1 day, and no shorter than the message retention, the new one when the same update
changes it), then kept in the front, which puts it in every subscription it returns
(`GetSubscription`, `ListSubscriptions`, `UpdateSubscription`) and enforces it in place of the
emulator's stored policy. A `CreateSubscription` or `DeleteSubscription` of the name, or the
subscription's expiry, drops it. An update that names the path with no policy is read as a
subscription created without one: Google's 31-day default (CloudBurrow's reading; the API reference
does not say). `state save` reads through the front, so it saves the updated policy, and `state load`
creates the subscription with it, which the emulator then keeps (unit
`TestPubSubStateKeepsAnUpdatedExpiration`). **The kept policies survive a restart of the front's
container alone** (#898): the front keeps them, its clock's offset, each subscription's last
activity and the projects calls have named in a file on an `emptyDir` volume of the Pub/Sub pod
(`/var/lib/pubsub-front/state.json`, `--state-file`), restored when it starts. The volume outlives
the front's container and goes with the pod, as the emulator's resources do, so a pod restart
loses both together and a front restart loses neither. A policy change, a clock advance and an
activity import are written before the call is answered; activity and projects at most a second
later, and when the front stops, so a front killed outright loses at most a second of activity.
A file that cannot be read as state is logged and replaced, and the front starts without it.
`TestPubSubFrontRestartKeepsItsState` stops the front's container alone through the node's
container runtime, the emulator's untouched, and through the official client a subscription whose
1-day ttl was updated to 2 days still reads 2 days after the restart and is gone once idle for 2
days, one untouched since before the restart is gone 25 hours on, and the clock has not gone back
(unit `TestStateSurvivesARestartOfTheFront`, `TestStateDropsADeletedSubscriptionsPolicy`,
`TestKeepStateRefusesAnUnwritablePathAndReplacesAnUnreadableFile`). The labels an update set
(#949) are kept in the same file, written before the update is answered, and dropped with the
topic or subscription (unit `TestStateKeepsLabels`). **Over REST too** (#908): a `PATCH .../subscriptions/{s}` whose `updateMask` names
`expirationPolicy` (what `gcloud pubsub subscriptions update --expiration-period` sends) is
checked the same way, the path is taken out of the mask and the subscription the emulator is sent,
a PATCH of that path alone is answered by the front, and the kept policy is in every REST read
(`PUT`, `GET`, `PATCH .../subscriptions/{s}`, `GET .../subscriptions`); a `PUT` of the name drops
it. `TestGcloudPubSubUpdateExpiration`: `--expiration-period=12h` is refused; 3d and `never` are
applied, read back by `describe`, `list`, REST `GET` and the gRPC client, and enforced (with a
1-day ttl raised to 3 days, kept 25 hours on and gone once idle for 73; never, kept), and a plain
REST PATCH of the policy with the ack deadline applies both (unit
`TestRESTPatchSetsTheExpirationPolicy`, `TestRESTUpdateChecksThePolicy`). **A mask in the URL**
(#928): Terraform's google provider sends `?updateMask=expirationPolicy,bigqueryConfig`, camelCase,
in the URL, with `bigqueryConfig` in every update. The emulator reads a URL mask as one path, commas
and all ("ack_deadline_seconds,bigquery_config is not a known Subscription field", measured), and
refuses a `bigquery_config` path whatever it sets. The front reads the URL's mask with the body's,
applies its rules to both, and sends the emulator the rest in the body, snake_case, with no URL
mask; an export config path (`bigqueryConfig`, `cloudStorageConfig`, `bigtableConfig`) that sets
nothing on a subscription with none of that export is a no-op, and is left out.
`TestTerraformPubSubPushAndExpiration`: a `google_pubsub_subscription` with
`push_config.push_endpoint` and `expiration_policy.ttl` applies through `cloudburrow terraform`,
plans clean (the provider reads the real endpoint and the ttl; its state never names the relay),
takes a change of the ttl (applied by the front, the emulator sent nothing) and of the endpoint
(sent to the emulator as the relay's), each read back by the official client and planning clean,
and destroys (unit `TestRESTPatchReadsTheMaskInTheURL`). A `labels` change is still refused, by the
emulator, as over gRPC (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`).

**REST clients get the same** (#908). A push endpoint sent over REST, in `PUT
.../subscriptions/{s}` (`pushConfig.pushEndpoint`), in a `PATCH` whose `updateMask` names
`pushConfig`, or in `POST .../subscriptions/{s}:modifyPushConfig`, reaches the emulator as the
relay's, and the answers to `PUT`, `GET` and `PATCH .../subscriptions/{s}` and `GET
.../subscriptions` name the real one, with every other field of the JSON as the emulator sent it.
Exactly-once delivery with a push endpoint or an export is refused 400 `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on `PUT`,
`PATCH` and `:modifyPushConfig`, by the same checks as gRPC. Every `/v1/projects/{p}/...` path
records its project for `Projects/List`, so `cloudburrow state save` captures a project only
gcloud or Terraform used. `TestGcloudPubSubPushSubscription`: of four push subscriptions gcloud
makes with a 1-day ttl, the three given a storage-server upload endpoint by `subscriptions create
--push-endpoint`, `subscriptions update --push-endpoint` and `modify-push-config` are kept 25 hours
on with pushes at 0 and 20, and the one whose endpoint refuses its pushes is gone; `describe`,
`list` and the gRPC client read each real endpoint. `TestGcloudPubSubRefusesExactlyOnceWithPush`
and `TestPubSubRESTPushAndExactlyOnce` (plain REST, including a BigQuery export and an endpoint set
over gRPC read back over REST) assert the refusals and the reads. `TestGcloudPubSubStateSave`: a
project only gcloud used is saved, reset and loaded, and gcloud describes its subscriptions as they
were. Unit `TestRESTPushEndpointsGoToTheRelay`, `TestRESTReadsNameTheRealEndpoint`,
`TestRESTRefusesExactlyOnceWithPush`, `TestRESTProjectsAreListed`. **An answer over 4 MiB**
(#926), the most the front holds whole to rewrite, is streamed: a list of subscriptions is
rewritten one subscription at a time as it passes, and a single subscription (never that large)
is passed on as it came; it was a 503 before. An oversized answer that is not a list object ends
in a failed read, never a short answer that looks whole. `TestPubSubRESTListOverFourMiB`: 900
subscriptions with 64 labels each, made by the official client, list in one page of about 4.9 MB
with `pageSize=1000` (a plain REST GET, as no official client asks for a page that large), every one
in it and the push subscription's real endpoint named (unit `TestRESTOversizedAnswersPassThrough`,
`TestRewriteListKeepsEveryField`).

### Control plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Publisher.CreateTopic` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubTopicLifecycle`. A `message_retention_duration` and `schema_settings` given at creation read back as given, and a topic with a schema refuses a message that does not conform to it with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions`, #852). **Labels are held to Google's rules** by CloudBurrow's front (#962; the emulator checks none): see the Label rules row. |
| `Publisher.GetTopic` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubTopicLifecycle`, including `NotFound` for an absent topic. |
| `Publisher.ListTopics` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubListTopicsAndSubscriptions` |
| `Publisher.DeleteTopic` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubDeleteTopic`, including `NotFound` on a second delete rather than a silent success. |
| `Publisher.UpdateTopic` | Partial | `TestPubSubUpdateTopic`: a `message_retention_duration` mask is applied and survives a re-read. **A `labels` mask is applied by CloudBurrow's front** (#949): the emulator refuses it ("Invalid update_mask provided in the UpdateTopicRequest: labels is not a known Topic field."), so the front takes the path out of the mask the emulator sees, keeps the labels, and answers every `GetTopic`, `ListTopics` and `UpdateTopic` with them, over gRPC and REST. `TestPubSubUpdateLabels`, through the official client: labels alone, and labels with the retention in one update, read back by `GetTopic` and `ListTopics`; an update naming labels with none clears them; a missing topic is `NOT_FOUND`. Terraform's in-place label change on `google_pubsub_topic` applies and plans clean (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`). Unit `TestUpdateTopicSetsLabels` shows the emulator is never sent the path, and `TestRESTPatchSetsLabels` the same over REST. A `DeleteTopic` or `CreateTopic` of the name drops the kept labels; they survive a restart of the front alone and go with the pod (`TestStateKeepsLabels`). Label keys and values are checked against Google's rules since #962 (the Label rules row). A retention mask with no value sets the retention to 31 days rather than removing it, and 5m is refused "message_retention_duration out of bounds" (`TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription`, #786). |
| `Publisher.ListTopicSubscriptions` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubTopicAndSnapshotListings` |
| `Publisher.ListTopicSnapshots` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubTopicAndSnapshotListings` |
| `Publisher.DetachSubscription` | **Refused** | `TestPubSubDetachSubscriptionIsRefused`: `UNIMPLEMENTED` "Method google.pubsub.v1.Publisher/DetachSubscription is unimplemented". |
| `Subscriber.CreateSubscription` | **Verified** | Pull configuration, and a push endpoint read back as given (`TestPubSubPushSubscriptionExpiresWithoutSuccessfulPushes`, #880). `TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions` (#852) reads back an ack deadline, `message_retention_duration`, `retain_acked_messages`, `enable_message_ordering`, a `filter`, `retry_policy` and `dead_letter_policy` set at creation, and the filter acts: of three messages, only the matching one is delivered. 2 delivery attempts is refused `OUT_OF_RANGE`. **A malformed filter is refused `UNKNOWN` "Application error processing RPC"**, a code the official client retries, so with its default settings the call waits out its deadline; the console sends it once. `expiration_policy` is checked and enforced by CloudBurrow's front, not the emulator (#873): see the Subscription expiration row. **A subscription created with no policy reads back Google's 31-day default** (`TestPubSubSubscriptionExpiresWhenIdle`), since the front gives it one; the emulator alone would store none. `enable_exactly_once_delivery`: see the Exactly-once delivery row. **Labels are held to Google's rules** by CloudBurrow's front (#962): see the Label rules row. |
| `Subscriber.GetSubscription` | **Verified** | `TestSubscriptionGetUpdateDelete`. |
| `Subscriber.ListSubscriptions` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubListTopicsAndSubscriptions` |
| `Subscriber.DeleteSubscription` | **Verified** | Same test; a subsequent get returns `NOT_FOUND`. |
| `Subscriber.UpdateSubscription` | **Verified** | Same test, with a field mask. The change is re-read, because a response echoing the request proves nothing. `TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription` (#786) re-reads `ack_deadline_seconds`, `message_retention_duration`, `retain_acked_messages`, `push_config` (pull to push and back), `retry_policy` and `dead_letter_policy`, set and cleared. `enable_exactly_once_delivery` is applied in both directions and acted on: `TestPubSubExactlyOnceCanBeChanged` (#880) turns it on for a subscription created without it, after which a stale ack ID is refused `INVALID_ARGUMENT` with `PERMANENT_FAILURE_INVALID_ACK_ID`, and off again, after which the same stale acknowledgement is accepted. **Refused by the emulator**, `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and asserted with its message: `filter` ("Updating the filter field is currently unsupported in the Pub/Sub Emulator."). **A `labels` mask**, which the emulator refuses too ("labels is not a known Subscription field"), is applied by CloudBurrow's front (#949), as `expiration_policy` is: `TestPubSubUpdateLabels` updates labels alone and with the ack deadline, and `GetSubscription` and `ListSubscriptions` read them; an update naming labels with none clears them, and a missing subscription is `NOT_FOUND`. Unit `TestUpdateSetsLabels` shows the emulator is never sent the path, and that a delete, or a create of the name, drops the kept labels; `TestRESTPatchSetsLabels` does the same over REST, with Terraform's URL mask; Terraform's in-place label change on `google_pubsub_subscription` applies and plans clean (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`). Label keys and values are checked against Google's rules since #962 (the Label rules row). An ack deadline of 601 is refused "ack_deadline_secs out of bounds". **`expiration_policy`**, which the emulator refuses too, is applied by CloudBurrow's front (#891, above): `TestPubSubExpirationCanBeUpdated` updates a 1-day ttl to 3 days together with the ack deadline, and another to a policy without a ttl; `UpdateSubscription`, `GetSubscription` and `ListSubscriptions` read the new policies, the deadline is applied too, and a ttl of 12h, or of 2 days with a 3-day retention in the same update, is refused `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (error code UNVERIFIED: Google documents the rule, not the code). Unit `TestUpdateSetsTheExpirationPolicy` (`internal/pubsubfront`) shows the emulator is never sent the path. Over REST, from gcloud's `subscriptions update --expiration-period`: `TestGcloudPubSubUpdateExpiration` (#908). |
| Label rules (topics and subscriptions) | **Verified** (message UNVERIFIED) | CloudBurrow's front checks the labels of `CreateTopic`, `CreateSubscription`, and an `UpdateTopic` or `UpdateSubscription` whose mask names `labels`, over gRPC and REST (`PUT` and `PATCH` of `topics/{t}` and `subscriptions/{s}`), against Google's [labels requirements](https://cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/labels) (#962): at most 64 labels; a key 1 to 63 characters, a value 0 to 63; only lowercase letters, international characters, digits, `_` and `-`; a key starting with a lowercase letter or an international character. Read as Unicode categories: lowercase is `Ll`, international a letter with no case (`Lo`), a digit `N`; characters, not bytes, are counted. A label that breaks a rule is refused **`INVALID_ARGUMENT`** (HTTP 400), the code Google's [error codes](https://cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/reference/error-codes) give an invalid argument, before the emulator is sent anything; the message names the label and the rule, and is CloudBurrow's own: Google's message for it was not measured. The emulator checks no label at all. `TestPubSubLabelsAreHeldToGoogleRules`, through the official client and over REST: an uppercase key, a key starting with a digit, a value with a space and 65 labels are refused on create, an uppercase value and a dot in a key on update, and nothing is stored; 64 labels, 63-character keys and values, `größe=日本` and an empty value are created and read back. Unit `TestCheckLabels`, `TestGRPCRefusesLabelsGoogleRefuses`, `TestRESTRefusesLabelsGoogleRefuses`. The console holds its label fields to the same rules (the console rows). |
| `Subscriber.ModifyPushConfig` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubModifyPushConfig`: pull to push and back to pull, each re-read. Delivery to the endpoint is the separate push row below. |
| Schema service (`ValidateSchema`, `CreateSchema`, `GetSchema`, `ListSchemas`, `ValidateMessage`, `CommitSchema`, `ListSchemaRevisions`, `RollbackSchema`, `DeleteSchemaRevision`, `DeleteSchema`) | **Verified** | `TestPubSubSchemaService`, through the generated `apiv1.SchemaClient` (which honours `PUBSUB_EMULATOR_HOST`): all ten RPCs — `ValidateSchema` (a malformed definition is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`), `CreateSchema`, `GetSchema`, `ListSchemas`, `ValidateMessage` (a non-conforming message is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`), `CommitSchema`, `ListSchemaRevisions`, `RollbackSchema`, `DeleteSchemaRevision` and `DeleteSchema` (then `NOT_FOUND`), with an AVRO schema. A topic bound to a schema refuses a message that does not conform (`TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions`, #852). **Protocol Buffer schemas are refused**: `ValidateSchema` of type `PROTOCOL_BUFFER` answers `UNIMPLEMENTED` "Protocol buffer support not implemented in emulator", and the only revision cannot be deleted (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` "Cannot delete last revision. Please use DeleteSchema."), both asserted by `TestConsolePubSubSchemas`. |
| Snapshots (`Subscriber.CreateSnapshot`, `Subscriber.GetSnapshot`, `Subscriber.DeleteSnapshot`) / `Seek` | **Verified** | `TestSnapshotsAndSeekAreSupported` — create, get, seek-to-snapshot and delete. **The matrix was wrong**: a test written to confirm these were refused found they work. That test does not show replay; `TestConsolePubSubSnapshotsAndSeek` (#787) does, through the official client: after two messages are pulled and acknowledged, a Seek to a snapshot taken while the first was waiting makes the next pull receive both again, and a Seek to a time after them marks every message acknowledged. A Seek to a snapshot of another topic is refused `FAILED_PRECONDITION`. **`CreateSnapshot` does not keep labels**: a snapshot created with labels reads back with none (same test). |
| `Subscriber.ListSnapshots` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubTopicAndSnapshotListings` |
| `Subscriber.UpdateSnapshot` | **Refused** | `TestPubSubUpdateSnapshotIsRefused`: `UNIMPLEMENTED` "Method google.pubsub.v1.Subscriber/UpdateSnapshot is unimplemented". |

### Data plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Publisher.Publish` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubPublishAndPull`, data and attributes both asserted. |
| `Subscriber.Pull` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubPublishAndPull` |
| `Subscriber.StreamingPull` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubStreamingPull` delivers 20 distinct messages through the SDK's default `Receive` path. |
| `Acknowledge` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubPublishAndPull` |
| `ModifyAckDeadline` | **Verified** | `TestPubSubRedeliveryAfterNack` |
| Ack-deadline expiry and redelivery | **Verified** | `TestPubSubRedeliveryAfterNack`: returning the deadline redelivers the message. At-least-once, so duplicates remain possible by design. |
| Push delivery to HTTP endpoint | **Verified** | The acceptance workflow pushes to a Cloud Run service at its cluster-local address. `TestPubSubPushSubscriptionExpiresWithoutSuccessfulPushes` (#880) pushes to the builtin storage server's upload endpoint by its Service name, which stores the push envelope the test reads back. Every push passes through the front's relay (see CloudBurrow's front). |
| Ordering keys | **Verified** | `TestPubSubOrderingKeys`: order preserved within a single key. Ordering *across* keys is not claimed. |
| Dead-letter topics | **Verified** | `TestDeadLetterPolicyIsRecorded`: the policy round-trips, topic and max attempts included. `TestPubSubDeadLetterDelivery`: with `max_delivery_attempts=5`, a message nacked on each delivery is delivered with `delivery_attempt` 1 to 5, then appears on the dead-letter topic's subscription with its data and `CloudPubSubDeadLetterSourceDeliveryCount=5`, in under a second. |
| Subscription expiration | **Verified** | Enforced by CloudBurrow's front (above), not the emulator. `TestPubSubSubscriptionExpiresWhenIdle` (#873): with a 1-day ttl, a subscription nothing touches is gone from `ListSubscriptions` 25 hours after creation and `GetSubscription` answers `NOT_FOUND`; one pulled at 20 hours is kept at 25 and gone at 45; one with a streaming pull open throughout is kept at 45; one whose policy has no ttl, and one created with no policy (which reads back a 31-day ttl), are kept. Time passes through the front's clock. `TestPubSubRefusesAnExpirationGoogleRefuses`: a ttl of 12h, a 1-day ttl with the default 7-day retention, and a 2-day ttl with a 3-day retention are each refused `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and create nothing (error code UNVERIFIED: Google documents the rule, not the code); a 1-day ttl with a 1-day retention is created. Unit tests in `internal/pubsubfront` also cover an `UpdateSubscription` that would raise the retention above the ttl (refused) and the 31-day default expiring. **Push subscriptions** (#880): the front relays the emulator's pushes (above), so a successful push is activity. `TestPubSubPushSubscriptionExpiresWithoutSuccessfulPushes`: of two push subscriptions with a 1-day ttl, the one whose endpoint (the builtin storage server's upload endpoint, in the cluster) stores its pushes, and so answers 200, is kept 25 hours after creation with a push at 20; the one whose endpoint refuses them (404) is gone. The same holds for push subscriptions gcloud makes over REST (`TestGcloudPubSubPushSubscription`, #908). The endpoint receives the emulator's push envelope unchanged, and `GetSubscription` and `ListSubscriptions` read the real endpoint, never the relay's (unit `TestPushRelayObservesPushes`, `TestPushRelayRefusesOtherAddresses`). **Gaps:** activity is any call naming the subscription except a read of its configuration (`GetSubscription` and its REST `GET`, which are not activity since #1039: Google names "open connections, active pulls, or successful pushes") (update, pull, streaming pull, acknowledge, modify ack deadline, modify push config, seek, create snapshot), which may count more as activity than Google does; a push subscription with no messages to push has no activity but calls, as on Google; the front's clock, its activity records and its kept policies survive a restart of the front's container alone (#898, `TestPubSubFrontRestartKeepsItsState`, above), a front killed outright losing at most the last second of activity, and are lost with the pod, as the emulator's state is. **An updated policy is enforced** (#891): in `TestPubSubExpirationCanBeUpdated` a subscription created with a 1-day ttl and updated to 3 days is kept 25 hours later and gone once idle for 73, and one updated to never expire is kept (unit `TestUpdatedExpirationIsEnforced`). |
| Exactly-once delivery | **Verified** | Implemented by the emulator itself; the front passes it through. `TestPubSubExactlyOnceDelivery` (#873), through the official client on a subscription with `enable_exactly_once_delivery` and a 10s deadline: `AckWithResult` reports `AcknowledgeStatusSuccess` and the acknowledged message is not delivered again in the 15 seconds after; a message left unacknowledged is not resent before its deadline, is resent with a new ack ID after it, and `Acknowledge` with the old ack ID is refused `INVALID_ARGUMENT` whose `ErrorInfo` marks it `PERMANENT_FAILURE_INVALID_ACK_ID`, as [Google's exactly-once page](https://cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/exactly-once-delivery) describes, while the new ack ID is accepted. **With a push endpoint, refused** (#880): Google says "Push and export subscriptions don't support exactly-once delivery", and the emulator accepts the pair, so the front refuses it `INVALID_ARGUMENT` ("exactly-once delivery is supported only for pull subscriptions: push and export subscriptions don't support exactly-once delivery") on `CreateSubscription`, on an `UpdateSubscription` whose result would have both, and on `ModifyPushConfig` giving an exactly-once subscription an endpoint, changing nothing (`TestPubSubRefusesExactlyOnceWithPush`; over REST, from gcloud and plain HTTP, `TestGcloudPubSubRefusesExactlyOnceWithPush` and `TestPubSubRESTPushAndExactlyOnce`, #908; error code and message UNVERIFIED: Google documents the rule, not the error). A BigQuery, Cloud Storage or Bigtable export with it is refused the same way (unit `TestExactlyOnceIsRefusedWithPush`). Changing it on an existing subscription: see `UpdateSubscription`. |

---

## Cloud Tasks — `google.cloud.tasks.v2`

**Backing component:** none — implemented by CloudBurrow. No official emulator exists and no
viable community implementation was found (#24).

**It runs in the CLI process, not the cluster**, since there is no upstream workload to
deploy. Its dispatch targets are therefore addresses reachable from the host. There is **no
emulator environment variable** in any official client, so the endpoint and insecure
credentials must be passed explicitly in client options — see `test/compat/tasks_test.go`
for the exact form.
Contract: `google/cloud/tasks/v2/cloudtasks.proto`.
**No emulator environment variable exists.** Callers must set an explicit endpoint and
disable authentication in client options. This is a documented ergonomic limit.

### Control plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CreateQueue` | **Verified** | `TestTasksQueueLifecycle`, via the official `cloudtasks/apiv2` client. Contract defaults are returned, each `retry_config` and `rate_limits` field defaulting on its own: before #692 a queue given only `max_dispatches_per_second` had it replaced by the default. |
| `GetQueue` | **Verified** | `TestTasksQueueLifecycle`, including `NotFound` for an absent queue. |
| `ListQueues` | **Verified** | `TestTasksListQueues`; pagination covered by unit tests. A **`filter` is `UNIMPLEMENTED`** rather than ignored, which would return every queue (`TestTasksRefusesWhatItWouldDrop`, #578). |
| `retry_config.max_retry_duration` | **Verified** | Stored, returned, and **honoured**: a task is retried until both it and `max_attempts` are exhausted, measured from its first attempt, as `RetryConfig` documents (`TestTasksRefusesWhatItWouldDrop`, unit `TestMaxRetryDurationExtendsRetryingUntilBothLimitsAreReached`, #578). |
| `stackdriver_logging_config` | **Not supported, refused** | **`UNIMPLEMENTED`** naming the field, on `CreateQueue` and on an `UpdateQueue` whose mask names it: no dispatch is written to Cloud Logging. Attempts are in the console's operations ledger and `/admin/events`. |
| `app_engine_routing_override` | **Not supported, refused** | **`UNIMPLEMENTED`** naming the field when it is set, on `CreateQueue` and `UpdateQueue` (#692): App Engine targets are not supported. Before, `CreateQueue` accepted and dropped it. |
