| `DeleteQueue` | **Verified** | Removes the queue's tasks too, so a recreated queue cannot inherit them. |
| `UpdateQueue` | **Verified** | `TestTasksUpdateQueue` (#692), via the official client: a mask of `retry_config.max_attempts` and `rate_limits` changes those and keeps the rest (`min_backoff` stays), and `GetQueue` reads them back; `gcloud tasks queues update --max-attempts --max-concurrent-dispatches` and Terraform's in-place change of `google_cloud_tasks_queue` do the same over JSON (`TestGcloudTasks`, `TestTerraformAppliesAndDestroysThroughTheWrapper`). **Mask semantics:** a path replaces the field it names, a whole message (`retry_config`) or one field of it (`retry_config.max_attempts`); an **empty mask replaces every settable field**, as the API documents, so a field the request leaves out is cleared and then defaulted as on create. Each field defaults on its own. A path naming `name`, an output-only field (`state`, `purge_time`, `rate_limits.max_burst_size`) or no field is **`INVALID_ARGUMENT` naming the path**; `state` is changed by `PauseQueue`/`ResumeQueue` only, and a `state` in the body is ignored. The fields `CreateQueue` refuses are refused when the mask names them: `stackdriver_logging_config` and `app_engine_routing_override` are **`UNIMPLEMENTED`**, as is `http_target`, which the contract this server is built from does not have. **A queue that does not exist is created**, as the method documents ("creates the queue if it does not exist"), with `CreateQueue`'s name rules. The dispatcher reads the stored queue on every pass and retry, so an update applies to tasks already queued: a failing task in a queue updated from 5 attempts to 2 stops at 2 (same test; unit `TestAnUpdatedRetryConfigChangesRetrying`). Queues have no etag. **Documented limits are refused** (#784), on `CreateQueue` and on an `UpdateQueue` whose mask names the field: `max_attempts` below -1 ("Must be >= -1"), `max_dispatches_per_second` above 500 and `max_concurrent_dispatches` above 5000 (the contract's "maximum allowed value"), and a negative `min_backoff`, `max_backoff`, `max_doublings` or `max_retry_duration`, each `INVALID_ARGUMENT` naming the field; before, a value out of range was stored or silently replaced by the default (unit `TestQueueLimitsTheContractDocumentsAreRefused`; through the console, `TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAndCreateTask`). |
| `PauseQueue` / `ResumeQueue` | **Verified** | `TestTasksPauseAndResume`. A paused queue genuinely stops dispatching. |
| `PurgeQueue` | **Verified** | `TestTasksPurgeQueue`: every task in the queue goes and no other queue's, the queue and its state stay, it takes tasks again, and `purge_time` is reported (#691). A missing queue is `NotFound`. |
| `CreateTask` | **Verified** | `TestTasksTaskLifecycle`. Name generation, method, body and headers all round-trip. |
| `GetTask` / `DeleteTask` | **Verified** | `TestTasksTaskLifecycle`. |
| `ListTasks` | **Verified** | `TestGcloudTasks`: `gcloud tasks list` over JSON (#591); also gRPC unit tests. |
| Durability | **Verified, measured** | Queues and tasks follow `--mode`: a store under the state directory in persistent mode, memory in ephemeral mode. CI's restart probe (#596): `TestTasksRestartSetup` runs alone just before `stop` and leaves a paused queue holding a task scheduled a day ahead. After `stop` and `up` in persistent mode, `TestTasksAcrossRestart` finds the queue still `PAUSED` and the task's body and `scheduleTime` unchanged; brought up again with `--mode ephemeral`, `GetQueue` is **NOT_FOUND**. |
| `GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy` on a queue | **Verified**, *stored, not enforced* | `TestQueueIamPolicyIsStoredNotEnforced` (#366, [ADR-0006](adr/0006-iam-policy-surface.md)): bindings set through the official client read back with a new etag, and a stale etag is **ABORTED**. **No RPC consults a stored policy**: a binding grants and denies nothing, and dispatch is unaffected. Conditions and audit configs are **UNIMPLEMENTED** with the field named. A version 3 policy without conditions is accepted. The policy is kept on the queue, so it goes with `DeleteQueue`, is cleared by `/admin/reset` and is captured by `state save`. From the console's Permissions tab: `TestConsolePermissionsOnAQueue` (#793). |
| `TestIamPermissions` on a queue | **Verified**, *stored, not enforced* | Same test: returns **every** requested permission, the literal truth when nothing is enforced. |
| JSON API (`/v2/…/queues`) | **Partial** | On the same port as gRPC, for REST clients such as Terraform (#366). Queues are hand-written routes: create, get, list, delete, pause, resume and purge, and their IAM methods (`:getIamPolicy` by GET or POST), each calling the gRPC server; on them an unknown query parameter is ignored. **`PATCH` (UpdateQueue) is transcoded** (#692), by Google's binding `PATCH /v2/{queue.name=projects/*/locations/*/queues/*}`: `updateMask` is a lowerCamelCase FieldMask converted to proto paths, the path's name wins over the body's, and an unknown query parameter is INVALID_ARGUMENT. Unknown JSON fields are refused. Unit-tested (`TestRESTQueueAndIamPolicy`, `TestRESTUpdateQueue`) and driven by Terraform and gcloud (below). |
| JSON API, tasks (`/v2/…/queues/*/tasks`) | **Verified** | Since #591, through the shared `Transcoder` by Google's own `google.api.http` bindings, with the same `GRPCServer` gRPC registers: CreateTask, ListTasks, GetTask and DeleteTask. RunTask answers **501 UNIMPLEMENTED**, as over gRPC. The transcoder's query policy applies to task paths and the queue `PATCH` only: `pageSize`, `pageToken` and `responseView` are fields, and an unknown parameter is INVALID_ARGUMENT, where the queue routes ignore it. The official client's REST transport drives a task's lifecycle (unit `TestRESTTasksWithTheOfficialRESTClient`, `TestRESTTaskPathsRefuseUnknownParameters`), and `TestGcloudTasks` does it with the real gcloud. JSON requests, queues and tasks, appear in `/admin/events`. |

### Data plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| HTTP target dispatch | **Verified** | `TestTasksHTTPDispatchCarriesCloudTasksHeaders`: a task created with the official client reaches a host HTTP server with its method, body and headers, and with `X-CloudTasks-QueueName`, `-TaskName`, `-TaskRetryCount` and `-TaskExecutionCount`. The names are **short IDs**, as the service sends them; full resource names were sent until #276. Non-2xx is retried, matching the real service including 4xx. **`X-CloudTasks-TaskETA`, `-TaskPreviousResponse` and `-TaskRetryReason` are not set.** |
| `oidcToken` / `oauthToken` on an HTTP task | **Not supported, refused** | **`UNIMPLEMENTED`** naming the field (`TestTasksRefusesWhatItWouldDrop`, #578). Before, the task was dispatched with no `Authorization` header. Cloud Scheduler refuses the same. To reach a target that checks a token, set the header in `httpRequest.headers`. |
| A body on a `GET`, `HEAD`, `DELETE` or `OPTIONS` task | **Verified** | **`INVALID_ARGUMENT`**, as Google; same test. |
| `dispatch_deadline` | **Verified** | Validated (15 s to 30 min, else **`INVALID_ARGUMENT`**), stored and returned by `GetTask` (same test), and **applied to each attempt**: an attempt runs under the task's deadline, or Google's default of 10 minutes, and one that runs past it is a failed attempt recorded at the deadline (unit `TestAttemptsRunUnderTheTaskDeadline`, #579). Before, every attempt was cut off at a fixed 30 s. |
| App Engine target dispatch | **Verified unsupported** | `TestTasksUnsupportedOperationsAreHonest`: returns `Unimplemented` rather than accepting a task that would never be dispatched. |
| Scheduled execution at `scheduleTime` | **Verified** | `TestTasksScheduleTimeIsHonoured`: a task scheduled 3s ahead is not delivered early, and is delivered within 3s of its time. The dispatcher polls every 200ms, so delivery can trail the time by that much. |
| Retry with backoff | **Verified** | `TestTasksAFailingTargetIsRetriedPerRetryConfig`: a target answering 500 is retried after `minBackoff`, then after the doubled delay capped at `maxBackoff`, and dispatch stops at `maxAttempts`. **`maxDoublings` is modelled** as the service documents it: double that many times, then grow linearly (`TestBackoffGrowsLinearlyAfterMaxDoublings`, `TestTheWorkerReschedulesOnTheLinearPhase`). Each `RetryConfig` field defaults on its own, as in the API, and `maxAttempts: -1` is unlimited. Driven by the queue's own `RetryConfig`, distinct from Pub/Sub redelivery. Exhausted tasks are dropped, as the real service does. |
| `RunTask` (forced immediate run) | **Verified unsupported** | Returns `Unimplemented` through the SDK (`TestTasksUnsupportedOperationsAreHonest`). |
| Concurrent dispatch, `max_concurrent_dispatches` | **Verified** | Attempts run concurrently, across queues and within one, bounded per queue by `max_concurrent_dispatches` (default 1000); a task is never dispatched again while an attempt is out (unit `TestQueuesDispatchConcurrently`, `TestMaxConcurrentDispatchesBoundsAQueue`, #579). Before, dispatch was serial, so one slow handler held up every queue. |
| `max_dispatches_per_second` | **Verified** | **Enforced** with a token bucket per queue, refilled at the queue's rate (default 500/s) and holding up to `max_burst_size` tokens; each dispatch, first attempt or retry, takes one, and the queue's other due tasks wait for the next (#716). `TestTasksMaxDispatchesPerSecondSpacesDeliveries`: a queue created with the official client at 1/s delivers 3 tasks enqueued together at least a second apart, over at least 2 seconds (measured at the target, with 50ms allowed for loopback jitter); without enforcement they arrived microseconds apart. Unit tests on a fake clock cover the spacing, a rate below 1/s, a burst followed by the rate, an idle queue banking no more than its burst, and queues not sharing a bucket (`TestMaxDispatchesPerSecondSpacesDispatches`, `TestAFractionalRateSpacesDispatchesBeyondASecond`, `TestMaxBurstSizeBoundsABurstThenTheRateApplies`, `TestAnIdleQueueBanksNoMoreThanItsBurst`, `TestEachQueueHasItsOwnBucket`). The worker polls every 200ms, so a dispatch can trail its token by that much. Before, the rate was stored and returned but not enforced. |
| `max_burst_size` | **Verified**, CloudBurrow's own value | Output only, and enforced as the bucket's size (#716). Google picks it "based on the value of `max_dispatches_per_second`" and does not document the rule, so the value is **CloudBurrow's choice, not Google's**: one second of the rate, rounded up, at least 1 (500 at the default rate, 1 at 1/s or less). A queue on Google may report and allow a different burst. `GetQueue` returns it (`TestTasksMaxDispatchesPerSecondSpacesDeliveries`, unit `TestBurstSizeIsOneSecondOfTheRate`); before, it was returned as 0. |
| Task name reuse | **Verified** | A task name that was executed, deleted or dropped after its last attempt is **`ALREADY_EXISTS`** for an hour, Cloud Tasks' "approximately one hour", which applications use to de-duplicate enqueues (`TestTasksExecutedNamesAreTombstoned`, unit `TestTaskNamesAreTombstoned`, #579). Deleting the queue, which `/admin/reset` does, clears them. |
| Queue and task IDs | **Verified** | Cloud Tasks' own rules: a queue ID is letters, digits and hyphens, at most 100; a task ID adds underscores, at most 500; anything else is **`INVALID_ARGUMENT`** (unit `TestCloudTasksIDRulesAndMissingQueue`). `ListTasks` on a queue that does not exist is **`NOT_FOUND`**, not an empty page (`TestTasksExecutedNamesAreTombstoned`). |

---

## Cloud Run — `google.cloud.run.v2`

**Backing component:** Knative Serving v1.23.0, behind a CloudBurrow-owned Cloud Run v2
adapter. Contract: `google/cloud/run/v2/*.proto`. Admin API is REST. No emulator environment
variable. Requires a local Kubernetes cluster; its absence is a clear capability error, not a
silent degradation.

> **Knative is not Cloud Run.** It is the closest available model. No blanket claim is made
> that it reproduces Cloud Run semantics. Revision traffic and scaling behavior are mapped and
> tested explicitly in #30; whatever is not tested there is not claimed here.
>
> The adapter **refuses configuration it cannot map** — service accounts, VPC access, volumes,
> encryption keys, binary authorization, `template.executionEnvironment`,
> `template.sessionAffinity` and traffic splitting all return `Unimplemented` with the field
> named. A caller who set one of these and had it silently dropped would believe it took
> effect.
>
> Secret-backed environment is **not** in that list and was listed there in error:
> `secretKeyRef` is mapped and tested — see the Secret Manager section's
> `TestCloudRunRevisionReadsASecretManagerSecret`, which also asserts the payload does not
> appear in the deployed Knative Service.

### Control plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CreateService` | **Verified** | `TestRunServiceLifecycle`: deploys a real container through the official SDK and returns a long-running operation. |
| `GetService` | **Verified** | `TestRunServiceLifecycle`, including `NotFound` for an absent service. |
| `ListServices` | **Verified** | `TestRunServiceLifecycle`. Only CloudBurrow-owned Services are listed. |
| `DeleteService` | **Verified** | Refuses to delete a Knative Service CloudBurrow did not create. |
| `UpdateService` | **Verified** | `TestRunUpdateService` (#300): changing the image and an env var applies the Knative template, which cuts a new revision. The operation completes only when Knative has observed the new generation and **that** revision is ready, not while the old revision still reports Ready. The URL then serves the new version, and `ListRevisions` returns both revisions, newest first. A failed new revision fails the operation with the revision's own reason. The whole service is replaced, as `gcloud run deploy` and Terraform send it; a partial `update_mask` is UNIMPLEMENTED rather than honoured partly. `allow_missing` creates and `validate_only` applies nothing. |
| Traffic | **Partial** | Only 100% to the latest revision is accepted. Any split, a pinned revision, a percentage below 100 or a tag is UNIMPLEMENTED naming `traffic` (tested on create and update). |
| `GetRevision`, `ListRevisions`, `DeleteRevision` | **Verified** | `TestRunRevisions` (#299), official `run.RevisionsClient` against Knative in CI. Knative Revisions are mapped onto `run.v2.Revision`: the name under the caller's service, the service, the generation (Knative's `configurationGeneration`: 1 for the first deploy), create time, uid, containers (image, env, ports), concurrency and every condition. A new service has exactly one revision; an unknown one is NOT_FOUND. **Deleting a revision that serves traffic is refused** with FAILED_PRECONDITION, as Cloud Run refuses it; a retired revision is deleted. Revision fields Knative has no counterpart for (scaling, VPC access, encryption, execution environment) are absent rather than invented. |
| Operations (`google.longrunning`) | **Verified** | `GetOperation` backs the SDK's `op.Wait`. Pending, succeeded and failed are all reachable; a failed revision reports Knative's own message. |
| JSON API (REST) | **Verified** | **One port serves gRPC and JSON** (#591), as `run.googleapis.com` does: the shared `Transcoder` in `internal/transport/rest` serves Google's own `google.api.http` bindings of every `google.cloud.run.v2` service, plus the four Operations bindings of `run_v2.yaml` (`GetOperation`, `DeleteOperation`, `ListOperations`, `:wait`), and calls the same registered servers gRPC does. Services, Revisions, Jobs, Executions and Operations are transcoded; a bound path of Builds, Instances, Tasks or WorkerPools, and `ListOperations` and `WaitOperation`, answer **501 UNIMPLEMENTED**; a path Google does not bind answers 404. The query-parameter policy is KMS's: request fields the path and body do not bind (`serviceId`, `jobId`, `validateOnly`, `allowMissing`, `etag`, `pageSize`, ...) are query parameters, and an unknown one is INVALID_ARGUMENT. `TestTerraformRun` drives it with Terraform's provider (below); unit `TestRESTJobLifecycle` and `TestRESTUnservedPaths` pin the wire. JSON requests appear in `/admin/events` with their project; fault injection and the gRPC request log are not interposed on them. |
| Terraform `google_cloud_run_v2_service`, `google_cloud_run_v2_job` | **Verified** | `TestTerraformRun` (#591): both apply through `cloudburrow terraform` with `hashicorp/google` 8.4.0 and egress blocked, plan clean, take a label and an environment-variable change as a `PATCH` each, plan clean again, and destroy; each step is read back through the official gRPC clients, and the event log shows the creates, operation polls and deletes arrived as JSON. The wrapper sets `cloud_run_v2_custom_endpoint`. Only what the adapter maps is accepted: JSON reaches the same server methods as gRPC, so a refused field (a service account, VPC access, volumes, a traffic split) is UNIMPLEMENTED naming it; no module setting one has been applied. |
| `gcloud run services list` / `describe` | **Not supported** | Measured with gcloud and `CLOUDSDK_API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDES_RUN`: these commands use Cloud Run's **v1** API (`/apis/serving.knative.dev/v1/namespaces/<project>/services`), which is not served, and prefix the region to the override's host (`us-central1-127.0.0.1`), which does not resolve. `gcloud-setup` writes no `run` override, so gcloud is not pointed at an endpoint that cannot answer it. |
| IAM methods | Planned | Non-goal. |

### Data plane

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Pull image and start container | **Verified** | Prebuilt images only. Local images are rewritten to `dev.local/` with `imagePullPolicy: Never`; untagged references are refused. |
| Readiness detection | **Verified** | Reported from Knative's `Ready` condition, including the failure message. A service still reconciling is pending, not failed. |
| Request routing to container | **Verified** | The acceptance workflow delivers a Pub/Sub push to a deployed Cloud Run service, which reads and writes Cloud Storage. |
| Injected environment (endpoints, `PORT`) | **Verified** | `TestCloudRunRevisionReachesStorageAndPubSubWithNoClientOptions` (#576): a service deployed with no `env` calls `storage.NewClient(ctx)` and `pubsub.NewClient(ctx, project)` with no options, lists a bucket and publishes to a topic, and the host pulls the message. `TestCloudRunRevisionUsesInjectedMetadataADCAndServedEndpoints` (#681): a service deployed with no `env` gets credentials from `google.FindDefaultCredentials`, which finds no file and asks the metadata server at the injected `GCE_METADATA_HOST`; an official Storage client sends that local token on a bucket listing. The same revision calls KMS `Encrypt`, Scheduler `ListJobs` and Logging `WriteLogEntries` at the injected `CLOUDBURROW_KMS_ENDPOINT`, `CLOUDBURROW_SCHEDULER_ENDPOINT` and `CLOUDBURROW_LOGGING_ENDPOINT`, and the host decrypts the ciphertext, finds its job in the listing and lists the entry. Every revision and job task is given the in-cluster `*_EMULATOR_HOST` of each enabled emulator, `CLOUDBURROW_*_ENDPOINT` and `GCE_METADATA_HOST` at `cloudburrow-host` (with Cloud Run, [networking.md](networking.md#reaching-your-machine-from-a-pod)), and `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` (the service's project). **Exercised from a pod, through the injected value:** `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST`, `PUBSUB_EMULATOR_HOST`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, `GCE_METADATA_HOST`, `CLOUDBURROW_KMS_ENDPOINT`, `CLOUDBURROW_SCHEDULER_ENDPOINT`, `CLOUDBURROW_LOGGING_ENDPOINT` and `CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_ENDPOINT`. `TestCloudRunRevisionGetsBigQueryRefusalsThroughTheFront` (#874, #902) calls BigQuery at the injected `CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_ENDPOINT`, the emulator's Service, whose REST port is the validating front in the emulator's pod. **Injected but not exercised from a pod:** `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`, `DATASTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`, `BIGTABLE_EMULATOR_HOST`, `SPANNER_EMULATOR_HOST` (the Spanner pod test sets its own), `CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_STORAGE_ENDPOINT`, and `CLOUDBURROW_TASKS_ENDPOINT`, `CLOUDBURROW_SECRETMANAGER_ENDPOINT` and `CLOUDBURROW_RESOURCEMANAGER_ENDPOINT` (the host probe is given its addresses); no CI shard enables Cloud Run with the opt-in emulators other than BigQuery (the run shard, #874). **A variable the caller sets wins.** In the pod the injected ones follow the caller's, each in name order. The Cloud Run API (`GetService`, `ListServices`, `GetRevision`, jobs and executions) shows **only the caller's** env, as Cloud Run keeps `K_SERVICE` and `PORT` out of the resource, so a Terraform plan sees no drift; `kubectl get ksvc -o yaml` shows the injected ones. No credential is injected. `PORT` is Knative's. The caller's `env` is still mapped and ordered deterministically. |
| Logs | Planned | Not served through the Cloud Run API. |
| Stop and cleanup | **Verified** | Deletion refuses any Knative Service lacking `cloudburrow.dev/owned=true`. |
| Scale to keep an instance warm (`minInstanceCount`) | **Verified** | `TestMinScaleKeepsAnInstanceWarm`: a pod stays running with no traffic. |
| Requests per instance (`maxInstanceRequestConcurrency`) | Partial | Mapped to Knative's `containerConcurrency` and readable on the console's Configuration tab. **Not tested under load** — proving a concurrency limit needs sustained traffic. |
| Request timeout (`template.timeout`) | Partial | Mapped to Knative's `timeoutSeconds` and asserted by `TestTimeoutReachesKnative`. It was previously **dropped in silence**, so a service deployed with a ten-minute timeout got Knative's default and failed at five with nothing to explain it. The mapping is asserted; the resulting behaviour under a long request is not. |
| Resource limits (`container.resources.limits`) | Partial | CPU and memory limits reach the manifest. Whether the kubelet enforces them identically to Cloud Run is not claimed. |
| Scale to zero | **Verified** | `TestScaleToZeroHappensWithoutTraffic`, observed on Knative's own schedule. Cloud Run also scales to zero but on a different schedule; the timing is **not** claimed to match. |
| Max instances (`maxInstanceCount`) | Partial | `TestMaxScaleIsRecordedOnTheRevision` proves the annotation reaches the revision. **The cap is not tested under load** — proving a ceiling needs sustained concurrency that would make the suite slow and flaky. |
| Traffic to the latest revision | **Verified** | `TestSingleRevisionTakesAllTraffic`: 100% to the latest revision. |
| Traffic splitting across revisions | **Verified unsupported** | Reported as `Unimplemented` rather than silently ignored. |
| Service name validated before apply | **Verified** | An invalid name previously reached the cluster and came back as `Internal` — which says the fault is ours when it is the caller's, and buries the rule. Now `InvalidArgument` naming the rule. A cluster rejection is also classified as `InvalidArgument` rather than `Internal`, and carries the cluster's own message. |
| Jobs / Executions | **Verified** | Served on Kubernetes `batch/v1` Jobs (#582); see [Jobs and executions](#cloud-run-jobs-and-executions) below. |
| **Service URL reachable from a browser** | **Verified** | `cloudburrow up` publishes the Knative gateway on a host port (default `9080`) and names services `<service>.<namespace>.cloudburrow.localhost`. Verified with headless Chrome and `test/k8s/ingress_test.go`. **Loopback only**, HTTP only. The mapping is fixed at cluster creation, and `up` reports when a cluster predates it rather than leaving a port that silently refuses. |
| Service URL DNS resolution | Partial | `*.cloudburrow.localhost` resolves on the macOS system resolver and musl, **not** on bare glibc or **Go's pure resolver** (`CGO_ENABLED=0`), which forwards the query to the configured nameserver. The `Host`-header path needs no resolver and always works. Measured matrix in [networking.md](networking.md). |
| HTTPS to a service | **Not supported** | No certificate is issued. Publishing a port answering with a self-signed certificate would look like support for something that does not work. |

### Cloud Run: Jobs and executions

`google.cloud.run.v2.Jobs` and `google.cloud.run.v2.Executions` are served on the Cloud Run port
over gRPC (#582). A job is configuration, stored as a ConfigMap the adapter owns
(`run-job-<id>`, labelled `cloudburrow.dev/owned=true`, the job kept as protojson); each
`RunJob` creates one `batch/v1` Job, which is the execution. Its containers are rendered by
**the same code as a service's revision**: image localisation and pull policy, untagged images
refused, `command`, `args`, `workingDir`, `env`, `secretKeyRef` resolved to the Secret Manager
store's Kubernetes Secret, and `resources.limits`. They are served over JSON too, on the same port (#591): Terraform's
`google_cloud_run_v2_job` applies, updates and destroys (`TestTerraformRun`). `gcloud run jobs`
uses Cloud Run's v1 API, which is not served. Instances, Tasks, Builds and WorkerPools are not
registered.

| Operation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CreateJob`, `GetJob`, `ListJobs`, `UpdateJob`, `DeleteJob` | **Verified** | `TestRunJobLifecycle`, with the official `run.JobsClient`. Each returns a long-running operation that is done at once: the job is ready when it is written. Unset task fields read back with Cloud Run's documented defaults (1 task, 3 retries, a 10-minute timeout; asserted in unit `TestJobRunsAsABatchJob`). `etag` is the ConfigMap's `resourceVersion`, and a stale one on update, delete or run is **`ABORTED`**, as for services (not measured against Google). `UpdateJob` replaces the whole job and bumps `generation`; `allow_missing` creates; `validate_only` writes nothing (unit, on create). `DeleteJob` deletes every execution of the job too. A job under another project or location is NOT_FOUND. `executionCount` counts the executions that **still exist**; Cloud Run may keep counting deleted ones. |
| `RunJob` | **Verified** | `TestRunJobLifecycle`: a two-task job runs as one batch Job with `completions: 2` and `completionMode: Indexed` (asserted on the cluster), each task printing its own `CLOUD_RUN_TASK_INDEX`. `task_count` is `completions`, `parallelism` is `parallelism` (0 runs every task at once), `max_retries` is `backoffLimitPerIndex` (retries per task, as Cloud Run counts them), and the task `timeout` is each pod's `activeDeadlineSeconds` (per attempt). `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, `CLOUD_RUN_EXECUTION`, `CLOUD_RUN_TASK_INDEX` and `CLOUD_RUN_TASK_COUNT` are set; **`CLOUD_RUN_TASK_ATTEMPT` is not**, since no pod field counts its retries. The operation's metadata is the execution from the start, and it completes when the execution does: with the execution on success, and on failure with an error carrying the failing task's message (code FAILED_PRECONDITION, not measured against Google). Overrides of `args`, `env`, `task_count` and `timeout` apply to that execution only (`TestRunJobFailureAndCancel`; unit `TestRunJobOverridesAndEtags`). `secretKeyRef` is verified by `TestRunJobReadsASecretManagerSecret`, which also asserts the batch Job carries the reference, not the payload. |
| `GetExecution`, `ListExecutions` | **Verified** | `TestRunJobLifecycle`. Read from the batch Job's status: `runningCount` is `active`, `succeededCount` is `succeeded`, `failedCount` the tasks in `failedIndexes`, `retriedCount` the other failed pods. Conditions are `Completed` and `Started`; `reconciling` is true until the execution ends. Listed newest first; a missing job is NOT_FOUND. The execution's `template` is the one it ran with, recorded on the batch Job. |
| A failed task | **Verified** | `TestRunJobFailureAndCancel`: a container that exits 3 yields `Completed` = `CONDITION_FAILED`, `executionReason` `NON_ZERO_EXIT_CODE`, and a message naming the task, the container, the exit code and **the container's own output** (the container's termination message is `FallbackToLogsOnError`, so the tail of its log is kept). A task timeout reports the pod's `DeadlineExceeded` the same way (unit `TestExecutionHelpers` only). |
| `CancelExecution` | **Verified** | `TestRunJobFailureAndCancel`: a running execution is cancelled by suspending its batch Job, which stops its pods; the operation completes when none is running, with the unfinished tasks in `cancelledCount` and `executionReason` `CANCELLED` (`CANCELLING` while pods stop). Cancelling an execution that has ended is FAILED_PRECONDITION (unit only; not measured against Google). |
| `DeleteExecution` | **Verified** | `TestRunJobLifecycle`: the batch Job and its pods are deleted, running or not. |
| Refused fields | **Verified** | The task template's `vpcAccess`, `serviceAccount`, `encryptionKey`, `volumes`, `executionEnvironment` and `nodeSelector`, a container's `volumeMounts`, `dependsOn`, `readinessProbe`, `baseImageUri` and `sourceCode`, are refused by **the same check as a service's template**, in the same words, prefixed `template.template.`; so are `binaryAuthorization`, `startExecutionToken`/`runExecutionToken`, `gpuZonalRedundancyDisabled`, and a job container's `ports`, `startupProbe` and `livenessProbe`, which a task has no use for. All UNIMPLEMENTED naming the field (`TestRunJobFailureAndCancel` for `vpcAccess` through the SDK; unit `TestJobRefusesWhatItDoesNotMap` for the rest). |
| IAM methods | Unimplemented | `GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy`, `TestIamPermissions` on Jobs return UNIMPLEMENTED. |
| Logs, `logUri` | **Not served** | Task output is read only for a failure's message; there is no `logUri`. |
| Console screen | **Verified** | `TestConsoleRunJobsCreateExecuteAndCancel` (#785): the console's Jobs page at `/run/jobs`, through this API. See the Console table. |

---

## Optional services

Opt-in, and **never started by default** — a developer should not pay memory for databases
they did not ask for. Enable with `--services firestore,datastore,bigtable,spanner`.

Each wraps a **Google-published emulator**; none is a reimplementation. Each has an official
emulator environment variable, so an application needs no code change to use it.

| Service | Backing component | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Firestore ([every RPC](coverage/firestore.md)) | Cloud SDK `cloud-firestore-emulator` | **Verified** | `TestFirestoreDocumentCRUD`: document CRUD, a `>` query, and a transaction |
| Datastore ([every RPC](coverage/datastore.md)) | Cloud SDK `cloud-datastore-emulator`, as Firestore in Datastore mode | **Verified** | `TestDatastoreEntityCRUD`: entity CRUD, a filtered query, and a transaction. `TestDatastoreQueriesSeeAFreshWrite`: 50 queries in a row, each made right after its write, all see it. The emulator runs in Firestore-in-Datastore mode, which is strongly consistent like a Datastore database in Google Cloud today. It does not reproduce legacy Datastore's eventually consistent non-ancestor queries. Left at its default (`--consistency=0.9`), about one query in ten missed a fresh write (#371). |
| Bigtable ([every RPC](coverage/bigtable.md)) | Cloud SDK `bigtable` (`cbtemulator`) | **Verified** | `TestBigtableTableAndRows`: table and column family creation, row write, read, and a filtered scan |
| Spanner ([every RPC](coverage/spanner.md)) | `cloud-spanner-emulator` 1.5.58 (own image, digest-pinned) | **Verified, host and pod** | `TestSpannerSchemaAndQuery`: instance, database, DDL, write, read, SQL query. `TestSpannerReadWriteTransactionIsAtomic`: a read-modify-write transfer, and an aborted transaction leaving nothing behind. `TestSpannerDatabasesAreIsolated`. `TestSpannerFromInsideAPod`: the same client from a Job, over cluster DNS. |

A **Verified** row above is for the RPCs a test exercises, not for the whole service. Each
service's generated page lists every RPC of its protos: an RPC an official-client test issues is
Verified and links that test, and every other RPC is **Unknown**, so nothing is claimed for it.
The RPCs are the ones each client method issues, read from the pinned client libraries: Firestore's
`DocumentRef.Get` is `BatchGetDocuments` and `Set` and `Delete` are `Commit`; Datastore's `Get` is
`Lookup` and `Put` and `Delete` are non-transactional `Commit`s; Bigtable's `ReadRow` is `ReadRows`
and an unconditional `Apply` is `MutateRow`; Spanner's `NewClient` creates a multiplexed session
with `CreateSession`, and a mutation-only `Apply` is `BeginTransaction` then `Commit`. A result a
test ignores (a cleanup's `DeleteTable` or `DeleteInstance`) claims nothing. Bigtable's instance
admin service has no page, and waiting on an admin operation polls `google.longrunning.Operations`,
which no page lists.

| RPCs a test exercises | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `Firestore.BatchGetDocuments`, `Firestore.Commit`, `Firestore.RunQuery`, `Firestore.BeginTransaction` | **Verified** | `TestFirestoreDocumentCRUD` |
| `Firestore.ListCollectionIds` | **Verified** | `TestConsoleFirestoreSubcollections`: `DocumentRef.Collections` lists the subcollections the console's Start collection made, and the document page's Collections tab reads them the same way |
| `Firestore.ListDocuments` | **Verified** | `TestConsoleFirestoreListsMissingDocumentsWithSubcollections` (#875): `CollectionRef.DocumentRefs`, which asks with `show_missing`, lists a document that does not exist but has a subcollection beside one that does, and the console's collection page lists the same, paging past 200 documents with the page token; `TestConsoleFirestoreCountsMissingDocuments` (#882): the Firestore screen's and a Collections tab's Documents counts are of the same list |
| `Datastore.Commit`, `Datastore.Lookup`, `Datastore.RunQuery`, `Datastore.BeginTransaction` | **Verified** | `TestDatastoreEntityCRUD`; `Commit` and `RunQuery` also `TestDatastoreQueriesSeeAFreshWrite` |
| `Bigtable.MutateRow`, `Bigtable.ReadRows`, `BigtableTableAdmin.CreateTable`, `BigtableTableAdmin.ModifyColumnFamilies` | **Verified** | `TestBigtableTableAndRows` |
| `BigtableTableAdmin.ListTables`, `BigtableTableAdmin.GetTable` | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigtableCreateAndDeleteTable`, including `NOT_FOUND` from `GetTable` after the console's delete |
| `Spanner.CreateSession`, `Spanner.BeginTransaction`, `Spanner.Commit`, `Spanner.StreamingRead`, `InstanceAdmin.CreateInstance`, `DatabaseAdmin.CreateDatabase` | **Verified** | `TestSpannerSchemaAndQuery`, `TestSpannerReadWriteTransactionIsAtomic`, `TestSpannerDatabasesAreIsolated` (a `StreamingRead` answering `NOT_FOUND` across databases) |
| `Spanner.ExecuteStreamingSql` | **Verified** | `TestSpannerSchemaAndQuery` |
| `InstanceAdmin.GetInstance`, `DatabaseAdmin.GetDatabase`, `DatabaseAdmin.GetDatabaseDdl` | **Verified** | `TestConsoleSpannerCreateAndDropDatabase`, including `NOT_FOUND` after the console's drops |

**All four are in-memory.** CloudBurrow provisions no volume for them and `status` lists them
as never surviving a restart. Nothing here is persistent, whatever `--mode` says.

For Spanner this is now **measured rather than quoted**: `TestSpannerStateDoesNotSurviveARestart`
writes a row, restarts the pod, waits for the endpoint to come back, and reads —

```
after the restart the data is gone: spanner: code = "NotFound",
desc = "Instance not found: projects/.../instances/cb-durab"
```

The test fails rather than passes if the endpoint does not return, because an unreachable
address is not evidence about durability. It is on CI's [retry-once list](#retried-once), so this
evidence tolerates one retry.

**Datastore keeps its data in persistent mode, measured (#307).** In persistent mode the
emulator's on-disk store is on a PersistentVolumeClaim, and the emulator's JVM is the container's
main process. The Cloud SDK emulator writes its store only when it shuts down cleanly, and both
`gcloud beta emulators datastore start` and the `cloud_datastore_emulator` script run the JVM as
a child that never receives the pod's SIGTERM, so under the wrapper an entity written before a
pod restart was lost. In ephemeral mode it runs through the wrapper with `--no-store-on-disk`,
as it always did. `TestDatastoreSurvivesAPodRestart` writes an entity with the Go client,
deletes the pod, and reads every entity written so far back from the new one, three times over.
A pod that is killed rather than stopped (OOM, node loss) still loses what was written since it
started. CI's restart probe reads a probe entity after `stop` and `up` in persistent mode, and
finds none after `up --mode ephemeral` (`TestDatastoreAcrossRestart`). Both tests are on CI's
[retry-once list](#retried-once), so this evidence tolerates one retry. `status` reports `volume` for Datastore, and for every
other service, only in persistent mode. Firestore and Bigtable are still documented from Google's description,
not measured.

### Generated coverage

[docs/coverage](coverage/README.md) is generated from the proto descriptors, not written by hand.
It lists every RPC of [Cloud Tasks](coverage/tasks.md), [Secret Manager](coverage/secretmanager.md),
[Cloud Run v2](coverage/run.md), [Cloud KMS](coverage/kms.md),
[Resource Manager v3 Projects](coverage/resourcemanager.md), [Cloud Scheduler](coverage/scheduler.md),
[Cloud Logging](coverage/logging.md), [Pub/Sub](coverage/pubsub.md), the
[Storage JSON API](coverage/storage.md) and the opt-in [Firestore](coverage/firestore.md),
[Datastore](coverage/datastore.md), [Bigtable](coverage/bigtable.md) and
[Spanner](coverage/spanner.md) emulators with its status, and every REST method of
[BigQuery](coverage/bigquery.md), read from the official generated client (#993), and links each Verified one to the compat
test that proves it. CI fails when it is stale. `TestCoverageMatchesCompatibility`
(`tools/coverage`) fails when a row of this document that names one of those methods gives it a
status the generated page does not, when a method the page calls Verified or Implemented is named
in no row of its section, or when a page is not linked here, and
`TestCompatibilityCitesOnlyDefinedTests` fails when a row cites a `Test*` or `test_*` name no test
in the repository defines. The statuses correspond as that test documents: **Verified** is
Verified; **Partial** is Verified or Implemented; **Implemented** is Implemented;
**Unimplemented**, **Refused**, **Not supported** and **Verified unsupported** are Unimplemented or
Refused; Planned is Unimplemented, Refused or Not served; **Not served** is Not served.

### BigQuery is a community emulator

`--services bigquery` runs [`goccy/bigquery-emulator`](https://github.com/goccy/bigquery-emulator)
0.8.1 (MIT), **built by CloudBurrow from its source with patches to the SQL engine it links**
(#1061): the CLI embeds linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 builds made from Go modules pinned and
checked by checksum, and `up` builds its image locally; nothing is pulled but the digest-pinned
base. The patches fix table functions (#1043, #1047), `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` (#976), a RECORD
inside a REPEATED RECORD (#900, upstream's open goccy/googlesqlite#76), a FLOAT64 NaN (#1066)
and an infinity inside an ARRAY or STRUCT (#1077), BYTES that are not UTF-8 text (#1075), the
`-` operator on a column, the engine's memory past 2 GiB (#989), its growth through DDL
(#1057, upstream's open goccy/googlesqlite#80), functions of another dataset (#1107, #1123) and by
one quoted path (#1122), INTERVAL signs and comparison (#1126, #1120), NULL arguments (#1109,
#1121), GEOGRAPHY text (#1119) and REPEATED fields a row leaves out (#1124); [third_party/bigquery-emulator/PROVENANCE.md](../third_party/bigquery-emulator/PROVENANCE.md)
lists each. Where a row below says "the pinned emulator" or "measured first", it was measured
against the unpatched v0.8.1. **Google publishes no BigQuery
emulator**, so this is the one opt-in backend that is neither Google's nor the real engine. It runs
ZetaSQL, so it accepts GoogleSQL, but the only fidelity claimed is what is listed below.

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| REST methods ([every method](coverage/bigquery.md)): `bigquery.datasets.insert`, `bigquery.datasets.get`, `bigquery.datasets.list`, `bigquery.datasets.delete`, `bigquery.tables.insert`, `bigquery.tables.get`, `bigquery.tables.list`, `bigquery.tables.delete`, `bigquery.tabledata.insertAll`, `bigquery.tabledata.list`, `bigquery.tables.patch`, `bigquery.tables.update`, `bigquery.jobs.query` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryDatasetTableInsertAndQuery`, `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetsSchemaAndQuery`, `TestBigQuerySameTableIDInTwoDatasets` (`tabledata.list`, #1015) and `TestBigQueryTableUpdateAddsColumns` (`tables.patch`, `tables.update`, #1010), official Go client, as the rows below describe. The coverage page lists every method of the official generated client (`google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2`), 47; a method no annotated test covers is Unknown, and nothing is claimed for it (#993). |
| Jobs: `bigquery.jobs.insert`, `bigquery.jobs.get`, `bigquery.jobs.list`, `bigquery.jobs.cancel`, `bigquery.jobs.delete` | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory` (#993), official Go client (`Loader.Run`, `Extractor.Run`, `Client.JobFromID`, `Job.Cancel`) and the generated client (`Jobs.Get`), through the console and directly; the load, extract and job-list rows below give what each carries. Measured on the front of #991: `jobs.cancel` of a done job, the emulator's or the front's, answers the job, still `DONE`; `jobs.delete` of either removes it, and `jobs.get` of it is then 404. |
| Datasets and tables: create, read metadata, delete | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryDatasetTableInsertAndQuery`, official Go client. The schema reads back as created. A deleted table and dataset answer 404 afterwards. |
| Streaming inserts (`tabledata.insertAll`) | **Verified** | Same test: four rows through `Inserter().Put`. |
| `SELECT` with `WHERE`, `GROUP BY`, `ORDER BY` | **Verified** | Same test: aggregates checked value by value. |
| `bq` CLI: `mk` (dataset, table), `insert`, `query --nouse_legacy_sql`, `rm` | **Verified**, with two flags | bq 2.1.38, from Google Cloud CLI 586.0.0 as CI installs it (checksum-pinned, #694), in a fresh gcloud configuration with every non-loopback request refused by a test proxy, each step checked through the Go client (#696). **Through the exported override:** `TestBqThroughTheExportedOverride` runs `bq --nouse_google_auth --oauth_access_token=<any value> …` with the variables `cloudburrow env` exports and nothing else; bq takes `CLOUDSDK_API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDES_BIGQUERY` and `CLOUDSDK_CORE_PROJECT` from `gcloud config list`. **Through `--api`:** `TestBqThroughTheAPIFlag` runs `bq --api "$CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_ENDPOINT" --project_id "$GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT" --oauth_access_token=<any value> …` with no override; gcloud's config-helper prints "no active account" on stderr, which `--nouse_google_auth` quiets. The token is a placeholder the emulator ignores; without it bq's wrapper refuses every command ("You do not currently have an active account selected"). **Without `--nouse_google_auth` the override is ignored**: bq reads gcloud's properties from `gcloud config config-helper`, which fails with no account, and its request goes to `bigquery.googleapis.com` (`TestBqIgnoresTheOverrideWithoutNouseGoogleAuth`). `gcloud-setup` does not configure bq. |
| Storage Read API, Arrow | **Verified**, every column type including `REPEATED` columns and RECORDs at any depth | `TestBigQueryStorageReadAPIStreamsArrowRows`: a read session over the second tunnel streams all four rows as Arrow batches, through the official Storage Read client. `TestBigQueryStorageReadOfRepeatedAndRecordColumnsInArrow` (#1095), official Storage Read client (`cloud.google.com/go/bigquery/storage/apiv1`): a table of `INT64`, `FLOAT64`, `NUMERIC`, `BIGNUMERIC`, `BOOL`, `STRING`, `BYTES`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME`, `TIMESTAMP`, `JSON`, a RECORD holding a `REPEATED STRING`, `REPEATED INT64`, `REPEATED TIMESTAMP` and a `REPEATED RECORD` holding a `REPEATED FLOAT64` (one row's empty), with a row of values, rows of `NULL`s and empty arrays, streams every value, a `REPEATED` column as an Arrow list and a RECORD as a struct; so does a session with `selected_fields` and a `row_restriction`; and the official Go client's `Table.Read` through the Storage Read API (`Client.EnableStorageReadClient`) reads every value, a `DATETIME` as a `civil.DateTime`. **Measured first against the pinned emulator: an Arrow read of a `REPEATED` column crashed the emulator and emptied the instance.** `ReadRows` of `(i INT64, r ARRAY<INT64>)` with rows `(1, [7,8])` and `(2, [9])` killed its process ("panic: arrow/array: field 1 has 3 rows. want=2", `server/storage_handler.go:349`): it appends each element of a repeated value to the list's builder as a list of its own (`TableCell.AppendValueToARROWBuilder`, `internal/types/types.go`), and a panic in a gRPC handler ends the process. The client got `UNAVAILABLE` "error reading from server: EOF", Kubernetes restarted the container, and every dataset, table and job was gone (the emulator keeps them in memory, row *Persistence*). A `DATETIME` column failed too ("invalid timestamp string"), and the session's Arrow type for a `DATETIME` was a timestamp in UTC, which the Go client read as a `time.Time`. **Now the emulator's `ReadRows` is never called:** `CreateReadSession` goes to the emulator, which reads the table and its schema by the whole name, and the front writes every stream's rows itself, from a `jobs.query` of `` SELECT <the session's columns> FROM `dataset.table` WHERE (<row_restriction>) `` (the front's own query, left out of `jobs.list`), in the session's schema: Arrow as BigQuery frames it (next row but two), a `REPEATED` column a list and a RECORD a struct at any depth, a `DATETIME` a timestamp with no zone, which is how the Go client tells one from a `TIMESTAMP` (its `arrow.go`); at most 1000 rows an answer, the schema in the first ([ReadRowsResponse](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/storage/rpc/google.cloud.bigquery.storage.v1#readrowsresponse)); `offset` skips rows. `CreateReadSession` without a `read_session` (whose field the emulator's handler reads without looking, a panic too), a table name or a format of `ARROW` or `AVRO` is **`INVALID_ARGUMENT`** before the emulator sees it. Rows are read when `ReadRows` asks, not as a snapshot at the session's creation; each `ReadRows` is one query, whose result the emulator keeps with its job (in memory) until it restarts. Units `TestStorageReadRowsAreWrittenByTheFront`, `TestStorageReadRowsInBatches`, `TestStorageReadRefusesWhatWouldCrashTheEmulator`, `TestArrowIsFramedAsBigQueryFramesIt`. |
| Storage Read API of a table whose ID another dataset has | **Verified** (Arrow) | `TestBigQueryStorageReadOfATableIDAnotherDatasetHas` (#1032, #1046), official Storage Read client (`cloud.google.com/go/bigquery/storage/apiv1`) and the official Go client with its Storage Read client (`Client.EnableStorageReadClient`: `Table.Read`, and `Query.Read` of a query job with a destination table). In BigQuery a read session names its table by project, dataset and table ([ReadSession](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/storage/rpc/google.cloud.bigquery.storage.v1#readsession)). Measured first against the pinned emulator, with `one.t (s STRING)` of three rows made first and `two.t (a INT64)` of two after: `ReadRows` of a session of `two.t` failed `Unknown` "strconv.ParseInt: parsing \"one\": invalid syntax", reading `one.t`'s rows into `two.t`'s Arrow schema; the emulator reads a session's rows with `` SELECT … FROM `<tableId>` `` (its `storage_handler.go`), the bare ID, which its engine resolves to the first table of that ID in any dataset (row *Tables of one ID in two datasets*); #1032 made such a session 501. Now the front serves the Storage Read port (9060) in the emulator's pod and writes the rows itself from a query of the table's whole name (row *Storage Read API, Arrow*, #1095), so nothing of the other dataset's table is read; the emulator reads the session's schema by the whole name. In the test: `two.same (a INT64 REQUIRED, b STRING)` streamed its own two rows and `one.same (s STRING)`, made first, its three, each in its own Arrow schema; a session made before another dataset made a table of its ID read its own row; after a column was added the new column streamed; `Table.Read` and a `Query.Read` into a destination whose ID another dataset has were read through the Storage Read API (`IsAccelerated`) with their own rows. (#1046 read such a table through a view of the front's, `_cloudburrow_storage_read`; since #1095 no view is made.) `ReadRows` of a stream the front did not see made (one made before it last started) is **501 `UNIMPLEMENTED`**. Avro reads the same way (row *Storage Read API, Avro*). The Storage Write API writes by the whole name (its `AddTableData`, read in the source, not measured) and is passed through unchecked. Units `TestStorageReadRowsAreWrittenByTheFront`, `TestArrowIsFramedAsBigQueryFramesIt`. |
| Storage Read API, Avro | **Verified**, every column type the Arrow row's test reads, on a project ID with a hyphen | `TestBigQueryStorageReadInAvroOnAHyphenatedProject` (#1098), official Storage Read client, on the instance's project (every CloudBurrow project ID has a hyphen): an Avro read session of the Arrow row's table streams its three rows, which [goavro](https://github.com/linkedin/goavro) v2.15.0 decodes with the session's schema, every value as written (`NUMERIC` and `BIGNUMERIC` as decimals, `DATE`, `TIME`, `TIMESTAMP` as their logical types, `DATETIME` as its text, `REPEATED` columns as arrays, a nullable RECORD as a union of a record). Measured first against the pinned emulator: every Avro `ReadRows` failed "failed to create avro codec …: Record ought to have valid name: schema name ought to have second and remaining characters contain only [A-Za-z0-9_]: <project>". The emulator names the schema's record by the namespace `<project>.<dataset>` and the table (`TableToAVRO`, `types/avro.go`), and an Avro name may hold only letters, digits and `_` ([names](https://avro.apache.org/docs/1.11.1/specification/#names)); a RECORD's record is named after its column, so two RECORD columns of one name at different depths were two records of one name, which Avro refuses too. Now the session's schema is the emulator's with every name made valid (a character a name may not have becomes `_`, a leading digit gets a `_` before it, a record whose name another has gets `_2`, `_3`…; field names, types and order unchanged), and the front writes the rows in it, in Avro's binary encoding, as it does Arrow's (row *Storage Read API, Arrow*). Units `TestStorageReadRowsAreWrittenByTheFront`, `TestBigQueryAvroSchemaNames`, `TestDecimalBytes`. |
| Go client accelerated reads (`Client.EnableStorageReadClient`) | **Verified** for `Table.Read` and `Query.Read` of a query job with a destination | `TestBigQueryStorageReadOfATableIDAnotherDatasetHas` (#1046) and `TestBigQueryStorageReadOfRepeatedAndRecordColumnsInArrow` (#1095, every column type, `REPEATED` and RECORD columns), `cloud.google.com/go/bigquery` v1.85.0. Measured first: `Table.Read` panicked "index out of range [0] with length 0" on a table of one row. The emulator writes the session's Arrow schema and each batch as whole IPC streams (schema, batch, end-of-stream), where BigQuery sends the schema's message and each batch's ([ArrowRecordBatch](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/storage/rpc/google.cloud.bigquery.storage.v1#arrowrecordbatch)), and the Go client reads a batch after the schema, so it read the schema's empty batch and stopped. The Storage Read front now sends them framed as BigQuery's, for every session (`TestBigQueryStorageReadAPIStreamsArrowRows` still streams its four rows); since #1095 it writes the batches itself (row *Storage Read API, Arrow*). The Go client reads the Storage Read API in Arrow only. |
| Storage Write API (`cloud.google.com/go/bigquery/storage/managedwriter`): the default stream and `COMMITTED`, `PENDING` and `BUFFERED` streams; `CreateWriteStream`, `AppendRows`, `GetWriteStream`, `FinalizeWriteStream`, `BatchCommitWriteStreams`, `FlushRows` | **Verified**, every column type below `RANGE`, `REPEATED` columns and RECORDs; **Not implemented** (501) where the next row says | `TestBigQueryStorageWriteDefaultStreamOfEveryType`, `TestBigQueryStorageWriteCommittedStreamsKeepTheirTables`, `TestBigQueryStorageWritePendingStreamCommitsOnce` and `TestBigQueryStorageWriteBufferedStreamFlushesOnce` (#1102), official managed writer, each stream's rows read back with the official Go client's `Table.Read`. A row of `INT64`, `FLOAT64`, `NUMERIC` and `BIGNUMERIC` (BigDecimalByteStringEncoder's bytes), `BOOL`, `STRING`, `BYTES`, `DATE` (days), `DATETIME` and `TIME` (CivilTimeEncoder's packed `int64`), `TIMESTAMP` (microseconds), `JSON`, `GEOGRAPHY`, a RECORD holding a `TIMESTAMP` and a `REPEATED STRING`, `REPEATED INT64`, `REPEATED TIMESTAMP` and a `REPEATED RECORD`, and a row of `NULL`s and empty arrays, read back as written; the conversions are BigQuery's table of protocol buffer types ([supported data types](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/supported-data-types)). A default stream's results carry no offset; a stream's result offset is the append's first row, an offset already written is `ALREADY_EXISTS` and one past the end `OUT_OF_RANGE`; two `COMMITTED` streams of two tables appended in turn, and two default streams on one multiplexed connection, each write to their own table; `Finalize` counts the rows and an append after it fails; a `PENDING` stream's rows are in the table only after `BatchCommitWriteStreams`, a stream not finalized is a stream error and nothing is committed, a second commit is `STREAM_ALREADY_COMMITTED`; a `BUFFERED` stream's rows are in the table up to each `FlushRows` offset, once each. **Measured first against the pinned emulator, to which the front passed the Write API: the rows after an `AppendRows` connection's first request, which the managed writer sends without `write_stream`, landed in another table; a `NUMERIC` was stored as 0, a packed `DATETIME` as `6483-06-17T05:36:51.664`; each result had the wrong offset, an offset already written was accepted again, and `FlushRows` past the rows crashed the emulator.** So the front serves the Write API itself and never calls the emulator's: it keeps each stream and writes its rows through its own `tabledata.insertAll`, whose checks every row has. **A restart of the front's container keeps every stream**, its offset, and the rows a `PENDING` stream holds until its commit and a `BUFFERED` one until its flush (#1115): the front keeps them in files on an emptyDir of the BigQuery pod, as the Pub/Sub front keeps its state (#898). `TestBigQueryStorageWriteStreamsOutliveAFrontRestart`, official managed writer: rows appended to a `PENDING`, a `BUFFERED` and a `COMMITTED` stream, the front's container stopped alone, then each stream found by `GetWriteStream`, reopened by name, appended to at its next offset, committed and flushed, every row in its table once (unit `TestStorageWriteStreamsOutliveAFrontRestart`, which also shows an append torn between its rows and its state is ignored). **A restart of the pod loses them**, with the emulator's tables: a stream made before is then `NOT_FOUND` (the default stream is always there). A `FlushRows` or commit whose rows reached the table just before the front's process ended, and whose state had not been written, writes them again when the client retries it. |
| Storage Write API: what is not implemented, and malformed requests | **Not implemented** (501): `arrow_rows`; `missing_value_interpretations` or `default_missing_value_interpretation` of `DEFAULT_VALUE` (column defaults are not applied); a `RANGE` column; a `google.protobuf.Duration` for an `INTERVAL`. Since the engine patches of #1094, a `BYTES` value that is not UTF-8 text is written as its bytes (`TestBigQueryStorageWriteDefaultStreamOfEveryType`), and a value in a RECORD nested in a RECORD with a `REPEATED` one among them is passed to `tabledata.insertAll` as a streamed row is (the *RECORD inside a REPEATED RECORD* row; not measured through the Write API). **Verified** refused: `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `OUT_OF_RANGE` or `NOT_FOUND` | `TestBigQueryStorageWriteRefusesMalformedRequests` (#1102), official raw `BigQueryWriteClient` and Storage Read client, then the table is read to show the instance kept its data: `AppendRows` naming no `write_stream`, without `writer_schema`, or without rows (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`, ending the connection); a `FIXED64` field, a kind no `INTEGER` column takes (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` in the append's answer); `FlushRows` with no offset (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`) or past the rows (`OUT_OF_RANGE`); `CreateWriteStream` of no type (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`); `GetWriteStream` of a stream never made, and `CreateReadSession` of a table in a project the instance does not have (`NOT_FOUND`). **Measured first against the pinned emulator: each of `AppendRows` naming no stream when none existed, the `FIXED64` field, `FlushRows` with no offset or past the rows, and that `CreateReadSession` panicked its gRPC handler (`server/storage_handler.go:497`, `:644`, `:809`, `:819`, `:919`), which ends the process: Kubernetes restarted it and every dataset, table and job was gone.** A row that does not decode or convert fails its whole append with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and `row_errors`, and the connection goes on (unit-tested, `TestStorageWriteRefusesMalformedRequests`). The emulator's gRPC API has no other handlers than the Storage Read and Write APIs; the Write API is not called and the Read API's only call the front passes through is `SplitReadStream`, which answers `UNIMPLEMENTED`. |
| REST reads of a `TIMESTAMP` in a RECORD or a `REPEATED` column (`tabledata.list`, `jobs.query`, `jobs.getQueryResults`) | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryRESTReadOfNestedTimestamps` (#1101), official Go client: `Table.Read` (tabledata.list) and a query's rows read the Storage Read test's table, a `TIMESTAMP` in a RECORD, a `REPEATED TIMESTAMP` and every other type at depth, as written, and a query's own nested `TIMESTAMP`s, one before the epoch. BigQuery gives every `TIMESTAMP` of a row in the form `formatOptions.useInt64Timestamp` asks for, microseconds or seconds ([DataFormatOptions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/DataFormatOptions)). **Measured first against the pinned emulator: a nested `TIMESTAMP` came back as its engine's text, `"2020-01-01 00:00:00+00"`, and `Table.Read` failed `strconv.ParseInt: parsing "2020-01-01 00:00:00+00": invalid syntax`**; the emulator converts a cell only when its column is a `TIMESTAMP`. `DATETIME`, `DATE`, `TIME`, `NUMERIC`, `BIGNUMERIC`, `BYTES`, `GEOGRAPHY`, `FLOAT64`, `BOOL`, `INT64` and `JSON` were measured the same at depth as at the top level, so the front rewrites only a nested `TIMESTAMP`. |
| DML: `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`, `TRUNCATE TABLE`, and the rows each changed | **Verified** for a statement run on its own; **Not implemented** (501) for a `MERGE` from a subquery inside a script of several statements; **Not supported**: a DML statement inside a script is run but its rows are not reported, and the script is not a `SCRIPT` parent job with a child job per statement as in BigQuery (#1028; read in the pinned emulator's source, goccy/bigquery-emulator v0.8.1 `server/handler.go`: it makes no child jobs, every query job's `statistics.query.statementType` is `SELECT`, and it sets no `parentJobId`, `numChildJobs`, `numDmlAffectedRows` or `dmlStats` anywhere, so there are no child-job statistics for the front to report; `jobs.list` with `parentJobId` lists nothing); since #1028 the counts the front reports and the client's job texts are kept on the front's state directory, so jobs.get of an earlier DML job still reports its count after a restart of the front's container (unit `TestJobTextsOutliveAFrontRestart`), but a view's text kept by #1014 is not; since #1027 `UPDATE … FROM` one table or subquery (which the emulator refuses, 400 "Update with joins not supported") runs, as a `MERGE … WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE` the front sends, and reports `UPDATE` and its rows (`TestBigQueryUpdateFrom`, unit `TestUpdateFromAsMerge`); **Not implemented** (501) for a `FROM` clause of several items (a comma, a `JOIN`, `UNNEST`), and inside a script it is still sent on as it is ; since #1026 an error of a lone DML statement names its table `dataset.table` rather than the emulator's storage name `<project>_<dataset>_<table>`, and an error of a statement the front rewrote (a `MERGE` from a subquery, an `UPDATE … FROM`) has no `[at line:column]`, which pointed into the front's statement (`TestBigQueryDMLErrorsNameTheClientsTable`, unit `TestDMLErrorNames`; BigQuery's exact wording not measured) | `TestBigQueryPlainDMLThroughTheFront` (#1008), official Go client (`Query.Run`, `Job.Wait`: `jobs.insert`, `jobs.get`; `Client.Jobs`: `jobs.list`) and the generated one (`jobs.query`). Measured first against the pinned emulator: each statement changed the rows as BigQuery does, but every job reported `statistics.query.statementType` `SELECT` and no `numDmlAffectedRows` or `dmlStats`, `jobs.query` answered with neither, and a `MERGE` whose source is a subquery (or a `UNION ALL`) failed 400 "MERGE: source must be a single-table reference". BigQuery reports the statement type and the rows ([JobStatistics2](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobStatistics2), [QueryResponse](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/jobs/query#response-body)). The front now counts them with queries of its own that change nothing: for `INSERT`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE TABLE` the table's rows before and after; for `UPDATE` the rows its `WHERE` matches, before it runs (a row counts whether or not its values change, as in BigQuery); for `MERGE` the rows each `WHEN` clause takes, before it runs, picked as BigQuery picks them (the first `WHEN MATCHED`, `WHEN NOT MATCHED [BY TARGET]` or `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE` clause whose `AND` holds). Each job reports `statementType` (`INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`, `TRUNCATE_TABLE`), `numDmlAffectedRows` and `dmlStats` (`insertedRowCount`, `updatedRowCount`, `deletedRowCount`, a zero left out) in `jobs.insert`'s answer, `jobs.get` and `jobs.list`, and `jobs.query` answers with `numDmlAffectedRows` and `dmlStats`: in the test, 2 inserted, 2 and 2 updated, 1 deleted, a `MERGE` from a subquery updating 1, a `MERGE` of three clauses from a `UNION ALL` inserting 1 and updating 1, a `DELETE` of nothing 0, and `TRUNCATE TABLE` 6 deleted, with the rows read back. A `MERGE` from a subquery is run from a table of the front's (`CREATE TABLE … AS` the subquery, in the target's dataset, deleted after), and its job shows the client's text; inside a script of several statements it is **501** before anything runs (`TestBigQueryMergeFromASubqueryInAScript`). The counts are exact while nothing else writes the table during the statement (the front does not lock it). Errors still name the emulator's internal table (#1026). The counts and the client's texts are in the front's memory (#1028). A statement that names its table without a dataset is sent, and counted, in the query's default dataset, as every query is (#1015, the row *Tables of one ID in two datasets*; `TestBigQuerySameTableIDInTwoDatasets` runs `INSERT`, `DELETE` and `MERGE` so): merged with #1015 first, the counts' queries and the statement went out unqualified and wrote the first table of the ID in any dataset (measured, #1054). Units `TestParseDML`, `TestParseMergeClauses`, `TestInsertJobReportsTheRowsItAdded`, `TestDMLIsQualifiedFirst`. The console's Read-write editor runs a DML statement of its own and answers with these counts (#1024, `TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDML`, the Console rows). |
| `bigquery.tables.patch` (`Table.Update`) from the console's Edit table: description and labels | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions` (#994, #1025), through the console's Edit table, which sends `Table.Update` with only what changed (a label set or changed, a label removed as `DeleteLabel`, the description changed or emptied), read back with the official Go client, on a table and on a view: a changed label keeps the others, a removed label is gone, an emptied description reads back empty, and every label can be removed. Measured first against the pinned emulator (#1009): a patch's labels replaced the table's, a removed label was kept with an empty value and an empty description left the old one, so the form refused both; the front now patches as BigQuery does (the `tables.patch` row, `TestBigQueryTablePatchLabelsAndDescription`). |
| Scripting (`DECLARE`, multi-statement) | **Partial**: script variables are scoped to their script; other statements are claimed only where a row below says so | `TestBigQueryScriptVariablesEndWithTheScript` (#933), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: its SQL engine (goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1, read from the image's binary) keeps a script's variables after the script and puts each one's value in place of every bare word naming it in **every later query of the instance**: after `DECLARE zz INT64 DEFAULT 5; SELECT zz`, a separate `SELECT zz AS v` returned 5, and after `DECLARE w INT64 DEFAULT 9`, `SELECT 1 AS w` failed with a syntax error. BigQuery scopes a variable to its script. The front sends each variable a script declares under a name of its own for that request (`renameVariables`), renaming exactly the words the engine would replace, so the script reads its variables as before (`DECLARE`, `DEFAULT` from an earlier variable, `SET`: 10 and 9 read back) and a later query gets "Unrecognized name: zz", and an alias `w` is an alias. An error naming a variable names it as the client wrote it, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text. Within the script that declares it, only the references to a variable are renamed (#956, next row). Unit `TestRenameVariables`, `TestScriptVariablesDoNotOutliveTheScript`. |
| A script variable of the name of an alias, a column or a table in its script | **Verified** for an alias, a column list, a STRUCT field and a subquery column of another name; **Not implemented** (501) where BigQuery may read the other name | `TestBigQueryScriptVariableNamedAsAnAlias` (#956), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: within its own script, its engine (goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1, `substituteScriptVariables`) put a variable's value in place of every bare word of its name before reading a statement, so `DECLARE n INT64 DEFAULT 1; SELECT 2 AS n` failed "Syntax error: Unexpected integer literal "1"", where BigQuery returns 2. The front renames only the words that refer to the variable (`renameVariables`, with its lexer): an alias after `AS` or in place of one, a table's, CTE's or function's name, a column in a `CREATE TABLE`'s or `INSERT`'s column list, a `STRUCT<…>` field, a column in `EXCEPT (…)` or `USING (…)`, and a column `UPDATE … SET` assigns keep the client's name, which the engine does not touch. Read back: `SELECT 2 AS n` returned 2; `SELECT n + 1 AS m, 3 n2` 2 and 3; `SELECT n, m FROM (SELECT 7 AS m) AS t` 1 and 7; `DECLARE s STRUCT<n INT64> …; SELECT s.n AS n` 5; `INSERT INTO t (a, n) VALUES (n, 4)` wrote 3 and 4. BigQuery's documentation does not say which of a variable and a column, alias or range variable of one name a query reads ([procedural language](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/procedural-language) says only that variable names are case-insensitive), so a statement that refers to a variable is **501 `notImplemented`**, before anything of the script runs, when the name is also one there: an alias or range variable it defines, a column after a `.`, a column of a table it reads (looked up with `tables.get`, or, for a table an earlier statement of the script makes, from its column list or its select list), or a table it reads whose columns are not known (measured cases: `SELECT n FROM (SELECT 7 AS n)`, `SELECT a FROM t WHERE a = n` with a column `n` in `t`, `INSERT INTO t (a) SELECT n FROM t`). Unit `TestRenameVariables`, `TestSelectColumns`. |
| A script that fails after a statement that changes data | **Verified** as a query job (`jobs.insert`) without a transaction; **Not implemented** (501) through `jobs.query`, and as a query job with `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, `COMMIT` or `ROLLBACK` | `TestBigQueryFailedScriptThatWroteIs501` (#935, #955), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. BigQuery keeps what a script's statements did before the one that fails. Measured against the pinned emulator: **through `jobs.query`** it runs a script in one transaction and rolls all of it back (`CREATE TABLE ds.e1 AS SELECT 1 AS a; SELECT * FROM nope.nope; CREATE TABLE ds.e2 …` failed 400 and `ds.e1` did not exist; the same for `INSERT`, and for a runtime error, `ERROR('boom')`); **as a query job** it commits what the statements before the failing one did, as it records the failed job in the script's transaction (after `INSERT INTO ds.t VALUES (9); SELECT * FROM nope.nope`, `ds.t` had the row; through `jobs.query`, it did not), and the job fails with the statement's error. So through `jobs.query`, when a script with a statement that changes data (anything but a query, `DECLARE`, `SET`, `ASSERT`, a `TEMP` table and DML on one) and a statement after it fails, the answer is **501 `notImplemented`** naming the emulator's error; as a query job, the job fails with the emulator's error, as in BigQuery, and `ds.e1` exists (read back), unless the script has a transaction, which BigQuery rolls back and the emulator does not undo: then the job is `DONE` with a 501 `notImplemented` `errorResult`. Either way the emulator's catalog and its list of tables are then put back in step with its tables (#955, row below). A failed script that changes nothing kept, and a syntax error (which fails a script before anything runs, in BigQuery too), are answered with the emulator's error as before. Unit `TestFailedScriptThatWroteIs501`. |
| The tables after a failed script | **Verified** for the tables and views a script's `CREATE` and `DROP` statements name, and its `TEMP` tables; functions and `DROP SCHEMA` are the next row | `TestBigQueryFailedScriptLeavesTheCatalogInStep` (#955), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator, a failed script left, for every later query, its SQL engine's catalog (goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1) or its list of tables (`tables.get`, `tables.list`) out of step with its tables. The engine changes its in-memory catalog as each statement runs and reloads it only from catalog rows updated since it last did (`Catalog.Sync`: `WHERE updatedAt >= lastSyncedAt`), so a rollback puts the rows back and not the catalog; the emulator updates its list of tables from a query only when the query succeeded. Through `jobs.query` (rolled back): after `DROP TABLE ds.keep; SELECT * FROM nope.nope`, `ds.keep` was listed and `SELECT a FROM ds.keep` failed "Table not found"; after `CREATE TABLE ds.c1 AS SELECT 1 AS a; …`, `SELECT * FROM ds.c1` failed "sqlite3: SQL logic error: no such table"; after `DROP TABLE ds.keep2; CREATE TABLE ds.keep2 (z STRING); …`, `SELECT a FROM ds.keep2` failed "Unrecognized name: a"; after `CREATE TEMP TABLE ghost AS SELECT 1 AS a; …`, the next `CREATE TEMP TABLE ghost AS SELECT 2 AS b; SELECT b FROM ghost` failed "Unrecognized name: b". As a query job (committed): a dropped table was still listed and not found by a query, and a table made was read by a query and not listed. After a failure the front puts them back with statements of its own (`resyncCatalog`; each measured): rolled back, the catalog is made to match the list (a listed table is taken out of the catalog by a script that drops it and fails, which `jobs.query` rolls back, and added again by `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` from its schema, which leaves the table and its rows; one not listed is dropped `IF EXISTS`); committed, the list is made to match the catalog (a table the engine reads and the list lacks is added again from the engine's columns, which lists it; a listed one the engine lacks is created and dropped, which unlists it; one listed with other columns is patched). Read back through `jobs.query`: `ds.keep` with a STRUCT and an ARRAY, `ds.keep2` and a view as before the scripts, `ds.c1` "not found" and then made again, and a new `ghost` with its own columns; as a query job: `ds.keep` and the view gone, `ds.keep2` listed and read with the new column `z`, `ds.c1` listed and read. The engine's copy of a table put back is made from its schema (names, types, modes, defaults), without its options; the front's statements are recorded by the emulator as jobs, as its other checks are. No upstream issue found (goccy/bigquery-emulator, goccy/googlesqlite, as of 2026-09-28). Unit `TestFailedScriptResyncsTheCatalog`. |
| Functions after a failed script, and `DROP SCHEMA` | **Verified** for `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [TABLE] FUNCTION` in a script given to `jobs.query`, and for a failed query job; **Not implemented** (501) for `DROP [TABLE] FUNCTION` in a script of several statements given to `jobs.query`; `DROP SCHEMA` is carried out (#990, row below) | `TestBigQueryFailedScriptFunctionsAndDropSchema` (#976), official Go client, through `jobs.query` (`Query.Read`) and `jobs.insert`. Measured first against the pinned emulator: its engine adds a function to its in-memory catalog as `CREATE FUNCTION` runs and takes it out as `DROP FUNCTION` runs, and a rollback does not put that back. Through `jobs.query`, after `CREATE FUNCTION ds.f(x INT64) AS (x + 1); SELECT * FROM nope.nope` (answered 501, "nothing the script did was kept") `SELECT ds.f(1)` read 2, and so for `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION`; after `DROP FUNCTION ds.g; …`, `ds.g(1)` failed "Function not found". As a query job both were kept, as BigQuery keeps what the statements before the failing one did. So, for a script of several statements given to `jobs.query`: each function a `CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION` makes is looked up before the script runs (calling it with no arguments: "Function not found" or "No matching signature", measured), and when the script fails, each that did not exist is taken out of the catalog again by a script that drops it and fails, which `jobs.query` rolls back: `f(1)` is then "not found", and the same `CREATE FUNCTION` run later makes it with its new body (`f(1)` read 3). A function that existed before is left as it was (`g(1)` still 10). A table function is taken out the same way, with `DROP TABLE FUNCTION`, which the engine CloudBurrow builds carries out (#1061): after `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION ds.tf …; SELECT * FROM nope.nope` (501), `ds.tf(1)` is "not found", and made again it reads its new body; the pinned emulator refused `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` (400 "Statement not supported: DropTableFunctionStatement", and `DROP FUNCTION` of one left it callable, measured), so this was 501 before anything ran. **501**, nothing run: a `DROP [TABLE] FUNCTION` in such a script (the emulator has no `routines.get`, so the function cannot be read to be made again). A query job is sent as it is (`k(1)` read 6 after its failed job). `DROP SCHEMA` the emulator refuses alone and in a script, through both (measured: 400 "currently unsupported DROP SCHEMA statement", and the dataset and its tables were kept), so a failed script never ran one; since #990 the front carries it out through `datasets.delete` after the query succeeds, and keeps the dataset when the script fails (row `DROP SCHEMA` below; this test's 501 for it is gone). `CREATE FUNCTION` of a function that exists, and `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` of one, are the row after it (#986). Units `TestFailedScriptFunctions`, `TestDropSchema`. |
| `CREATE FUNCTION` of a function that exists, and `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` | **Verified** for a query's first statement and a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE`; **Not implemented** (501) after other statements of a script, for `CREATE OR REPLACE` in a script of several statements, and for `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE FUNCTION` of one that exists | `TestBigQueryCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction` (#986), official Go client, through `jobs.query` (`Query.Read`) and `jobs.insert` (`Query.Run`). BigQuery: `CREATE FUNCTION` of a function that exists fails "Already Exists", `OR REPLACE` replaces it and `IF NOT EXISTS` does nothing ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#create_function_statement)). Measured first against the pinned emulator: `CREATE FUNCTION ds.h(x INT64) AS (x + 1)` then `… AS (x + 2)` **succeeded** and `SELECT ds.h(1)` still read 2; `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION ds.h … (x + 3)` succeeded and `h(1)` **still read 2**; so for `CREATE [OR REPLACE] TABLE FUNCTION`; `IF NOT EXISTS` did nothing; `DROP FUNCTION` took one out, and a function whose body does not analyse was not made. The front looks the function up before the query runs (calling it with no arguments: "Function not found", or "No matching signature" or "Table-valued function is not expected here" when it exists, measured). Now: `CREATE FUNCTION` of one that exists as the query's first statement fails **409** `duplicate` through `jobs.query`, and as a query job the job fails `duplicate` (the emulator is sent a statement that does nothing so it records the job; `jobs.get` shows the client's text), and `h(1)` still reads 2; `IF NOT EXISTS` still does nothing; a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` is carried out, through both: the statement is first run under a scratch name in the same dataset, then that and the old function are dropped (`DROP FUNCTION`) and the statement is sent, so `h(1)` read 11, then 12; one whose body does not analyse fails with the engine's error and `h(1)` still read 12. **501**, nothing run: `CREATE FUNCTION` of one that exists (or that the script made) after other statements of a script (BigQuery runs those and fails there; the emulator cannot be stopped in between), `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` of one that exists in a script of several statements, and `CREATE OR REPLACE [TABLE] FUNCTION` where the old or new one is a table function (the engine cannot drop one, #976); since #1043 every `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION` is 501 (row *Table functions*), so a table function's duplicate is no longer reached. A TEMP function is not looked up. Unit `TestCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction`. |
| `DROP SCHEMA` | **Verified** alone and in a script whose other statements do not name the dataset, `CASCADE` and `RESTRICT`, `IF EXISTS`; **Not implemented** (501) where the other statements of a script could see the dataset | `TestBigQueryDropSchema` (#990) and `TestBigQueryDropSchemaCascadeDropsTableFunctions` (#1061), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. BigQuery deletes the dataset, with `CASCADE` its tables, views and functions, and with `RESTRICT` (the default) "only if it's empty. Otherwise, returns an error" ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#drop_schema_statement)). Measured first against the pinned emulator: every `DROP SCHEMA`, `IF EXISTS` of a dataset that does not exist too, was 400 "currently unsupported DROP SCHEMA statement" (501 in the front since #976).
