Its `datasets.delete` does delete a dataset: without `deleteContents` it refuses one with a table, 400 `resourceInUse` "Dataset project:ds is still in use", and with it the tables go too; it does not count a dataset's functions, and leaves them callable after the dataset is gone (`ds.f(1)` still read 2) until a `DROP FUNCTION`; its engine has no `routines.list` (500 "unsupported") nor `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.ROUTINES`. So the front sends the statement as one that does nothing and, when the query succeeds, deletes the dataset through `datasets.delete` (with `deleteContents` for `CASCADE`) and drops each function it knows in the dataset: one it saw a `CREATE FUNCTION` statement or `routines.insert` make, and, since #1001, one a `CREATE FUNCTION` of the emulator's jobs from before the front started made (the emulator keeps every job and its query in memory while it runs, so a front whose container restarted reads them there: `jobs.list`, then `jobs.get` of each job older than the front, once per project). **Since #1115 a restart of the front's container does not scan again**: what the front knows of the functions, and that it has scanned, is kept in a file on the pod's emptyDir beside the Storage Write streams (unit `TestKnownFunctionsOutliveAFrontRestart`). Measured before: on an instance with 587 jobs, the first `DROP SCHEMA` after a restart of the front's container took 97 seconds, past the Go client's deadline in `TestBigQueryDropSchemaCascadeDropsTableFunctions`. A front that starts without the file (a new pod, whose emulator has no jobs, or an unreadable file) still scans (#1135). Measured for #1001: the engine has no other list of its functions (`routines.get` is 404 even for a function `routines.insert` made, and a GoogleSQL query cannot read the engine's catalog table, "Table not found"), and `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` makes none, since the emulator runs no `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` at all (#1011). **Not found**, and so left callable after `CASCADE` and not counted for `RESTRICT`: a function made through `routines.insert` before the front's container last restarted. Read back: an empty dataset is dropped by `DROP SCHEMA`; one with a table and a function by `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS … CASCADE`, after which the function is "not found" and the dataset made again has no table `t`; `DROP SCHEMA` of one with a table fails `resourceInUse` (400 through `jobs.query`, a failed query job) and the table keeps its row; of one that does not exist, `notFound` (404), or with `IF EXISTS` nothing; `SELECT 1; DROP SCHEMA d CASCADE; SELECT 2` drops it. A dataset whose only content is a function the front saw made fails `DROP SCHEMA` (`RESTRICT`) `resourceInUse` too, and so does one whose only content is a function `Routine.Create` (`routines.insert`) made, which `CASCADE` then drops with it (`TestBigQueryDropSchemaDropsRoutinesInsertFunctions`, #1001; the function called before, "Function not found" after). When the script fails the dataset is kept: `DROP SCHEMA d CASCADE; SELECT * FROM nope.nope` is 501 through `jobs.query` (the emulator rolls the whole script back, #935) and a failed query job answered 501 naming the `DROP SCHEMA`, and `d` is still there. **501**, nothing run: a script whose other statements name the dataset (or whose default dataset it is), a `DROP SCHEMA` in an `EXCEPTION` handler or after a `RETURN`, one that would fail (not found, not empty) after other statements, and anything after the dataset's name other than `CASCADE` or `RESTRICT`. A table function the front knows in the dataset is dropped with `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` since #1061 (501 before: the pinned engine refused it): `RESTRICT` of a dataset holding one fails `resourceInUse`, `CASCADE` drops it, a call is then "not found", and the dataset made again has none (`TestBigQueryDropSchemaCascadeDropsTableFunctions`). Unit `TestDropSchema`, and `TestDropSchemaFindsEveryFunction` (#1001: a function `routines.insert` made, and ones in jobs from before the front started, one named through the job's default dataset; a job after its start is not read). |
| Copy jobs (`Table.CopierFrom`) | **Verified** within the instance's project, one or several sources, every `writeDisposition` and `createDisposition`; **Not implemented** (501) for snapshots, clones and restores, and where noted | `TestBigQueryCopyJobs` (#987), official Go client. Measured first: the emulator answered a copy job **400** "unspecified job configuration query", `jobInternalError`, and copied nothing (it runs only query, load and extract jobs). The front now carries it out as a job of its own (answered by the front, as the extracts it writes are), with `statistics.copy.copiedRows`: a new destination is made through `tables.insert` with the first source's schema (a DDL `CREATE TABLE` would not keep `REQUIRED`: measured, `NOT NULL` read back `NULLABLE`), made as every `tables.insert` is since #1000, a `FLOAT` column as `FLOAT64` (next row); the rows are written by one `INSERT … SELECT … UNION ALL SELECT …` of the sources. Read back: two sources with a `REQUIRED` column, a `RECORD` with a `REPEATED` field, `FLOAT64`, `TIMESTAMP` with microseconds and `NUMERIC` copied into a new table hold the same rows (`TO_JSON_STRING` of each) and its schema reads back as the sources' (names, types, modes, a description), and takes a `FLOAT64` insert; `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` read the job back. `WRITE_EMPTY` (the default) into a table with rows fails `duplicate` and leaves it; `WRITE_APPEND` adds; `WRITE_TRUNCATE` replaces rows and schema (written to a scratch table first, so a failure keeps the destination; the destination is then deleted and made again from the source's schema); `CREATE_NEVER` of a missing table and a missing source fail `notFound`, and nothing is made; a view as a source fails `invalid`. **501**, nothing copied: `operationType` `SNAPSHOT`, `RESTORE` or `CLONE` (the emulator has no snapshots or clones), `destinationEncryptionConfiguration`, `destinationExpirationTime`, a table of another project, a `WRITE_APPEND` (or `WRITE_EMPTY` into an empty table) whose destination's schema differs from the source's (BigQuery's rules for which differences it accepts are not in its documentation), a view as the destination, and a dry run (#1002). Several sources whose schemas are not identical fail the job, `invalid` (BigQuery's documentation: all source tables must have identical schemas; its message is UNVERIFIED), and nothing is copied (#1002). **Why the destination is deleted even when its schema is the source's (#1092, closed):** the front could empty it with `DELETE … WHERE true` and insert the rows, which spares the engine's catalog rebuild of a `DROP TABLE` (*The SQL engine failing* row), only if that gave what BigQuery gives, and BigQuery's documentation does not say what a copy with `WRITE_TRUNCATE` does to the destination's description, labels, expiration or creation time: the copy configuration says only "If the table already exists, BigQuery overwrites the table data and uses the schema and table constraints from the source table" ([JobConfigurationTableCopy.writeDisposition](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationTableCopy)), and Copy tables says "If the copy operation overwrites an existing table, then the table-level access for the existing table is maintained. Tags from the source table aren't copied to the overwritten table, while tags on the existing table are retained" ([managing-tables](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-tables#copy-table)), which the emulator has neither of; row access policies are overwritten and policy tags removed ([row-level-security-intro](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/row-level-security-intro), [column-level-security-intro](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/column-level-security-intro)), which the emulator has neither of either. Whether the destination keeps its own description, labels and expiration or takes none cannot be read, and CloudBurrow does not measure against BigQuery, so the front does not change what it does on a guess. Unit `TestCopyJobs`. |
| `CREATE [TABLE] FUNCTION` of a function that exists, and `CREATE OR REPLACE [TABLE] FUNCTION` | **Verified** for a query's first statement and a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE`, for functions and table functions; **Not implemented** (501) after other statements of a script, and for `CREATE OR REPLACE` in a script of several statements | `TestBigQueryCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction` (#986), official Go client, through `jobs.query` (`Query.Read`) and `jobs.insert` (`Query.Run`). BigQuery: `CREATE FUNCTION` of a function that exists fails "Already Exists", `OR REPLACE` replaces it and `IF NOT EXISTS` does nothing ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#create_function_statement)). Measured first against the pinned emulator: `CREATE FUNCTION ds.h(x INT64) AS (x + 1)` then `… AS (x + 2)` **succeeded** and `SELECT ds.h(1)` still read 2; `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION ds.h … (x + 3)` succeeded and `h(1)` **still read 2**; so for `CREATE [OR REPLACE] TABLE FUNCTION`; `IF NOT EXISTS` did nothing; `DROP FUNCTION` took one out, and a function whose body does not analyse was not made. The front looks the function up before the query runs (calling it with no arguments: "Function not found", or "No matching signature" or "Table-valued function is not expected here" when it exists, measured). Now: `CREATE FUNCTION` of one that exists as the query's first statement fails **409** `duplicate` through `jobs.query`, and as a query job the job fails `duplicate` (the emulator is sent a statement that does nothing so it records the job; `jobs.get` shows the client's text), and `h(1)` still reads 2; `IF NOT EXISTS` still does nothing; a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` is carried out, through both: the statement is first run under a scratch name in the same dataset, then that and the old function are dropped (`DROP FUNCTION`) and the statement is sent, so `h(1)` read 11, then 12; one whose body does not analyse fails with the engine's error and `h(1)` still read 12. **501**, nothing run: `CREATE FUNCTION` of one that exists (or that the script made) after other statements of a script (BigQuery runs those and fails there; the emulator cannot be stopped in between), `CREATE OR REPLACE [TABLE] FUNCTION` of one that exists in a script of several statements. **Table functions** (#1047, #1061), the same test: `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION ds.tf` of one that exists fails 409 `duplicate`, and as a query job `duplicate`; `IF NOT EXISTS` does nothing (`tf(1)` still 1); a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE FUNCTION` is carried out as a function's is, through both, the old one dropped with `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` (which the engine CloudBurrow builds carries out; the pinned one refused it, so this was 501), `tf(1)` then 11, then 12; one whose body does not analyse fails and keeps it; in a script, 501. A `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` of a table function, or the reverse, drops the old one by its own kind (unit). A TEMP function is not looked up. Unit `TestCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction`. |
| `FLOAT` columns (`bigquery.FloatFieldType`) | **Verified** for tables made through `tables.insert`, loads with a schema and CSV loads with autodetect | `TestBigQueryFloatColumnsTakeFloat64` (#1000), official Go client. In BigQuery `FLOAT` and `FLOAT64` are one 64-bit type ([TableFieldSchema.type](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/tables#TableFieldSchema.FIELDS.type)), and the Go client sends `FLOAT` for every float field. Measured first against the pinned emulator: a `FLOAT` column made through `tables.insert` was its engine's 32-bit `FLOAT`, which refused every `FLOAT64` value (400 "Value has type DOUBLE which cannot be inserted into column f, which has type FLOAT": `CAST(0.1 AS FLOAT64)`, a `FLOAT64` query parameter, `INSERT … SELECT` of a `FLOAT64` column, a `STRUCT` with a `FLOAT64` field into a `RECORD` with a `FLOAT` one); so was one a load's schema or a CSV load with autodetect made. An untyped literal and `tabledata.insertAll` were accepted, and read back without losing precision (`0.30000000000000004`, `1.7976931348623157e308` and `123456789.12345679` as sent). `INTEGER`, `BOOLEAN` and `RECORD` are the engine's `INT64`, `BOOL` and `STRUCT` (measured: `CAST(9007199254740993 AS INT64)` and a `BOOL` inserted), and are sent as they are; a field sent by its GoogleSQL name (`FLOAT64` among them) is sent by its legacy name since #1034 (row *GoogleSQL type names in a schema*), so a `FLOAT64` field is made `FLOAT64` in the engine and reads back `FLOAT`. Now the front sends `tables.insert` and a load's schema with each `FLOAT` field (at any depth) as `FLOAT64`, and then sets the schema back to the client's through `tables.patch`, which changes only what `tables.get` reads; a CSV load with autodetect into a new table that detected a `FLOAT` column is made again with it `FLOAT64` and its rows copied in (a scratch table first, as a copy job's `WRITE_TRUNCATE`; if that fails, the load job fails `backendError` naming why). Read back through the Go client: a table with a `FLOAT` column and a `RECORD` with a `FLOAT` field reads back `FLOAT`, and takes `0.1`, `0.30000000000000004`, `1.7976931348623157e308`, `-2.2250738585072014e-308`, `16777217`, `123456789.12345679`, `5e-324` and `-1e300` through `INSERT` with a `FLOAT64` parameter (the column and the struct field), `CAST(0.1 AS FLOAT64)`, `INSERT … SELECT` from a DDL `FLOAT64` table (a query job), `Inserter.Put` and a CSV load appending, and every value reads back exactly (`float64` equality); a table made by a load with that schema, and one by a CSV load with autodetect, read back `f FLOAT` and take a `FLOAT64` `INSERT`. A `FLOAT` column that `tables.update` or `tables.patch` adds is made `FLOAT64` the same way, as the front makes the table again through `tables.insert` to add it (#1010, the schema-update row below). Unit `TestFloatColumnsAreMadeFloat64`. |
| Tables of one ID in two datasets: `tabledata.list`, queries with a `defaultDataset`, DML, loads, extracts | **Verified** | `TestBigQuerySameTableIDInTwoDatasets` and `TestBigQueryExtractReadsItsOwnTable` (#1015), official Go client for both services. In BigQuery a table is named by its project, dataset and table ID, and a query's table name without a dataset is in its default dataset. Measured first against the pinned emulator, with `b1.same (s STRING)` made first and `b2.same (a INT64)` after: `tabledata.list` of `b2.same` answered `b1.same`'s rows (the Go client's `Table.Read` failed "schema length does not match row length"); with `b2` the default dataset, `SELECT * FROM same` read `b1.same`, `INSERT INTO same (a)` failed "Column a is not present in table …_b1_same", and `DELETE FROM same WHERE true` and `TRUNCATE TABLE same` emptied `b1.same`; with no default dataset, `SELECT * FROM same` read `b1.same`. The cause, read in the pinned source: its engine (goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1) registers each table in its catalog under every suffix of its path, the bare table ID included, only when no table holds that name yet, and resolves a table name only in that catalog, so a bare ID names the first table of that ID made in any dataset; the emulator's `tabledata.list` and extract jobs read `` SELECT * FROM `<tableId>` ``. Now the front sends each table name a query gives without a dataset (after `FROM`, `JOIN`, a comma in a `FROM` clause, `MERGE … USING`, and the tables of `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`, `TRUNCATE TABLE`, `CREATE TABLE`/`VIEW` and `DROP TABLE`/`VIEW`; not a CTE's or a script's `TEMP` table's, and not one in a `CREATE VIEW`'s query, which is refused (#1049, the row *A view's table names without a dataset*)) as `` `dataset.table` `` of the default dataset, through `jobs.query` and query jobs (`jobs.get` shows the client's text); answers `tabledata.list` itself, paged as BigQuery pages it (`maxResults`, `startIndex`, `pageToken`: `Table.Read` a row a page reads every row; the emulator pages nothing), with the rows of a `SELECT *` of `dataset.table` when another dataset has a table of the ID (it looks through `datasets.list` and `tables.list`, below), else with the emulator's own read, which is then of this table; and writes a CSV extract of such a table itself. Read back through the Go client, each table's rows are its own after `Table.Read`, `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`, a query job, a CSV load appending, `TRUNCATE TABLE` and an extract. **A table name with no dataset in a query with no default dataset is 400** `invalid` "Table "same" must be qualified with a dataset (e.g. dataset.table)." in the query's first statement (a failed job for `jobs.insert`), BigQuery's message (its wording as the Go client gives it is UNVERIFIED against BigQuery); in a later statement of a script it is sent in a dataset that does not exist, so the statements before it run and it fails ("Table not found: `_cloudburrow_no_default_dataset.same`"). `tabledata.list` with `selectedFields` keeps the cells named itself (the emulator returned every column), top-level and nested (`a.b`, also inside a REPEATED RECORD), in the schema's order, names case-insensitive; a name the schema lacks is 400 `invalid` (#1038, unit `TestSelectedFields`; the order and the 400 are UNVERIFIED against BigQuery). The emulator's Storage Read API reads a table by its bare ID too (read through a view of the front's since #1046), and a view made through `tables.insert` whose query names a table without a dataset read the first table of that ID: both are the rows *Storage Read API of a table whose ID another dataset has* (#1032) and *A view's table names without a dataset* (#1035) below. A function name without a dataset is not changed (#1033). **Cost (#1063):** which table IDs more than one dataset has is read once with `datasets.list` and one `tables.list` a dataset, the front's own datasets (the query results dataset, #1017, and the Storage Read views' dataset) left out, and kept until a request that may make, delete or rename a table (any but a read, or a query of one lone `SELECT` with no destination), or the emulator restarting; a page of such a table is read by `LIMIT` and `OFFSET` of `SELECT *` of `dataset.table`, in the engine's storage order, with the table's `numRows` as `totalRows`, rather than the whole table per page. Before, each request asked `tables.get` of the ID in every other dataset and read the whole table. Measured on my own instance (arm64, `--services storage,bigquery`), each time in a run of the whole BigQuery compat suite and then alone after it on the same instance: before, `TestBigQuerySameTableIDInTwoDatasets` took 78.7 s in the suite (its five checks 8.8–17.8 s each) and 143.7 s after it (18.4–34.8 s each); after, 46.3 s in the suite (3.2–7.6 s each) and 3.3 s after it (0.19–0.36 s each). The before run left 10 datasets on the instance and the after run 1, so the two late runs did not start from the same state. The test still gives each step a context of its own (#1051). Units `TestTableDataListPagesByQueryAndKeepsTheSharedIDs`, `TestTableIDsMayChange`, `TestTableIDsAreNotKeptAcrossAChange`, Units `TestTableNamesAreQualified`, `TestQueriesAreSentQualified`, `TestTableDataListReadsTheWholeName`. |
| Function names without a dataset in a query with a `defaultDataset` | **Verified** for a persistent function the front knows (made by a `CREATE FUNCTION` it sent on, `routines.insert` through it, or the script), and since #1107 for a call of another dataset's function beside the default dataset's, in a query, DML, a script, DDL and beside `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` or `@@dataset_id` (#1123), and since #1122 for a call by one quoted path (`` `project.dataset.fn` ``); **Not supported**: `@@dataset_id` reads NULL with a default dataset (#1137) | `TestBigQueryFunctionNameInTheDefaultDataset` (#1033), official Go client (`Query.DefaultDatasetID`, `Query.Run`, `Client.JobFromID`). In BigQuery the default dataset is "the default dataset to use for unqualified table names in the query … Setting the system variable `@@dataset_id` achieves the same behavior" ([JobConfigurationQuery.defaultDataset](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationQuery.FIELDS.default_dataset)), `@@dataset_id` "is used when a dataset is not specified for a project in the query" ([system variables](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/system-variables)), and a persistent function must be named with its dataset only "from another persistent UDF or a logical view" ([persistent UDF limitations](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/user-defined-functions)). The emulator's engine registers a function under its bare name only when no function holds it yet, as a table (#1015), and never reads the default dataset for one. Measured first against the pinned emulator, with `one.fn(x) AS (x + 1)` made first and `two.fn(x) AS (x + 2)`: `SELECT fn(0)` with the default dataset `two` failed 400 "failed to query SELECT fn(0): sqlite3: SQL logic error: no such column: <project>"; `SELECT two.fn(0)` read 2. So the front sends a call without a dataset of a function the default dataset has as `dataset.name`, and leaves a built-in's name (the engine takes `SAFE.` only for a built-in, measured), a `TEMP` function's, one after `FROM` or `JOIN`, and those in a `CREATE FUNCTION`, `CREATE PROCEDURE` or `CREATE VIEW` body, as they are. Read back: `SELECT fn(0)` reads 2 with the default dataset `two` and 1 with `one`, `FN(0)` and `` `fn`(1) `` 2 and 3 with `UPPER('a')` `A`, a query job `SELECT fn(10)` reads 12 and `jobs.get` shows the client's text, and a script's `CREATE FUNCTION two.g …; SELECT g(2)` runs and `SELECT g(3)` reads 9. **Not qualified**: a function the front does not know (one `routines.insert` made before the front started), which the engine resolves by its bare name. **A function of another dataset** (#1107): in BigQuery each call of `dataset.name` calls that dataset's function whatever the default dataset. Measured first (every name qualified): `SELECT one.fn(0)` with the default dataset `two`, and `SELECT two.fn(1), one.fn(2)` with the default dataset `one` or `two`, failed "sqlite3: SQL logic error: no such column: <project>". The engine resolves the call, but collects the SQL functions it may inline from the default dataset's name path only (its analyzer's `getFunctions` keeps a function whose path the key `project_dataset` is a substring of), and sends any other as a bare storage name `<project>_one_fn(…)`, which SQLite reads as a subtraction; with no default dataset every function of the project is collected. Fixed in the engine CloudBurrow builds (googlesqlite patch 0007: the analyzer collects every dataset's functions and table functions, keyed by their full storage names), so the front sends such a query as it is, with its default dataset. Read back: `SELECT one.fn(0)` with the default dataset `two` reads 1, `SELECT two.fn(0)` with `one` 2, `SELECT two.fn(1), one.fn(2)` 3 and 3 with no default dataset and with `one`, `SELECT fn(1), one.fn(2), `` `project`.one.fn(3) `` 3, 3 and 4 with `two`; a query job `INSERT INTO t (v) SELECT one.fn(fn(0))` and `SELECT v, one.fn(v) FROM t` with the default dataset `two` write and read 3 and 4, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text and default dataset; with the default dataset `two` (#1123, 501 before), a script `DECLARE x INT64 DEFAULT one.fn(1); SELECT one.fn(x)` reads 3, `SELECT one.fn(1), @@dataset_id IS NULL` 2 and true (`@@dataset_id` reads NULL with a default dataset, #1137), `SELECT one.fn(1), (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM two.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES) > 0` 2 and true, and `CREATE TABLE c AS SELECT one.fn(5) AS v` then `CREATE VIEW vw AS SELECT one.fn(v) AS w FROM c` make a view that reads 7. **A call by one quoted path** (#1122): the [user-defined functions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/user-defined-functions) guide names a function so (``CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION `project.dataset.masking_routine1`(ssn STRING)``). Measured first: `` `project.dataset.fn`(3) `` and `` `dataset.fn`(1) `` failed "Function not found" with or without a default dataset; the engine registered a table, but not a function, under its whole dotted path. Fixed in the engine (googlesqlite patch 0008, table functions too): `` `project.one.fn`(3) `` reads 4 with the default dataset `two` and with none, `` `one.fn`(1), `two.fn`(1) `` 2 and 3. Units `TestFunctionNamesInTheDefaultDataset`, `TestFunctionOfAnotherDataset`. Since #1137 `@@dataset_id` in a query with a default dataset reads that dataset's ID (the emulator read NULL; the front sends it as a string literal unless the query `SET`s it), unit `TestDatasetIDVariable`, compat `TestBigQueryDatasetIDIsTheDefaultDataset`. |
| `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` | **Verified** for a string literal, or a script variable set to one by its `DECLARE`'s `DEFAULT` or a `SET` (a `CONCAT` or `||` of string literals and such variables counts as one, #1037, unit only), with `USING` literals, script variables or query parameters, named or positional; **Not implemented** (501) for the rest | `TestBigQueryExecuteImmediate` (#1011), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and query jobs. Measured first against the pinned emulator: every `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` was answered done and ran nothing: `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE 'SELECT 1 AS a'` returned no rows and no schema, `'CREATE TABLE ds.t (x INT64)'` made no table (a query job ended `DONE` with no error), and `'CREATE FUNCTION …'`, alone, in `BEGIN … END` and from a `DECLARE`d variable, made no function. BigQuery runs the statement ([EXECUTE IMMEDIATE](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/procedural-language#execute_immediate)). Now the front replaces each one it can carry out with its statement before the query is sent, each query parameter (`@name`, `?`) with its `USING` value in parentheses, and then checks and runs the query as any other (`jobs.get` shows the client's text). Read back: `SELECT 1 AS a` returns 1, `'SELECT @a + 1' USING 1 AS a` returns 2, `'SELECT ? * 10, ?' USING 4, 'four'` returns 40 and `four`, a variable's statement and a `USING` variable are read; `CREATE TABLE` makes the table, and `CREATE FUNCTION` alone, in `BEGIN … END` and from a variable set by `SET` makes the function, which is then called. **501** `notImplemented`, before anything of the query runs (a `CREATE TABLE` before it is not made): SQL that is an expression other than a `CONCAT` or `||` of string literals and variables set to them (`CONCAT('SELECT ', CAST(x AS STRING))`), a variable set by any other expression, `INTO` (not carried out: unmeasured whether the emulator's scripts run the `SET … = (SELECT …)` it would become), a `USING` value that is an expression (`1 + 1`, which the front would evaluate once per reference), a statement of several statements or with an `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` of its own, and a bytes literal (#1037). A parameter with no `USING` value, a count of positional values that differs, and named and positional mixed are **400** `invalidQuery` (a failed job for `jobs.insert`), BigQuery's wording UNVERIFIED. Units `TestExecuteImmediateIsExpanded`, `TestExecuteImmediateOfAConstant`. |
| Schema updates: `tables.patch` and `tables.update` (`Table.Update`) | **Verified** for new top-level columns at the end (NULLABLE or REPEATED, of any type the emulator makes, `RECORD` included), fields added at the end of a `RECORD` or a `REPEATED RECORD` at any depth (#1036), a schema given in another order (the table keeps its own), a mode relaxed from `REQUIRED` to `NULLABLE`, and descriptions; **400** for what BigQuery refuses; **Not implemented** (501) for a new column or field given before one the table has (#1111), a renamed column and a view's columns | `TestBigQueryTableUpdateAddsColumns` (#1010, #1013, #1036), the official Go client's `Table.Update` (`tables.patch`) and the generated client's `Tables.Update` (`tables.update`, google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2). Measured first against the pinned emulator: both answered 200 and `tables.get` read the new columns, but the emulator changed only the table's metadata (its `tablesPatchHandler` and `tablesUpdateHandler` call `metadata.Table.Patch`/`Replace`), never its engine's columns: `INSERT INTO ds.t (f, g)` failed 400 "Column g is not present in table", `SELECT h` failed "Unrecognized name: h", and every later `tabledata.insertAll`, with or without the new column, failed **500** "Column … is not present", which the Go client retries until its deadline (#1013). In BigQuery an added column is added to the table, NULL (or empty) in its rows ([Add a column](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-table-schemas#add_columns)). Now the front, before it sends the request on, makes the table again when its schema adds columns: its rows are copied to a scratch table made with the new schema (in `_cloudburrow_query_results`, deleted later while the instance is idle, #1091), the table is deleted and made again with the new schema and its description, labels, expiration, partitioning and clustering (through `tables.insert`, so a `FLOAT` column is `FLOAT64`, #1000), the rows are copied back, and its creation time is set back; then the request is sent on. Read back through the Go client: a table with a `REQUIRED` `INTEGER` and a `FLOAT` column and two rows, after `Table.Update` adding a `STRING`, a `REPEATED STRING` and a `RECORD` and relaxing the first to `NULLABLE`, reads the new schema, keeps its description, labels and creation time, and its two rows read the new columns NULL (the `REPEATED` one empty); it then takes `Inserter.Put` with all the columns, with the old ones only and with none of the old ones (a NULL `id`), a DML `INSERT` of the new ones (a query job), and a CSV load with the new `STRING` column, and `Table.Read` and a `SELECT` of the new column read every row; `Tables.Update` adding an `INTEGER` then takes an `UPDATE … SET` of it. **400** `invalid`, the table unchanged ("Provided Schema does not match Table p:ds.t. Field id is missing in new schema", "… Field id has changed type from INTEGER to STRING", "… Field id has changed mode from NULLABLE to REQUIRED", "… Cannot add required fields to an existing schema. (field: req)"; BigQuery refuses each, its wording UNVERIFIED). **Fields added to a `RECORD`** (#1036): BigQuery adds a nested field through the same methods, at the end of its `RECORD` ("New columns and nested fields are always added at the end of the table or field", [Add a nested column to a RECORD column](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-table-schemas#add_a_nested_column_to_a_record_column)). The front makes the table again as above, and copies each row with every `RECORD` rebuilt by name in the new schema's order, the new fields NULL (a `REPEATED` one empty), a NULL `RECORD` NULL and a `REPEATED RECORD`'s elements in their order (`IF(r IS NULL, NULL, STRUCT(r.x AS x, …, CAST(NULL AS T) AS y))`, `ARRAY(SELECT STRUCT(…) FROM UNNEST(rr) … ORDER BY offset)`; measured first: read back every row as it was, the new fields NULL). Read back: after `Table.Update` adding a `RECORD` `n` to the `RECORD` `r` and a new `REPEATED RECORD` `rr`, a DML `INSERT` of both, and a second `Table.Update`, given with its first two columns swapped, adding `r.n.w` (`BYTES`, two levels down), `r.q` (`REPEATED STRING`) and `rr.v` (`NUMERIC`, in the `REPEATED RECORD`), the table reads the new schema in its own order, every earlier row reads as it did (id, f, g, tags, whether `r` is NULL, `r.x`, h), the new fields read NULL or empty and `rr`'s elements in order, and the table takes an `INSERT` of `b'\xff\x00'`, `['q1']` and `NUMERIC '1.25'` into them, which read back so. **A schema in another order** is sent in the table's order, the new columns and fields after its own: "Modifying schema order after table creation doesn't have an effect on column or nested field order" (same page, Add columns). **501** `notImplemented`, nothing changed: a new column or field given before one the table has (BigQuery refuses it: "If you attempt to add new columns elsewhere in the array, the following error is returned: BigQuery error in update operation: Precondition Failed"; its REST answer is not documented), a column's name in another case, and a view's columns other than descriptions. The emulator's engine keeps no `NOT NULL`, so a mode and a description are metadata alone and are sent as they are. A `tables.update` with no schema is sent as it is. A `tables.patch` that also gives labels or a description is then carried out as the next row says, the table read once for both (#1054). Units `TestSchemaUpdates`, `TestSchemaChange`, `TestAlterTableAdviceIsTrue`, `TestConvertColumns`. |
| GoogleSQL type names in a schema (`INT64`, `BOOL`, `FLOAT64`, `STRUCT`, `DECIMAL`, `BIGDECIMAL`) | **Verified** for `tables.insert`, a load job's schema and `tables.patch` | `TestBigQueryGoogleSQLTypeNamesReadBack` (#1034), official Go client. BigQuery's reference lists each field type by its legacy name with the GoogleSQL name as an accepted alias: "INTEGER (or INT64)", "FLOAT (or FLOAT64)", "BOOLEAN (or BOOL)", "RECORD (or STRUCT)" ([TableFieldSchema.type](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/tables#TableFieldSchema.FIELDS.type)); the Go client says "The API will accept alias names for the types based on the Standard SQL type names" (its `fieldAliases`, with `DECIMAL` and `BIGDECIMAL` for `NUMERIC` and `BIGNUMERIC`) and reads only the legacy names back (`Table.Read` fails "unrecognized type: INT64" on any other). Measured first against the pinned emulator: `tables.insert` of fields typed `INT64` and `BOOL` read back `INT64` and `BOOL`, and `Table.Read` failed "unrecognized type: INT64"; a table made by `CREATE TABLE … (n INT64, b BOOL, f FLOAT64, r STRUCT<x INT64>)`, and a query's result schema, read back `INTEGER`, `BOOLEAN`, `FLOAT` and `RECORD`. Now the front sends every alias in the schema of `tables.insert`, `tables.update`, `tables.patch` and a load job (at any depth) by its legacy name; the engine's types are the same (a `FLOAT` field is then made `FLOAT64` as in the `FLOAT` row above). Read back through the Go client: a table made with `INT64`, `BOOL`, `FLOAT64`, `DECIMAL` and a `STRUCT` of an `INT64` reads `INTEGER`, `BOOLEAN`, `FLOAT`, `NUMERIC` and `RECORD` of an `INTEGER`, and its row through `Table.Read`; so does a JSON load with that schema, and a column added as `INT64` through `Table.Update` reads `INTEGER`. That BigQuery's own `tables.get` of a field made as `INT64` reads `INTEGER` is **UNVERIFIED** here: it is inferred from the official client, which could not read the table otherwise, and from the emulator's own DDL. Units `TestGoogleSQLTypeNamesAreSentAsLegacyNames`, `TestFloatColumnsAreMadeFloat64`, `TestSchemaUpdates`. |
| A view's table names without a dataset (`tables.insert`, `tables.update`, `tables.patch` of a view) | **Verified**: refused 400 | `TestBigQueryViewsNameTheirTablesWithADataset` (#1035), official Go client. BigQuery: "A reference inside of a view must be qualified with a dataset. The default dataset doesn't affect a view body." ([Views, limitations](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/views)). Measured first against the pinned emulator, with `one.t` made first and `two.t` after: a view `two.v` made through `Table.Create` with `ViewQuery` `SELECT * FROM t` was made and read `one.t`'s rows, through `Table.Read` and a query. Now a view's (or materialized view's) GoogleSQL query that names a table without a dataset, where a query reads or writes one (as in *Tables of one ID in two datasets*: not a CTE's name), is **400** `invalid` "Table \"t\" must be qualified with a dataset (e.g. dataset.table). A view's query is not read in any default dataset, not even the view's own.", and nothing is made (BigQuery's wording for a view is UNVERIFIED); one naming `dataset.t` reads that table. A legacy SQL view is not read. **A `CREATE VIEW` statement's query** (and `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW`'s) is held to the same rule whatever the query's default dataset (#1049, `TestBigQueryCreateViewNamesItsTablesWithADataset`, official Go client, through `jobs.query` and as a query job): since #1015 the front had sent its table names in the default dataset, so `CREATE VIEW v AS SELECT * FROM t` with `two` the default dataset made a view of `two.t`. Now, in the query's first statement, it is **400** `invalid` with the message above (a query job fails so), for `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW` too (before its 501), and nothing is made; in a later statement of a script the name is sent in a dataset that does not exist, so the script runs the statements before it and fails there, as for a query with no default dataset. The view's own name is in the default dataset, and `CREATE VIEW v AS SELECT * FROM one.t` with `two` the default makes `two.v` reading `one.t`. What BigQuery answers such a statement was **not measured** (no live GCP); the refusal follows the reference quoted above, and its wording is UNVERIFIED. Units `TestViewsNameTheirTablesWithADataset`, `TestTableNamesAreQualified`. |
| Table functions: `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [TEMP] TABLE FUNCTION`, `routines.insert` of a `TABLE_VALUED_FUNCTION`, calls, `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryTableFunctions` (#1061), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and query jobs: a table function made by `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION` (two arguments, two columns), by a query job, and by `Routine.Create` of a `TABLE_VALUED_FUNCTION` is called with its arguments (`SELECT * FROM ds.tf(1, 'a')`, with an alias and a `WHERE`, and under `SUM`); each still answers after 40 queries of 50,000 rows each and a `CREATE TABLE` and `DROP TABLE` every tenth (which rebuild the engine's catalog), run between its creation and its call; `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE FUNCTION` replaces it, through both, and the new one survives 40 more; `DROP TABLE FUNCTION` drops one (a call is then "not found"), `IF EXISTS` of one that is gone does nothing and without it fails; one made again after a drop has its new body; a `TEMP` table function is called in its script; and the dataset outlives it all. Duplicates, `IF NOT EXISTS` and a failed `CREATE OR REPLACE` are `TestBigQueryCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction` (row above), a failed script `TestBigQueryFailedScriptFunctionsAndDropSchema`, `DROP SCHEMA … CASCADE` `TestBigQueryDropSchemaCascadeDropsTableFunctions`. **Why it needed CloudBurrow's build:** in the pinned v0.8.1, goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1's `tvfHandleForSpec` (`internal/catalog.go` line 2651) kept only the `*TableValuedFunction` embedded in the `*FixedOutputSchemaTVF` go-googlesql's `NewFixedOutputSchemaTVF` returned, while go-googlesql's finalizer that deletes the C++ object is on the outer handle, so a garbage collection freed a table function its catalog still used (#1043); and its analyzer refused `DROP TABLE FUNCTION`. The front answered every table function 501 (#1043) until the patches (third_party/bigquery-emulator, googlesqlite 0002 and 0003). Measured against v0.8.1's image: on the emulator alone, `CREATE TABLE FUNCTION`, 30 queries of 50,000 rows and a call exited it (SIGSEGV, exit code 2); and `TestBigQueryTableFunctions`, run against it on an instance, failed at the first check after the queries ("wasm trap: runtime error: index out of range", "interface conversion"), and the emulator then crashed and restarted with the dataset lost. The same test passes against CloudBurrow's build, and so did the emulator-alone sequence with 90 queries. The patches carry their own regression tests (`TestTableFunctionSurvivesGC`, `TestDropTableFunction`). |
| `CREATE OR REPLACE TEMP TABLE` and `DROP TABLE` of a `TEMP` table the script created | **Not implemented** (501) | `TestBigQueryReplacingATempTableIs501` (#936), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: `CREATE TEMP TABLE t AS SELECT 1 AS a; CREATE OR REPLACE TEMP TABLE t AS SELECT 2 AS a; SELECT * FROM t` failed 400 "failed to scan rows: failed to delete table spec: failed to find table spec from map by <project>_t", and so did the same with `DROP TABLE t` (with or without `IF EXISTS`, with or without a new `CREATE TEMP TABLE t` after it) and with a `TEMP` table made with a column list; BigQuery runs each. Such a script is **501** before anything of it is run. `CREATE OR REPLACE TEMP TABLE` of a name the script had not used runs (read back). Upstream: goccy/googlesqlite#92 and goccy/bigquery-emulator#522 (open) look related. Unit `TestReplacingATempTableIs501`. |
| Columns of `CREATE TEMP TABLE … AS SELECT` whose query cannot run alone | **Verified** when no statement before it changes data; **Not implemented** (501) after one that does | `TestBigQueryTempTableColumnsAreChecked` (#938), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: it made `` DECLARE x …; CREATE TEMP TABLE tt AS SELECT x AS `b!` `` and a `TEMP` table from another with a column `` r.`c?` ``. A `TEMP` table is gone when its script ends, so it cannot be checked after the script as other tables are (the row above), and the emulator has no sessions. The front instead runs the statements before it and then `SELECT * FROM (query) LIMIT 0` as a script of its own, whose result's columns are the table's (measured: such a script returned its last query's columns, `b!` and a STRUCT's `c?` included), with the variables renamed as above: a name the rules refuse is **400 `invalidQuery`** and the script is not run; names BigQuery takes run (8 read back from a `TEMP` table made from another and a variable). That runs the statements before it twice, so only when none of them changes data (a query, `DECLARE`, `SET`, `TEMP` tables and DML on them); after one that does, it is **501** and nothing is run. If the columns cannot be read so, the script is run: its failure is the answer, and if it does not fail the query is failed 501, saying that the script ran. Unit `TestTempTableColumnsAreChecked`. |
| The text of a job the front changed | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryReplacedTableJobShowsTheClientsQuery` (#939), official Go client (`Job.Config`, `Client.JobFromID`). The front sends the emulator another query than the client's for a lone `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` of an existing table, a `CREATE … IF NOT EXISTS` of one that exists, and a script with variables (#933); and another destination URI for a wildcard extract (below). The emulator records the job it was sent, so its `configuration.query.query` read `` … AS SELECT * FROM `ds._cloudburrow_replace_…` `` (measured). The front keeps the client's text of the last 1,000 such jobs and puts it back in `jobs.insert`'s answer, `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` (the emulator's `jobs.list` gives no configuration, measured; the front gives it, #958, row below). Unit `TestReplacedTableJobShowsTheClientsQuery`. |
| Extract jobs to Cloud Storage (`Table.ExtractorTo`) | **Verified** for CSV of a table to one URI (the emulator's, uncompressed with `,`, unless another dataset has a table of the ID, #1015; the front's for the rest, next row); **Not implemented** (501) for the rest listed | `TestBigQueryExtractToCloudStorage` (#939), official Go client for both services. The file is written to **the instance's own Cloud Storage**, which the emulator is given as `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` (#919), never `storage.googleapis.com`; the front looks the bucket up there first (`--storage`). Read back byte for byte: a header row, then `1,"x,y",,true,YWI=,2020-01-02,1.25` (INT64, STRING, a NULL, BOOL, BYTES as base64, DATE, NUMERIC), a quote doubled; `DisableHeader`; a wildcard URI `out-*.csv` is written as `out-000000000000.csv`, BigQuery's name for the first file, and `jobs.get` shows the client's URI. Measured against the pinned emulator and handled: with no `destinationFormat` (what the Go client sends for CSV) it failed "unsupported destination format" and left an empty object, so CSV, BigQuery's default, is sent; it named a wildcard URI's file `out-*.csv`; it **created a bucket that did not exist**, now **404** `notFound`, the bucket still missing; it wrote a view's rows and a nested schema as JSON text in a field, now **400** `invalid` as BigQuery refuses both; `printHeader: true` wrote no header, so it is sent without. The emulator also wrote `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` values all as JSON strings (a FLOAT64 1.5 as `"1.5"`, a BOOL as `"true"`), ignored a `compression` and a `fieldDelimiter`, and wrote an empty table with a header row as an empty object: those the front writes itself (#957, next row). **501**, nothing written: `PARQUET`, and `AVRO` of other than the columns of the next row (the emulator: 400 "unsupported destination format", leaving an empty object); `DEFLATE` or `SNAPPY` for CSV or JSON (BigQuery documents them only for Avro and Parquet) and a `fieldDelimiter` of more than one character or not printable ASCII or a tab; several URIs (each was written the whole table, where BigQuery shares the rows out, in a way its documentation does not give); a CSV column other than STRING, INT64, BOOL, BYTES, DATE or NUMERIC (a TIMESTAMP was written `2020-01-02 03:04:05+00`, not BigQuery's `… UTC`; the rest were not measured against BigQuery's form, and the [export documentation](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/exporting-data) does not give them); a model; a CSV extract of a table whose STRING or BYTES column holds an empty value (`''`, not NULL) (#975): the emulator and the front's CSV writer write it as they write a NULL, an empty field (Go's `encoding/csv` quotes neither), and BigQuery's [export documentation](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/exporting-data) and [`EXPORT DATA`](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/export-statements) give the form of neither, so CloudBurrow cannot write it as BigQuery does; the front looks for one with a query of its own first (`TestBigQueryCSVExtractOfAnEmptyString`: 501 and no object written; a table with a NULL is extracted, the NULL as an empty field; a JSON extract writes `"s":""`). The bytes of a field that needs no quoting in BigQuery but begins with a space are quoted by the emulator's CSV writer (Go's `encoding/csv`); a CSV reader reads both the same. Units `TestExtractJobs`, `TestCSVExtractOfAnEmptyString`. |
| Extract jobs the front writes: `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON`, `GZIP`, a `fieldDelimiter`, an empty table's header row | **Verified** for CSV with a one-character delimiter or `GZIP` (the column types of the row above), the header row of an empty table, and JSON of STRING and INT64 columns, plain or `GZIP`; **Not implemented** (501) for JSON of other types, of a NULL, or of a nested schema; **Implemented, not compat-tested** (#957): `AVRO` of STRING, INT64, FLOAT64, BOOL and BYTES columns, uncompressed, `DEFLATE` or `SNAPPY` (501 for other column types, a nested schema and `GZIP`) | `TestBigQueryExtractsTheFrontWrites` (#957), official Go client for both services. The front reads the table's rows from the emulator (`tabledata.list`, or a `SELECT *` of `dataset.table` when another dataset has a table of the ID, #1015; `TestBigQueryExtractReadsItsOwnTable` extracts a table whose ID another dataset's table, made first, has, and reads back its own rows), writes the file as the [export documentation](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/exporting-data) gives it, and uploads it to **the instance's own Cloud Storage** (`--storage`); the job is the front's, answered by `jobs.get`, `jobs.list`, `jobs.cancel` and `jobs.delete` (the last 1,000, in its memory), with `destinationUriFileCounts` 1 and times in milliseconds; a job ID in use is 409. CSV ("You can control the CSV delimiter"; "GZIP" is listed for CSV and JSON): the bytes are the emulator's comma-separated ones of the row above, measured to be BigQuery's, with the delimiter (a tab read back byte for byte, `x,y` unquoted) or compressed (the `GZIP` object read back decompressed equal to the emulator's `,` file); a wildcard URI is written as its `000000000000` file. An empty table with a header ("header rows are printed to the exported data if the data format supports headers"): its header row, `a,b`. JSON: one object per row, members in the schema's order, "INT64 (integer) data types are encoded as JSON strings" (`"9007199254740993"` read back) and "the symbols `<`, `>`, and `&` are converted by using the unicode notation" (`\u003c`, `\u003e`, `\u0026` read back); written with no spaces, which the documentation does not give. **501**, nothing written: a JSON row with a NULL (the documentation does not say how one is written), a JSON column of another type (the documentation gives none), a JSON extract of a nested schema, a dry run, and a front with no Cloud Storage. AVRO: an Avro object container file, one record field a column in the schema's order, each of the Avro type the [Avro export details](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/exporting-data#avro_export_details) map it to (INT64 `long`, FLOAT64 `double`, BOOL `boolean`, STRING `string`, BYTES `bytes`), a NULLABLE column a union with `null`; the record's name `Root` is UNVERIFIED against BigQuery, and the sync marker and block sizes are the writer's (an Avro reader reads the same rows, not the same bytes). Unit `TestExtractsTheFrontWrites`, `TestAvroExtractReadsBack`. |
| BQML (`CREATE MODEL` …) | **Not supported** | Measured: `Statement not supported: CreateModelStatement`. |
| Projects | **One only** | The emulator serves the project it is started with, which is the instance's default. Every other project is 404 "project … is not found" (`TestBigQueryOtherProjectsAreNotFound`). |
| Persistence | **None, measured** | A dataset created before a container restart was gone after it. CloudBurrow provisions no volume, whatever `--mode` says. |
| The SQL engine failing after many queries | **Fixed** for the 2 GiB panic (#1061); the mitigation below is kept for any other failure of the engine | #989. **Since #1061** CloudBurrow builds the emulator with go-googlesql patched to address WebAssembly memory with unsigned offsets (third_party/bigquery-emulator, go-googlesql patch 0001): its test `TestInvokeAboveTwoGiB` fills the engine's heap below 2 GiB and makes a call whose buffers lie above it, which panicked "slice bounds out of range [-2110640288:]" without the patch and passes with it. `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh --jobs 150` on an instance of CloudBurrow's build (with the rest of #1061 and the fronts of main at the time) ran 150 query jobs and 30 `CREATE TABLE`/`DROP TABLE` pairs with no restart, the emulator container at 754 MiB at the end (281 MiB at the start), so the soak no longer reaches 2 GiB and the patched path past it is measured by the patch's test alone. **Growth through DDL** (#1057): every `DROP` of a table, view or function rebuilds every catalog, and each project's and dataset's sub-catalog registered the whole builtin function set again; CloudBurrow's build carries upstream's open goccy/googlesqlite#80 (googlesqlite patch 0006), which builds those sub-catalogs bare. Measured on an instance with 10 datasets of a table each, `bigquery-engine-soak.sh` from PR #1093, 40 operations: `--phase ddl` (CREATE and DROP TABLE) 4342 MiB before the patch, 231 MiB with it (0.50 s and 0.12 s an operation); `--phase orreplace` (CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE … AS SELECT) 11147 MiB and a 500 at the 25th before, 341 MiB and all 40 with it. The BigQuery compat suite (but `TestBigQueryJobsAcrossAnEmulatorRestart`, which restarts the emulator) on one fresh emulator took it from 62 to 2462 MiB before, and from 60 to 1372 MiB with it: less, not gone. What follows is the pinned emulator, as measured before #1061. The pinned emulator's SQL engine, goccy/go-googlesql v0.3.0 (a WebAssembly module translated to Go), lets its memory grow to 4 GiB but addresses it with signed 32-bit offsets (`(*Module).invoke`, `googlesql.go:614`, `624`), so once that memory passes 2 GiB every call panics "slice bounds out of range [-2147…:]": in a request, which the emulator answers 400 or 500 "googlesqlite: panic …" (from then on to every request, `SELECT 1` and `datasets.list` alike), or in a finalizer, which kills the process. The memory grows with each query job that returns rows (the emulator kept each result in a dataset of its own, and the engine builds a catalog of every builtin function for each) and each `DROP TABLE` (which rebuilds every dataset's catalog). The engine's objects are freed by Go finalizers, which run only when the emulator's Go heap is collected, and the engine's memory is one large `[]byte` in that heap, so a collection waits for the heap to double past it: freed engine memory is reused late, and the memory does not shrink. Measured with `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh`: the emulator container went from 455 MiB to 3840 MiB in 40 query jobs and 8 `CREATE TABLE`/`DROP TABLE` pairs, and the 60th job's `DROP TABLE` was answered the panic; one run of the BigQuery compat suite left it at 2692 MiB. go-googlesql v0.4.0 is the same. Upstream: the growth per query is goccy/bigquery-emulator#512 and #313, with a fix in review, goccy/googlesqlite#80; no upstream issue names the signed offsets. **Mitigation:** the front watches the emulator's error answers for that panic (`engineGuard`); at the first it answers that request **501** `notImplemented` naming this, sends the emulator nothing more, and fails the engine's liveness path (`/cloudburrow/bigquery-engine-live` on the front's port), so the emulator is restarted; every request until its port has closed and opened again is answered **503** `backendError` with `Retry-After: 1` (the official clients retry a 503), and then requests pass. Measured before #1091: the soak's panic was answered 501, the container's liveness probe failed, and 14 s later the emulator had restarted (restart count 1) and `SELECT 1` answered 200. **Restarts without Kubernetes' back-off (#1091):** Kubernetes restarted the emulator's container (for the probe, or when the process crashed, #1058), and the kubelet waits longer before each restart of a container that keeps failing (`CrashLoopBackOff`: 10 s, doubled at each restart up to 5 minutes, and reset only after the container has run 10 minutes). On one instance the BigQuery compat suite run four times in a row (#1057, below) reached "back-off 5m0s restarting failed container=bigquery" after the seventh restart, and in two of four series its fourth run failed most tests with 502 "the BigQuery emulator did not answer: dial tcp 127.0.0.1:9051: connect: connection refused" (545 s and 562 s). Now the emulator's container runs `cloudburrow-storage supervise` (`internal/supervisor`), which an init container copies in from the front's image (`install-self`), and which runs the image's entrypoint (`/bin/bigquery-emulator`) as its child: when the child ends, for any reason (the panic in a finalizer, a SIGSEGV, an out-of-memory kill of it), it starts it again within at most 1 s (100 ms, doubled while it keeps ending within 2 s of its start); it gets the front's liveness path every 0.5 s from 1 s after each start and kills the child (SIGKILL) after two 503s in a row, and restarts it the same way; no answer from the front (the front restarting) is not a failure. The container never restarts, so the back-off never applies, and it has no liveness probe. While the emulator's port refuses connections the front answers 503 with `Retry-After` too, not 502. `POST /cloudburrow/bigquery-restart-emulator` on the front's port restarts the emulator as the panic does (answered 202), for tests, soaks, or an instance grown slow (it empties the emulator). Measured on an instance of my own: `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh --phase restart --jobs 10` (a restart, then `datasets.list` until 200) took 1.0–2.6 s each, answering 503 then 200, with the container's restart count 0 and its memory at 78 MiB throughout (the first build, which waited 5 s after a start before checking, took 2.0 s and then 5.9–6.3 s); killing the emulator's process (`kill -9`) three times, it answered 503 and was back in 0.2 s each; the BigQuery compat suite passed, with `TestBigQueryEmulatorRestartsPromptly`'s eight restarts in a row back in 1.0–2.7 s. The suite run four times in a row on that emulator: 305 s, all passed; then 605 s, 610 s and 607 s, each stopped by the 10-minute limit (one test failed "context deadline exceeded" in the third, two in the fourth), with no restart of the emulator, no "connection refused" and no 502 in any of them: nothing failed the engine or crashed the process (table functions, whose SIGSEGV restarted it before, are 501 since #1052), so the emulator was never emptied and the growth through DDL below made it slower with each run (#1103). Units `TestCrashesRestartPromptly` (ten starts of a child that crashes, each within the delay), `TestLivenessFailuresRestartPromptly` (eight restarts in a row through the liveness path, none while it answers 200 or does not answer), `TestRestartEmulatorPath`, `TestProxyAnswersARefusedPort503`; the manifest in `TestBigQueryManifestPutsTheFrontOnTheServicePort`. **Growth per query job, fixed (#1017):** the front names a query job's destination table itself, so the emulator writes every result to one dataset (next row) and a job no longer adds a catalog. Measured with the soak script on one instance, from a fresh emulator: 60 query jobs and 12 `CREATE TABLE`/`DROP TABLE` pairs took the container from 62 MiB to 4301 MiB in 23.7 s before, and from 66 MiB to 701 MiB in 7.8 s after; 400 jobs and 80 pairs held it at 799–802 MiB from the 100th job on (100 s). Tried and measured worse: deleting each job's result dataset right after the job (64 → 1964 MiB in 60 jobs: every deletion is a `DROP TABLE`, which rebuilds the catalogs), and deleting the results of all but the last 20 jobs from the one dataset (3272 MiB at 100 jobs, 300 jobs in 110 s); so the results are kept, as the emulator kept its per-job datasets, until it restarts. Collecting the emulator's heap sooner (`GOGC=10`) held the soak at 200–205 MiB, but the compat suite's `TestBigQueryCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction` then failed in each of four runs ("wasm trap: interface conversion: interface {} is nil", "index out of range", a SIGSEGV that killed the process), so it is not set. **Records across a restart, fixed (#1016):** next row but one. **Growth through DDL, loads and scripts, measured (#1057):** it is the catalog rebuild, whatever the statement. Read in the engine (googlesqlite v0.3.1, `internal/catalog.go`, `stmt_action.go`): it builds a catalog with every builtin function (`newSimpleCatalog`) for the root, one for each project, and four for each dataset that holds a table (the dataset under the root and under its project, and each one's `INFORMATION_SCHEMA`: `addTableSpecRecursiveImpl`, `aliasInfoSchemaTable`), and builds them all again at each `DeleteTableSpec`, `DeleteFunctionSpec` and `DeleteTVFSpec` (`resetCatalog`): a `DROP TABLE`, `DROP VIEW` or `DROP FUNCTION` of one that exists, `tables.delete`, each table of a `datasets.delete` with `deleteContents`, and the end of a script that made a TEMP table or function (`Cleanup`); a load, `CREATE TABLE`, the engine's `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` and a `DROP … IF EXISTS` of one that is not there rebuild nothing. So the cost of each rebuild grows with the datasets that hold tables. Measured with `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh --phase <p> --jobs 20 --datasets 10` (each phase from a new emulator, first 10 datasets of one table, about 240 MiB), the container after 20 operations: a `CREATE`/`DROP TABLE` pair 2,247 MiB (10 s); a dataset made and deleted with its table 2,249 MiB; a CSV load with a schema and its `tables.delete` 2,242 MiB; a failed script given to jobs.query 2,248 MiB; `CREATE`/`DROP FUNCTION` 2,197 MiB; `MERGE` from a subquery 2,262 MiB (11 s); a copy job with WRITE_TRUNCATE 4,357 MiB (18 s); `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE … AS SELECT` 4,349 MiB (19 s); `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` of one that exists 4,243 MiB (16 s); a script with a TEMP table 4,218 MiB; a load with autodetect of a FLOAT column (#1000) and its `tables.delete`: the engine failed at the 15th (4,473 MiB); 20 query jobs 902 MiB. **The front's share, cut:** the front's own scratch tables (the copy with WRITE_TRUNCATE, the FLOAT load, `MERGE` from a subquery, `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, and since #1091 a Parquet load the front carries out and a schema update that adds columns) and the scratch function of `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` are made in `_cloudburrow_query_results` (next row) rather than the client's dataset, and the front's deletion of each is answered at once and carried out later, with the expired results, while the instance is idle; an error naming one names the client's dataset. Measured the same way after: `MERGE` from a subquery 428 MiB (2 s); `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` 2,249 MiB (10 s); the copy 3,683 MiB (12 s); `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` 3,555 MiB (11 s); the FLOAT load failed at the 20th; a `CREATE`/`DROP TABLE` pair unchanged (2,245 MiB). The Parquet load and the schema update, measured the same way (each phase from an emulator just restarted, twice each), before and after: a Parquet load with `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` of a table that exists (`--phase parquet`) 2,270 MiB (11–19 s) and 447–449 MiB (3–4 s); `tables.patch` adding a column (`--phase schemaupdate`, which also deletes and makes the table again) 4,365 MiB (21–22 s) and 3,528–3,531 MiB (11–12 s). Units `TestScratchTablesInResultsDataset`, `TestScratchFunctionDroppedLater`, `TestUnscratch`; the copy, FLOAT, `MERGE`, `CREATE OR REPLACE` compat tests unchanged. The rest is the clients' own drops (a compat test's cleanup deletes its dataset table by table), which the front cannot leave out without changing their results; it takes an engine whose catalogs do not each hold every builtin (goccy/googlesqlite#80, not released). The BigQuery compat suite run four times on one emulator (`-run BigQuery`, 10-minute limit; each run's time, the container's memory after it and the emulator's restarts so far), in two series on the front before this and two after (two builds that differ only in the cap of the next row, which the suite does not reach): before, 204 s, 1545 MiB, 1; 321 s, 1332 MiB, 2; 381 s, 1480 MiB, 3; 294 s, 2451 MiB, 4; and 214 s, 2476 MiB, 1; 250 s, 2471 MiB, 3; 283 s, 2444 MiB, 5; 545 s, 1311 MiB, 7; after, 169 s, 2453 MiB, 1; 182 s, 2455 MiB, 3; 289 s, 2438 MiB, 5; 562 s, 1604 MiB, 7; and 226 s, 2456 MiB, 1; 271 s, 2447 MiB, 3; 332 s, 2454 MiB, 5; 445 s, 2437 MiB, 6: no change beyond the series' spread, the suite's drops being its tests' own. In two series (one before, one after) the fourth run failed most tests "connection refused": after seven restarts Kubernetes' back-off kept the emulator down for minutes (#1091, fixed above). **What is left:** the restart loses every dataset, table and job, as the crash did (Persistence row); a finalizer can still kill the emulator before any request sees the panic, which the supervisor restarts at once (#1091); a request in flight when the emulator ends is answered 502 (it was sent before the port closed); and the growth through DDL: the BigQuery compat suite is mostly DDL, loads and scripts, and its tests run one at a time from a fresh emulator took the container from 87 MiB to 2427 MiB by its 20th test (the most at `TestBigQueryFailedScriptLeavesTheCatalogInStep`, +840 MiB, and the `CREATE FUNCTION` and `DROP SCHEMA` tests), where it stayed for the other 44. The suite run four times in a row on one emulator (`-run BigQuery`, 10-minute limit; each run's time, the container's memory after it, the emulator's restarts so far, and a `jobs.list` of 200 jobs with `projection=full` after it): before #1017, 218 s, 2872 MiB, 0, 7.4 s; 239 s, 2694 MiB, 1, 5.9 s; 250 s, 2698 MiB, 2, 6.1 s; 270 s, 2721 MiB, 3, 5.8 s; after, 194 s, 2449 MiB, 0, 7.7 s; 606 s (the limit), 4425 MiB, 0, 22.3 s; 275 s, 2830 MiB, 1, 4.9 s; 202 s, 2919 MiB, 2, 5.3 s. Every restart came during `TestBigQueryCreateFunctionOfAnExistingFunction`'s table function, which then failed, before and after alike (after, the emulator's log names a SIGSEGV in the engine, exit code 2); that test passes on a fresh emulator. After a restart, `TestBigQueryDropSchema` failed once (409 for a function it was making), and passed alone. So a long-lived instance that runs much DDL still gets slow and fails; restart it (`cloudburrow stop`, `up`). Follow-ups: the crash #1058; the restart back-off #1091, fixed; the copy with WRITE_TRUNCATE #1092, closed, see *Copy jobs*. Soak: `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh`. Units `TestEngineGuard`, `TestEngineGuardWithoutWatch`, `TestEngineGuardPassesLargeErrors`, `TestBigQueryManifestPutsTheFrontOnTheServicePort`; compat `TestBigQueryEmulatorRestartsPromptly` (#1091), official Go client. |
| Console screen | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetsSchemaAndQuery` (#698): datasets, tables, schema, a preview and the query editor, read-only by default; `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetTableAndRowWrites` (#854): create and delete datasets and tables, with a schema, and insert rows, through the official Go client; `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory` (#993): loads from and exports to Cloud Storage, and Job history; `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadFromAFile` (#999): Load from a file uploaded from the browser; `TestConsoleBigQueryJobPageShowsDMLRows` (#1041): a query job's statement type and DML row counts on its page; `TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDDLAndScripts` (#994): the editor's opt-in Read-write mode, through `jobs.query`: `CREATE SCHEMA`, `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT`, `CREATE VIEW`, `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, a script with `DECLARE`, and `DROP TABLE`, each read back with the official Go client; `TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDML` (#1024): a DML statement of its own, answered with the rows it changed, DML inside a script and `UPDATE … FROM` refused (the DML row); `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions` (#994, #1025): flexible field names, a view from Create table, Edit table's description and labels (removing a label and clearing the description included), and Insert rows with `skipInvalidRows` and `ignoreUnknownValues`. See the Console table. |
| Query results: the destination table of a query job that names none | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryQueryResultsInOneHiddenDataset` (#1017), official Go client (`Query.Run`, `Job.Config`, `Job.Read`, `Table.Read`, `Client.Datasets` with and without `ListHidden`). BigQuery writes such a result to a temporary table in an anonymous, hidden dataset, which the job names ([docs](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/writing-results#temporary_and_permanent_tables)); the front has the emulator write it to a table named after the job in one hidden dataset, `_cloudburrow_query_results` (a name beginning with `_` is hidden, and `datasets.list` leaves it out unless `all=true`), which it makes when a project first needs it and again if it is deleted. The job's `configuration.query.destinationTable` names it, `jobs.getQueryResults` of every job and `tabledata.list` of its table read the rows back, and no dataset is named after a job. Only a query that is one `SELECT` (or `WITH`, or a parenthesised query) is given one; a DDL or DML statement, a script and a dry run are sent as they were (unit `TestQueryResultsInOneDataset`, `TestDatasetsListHidesResults`, `TestIsLoneQuery`). **Expired as BigQuery expires them (#1059):** BigQuery keeps such a table up to about 24 hours, best-effort, and "may be invalidated sooner" ([docs](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/cached-results)); the front deletes a result table once it is 24 hours old, or, oldest first, once there are more than 200, in batches of 20 while the instance is idle (no client request for 30 s; the liveness probe does not count), a batch stopping at the first request that comes in, and without waiting for idle time only past 1,000. A job whose table was deleted is answered 404 `notFound` "Not found: Table …" at `jobs.getQueryResults`, and by the emulator 404 `notFound` at `tabledata.list` of the table, as BigQuery answers a table that does not exist ([errors](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/error-messages)); `jobs.get` still names the destination; a later job's result still reads (`TestBigQueryExpiredQueryResultsAreNotFound`, official Go client: `Job.Read`, `Job.Config`, `Table.Read`, deleting one job's table through the front's `POST /cloudburrow/bigquery-expire-query-results?job=<id>`, which also takes `?keep=N`, for a test or to free the engine; units `TestResultsExpireAfterADay`, `TestResultsExpireOverTheCap`, `TestExpireResultsPath`, `TestLivenessProbeIsIdle`). Measured first: after `tables.delete` of a job's result table, the emulator answered `jobs.getQueryResults` of the job with its rows. The front forgets its tables when the emulator restarts (they are gone with it). Why a cap and idle time: every deletion is a `DROP TABLE`, which rebuilds the engine's catalogs (previous row), and costs more the more tables the engine has, not more memory: measured with `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh` on an emulator with only the soak's dataset, 50 deletions took 25 s with 300 result tables (container 398 → 789 MiB) and 195 s with 1,000 (622 → 627 MiB), a `CREATE TABLE`/`DROP TABLE` pair 0.8 s with 300 and 5.2 s with 1,000; deleting past a cap of 400 while busy made 600 query jobs (a pair every 5) take 454 s rather than 308 s with all kept; with this, 600 such jobs took 267 s, the idle front then deleted 400 in 481 s with the memory flat at 1,472 MiB, and a pair went from 2.7 s to 1.8 s. **Differs:** the dataset and table names are not BigQuery's random ones; a busy instance may hold up to 1,000. Measured first: the emulator made a dataset named after each job, which `datasets.list` listed. |
| The emulator's jobs: what each request reads, and how long a query's result rows are kept | **Verified** that each request's time no longer grows with the jobs the emulator keeps, and that `jobs.getQueryResults` of recent jobs reads their rows; **Differs** in how long a result is kept | CloudBurrow's build, bigquery-emulator patch 0002 (#1086). **Measured first**, on the pinned v0.8.1 and on #1061's build: every request, whatever it was, read the project with every job the emulator had run, each job's metadata and its whole result decoded from JSON, and each new job wrote the project's list of jobs again, so each request got slower with each job kept. On the emulator alone (an Apple silicon Mac), `SELECT 1` through `jobs.query` took 0.006 s fresh, 0.14 s after 20 queries of 50,000 rows and 0.27 s after 40, and `datasets.list` the same; 0.37 s after 1,000 queries of 10 rows and 1.43 s after 2,000. Through an instance, `scripts/bigquery-engine-soak.sh --phase rows --rows 50000 --max-probe 1` timed `SELECT 1` at 0.034 s, 0.43 s after 50 such queries and 1.05 s after 100, where it stopped, the container at 1,378 MiB. **Now** a project is read with no jobs, a job when a request names it (its metadata alone), its result only by `jobs.getQueryResults`, and the order `jobs.list` gives them is kept in a table of its own. The same soak: `SELECT 1` 0.034 s before and 0.067 s after 300 queries of 50,000 rows (`datasets.list` 0.023 s and 0.049 s), then 0.033 s and 0.038 s across 3,000 more of 10 rows (`datasets.list` 0.023 s throughout), the container at 925 MiB; on the emulator alone, 0.005 s after 40 queries of 50,000 rows, and 0.009 s after 3,000 of 10. `jobs.getQueryResults`, `jobs.get`, `jobs.list` and `jobs.delete` of recent jobs are the compat suite's (104 `BigQuery` tests run against this build through the official Go client: 92 passed, 10 skipped for a service the instance did not run; `TestBigQueryWriteTruncateDataReplacesTheRows` failed, as it does against #1061's build, and `TestBigQueryStorageReadInAvroOnAHyphenatedProject` needs a project with a hyphen, which that instance's had not). **Differs:** BigQuery keeps a query's result, its temporary table, "up to 24 hours" and best-effort ([docs](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/writing-results#temporary_and_permanent_tables)); the emulator keeps a job's result rows up to 24 hours and while the results it keeps come to at most 256 MiB of JSON, the oldest dropped first when the next job's result is kept, so a result may outlive 24 hours on an idle emulator until then. `jobs.getQueryResults` of a job whose result was dropped is 404 `notFound`, and the job itself is kept, for `jobs.get` and `jobs.list`, as long as the emulator runs; a query job whose result table the front keeps (row above) meets the front's rule first. Units, in the patch: `TestFindProjectReadsNoJobResults` (fails without it), `TestResultsExpire`, `TestJobsKeepTheirOrder`, `TestJobOrderMigrates`. |
| Jobs across a restart of the emulator | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryJobsAcrossAnEmulatorRestart` (#1016), official Go client: a load the front failed, the emulator's container restarted alone through the node's container runtime, then a load of the same job ID on the new emulator is done, not failed, in `jobs.get` and `jobs.list`, listed once. The front holds a connection open to the emulator's port and redials it (every 10 s, under the emulator's 15 s read timeout); the process ending closes it, and the front then drops the failures it gave, the client's texts of jobs it changed, configurations, times, its own jobs, the functions it saw made and the client's texts of views, once when the process ends and once when it is back (units `TestEmulatorWatch`, `TestEmulatorWatchNotBeforeFirstUp`, `TestFrontDropsJobsAtRestart`). Measured first (the unit, without the watch): the new job was answered with the old one's failure and times. Resumable upload sessions are kept: they hold the client's data, and their load is answered by the emptied emulator. |
| Dataset and table IDs, field names | **Verified** | CloudBurrow's front (`internal/bigqueryfront`, #861) checks BigQuery's documented rules before the emulator, which checks none (measured: it created `d-1` and `t!`). A dataset ID is letters, digits and underscores, at most 1,024 characters (`TestBigQueryRefusesInvalidDatasetID`: a hyphen is 400). A table ID is Unicode letters, marks, numbers, connectors, dashes and spaces, at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes (`TestBigQueryRefusesInvalidTableID`: `t!` is 400, `étudiant-01 x` is created). A field name follows BigQuery's **flexible column names**, at most 300 characters and none of the reserved prefixes (`_TABLE_`, `_PARTITION` and the rest): `TestBigQueryRefusesInvalidFieldName` has `a!` refused with 400, and **a space accepted**, as BigQuery now accepts it, and the column written and read back. Two fields at one level whose names differ only in case are a 400 (`TestBigQueryDuplicateColumnIs400`; the emulator's was a 500); an unknown type or mode, and a RECORD with no fields, are 400 too (unit `TestTableIDsAndSchemas`). The same schema checks apply to `tables.update` and `tables.patch`. |
| Duplicate dataset | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryDuplicateDatasetIs409`: a second dataset of one ID is 409 `duplicate`, "Already Exists". The emulator answered 500 "already created", which the Go client retried until its deadline; the front looks the dataset up first. A duplicate table is 409 from the emulator itself, as in BigQuery. Deleting a dataset that still has tables is a 400 `resourceInUse` without `deleteContents`, as in BigQuery (measured, #854). |
| Streamed rows checked against the schema | **Verified** | Before a row reaches the emulator, the front reads the table's schema and checks every value: a missing or null REQUIRED value (`TestBigQueryInsertRefusesMissingRequiredValue`), a field the table does not have, an array for a non-REPEATED field and the reverse, a NULL array element, a non-object RECORD, and a value that does not convert to its type (`TestBigQueryInsertRefusesWrongTypeValue`: `"x"` in NUMERIC, which the emulator stored as 0, and `"abc"` in INTEGER, which it answered with 500). INTEGER, FLOAT, NUMERIC (and its range), BIGNUMERIC, BOOLEAN, BYTES (base64), DATE, TIME, DATETIME, TIMESTAMP (the canonical formats, or epoch seconds), JSON text, and GEOGRAPHY as a string are checked (unit `TestInsertAllChecksEachValue`). **A table stays readable after a refused row**: `TestBigQueryTableReadableAfterRefusedRepeatedValue` sends `["q"]` for a REPEATED INTEGER, which the emulator had stored and then failed every read with "failed to scan rows"; now the row is an `insertErrors` entry and `Table.Read` returns the one valid row. Each failure is an `insertErrors` entry with reason `invalid` and the field as `location`; the messages are CloudBurrow's own, not BigQuery's text. **Not checked:** a GEOGRAPHY's geometry, INTERVAL and RANGE text, and fractional seconds past six digits (accepted, as BigQuery documents six). |
| `insertAll` `skipInvalidRows` | **Verified** | Without it, a batch with an invalid row inserts nothing and every row is reported, `invalid` or `stopped` (`TestBigQueryInsertAllWithoutSkipInvalidRowsInsertsNothing`); the emulator had inserted the good rows. With it, the valid rows are inserted and only the invalid ones reported, at their own index (`TestBigQueryInsertAllSkipInvalidRows`). |
| `insertAll` `ignoreUnknownValues` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryInsertAllIgnoreUnknownValues`: a field the table does not have makes the row invalid, and with the flag the field is dropped and the row inserted. |
| RECORD inside a REPEATED RECORD, and REPEATED RECORD inside a RECORD | **Verified**: created, streamed, loaded, written by DML and read back | `TestBigQueryNestedRecordsInRepeatedRecordsReadBack` (#881, #900), official Go client: both schemas are created through `tables.insert` as BigQuery creates them, and rows written by `tabledata.insertAll` (a value in the nested RECORD, and one with `null` or `[]` there), a DML `INSERT` and five one-row JSON load jobs, one after another, are read back with their values through `tabledata.list` and a `SELECT` after each load. Against the pinned v0.8.1 a streamed value there left every later read failing 500 "failed to scan rows: failed to convert struct from array" (measured, #874), and a load into a RECORD inside a REPEATED RECORD did so in 5 of 60 one-row loads ("failed to convert struct from string"), so the front answered both 501; the fault was the SQL engine's ([googlesqlite](https://github.com/goccy/googlesqlite)), which did not reshape a STRUCT under an ARRAY from the form the emulator binds it in. CloudBurrow's build carries its maintainer's open fix, [goccy/googlesqlite#76](https://github.com/goccy/googlesqlite/pull/76), unchanged (#1061), and the 501s are gone (units `TestInsertAllSendsNestedRecordsOn`, `TestJobsCheckTheTablesTheyMake`). RECORDs nested with none REPEATED, a REPEATED RECORD of scalars and REPEATED scalars in a RECORD are streamed, loaded and read back. The Storage Write API is not checked. |
| Tables made by jobs: DDL, load, copy, query destination | **Verified**, names only | `TestBigQueryJobsAreHeldToTheNamingRules` (#881), official Go client.
