The emulator ran `` CREATE TABLE ds.`t!` ``, a column `` `a!` `` and `` CREATE SCHEMA `bad-name` ``, and loaded rows into a table `t!` (measured); the front now answers each with 400 and nothing is made, while a table `ok table-1` with a column `first name` is created and a load into `loaded-1` writes its row. **`jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`:** a `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [TEMP] TABLE` or `CREATE SCHEMA` statement is read with a lexer, and its table ID, each column of its column list, each field of a `STRUCT` column type and the dataset ID of `CREATE SCHEMA` are held to the rules in the next rows, 400 `invalidQuery`; a load, copy or query job's `destinationTable` is held to the table ID rule and a load's `schema` to the schema rules, 400 `invalid`, including a load from a local file, whose multipart upload is checked by its first part only (unit `TestDDLNames`, `TestJobsCheckTheTablesTheyMake`, `TestLoadUploadIsCheckedByItsFirstPart`). A column named twice is refused by the emulator's own analyser, 400 (measured). `ALTER TABLE`, the columns of `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT`, the DDL inside a script's blocks and loads with `autodetect` are the next rows (#901). **Not checked:** a statement in a string (`EXECUTE IMMEDIATE`), and `CREATE VIEW`. The refusals are at insertion, as `tables.insert`'s are; BigQuery reports some query errors on the job instead, and the messages are CloudBurrow's own. |
| `ALTER TABLE` | **Not implemented** (501); a name BigQuery refuses is 400 | `TestBigQueryAlterTableNamesAndNotImplemented` (#901), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: `ADD COLUMN`, `DROP COLUMN`, `RENAME TO`, `SET OPTIONS` and `ALTER COLUMN … SET OPTIONS` each returned success, and the first four left the table as it was; `RENAME COLUMN` it refuses itself. So every `ALTER TABLE` is **501 `notImplemented`**, and nothing is sent: `ADD COLUMN` of `first name`, `RENAME TO renamed` and `SET OPTIONS (description …)` are 501, and the table's schema and description are unchanged and no `renamed` table exists. Before that, the names it gives are held to the rules above, **400 `invalidQuery`**: an `ADD COLUMN` named `` `b!` ``, a `STRUCT` field `` `q?` `` in one, `` RENAME TO `t!` `` and `` RENAME COLUMN a TO `c!` `` (unit `TestDDLInBlocksAndAlterTable`). A table's columns are added, a column relaxed to `NULLABLE` and descriptions and options changed through `tables.update` and `tables.patch` (`Table.Update`), which CloudBurrow carries out (#1010, the schema-update row), and the 501 says so. |
| `tables.patch`: labels, description, and schema changes BigQuery refuses | **Verified** for labels and the description; a schema that drops a column, changes a column's type or mode, or adds a `REQUIRED` column is **400**; adding columns is the *Schema updates* row (#1010) | `TestBigQueryTablePatchLabelsAndDescription`, `TestBigQueryTablePatchRefusesSchemaChanges` (#1009), official Go client (`Table.Update`, which sends `tables.patch`). Measured first against the pinned emulator: a label deleted with `DeleteLabel` (sent as `null`) read back as `""`, a patch of some labels dropped the others (it replaces the map), an empty description was ignored, and it took a schema that dropped or retyped columns and one that added a `REQUIRED` column. BigQuery's patch merges the labels, a `null` one removed ([Deleting a table label](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/deleting-labels#table)), clears a description given empty, and refuses those schemas, "Provided Schema does not match Table" ([Modifying table schemas](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-table-schemas)). The front carries out a patch that gives labels or a description as the emulator's `tables.update` of the whole table with the patch applied (labels merged, `null` ones removed, an empty description cleared; every label deleted leaves none), and checks a patch's or update's schema against the table's first: a column left out, retyped or changed in mode (other than `REQUIRED` to `NULLABLE`), or a `REQUIRED` column added (at any depth), is **400 `invalid`** and the table is unchanged. This and the *Schema updates* row are one path (#1054): the table is read once, the schema checked against it (the 400s first, then the 501s of that row), columns added, and then a patch's labels and description applied, so one `Table.Update` can add a column and change the labels. The console's Edit table sends `Table.Update` too (the row *`bigquery.tables.patch` (`Table.Update`) from the console's Edit table*). Units `TestPatchTableMergesLabels`, `TestSchemaChange`, `TestSchemaUpdates`. |
| Columns of `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryCreateTableAsSelectColumnsAreChecked` (#901), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. The emulator made `` CREATE TABLE ds.c AS SELECT 1 AS `x!`, STRUCT(2 AS `y?`) AS s `` with both names (measured). The front runs the statement's query alone first, as `SELECT * FROM (query) LIMIT 0` through `jobs.query` with the statement's default dataset and parameters, so the engine names the columns and no row is read or written; each result column and nested `STRUCT` field is held to the column rules, and a refused one is **400 `invalidQuery`** before the statement is sent: `` `x!` ``, `s.y?`, and `` `z/` `` through a `WITH`, and nothing is made. `` `first name` `` and a field `n-1` are made. A statement with a column list is checked by that list; `LIKE`, `COPY` and `CLONE` are 501 (a later row) (unit `TestCreateTableAsSelectColumnsAreChecked`, `TestDDLFindsTheQueryOfCreateTableAsSelect`). A query that cannot run alone is checked after its script (next row). A column with no alias the emulator's analyser refuses itself, 400 "CREATE TABLE columns must be named" (measured). |
| `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT` whose query cannot run alone | **Verified**, checked after the script | `TestBigQueryCreateTableAsSelectCheckedAfterTheScript` (#917), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. A query that names a script variable, or a table an earlier statement of the same script makes, fails when run alone; the emulator then made tables with columns `` `a!` `` (`DECLARE x …; CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT x AS `a!` ``) and `` `b!` `` (from a table the script's first statement made) (measured). The emulator has no sessions (a query with `createSession` got none back, measured), so the script's earlier statements cannot be run first; the front checks such a statement **after the script ran**, in the table it made: a column the rules refuse deletes that table and fails the query, **400 `invalidQuery`** through `jobs.query`, and through `jobs.insert` a job `DONE` with an `invalidQuery` `errorResult`, which `Job.Wait`, `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` report. The error names the statements after it in the script, which were run and **are not undone** (BigQuery would not have run them); the earlier statement's table is kept, as BigQuery keeps it. Names BigQuery takes are made (a variable's value read back). A `TEMP` table, gone when the script ends, is checked before the script instead (#938, row below). The emulator kept a script's variables after it, so a query run alone could read one of an earlier script (measured); the front now gives them names of their own (#933). Unit `TestCreateTableAsSelectCheckedAfterTheScript`. |
| Views: `CREATE [OR REPLACE] VIEW`, and `tables.insert` with a view | **Verified**; **Not implemented** (501) for a column list and for materialized views | `TestBigQueryViewNamesAndColumns` (#916), official Go client, through `jobs.query`, `jobs.insert` and `Table.Create` with a `ViewQuery`. Measured against the pinned emulator: it made `` CREATE VIEW ds.`v!` `` and a view whose query gave a column `` `x!` `` or a `STRUCT` field `` `y?` ``, and `tables.insert` made a view with a column `` `w!` ``. The view ID is held to the table ID rule, and the columns its query gives, read by running the query alone as for `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT`, to the column rules: **400 `invalidQuery`** (`invalid` through `tables.insert`), and nothing is made. A view with names BigQuery takes is made and read. `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` of an existing view failed 400 "table is already created" and left the view unreadable ("no such column", measured); the front deletes the view and sends the statement, once its query has run alone, so it **replaces** the view as BigQuery does (the new column reads back). A view's column list (`CREATE VIEW v (c, d) AS …`) is **501**: the emulator answered 400 "CREATE VIEW with explicit column list is not supported". `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW` and `DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW` are **501**: 400 "Statement not supported" (measured). A legacy SQL view through `tables.insert` is not read: the emulator parses every view as GoogleSQL (measured). `CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS` of an existing view does nothing, as in BigQuery (#932, a later row). No upstream issue found. Unit `TestViewsAreChecked`. **The view's query reads back as written** (#1014, `TestBigQueryCreateViewReadsBackAsWritten`, official Go client, `CREATE VIEW` through `jobs.query` and `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` through `jobs.insert`, then `Table.Metadata().ViewQuery`): measured first, `tables.get` of a view made by `CREATE VIEW` gave the emulator's rewritten SQL, which names its internal table (`` `project_dataset_table` ``) and functions (`googlesqlite_greater`). The front keeps the client's text of each view a query makes, with the emulator's, and `tables.get` answers with the client's while the emulator's is unchanged (a view replaced or dropped since, by any means, reads as the emulator has it); `CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS` of an existing view keeps the old text. The texts are in the front's memory (the last 1,000): after the front restarts, such a view reads back rewritten again. `tables.list` gives no view's query, as in BigQuery. Unit `TestCreateViewReadsBackAsWritten`. |
| A view's query read back (`tables.get` `view.query`) | **Verified** for a view made with `tables.insert` and for one made with `CREATE VIEW` while the front has its text | `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions` (#994): a view made through the console's Create table (`Table.Create` with a `ViewQuery`) reads back its query as written. A view made with `CREATE VIEW` or `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` reads back as written through the front (#1014, `TestBigQueryCreateViewReadsBackAsWritten`, the Views row); the emulator alone gives its rewritten SQL. The front keeps the client's texts in `viewtexts.json` on its state directory, beside `jobtexts.json`, so they outlive a restart of the front's container (#1028, unit `TestViewTextsOutliveAFrontRestart`; not yet measured across a real restart), and the emulator's restart empties them. The view's rows are right (`TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDDLAndScripts` reads them). |
| `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` of an existing table | **Verified** for a statement on its own; **Not implemented** (501) inside a script of several statements | `TestBigQueryExceptionHandlersRaiseAndCreateOrReplace` (#918), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. The emulator failed it with 400 "duplicate: table … is already created" and kept the old table (measured); on a table that does not exist it creates it. The front carries it out: it runs the statement into a scratch table first (in `_cloudburrow_query_results`, deleted later, since #1057), and **if that fails the answer is its error and the table is kept** (a replacement from a missing table left the table as it was); then it deletes the table, sends the statement with its query replaced by `SELECT * FROM` the scratch table, and deletes the scratch table. So a query that reads the table itself works (`CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE t AS SELECT a + 10 AS a FROM t` read back 21 and 22, twice), a column list replaces the schema, and no other table is left in the dataset. The job the emulator records shows the rewritten query; `jobs.insert`'s answer and `jobs.get` show the client's (#939). Inside a script of several statements the front cannot do this between them, so an existing table is **501**. `CREATE OR REPLACE TEMP TABLE` of a temp table the script made fails in the emulator, and is 501 (#936). The table is deleted even when the new schema is the old one (#1092, closed): an unchanged-schema `DELETE` and `INSERT` would spare the engine's catalog rebuild, but BigQuery's documentation does not say whether the replaced table keeps its creation time, description, labels or expiration when the statement's `OPTIONS` do not set them: `OR REPLACE` "Replaces any table with the same name if it exists", and the example says a table that exists "is overwritten with an empty table" ([CREATE TABLE](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#create_table_statement)); a replaced table's row access policies and policy tags are dropped ([row-level-security-intro](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/row-level-security-intro), [column-level-security-intro](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/column-level-security-intro)), where `TRUNCATE TABLE` keeps them, so a replacement is not a truncation; and the previous contents stay readable through time travel ([time-travel](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/time-travel)). Unit `TestCreateOrReplaceExistingTable`. |
| `CREATE TABLE` and `CREATE VIEW` … `IF NOT EXISTS` of one that exists | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryCreateIfNotExistsOfAnExistingOne` (#932), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. BigQuery: "If any table exists with the same name, the `CREATE` statement has no effect", and the same for a view ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language)). The emulator failed every form on an existing table or view (measured): 400 "SQL logic error: table … already exists" for `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS … AS SELECT`, and 400 "table is already created" for one with a column list, for `CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS`, and for either on a name the other kind holds; a script with one failed as a whole. The front looks the table up and, when it exists, or an earlier statement of the same script makes it, sends the statement as `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` of a table that is not there, in the same dataset, which the emulator runs as a statement with no rows that changes nothing (measured). So the statement succeeds and the table or view keeps its schema and rows (`t` and `v` still read `[[1]]`), a script with one runs its later statements, and one that does not exist is created. The job the emulator records shows that `DROP` as its query, as for `CREATE OR REPLACE` (#939). After a `DROP` of the same table or its dataset earlier in the script the statement is sent as it is: the emulator creates the table if the `DROP` removed it, and fails the script if not. **Not covered:** a `TEMP` table, which cannot be looked up from outside its script. `CREATE SCHEMA` of an existing dataset is the next row (#946). Unit `TestCreateIfNotExistsOfAnExistingOneDoesNothing`. |
| `CREATE SCHEMA` of a dataset that exists | **Verified** when it is a query's first statement; **Not implemented** (501) later in a script | `TestBigQueryCreateSchemaOfAnExistingDataset` (#946), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. BigQuery fails it "Already Exists: Dataset *project*:*name*", and `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS` of one "has no effect" ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#create_schema_statement)). Measured first: the emulator ran `CREATE SCHEMA` of an existing dataset as done, through both, and kept the dataset's tables, `OPTIONS(description=…)` included. The front looks the dataset up and, when it exists, answers `jobs.query` **409 `duplicate`** and fails a query job with that `errorResult`, which `jobs.get` reports too (the emulator records the job with a statement that does nothing in its place); nothing is run, and the dataset's table is still there. `IF NOT EXISTS` succeeds through both. A later statement of a script that creates a dataset that exists, before the script or by an earlier statement, is **501**: BigQuery runs the statements before it and fails there, which the emulator cannot be stopped to do. Unit `TestCreateSchemaOfAnExistingDataset`. `CREATE SCHEMA` after a `DROP SCHEMA` of the same dataset in a script is **501** (#951, below). |
| `CREATE SCHEMA` of a dataset that does not exist | **Verified** with `description`, `friendly_name`, `labels` and `location`, alone and in a script; **Not implemented** (501) for other options, `DEFAULT COLLATE`, and a script whose other statements could see the dataset before the statement runs | `TestBigQueryCreateSchemaMakesTheDataset` (#951), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. BigQuery: "Creates a new dataset" ([DDL](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-definition-language#create_schema_statement)). Measured first: the emulator ran it as done, through both, and made **no dataset**: `datasets.get` of it answered 404 and a `CREATE TABLE` in it failed "dataset … is not found"; its `datasets.insert` does make one and keeps a `description`, `friendlyName`, `labels` and `location`. The front makes the dataset through `datasets.insert`, with those options (with no `location`, in the query's location), when it sends the query on, and then sends the query unchanged, which the emulator runs as done; a dataset made with `OPTIONS(description=…, friendly_name=…, labels=[…], location='EU')` reads each back, and `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS` of a new one makes it. In a script, `CREATE SCHEMA d; CREATE TABLE d.t …; INSERT …` runs and the table holds the row, and so does one that starts `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS d CASCADE` of a dataset that is not there (sent as a statement that does nothing); a script that fails leaves no dataset (the front deletes it, as the emulator keeps nothing of a failed script). **501**, with nothing run: any other option (`default_table_expiration_days`, `is_case_insensitive` and the rest change how the dataset's tables behave, which the emulator does not do), `DEFAULT COLLATE`, an option value other than a string literal (or an array of string pairs for `labels`), and in a script a `CREATE SCHEMA` after a statement that names the dataset, after a `DROP SCHEMA` that may remove it, after a `RETURN`, or in an `EXCEPTION` handler: the front makes the dataset before the script runs, and those could tell. Units `TestCreateSchemaMakesTheDataset`, `TestCreateSchemaNotCarriedOut`, `TestCreateSchemaUndoneWhenTheQueryFails`. |
| `CREATE TABLE … LIKE`, `COPY` and `CLONE`, and snapshot tables | **Not implemented** (501) | `TestBigQueryCreateTableLikeCopyCloneNotImplemented` (#937), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`. Measured against the pinned emulator: `CREATE TABLE … LIKE`, `… COPY` and `… CLONE`, with or without `OR REPLACE`, `IF NOT EXISTS` or an `AS` query after `LIKE`, each failed 400 "CREATE TABLE LIKE is not supported" (`COPY`, `CLONE`), and `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE` and `DROP SNAPSHOT TABLE` 400 "Statement not supported". Upstream: [goccy/googlesqlite#75](https://github.com/goccy/googlesqlite/issues/75) (open). Each is **501 `notImplemented`**, naming the statement ("Not implemented here: CREATE TABLE CLONE."), before anything is sent, and no table is made; a table ID BigQuery refuses is still 400 first. `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT * FROM` the source copies its columns and rows, not its options. `DROP SNAPSHOT TABLE` is unit-tested only. Unit `TestCreateTableLikeCopyCloneAreNotImplemented`. |
| `BEGIN … EXCEPTION` handlers, and `RAISE` | **Not implemented** (501) when the block fails, and for `RAISE` | `TestBigQueryExceptionHandlersRaiseAndCreateOrReplace` (#918), official Go client. Measured against the pinned emulator: `BEGIN SELECT * FROM nope.nope; EXCEPTION WHEN ERROR THEN CREATE TABLE …; END` returned the block's error, 400 "Table not found", and did not run the handler, where BigQuery runs it; and `RAISE USING MESSAGE = 'boom'` returned success, where BigQuery ends the script with that error. A script with a handler that fails is **501 `notImplemented`**, naming the emulator's error, through `jobs.query`, and through `jobs.insert` a job failed so, which `Job.Wait` (`jobs.getQueryResults`), `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` report; the handler's table is not made. Through `jobs.query` the emulator rolls a failed script back whole; as a query job it keeps what the statements before the failing one did (measured, #935, #955). A script whose block succeeds runs and returns its rows. `RAISE` is **501** before it is sent. No upstream issue found. Unit `TestFailedScriptWithAHandlerIs501`. |
| DDL in a script's blocks, and control-flow blocks | **Not implemented** (501) for `IF`, `LOOP`, `WHILE`, `REPEAT`, `FOR` and a script `CASE`; the DDL inside is checked | `TestBigQueryScriptBlocks` (#901), official Go client. Measured against the pinned emulator: the statements inside `IF` (either branch), `LOOP`, `WHILE`, `REPEAT`, `FOR` and a script `CASE` were not run, and the script returned success: `IF TRUE THEN INSERT …` inserted nothing, `IF TRUE THEN CREATE TABLE …` made nothing. So a script with such a block is **501 `notImplemented`** and nothing is sent: `IF TRUE THEN CREATE TABLE ds.ifok …` is 501 and `ifok` is not made. Every statement of a script is read, inside those blocks and a `BEGIN … EXCEPTION` handler too, whether or not its branch would run, and a name BigQuery refuses is **400** first: `` CREATE TABLE ds.`if!` `` in an `IF`, `` `loop!` `` in a `LOOP`, `` `handler!` `` in an `EXCEPTION` handler, and nothing is made (unit `TestDDLInBlocksAndAlterTable`, labels and a `CASE` expression inside an `IF` condition included). A `BEGIN … END` block, which the emulator runs, is sent: its table is made. **An `EXCEPTION` handler is never run**: when the block fails, the emulator returns the error instead (measured). |
| Loads with `autodetect` and no schema | **Verified** for CSV; **Not implemented** (501) for `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` into a new table, and for `columnNameCharacterMap` `V1` or `V2` when a detected name needs renaming | `TestBigQueryAutodetectLoadNames` (#901), official Go client, a load from a local file. The emulator names the columns of a CSV from its first row whatever they hold: a header `a b!,c` made a column `a b!` (measured). BigQuery's rule is the load's `columnNameCharacterMap`: `STRICT`, the default, "reject[s] invalid column names"; `V1` and `V2` normalize them ([JobConfigurationLoad](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationLoad)). The front does not read the data, so it checks the table the load made, after the emulator ran it: under `STRICT` a name the column rules refuse **fails the job as BigQuery fails it**: the job is `DONE` with an `invalid` `errorResult`, which `Job.Wait`'s status and a later `jobs.get` (`Job.Status`) report, and the table is deleted, as BigQuery creates a load's table only when it succeeds. Under `V1` (the classic rule) or `V2`, whose renaming the emulator ignores (measured: `a!` kept under `V2`), such a load is **501** and the table deleted. A header BigQuery takes, `first name,n`, loads its row. A `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` load with `autodetect` into a new table is **501** before it is sent: the emulator answered it 500 "nil pointer dereference" (measured), which the Go client retries until its deadline. A load into an existing table keeps that table's schema (measured) and is not checked; with no `skipLeadingRows` it is **501** (#945, the CSV loads row below) (unit `TestAutodetectedNamesAreHeldToTheCharacterMap`). The header, a resumable upload, a load from Cloud Storage and `jobs.list` are in the next rows (#919). |
| CSV header detection with `autodetect` | **Verified** where BigQuery and the emulator agree; **Not implemented** (501) where they do not | `TestBigQueryAutodetectHeaderAndResumableLoads` (#919), official Go client. The emulator takes a CSV's first row as the header whatever it holds (measured: `a,b / c,d` gave columns `a` and `b`, `1,2 / 3,4` columns `1` and `2`) and ignores `skipLeadingRows`. BigQuery takes the first row as the header only "if the first line contains only strings, and the other lines contain other data types" ([schema detection](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/schema-detect#csv_header)); otherwise it loads it as data. The front reads both halves of that rule from the table the load made (the first row is the column names; the other rows' types are the columns'), and a load BigQuery would read otherwise is **501**, the table deleted: every column `STRING`, or a name that reads as a number, boolean, date or timestamp. `first name,n / bob,3` loads its row. `skipLeadingRows` other than 1 with `autodetect` into a new table is **501** before it is sent (2 checked; 0, which the Go client cannot send, unit only). Unit `TestCSVHeaderDetection`. |
| Loads with no `sourceFormat` | **Verified** as CSV | Every load in `TestBigQueryAutodetectHeaderAndResumableLoads` sets none, as the Go client sends none for CSV unless told. BigQuery's default is CSV; the emulator has none (measured: 400 "not support sourceFormat: " with a schema, and 500 "nil pointer dereference" with `autodetect`, which the Go client retries until its deadline). The front sets `sourceFormat` `CSV` in the job, in `jobs.insert`'s body or a multipart upload's first part, whose data is sent as it came (unit `TestLoadWithoutSourceFormatIsCSV`). |
| CSV loads with a schema, or into an existing table | **Verified** for a load from the client (multipart and resumable uploads) and from Cloud Storage (#944) | `TestBigQueryCSVLoadWithSchemaLoadsEveryRow` (#931) and `TestBigQueryCSVLoadFromCloudStorage` (#944), official Go client. BigQuery reads every row as data unless told otherwise: `skipLeadingRows` "The default value is 0" ([JobConfigurationLoad](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationLoad)), and each value goes to the column at its position. The emulator ignores `skipLeadingRows` and always takes the first row as a header (measured, and in its source): a file of three rows with a schema and no header **loaded two, the first lost with no error**, and a file of one row loaded none; `skipLeadingRows` 2 failed the load; and a first row whose values are the columns' names maps the columns by name. The front drops the rows BigQuery skips and puts a header of its own before the rest, the columns' names in order (the load's schema, or the table's when the load gives none), which the emulator takes as the header; the data is streamed, not held in memory. So three rows load three, one loads one, `skipLeadingRows` 1 skips a header `b,a` and still loads by position, `skipLeadingRows` 2 skips a title and a quoted value over two lines, a load into an existing table with no load schema loads every row, and a resumable upload of over 768 KiB loads every row, its first included. **From Cloud Storage (#944):** the emulator reads a `gs://` load's objects itself, so the front reads them instead, from the instance's own Cloud Storage (the in-cluster server the front is started with, `--storage`, never `storage.googleapis.com`), and sends them to the emulator as a multipart upload of the same job without its `sourceUris`, through the same path: each file's leading rows are skipped, the files are loaded as one job in the order of their URIs, and a URI with one `*` wildcard reads every object it matches, in name order, as the emulator's own reader does. So a file of three rows and no header loads three, `skipLeadingRows` 1 skips a header `b,a` and loads by position, `parts/*.csv` with a header in each loads every data row of the two `.csv` objects and not the `.txt`, and two URIs into an existing table load six rows. An object that does not exist, a bucket that does not, or a wildcard that matches nothing is **404 `notFound`** from `jobs.insert` and makes no table (BigQuery fails the job "Not found: URI" instead); so is Cloud Storage that does not answer. With `autodetect` and columns given, `skipLeadingRows` says how many rows are not data (BigQuery: "0 - … no headers", "N > 0 - Autodetect skips N-1 rows and tries to detect headers in row N"); unset, BigQuery decides from the data whether the first row is a header, which the emulator always drops (measured), so that is **501** (the Go client cannot send 0). A load with `autodetect` and no columns, into a new table, keeps its header for the emulator, and a later file's first row is dropped. Unit `TestCSVLoadWithColumnsLoadsEveryRow`, `TestCSVLoadFromCloudStorage`, `TestCSVLoadFromCloudStorageIsRead`, `TestSkipRecords`. |
| CSV loads: `fieldDelimiter`, `quote`, `allowJaggedRows`, `nullMarker` | **Verified** for a one-character ASCII delimiter or quote (or none); **Not implemented** (501) otherwise, and for an empty value with a `nullMarker` in a `STRING` column | `TestBigQueryCSVLoadOptions` (#945), official Go client (`ReaderSource.FieldDelimiter`, `Quote`/`ForceZeroQuote`, `AllowJaggedRows`, `NullMarker`), from the client and from Cloud Storage. Measured first: the emulator reads a load's data with Go's `encoding/csv` defaults: a tab- or pipe-delimited file, a file quoted with `'` and a short row with `allowJaggedRows` failed 400 "wrong number of fields"; with `quote` `""` it loaded `"x"` as `x`; with `nullMarker` `\N` it loaded the text `\N`; it loads every empty value as NULL. The front reads the data by the load's options as it passes and writes it on as the CSV the emulator reads: a tab (`\t` too) or pipe delimiter, `'` quotes with `''` inside, no quoting, short rows filled with NULLs, and `\N` loaded as NULL all read back as BigQuery documents ([JobConfigurationLoad](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationLoad)); a short row without `allowJaggedRows` still fails. With a `nullMarker`, BigQuery loads an empty value into a `STRING` or `BYTES` column as an empty string, which the emulator cannot load (NULL): **501**, and into another column refuses it: **400 `invalid`**; a quote that is not closed, or data after a closing quote, is 400 `invalid`. These fail the load while its data passes, before the emulator loads anything, and `jobs.get` reports the job failed (unit `TestCSVDialectFailureReadsBackFailed`). A delimiter or quote of more than one character, or outside ASCII, is **501** before anything is sent. **Not covered:** whether BigQuery reads a quoted `"\N"` as the marker (the front does not). The other CSV options are in the next row (#952). Unit `TestCSVDialect`. |
| CSV loads: `allowQuotedNewlines`, `encoding`, `maxBadRecords`, `ignoreUnknownValues`, `nullMarkers`, `preserveAsciiControlCharacters`, `sourceColumnMatch`, REQUIRED columns; `timeZone` and the date and time formats | **Verified** for each but `encoding` other than UTF-8 and ISO-8859-1, `sourceColumnMatch` `NAME` with a `skipLeadingRows` other than 1, `timeZone`, `dateFormat`, `datetimeFormat`, `timeFormat` and `timestampFormat`, which are **Not implemented** (501) | `TestBigQueryCSVLoadOtherOptions` (#952), official Go client (`ReaderSource.AllowQuotedNewlines`, `Encoding`, `MaxBadRecords`, `IgnoreUnknownValues`, `NullMarkers`, `PreserveASCIIControlCharacters`, `SourceColumnMatch`, `TimeZone`, `DateFormat`, a `Required` field). Measured first, with a header row the emulator takes as one: it loaded a quoted newline whatever `allowQuotedNewlines` said; under `encoding` ISO-8859-1 it stored the byte 0xE9 as U+FFFD; with `maxBadRecords` 1 it failed a load with one bad value (400 "strconv.ParseInt") or one row too wide (400 "wrong number of fields"), and failed the wide row the same way with `ignoreUnknownValues`; it loaded the values of `nullMarkers` as text; it stored `\x00` and `\x01` as they were whatever `preserveAsciiControlCharacters` said; it stored a TIMESTAMP with no zone as UTC whatever `timeZone` said, and failed a DATE in `dateFormat` `MM/DD/YYYY`; it stored NULL in a REQUIRED column given an empty value; it matched a header's names to the columns by name. The front reads each record by BigQuery's rules as the data passes ([JobConfigurationLoad](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationLoad)): a quoted newline loads with `allowQuotedNewlines` and is **400 `invalid`** without it; ISO-8859-1 data reads back as `café`; a record that is too short (without `allowJaggedRows`), too wide (without `ignoreUnknownValues`, which drops the trailing values) or leaves a REQUIRED column without a value is bad, up to `maxBadRecords` of them are left out and one more is **400 `invalid`**; each value of `nullMarkers` loads as NULL; a control character loads with `preserveAsciiControlCharacters`; with `sourceColumnMatch` `NAME` and `skipLeadingRows` 1, each value goes to the column its header names. **501**, and nothing is loaded: a load with `maxBadRecords` the emulator then fails (on a value its column's type refuses, which BigQuery may count as a bad record); a quoted newline without `allowQuotedNewlines` in a row `skipLeadingRows` skips, or with `maxBadRecords`; a control character other than tab, newline and carriage return without `preserveAsciiControlCharacters`; an empty value that is not one of the `nullMarkers`; an empty quoted value in a REQUIRED `STRING` or `BYTES` column; a `NAME` header that does not name each column once (the reference documents none of these cases). `nullMarker` with `nullMarkers` is **400 `invalid`**. The job reports the records it left out (next row, #960). **Not covered:** a load from Cloud Storage with no Cloud Storage for the front to read passes these checks by (#944). Units `TestCSVLoadOptions`, `TestCSVLoadOptionsRefused`. |
| CSV loads: the job's `statistics.load` (`badRecords`, `outputRows`, `inputFiles`, `inputFileBytes`) and the bad records left out in `status.errors`, on `jobs.insert`, `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` | **Verified** for a load whose records the front reads; `outputBytes` is **Not covered** | `TestBigQueryCSVLoadReportsBadRecords` (#960), official Go client (`JobStatus.Errors`, `JobStatus.Statistics.Details.(*bigquery.LoadStatistics)` `OutputRows`, `InputFiles`, `InputFileBytes`, from `Loader.Run`, `Job.Wait` and `Client.Jobs`), and, as that client's `LoadStatistics` has no `BadRecords`, the official generated client it is built on, `google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2` (`JobStatistics3.BadRecords` from `Jobs.Get` and `Jobs.List`). Measured first, on the front of #952: a load with `maxBadRecords` 2 left two bad records out and read back from `jobs.get` with no statistics and no errors (the emulator's upload job is the job as sent, [JobStatistics3](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobStatistics3)). The front counts a load's data as it reads it: the files and their bytes, the rows written on, the bad records left out; the first 100 bad records (CloudBurrow's bound; BigQuery lists "the first errors") are each an `invalid` error in `status.errors`, located at the file's `gs://` URI for a load from Cloud Storage, and the job does not fail. A load with `ignoreUnknownValues` trims its wide records and reports `badRecords` 0. `outputBytes` is not reported (#965, next row). A load whose records the front does not read is counted around it (#966, the row after). Units `TestCSVLoadReportsBadRecords`, `TestCSVLoadReportsNoBadRecords`, `TestCSVLoadBadRecordsListed`, `TestCSVLoadFromCloudStorageReportsBadRecords`, `TestCSVLoadReportForgotten`. |
| A load's `statistics.load.outputBytes` | **Not covered**: left out | BigQuery documents it only as "Output only. Size of the loaded data in bytes. Note that while a load job is in the running state, this value may change." ([JobStatistics3](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobStatistics3); the same words in the official generated Go client, `google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2` `JobStatistics3.OutputBytes`). That is not the size of the data sent, which is `inputFileBytes`, and neither the reference nor any public source found says how the size is taken (whether it is the table's `numBytes` growth, or the logical sizes of [data size calculation](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/pricing#data_size_calculation), which are documented for query pricing, not for loads). The emulator keeps no stored size to read: its `tables.get` reports `numBytes` 0 after a load (measured, with the official Go client's `TableMetadata.NumBytes`). So the front does not report it, and the official Go client reads `LoadStatistics.OutputBytes` as 0 (`TestBigQueryLoadsReportCounts` checks it is 0, #965; unit `TestCSVLoadReportsBadRecords` that it is left out). |
| Loads whose records the front does not read: the job's `statistics.load` (`outputRows`, `inputFiles`, `inputFileBytes`) and status, on `jobs.insert`, `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` | **Verified** for a `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` or Parquet upload (new table, appended, `WRITE_TRUNCATE`), a CSV upload the front passes on as it is, and a `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` load from Cloud Storage (two URIs, and a wildcard); **Not covered**: `badRecords` for these, and `inputFiles` and `inputFileBytes` of a load from Cloud Storage with no Cloud Storage given to the front | `TestBigQueryLoadsReportCounts` (#966), official Go client (`JobStatus.State`, `JobStatus.Statistics.Details.(*bigquery.LoadStatistics)` `OutputRows`, `InputFiles`, `InputFileBytes`, from `Loader.Run`, `Job.Wait` and `Client.Jobs`), and the official generated client, `google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2` (`Jobs.Get`). Measured first, on the front of #960: each of these loads read back from `jobs.get` with `DONE` and no `statistics.load`, and from `jobs.insert` and `jobs.list` with no status at all (for an upload) or with no `statistics.load` (from Cloud Storage), so the Go client's `JobStatus.Statistics` was nil or had no load details; an `AVRO` or `ORC` load is 400 "not support sourceFormat" from the emulator, which loads no other format, so it has nothing to report. The front counts such a load around it: **`outputRows`** is the destination table's rows after the load (`tables.get`'s `numRows`, which the emulator counts in the table) less those before it, or all of them after a `WRITE_TRUNCATE`, which the emulator carries out in the same transaction as the load; **`inputFiles`** and **`inputFileBytes`** are the upload's, one file and the bytes of its data as they pass through the front unchanged, or for a load from Cloud Storage the objects the emulator reads (a URI with no wildcard, or every object listed under a wildcard's prefix whose name ends with the text after it, as its source, `importFromGCS`, matches them) and their sizes in the instance's Cloud Storage; a CSV load from Cloud Storage the front reads (#944), and since #1079 every JSON load from Cloud Storage (row "A load of several objects from Cloud Storage"), has the files and bytes it read. The job's status is `DONE`, as the load has run when `jobs.insert` answers. `badRecords` is left out (the Go client reads 0): the emulator fails a load on its first bad record (measured, #952), so a load that succeeded left none out, but the front does not read the records to say what BigQuery would count. **Not covered:** a write to the same table by another request while the load runs is counted in its `outputRows`; a load into another project's table has no `outputRows`; a load from Cloud Storage with no Cloud Storage given to the front (`--storage`) has no `inputFiles` or `inputFileBytes`. A Parquet load with no schema in the job is 500 from the emulator (measured; #970), and a load job's times are not BigQuery's (#971). A failed load reports no counts. Units `TestJSONLoadReportsCounts`, `TestCloudStorageLoadReportsCounts`, `TestCSVLoadReportForgotten`. |
| Parquet loads: the file's columns and types against the table's, nested and repeated columns, converted types and schema changes | **Verified** for flat, nested and repeated columns of the types listed, into a new table, appended to one that exists (by name, in any case), with `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` and `ALLOW_FIELD_RELAXATION`, and replacing one (`WRITE_TRUNCATE`, `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA`), from an upload and from Cloud Storage; **400** where BigQuery refuses the file's columns; **Not implemented** (501) for the rest listed | `TestBigQueryParquetLoadWithoutSchema` (#970), `TestBigQueryParquetLoadColumns` and `TestBigQueryParquetLoadTypes` (#988), `TestBigQueryParquetConvertedTypes` (#1005), `TestBigQueryParquetNestedColumns` (#1004), `TestBigQueryParquetSchemaChanges` (#1006), official Go client (`ReaderSource` and `GCSReference` with `SourceFormat` Parquet, `ParquetOptions`, `DecimalTargetTypes`, `SchemaUpdateOptions`, `WriteDisposition`) and, for `parquetOptions.mapTargetType`, which that client lacks, the generated one; files written by Apache Arrow's Parquet writer (pyarrow 25.0.1, `internal/bigqueryfront/testdata/parquet/gen.py`; the ENUM files by Arrow's Go writer, `genenum.go`, as pyarrow writes no ENUM). BigQuery reads a Parquet file's schema from the file ("the table schema is automatically retrieved from the self-describing source data", [Parquet loads](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/loading-data-cloud-storage-parquet)). Measured first: the emulator reads a Parquet file's columns only from the job's schema and each row's values by those names, checking nothing (its source: `uploadContentHandler`, `load.Schema.Fields`): with no schema a load was 500 "nil pointer dereference"; given the table's, into `(a INT64)` a file `(a INT64, b STRING)` dropped `b`, into `(z STRING)` it loaded three rows of NULL, into `(a STRING, b STRING)` it loaded `a` as text, and a file's `A` and `B` loaded NULL into `a` and `b`; it loaded TIME(MILLIS) 01:02:03.004 as 00:12:06.004, TIME(MICROS) 01:02:03.004567 as 01:02:07.567, TIMESTAMP(MILLIS) in 2024 as January 1970, BYTES 0xFF as EF BF BD, and refused INT96 ("failed to convert … to time.Time"); against the image directly, given BigQuery's schema for each shape, a LIST read without list inference failed "failed to convert struct from string", with list inference a NULL list read back NULL and a NULL element was kept, and a MAP left the emulator hung, answering nothing until it was restarted. Its JSON load is no way round it: BYTES given as base64 loaded the base64 text (`/w==` read back `2f773d3d`). So the front reads the file's schema from its footer (`parquetfooter.go`) before anything is sent, and holds it to BigQuery's rules. A load the emulator carries out as BigQuery does (flat columns of BOOLEAN, INT32 with no annotation, INT(8, 16, 32, signed or not), DATE, INT64 with no annotation, TIMESTAMP(MICROS) and BYTE_ARRAY STRING, into a new table, appended by exact names, or replacing a table of the file's schema) is sent to it with the schema to read the file by. Any other is **the front's own job** (`parquetload.go`, as a copy job is): it reads the rows with Apache Arrow's Parquet reader for Go (`github.com/apache/arrow/go/v15/parquet/file`, already in the module graph through the BigQuery client; tested with uncompressed, SNAPPY and ZSTD files, dictionary pages, version 2 data pages and several row groups), puts nested rows back together from their definition and repetition levels, writes each as a GoogleSQL literal of the table's row type, and inserts them with DML into a scratch table and then the table (measured: the emulator stores DML values of every type here exactly, RECORD inside REPEATED RECORD included); the job reports `inputFiles`, `inputFileBytes`, `outputRows` and `badRecords` 0, and `jobs.get` reads it back with the client's configuration (the source format PARQUET). **Types** by the page's "Type conversions" table, each read back as written with a row of NULLs: BOOLEAN; INT32 with no annotation, INT(8, 16, 32, signed or not) and DATE; INT64 with no annotation and TIMESTAMP(MICROS); FLOAT and DOUBLE (FLOAT64, ±Inf); BYTE_ARRAY STRING (`é☃` included); converted by the front: TIME(MILLIS), TIME(MICROS), TIMESTAMP(MILLIS), INT96 (nanoseconds of the day and Julian day), BYTE_ARRAY and FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY BYTES, unsigned INT64 up to 9,223,372,036,854,775,807 (above it the job fails `invalid`, as the page says), DECIMAL as INT32, INT64, FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY and 256-bit into NUMERIC or BIGNUMERIC by `decimalTargetTypes` ("In the order of NUMERIC, BIGNUMERIC, and STRING, a type is picked if it is in the specified list and if it supports the precision and the scale", default `["NUMERIC"]`; a value beyond the widest type's range fails the job `invalid`), and ENUM as BYTES or, with `enumAsString`, STRING. **Nested and repeated**: "Nested groups are converted into STRUCT types", so a group is a RECORD and, without list inference, a LIST or MAP a RECORD holding a REPEATED RECORD (`l RECORD (list REPEATED RECORD (element))`, `m RECORD (key_value REPEATED RECORD (key, value))`); with `parquetOptions.enableListInference` a LIST in the standard form is `repeated <element-type>`, a NULL list read back empty, and so is one in Parquet's backward-compatible forms (a repeated primitive, a repeated group of several fields or named `array` or `<name>_tuple`, pyarrow's `item`; #1069, unit `TestParquetListElement` only, not yet run against files from those writers); with `mapTargetType` `ARRAY_OF_STRUCT` a MAP is a REPEATED RECORD of key and value. **Into a new table** the table takes the file's schema, a REQUIRED column REQUIRED. **Appending**, columns match by name without regard to case at every depth ("Column names from Parquet files are treated as case-insensitive"), and a table column the file lacks is left NULL (as [LOAD DATA](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/load-statements) documents for a self-describing file; not measured against BigQuery). **Schema changes** ([Modifying table schemas](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-table-schemas)): `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` adds the file's new NULLABLE columns at the end, and a field it adds inside a RECORD (REPEATED included) at the end of that RECORD ("New columns and nested fields are always added at the end of the table or field"; #1068, unit `TestParquetAddsNestedFields` only, not yet run against an instance), NULL in the table's rows; the emulator adds no engine column through `tables.patch` (#1010) or `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` (measured: success, then "Column c is not present"), so the table is made again with the new schema, keeping its description, labels, partitioning and clustering (its creation time is new); `ALLOW_FIELD_RELAXATION` sets the column NULLABLE (`tables.patch`: the engine does not hold a REQUIRED column to it, measured); `WRITE_TRUNCATE` with a file whose schema is not the table's makes the table again with the file's schema ("overwrites the data, removes the constraints and uses the schema from the load job"); `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` keeps the table's schema and replaces its rows. The rows go to a scratch table first (in `_cloudburrow_query_results`, deleted later while the instance is idle, #1091, as the front's other scratch tables, #1057), so a load that fails leaves the table as it was, or, if it fails after the table was made again, names the scratch table holding the rows. **400** `invalid`, nothing loaded and the table unchanged: a file column the table lacks ("Cannot add fields", at any depth), a column of another type ("has changed type"), a NULLABLE file column into a REQUIRED one without `ALLOW_FIELD_RELAXATION` ("has changed mode"), a REQUIRED table column the file lacks, data that is not a Parquet file, files of one load from Cloud Storage whose columns differ only in mode, an unknown `writeDisposition` or `mapTargetType`. The messages follow BigQuery's form but were not measured against it. **501** `notImplemented`, nothing loaded: a NaN (the emulator's engine stores it as NULL, measured); an INT96 with a part of a microsecond, and a DECIMAL that NUMERIC or BIGNUMERIC would have to round (the page does not say whether BigQuery truncates or rounds); DECIMAL to STRING (the page does not say how it is written); a STRING or ENUM-as-STRING value that is not UTF-8; with list inference a NULL element, a LIST of lists, and a LIST in neither the standard form nor a backward-compatible one; `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` of a REQUIRED column ("allow adding a nullable field"); a STRING column into GEOGRAPHY; types the table does not list (TIMESTAMP(NANOS), TIME(NANOS), JSON, UUID); a column name BigQuery takes only as a flexible column name; `referenceFileSchemaUri`; files of one load whose schemas differ otherwise (BigQuery takes the alphabetically last file's); another project's table; a view; a job schema that is not the file's; a dry run of a load the front carries out. Units `TestParquetFooterSchema`, `TestParquetFooterRefusesOtherData`, `FuzzParquetFooter`, `TestParquetLoadColumns`, `TestParquetUploadIsSentWhole`, `TestParquetLoadFromCloudStorage`, `TestParquetLoadsTheFrontCarriesOut`, `TestParquetLoadValuesRefused`, `TestParquetLoadFailuresLeaveTheTable`, `TestParquetLoadFromCloudStorageTheFrontCarriesOut`, `TestParquetLiterals`. |
| `BYTES` values written by a JSON load, a CSV load and a streamed row (`tabledata.insertAll`) | **Verified**, any bytes, at the top level, in a `RECORD` and in a `REPEATED` column, from an upload and from Cloud Storage | `TestBigQueryBytesValuesAreWrittenAsTheirBytes` (#1065, #1075), official Go client (`ReaderSource`, `GCSReference`, `Inserter.Put` of a `[]byte`). BigQuery reads a JSON, CSV or streamed `BYTES` value as base64. Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: `/w==` (0xff) loaded by `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` and by CSV, and `[]byte{0xff}` put with `Inserter.Put`, each read back `TO_HEX` `2f773d3d`, the base64 text, with no error: the emulator bound each value to its engine as the string it read (`AddTableData`), and the engine stores a string as its bytes. #1074 had the front send the decoded bytes as a string, which could carry only UTF-8 text (501 for the rest). The emulator CloudBurrow builds decodes the base64 itself (#1061, bigquery-emulator patch 0001, which also hands a JSON load's nested fields to it), so every value reads back as its bytes: `YQBi`, `4piD`, `YWJj`, `DQo=`, `AA==` and `/w==` read back `610062`, `e29883`, `616263`, `0d0a`, `00` and `ff`, at the top level, in a `RECORD` and in a `REPEATED` column, by a JSON upload and load from Cloud Storage, a CSV upload (a carriage return included) and load from Cloud Storage, and `Inserter.Put` (`a\x00b`, `0xff`). The front still reads the records of a JSON or CSV load into a `BYTES` column, and a streamed row, to refuse a value that is not base64 as BigQuery does and to send one in the URL-safe alphabet, which BigQuery reads too, in the standard one the emulator decodes; a JSON load from Cloud Storage is read by it and sent as one upload when it has Cloud Storage, and sent on as it came when it has none. **400** `invalid`: a JSON value that is not base64 (nothing is loaded), and a line of a JSON load that is not one JSON object; a streamed value that is not base64 makes its row invalid. Units `TestJSONLoadChecksBytes`, `TestJSONLoadRefusesWhatItCannotWrite`, `TestJSONLoadFromCloudStorageIsRead`, `TestCSVLoadChecksBytes`, `TestInsertAllChecksBytes`, and the patch's `TestDecodeBytesValues`. |
| `FLOAT64` NaN, ±Infinity and -0 | **Verified** for NaN, written by every path and computed, and for ±Infinity; **Not supported** for -0 | `TestBigQueryNaNIsWrittenAndReadBack` (#1066), official Go client (`ReaderSource`, `GCSReference`, `Query.Parameters`) and, for `tabledata.insertAll`, the generated client (the Go client cannot marshal a NaN or an infinity). BigQuery's `FLOAT64` "includes NaN". Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: its engine keeps `FLOAT64` values as SQLite REALs and SQLite makes a NaN NULL, both when it is bound and when a function returns one, so a NaN from a JSON load, a CSV load, a query parameter and `IEEE_DIVIDE(0, 0)` each read back NULL (`IS_NAN` NULL), even `SELECT IEEE_DIVIDE(0, 0)`, and `CAST('NaN' AS FLOAT64)` was refused, 400 "failed to format query"; #1074 had the front answer 501 for the NaNs it could see. The engine CloudBurrow builds keeps a NaN (#1061, googlesqlite patch 0004, whose `TestNaNIsKept` covers `CAST('NaN' AS FLOAT64)`, a NaN parameter, arithmetic, `SUM` and `COUNTIF(IS_NAN(f))`), and the front sends each write on: a NaN in a `FLOAT64` column (top level, `RECORD`, `REPEATED`) of a JSON load (upload and Cloud Storage) and a CSV load (`NaN`, `nan`; upload and Cloud Storage), a streamed row, a query parameter of an `INSERT`, and `IEEE_DIVIDE(0, 0)` in an `INSERT`, each reads back `IS_NAN` true, and `SELECT @p` of a NaN parameter and `SELECT IEEE_DIVIDE(0, 0)` read NaN. **Not supported**: -0 reads back 0 through each write (its sign is lost, #1076; `CAST(x AS STRING)` of a `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT -0.0` reads `-0`), and is not refused, as BigQuery's handling of it is not documented; an infinity inside a `RECORD` or an `ARRAY`, from a JSON load or computed by a statement (`INSERT … SELECT [CAST('inf' AS FLOAT64)]`), which the pinned engine refused, 400 "json: unsupported value" (#1077), is written and read back since #1061 (`TestBigQueryInfinityInsideCompositeValues`; googlesqlite patch 0004). **±Infinity** from a JSON load (`"Infinity"`, `"-Infinity"`), a CSV load (`inf`, `-inf`), `tabledata.insertAll` and a query parameter reads back `IS_INF` with its sign. Units `TestNaNQueryParameters`, `TestJSONLoadChecksBytes`, `TestCSVLoadChecksBytes`, `TestInsertAllChecksBytes`. |
| `writeDisposition` `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` of a load, and a query job's `writeDisposition` | **Verified** for JSON and CSV loads, from an upload and from Cloud Storage (Parquet: row above), and for a query job's `WRITE_TRUNCATE`, `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` (with the result's columns in another order, missing a NULLABLE column, into `REQUIRED` columns and fields, #1083), `WRITE_EMPTY` and `WRITE_APPEND`; **Not implemented** (501) for a query job's `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` whose result column has another type or `REPEATED` mode than the table's, that leaves out a column with a default value, or whose `schemaUpdateOptions` would relax a column or add a field inside a RECORD (#1110); `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` adds the result's new top-level columns at the end of the table, NULLABLE (#1110, unit `TestQueryJobWriteDispositions` only) | `TestBigQueryWriteTruncateDataReplacesTheRows` (#1067, #1080, #1083), official Go client (`Loader.WriteDisposition`, `Query.Dst`, `Query.WriteDisposition`, `Client.JobFromID`). BigQuery: "WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA: If the table already exists, BigQuery overwrites the data, but keeps the constraints and schema of the existing table". Measured first through the front: a JSON and a CSV upload, and a JSON load of two objects from Cloud Storage, each with `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` into a table with rows, kept the rows and added the load's (the emulator knows only `WRITE_TRUNCATE` and `WRITE_EMPTY`), and the job succeeded. The emulator's `WRITE_TRUNCATE` of a table that exists deletes its rows in the same transaction as it inserts the load's, and keeps the table's schema; so the front sends `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` to it as `WRITE_TRUNCATE`, and the job reads back `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` (`jobs.insert`, `jobs.get`) with `outputRows` the rows loaded. Read back: after each of a JSON upload, a CSV upload, a JSON load of two objects and a CSV load from Cloud Storage, the table holds only the load's rows, with its schema and description; into a table that does not exist the load makes it. A query job with a destination table: the emulator appends the result whatever `writeDisposition` says (measured: `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA`, `WRITE_TRUNCATE` and `WRITE_EMPTY` each appended to a table with rows; 501 from #1067), so since #1080 the front carries them out into a table that exists ([JobConfigurationQuery](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationQuery), `querywrite.go`). **`WRITE_EMPTY`** (the default) into a table with rows fails the job, reason `duplicate`, "Already Exists: Table project:dataset.table", and runs nothing ("If the table already exists and contains data, a 'duplicate' error is returned in the job result"); read back: the table unchanged. **`WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA`** and **`WRITE_TRUNCATE`** run the query into a scratch table of the dataset first (so a query that reads the table reads it as it was, and one that fails leaves it: read back after `SELECT ERROR('no')`), then replace the table's rows with the result's in one script, `DELETE` and `INSERT … SELECT`, which the emulator runs in one transaction (measured: a failed script given to `jobs.query` left the rows), when the result's columns are the table's, or the job is `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA`; read back: `SELECT id + 1, … FROM t UNION ALL SELECT 20, 'u'` into `t` replaced its rows with those two, and the table kept its schema and description. **`WRITE_TRUNCATE`** with other columns "uses the schema from the query result": the table is made again with the result's schema, keeping its description, labels, expiration, partitioning and clustering and not its constraints (as a Parquet load's `WRITE_TRUNCATE`), and the result copied in; read back: `SELECT 1.5 AS z, [1, 2] AS arr` made the table `(z FLOAT, arr REPEATED INTEGER)` with its description. Those are two steps (the emulator adds no column through `tables.patch`, #1010): should the copy fail, the job fails `backendError` naming the scratch table that holds the result. `jobs.insert` and `jobs.get` show the client's destination and `writeDisposition`; the job's rows are read through `jobs.getQueryResults`, which the emulator answers from the job (measured: read back after the scratch table was deleted); no scratch table is left. **`WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` keeps the table's schema** (#1083), and the result is written into it as BigQuery writes a query's result into a table it appends to: [`schemaUpdateOptions`](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobConfigurationQuery.FIELDS.schema_update_options) are "supported … when writeDisposition is WRITE_APPEND; when writeDisposition is WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA", and BigQuery's samples of such an append ([Add columns in a query append job](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-table-schemas#add_columns_in_a_query_append_job), and relaxing a column in one) write `SELECT "Timmy" as full_name, 85 as age, "Blue" as favorite_color` into a table of `full_name` and `age`, both `REQUIRED`, with `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION`, and `SELECT "Beyonce" as full_name` into it with `ALLOW_FIELD_RELAXATION`, which makes `age` NULLABLE for the row that has none: a result's columns are the table's by name, a result column is written into a `REQUIRED` one, a column the result does not have is NULL only when it is NULLABLE, and a result column the table does not have needs `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION`. So the front writes each of the table's columns from the result's of its name, at any depth, a `RECORD` rebuilt by name in the table's order, and a column the result does not have NULL (a `REPEATED` one empty); the engine has no `NOT NULL`, so the front first counts the result's rows with NULL where the table has a `REQUIRED` column or field. Read back: `SELECT 'r' AS s, 41 AS id` into `(id, s)` wrote `41, r`; `SELECT 'only s' AS s` wrote `NULL, only s`; `SELECT 'a1' AS a, 1 AS b, STRUCT(9 AS y, 'k' AS x) AS r` into `(a REQUIRED, b, r RECORD<x REQUIRED, y>)` wrote `a1, 1, k, 9` and kept the schema. **The job fails**, reason `invalid`, the table unchanged: a result column the table does not have (`SELECT 1.5 AS z`), a NULL for a `REQUIRED` column or field (`CAST(NULL AS STRING) AS a`, `STRUCT(CAST(NULL AS STRING) AS x, …)`), and a `REQUIRED` column the result does not have (`SELECT 3 AS b`); BigQuery's wording of these errors is UNVERIFIED. **501** `notImplemented`, nothing run: a result column of another type (`SELECT 1.5 AS id` into an `INTEGER` column: whether BigQuery coerces is not documented) or `REPEATED` mode, a missing column that has a default value (whether BigQuery writes its default is not documented), and `schemaUpdateOptions` `ALLOW_FIELD_RELAXATION` into a table with a `REQUIRED` column, or `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` of a field inside a RECORD, which CloudBurrow does not carry out for a query job; `ALLOW_FIELD_ADDITION` of a top-level column is carried out, the table made again with the column at its end, NULLABLE, keeping its other properties and constraints (the emulator adds no engine column through `tables.patch`, #1010); either destination when it is a view, or when the query is not one `SELECT`. A dry run, `WRITE_APPEND` and a destination that does not exist are sent on. A load of several objects from Cloud Storage is one transaction since #1079 (row "A load of several objects from Cloud Storage"). Units `TestWriteTruncateDataIsSentAsWriteTruncate`, `TestQueryDestinationWriteDispositions`, `TestQueryJobWriteDispositions`, `TestConvertColumns`, `TestNullViolations`. |
| ±Infinity inside a `RECORD` or `ARRAY` value, and `FLOAT64` ±Infinity as the REST API reads it | **Verified** for a `RECORD`, a `REPEATED RECORD` and a `REPEATED` column written by a JSON load (upload and Cloud Storage) and a streamed row, and for the text of ±Infinity in the rows of `tabledata.list`, `jobs.query` and `jobs.getQueryResults`, and, since #1061, for an infinity inside a `STRUCT` or `ARRAY` that a statement computes; **Not supported** for one a query parameter carries | `TestBigQueryInfinityInRecordsAndArrays` (#1077) and `TestBigQueryInfinityInsideCompositeValues` (#1061), official Go client (`ReaderSource`, `GCSReference`, `Query.Read`) and, for `tabledata.insertAll`, `tabledata.list`, `jobs.query` and `jobs.getQueryResults`, the generated client, whose rows are the wire's text. Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: a `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` load of `{"r":{"g":"-Infinity"}}` (or `"Infinity"`) into a `RECORD` with a `FLOAT64` field failed 400 "json: unsupported value: -Inf": the engine keeps a `STRUCT` or `ARRAY` value as JSON (googlesqlite, `valueLayoutFromValue` through `json.Marshal`), which has no infinity. The same value streamed was stored and read back, because the emulator hands a streamed `RECORD` to its engine as a list of one-field objects in the schema's order (`types.go`, `normalizeData`), whose values the engine keeps as their text, and a load's as the object it read; a load of `{"r":[{"g":"Infinity"}]}`, that list, was stored and read back `+Inf` (measured). So the front sends a JSON load's `RECORD` value that holds ±Infinity, at any depth, in that form, and every other value as it was; read back: `r.g` `-Infinity`, `rr` `[1.5, Infinity]`, `a` `[-Infinity, 2]`, and a `RECORD` of a `FLOAT64` and a `STRING` from Cloud Storage. **Reading**: BigQuery writes a `FLOAT64` ±Infinity in a row as `"Infinity"` and `"-Infinity"`; the emulator wrote Go's `"+Inf"` and `"-Inf"` (measured, at the top level, in a `RECORD` and in a `REPEATED` column), which the Go client reads (its `strconv`), but which are not BigQuery's text. The front writes them as BigQuery does in the rows of `tabledata.list`, `jobs.query` and `jobs.getQueryResults`, by the schema: a `STRING` value `"+Inf"` reads back as it was (same test). **In a statement**: `STRUCT(CAST('inf' AS FLOAT64))` and `[CAST('-inf' AS FLOAT64)]` failed 400 on the pinned engine ("failed to format query … strconv.ParseFloat", and "json: unsupported value"); CloudBurrow's build parses the literal and keeps an infinity inside a composite (googlesqlite patch 0004), so `SELECT` and `INSERT … SELECT` of an `ARRAY` with `CAST('inf' AS FLOAT64)`, a NaN and a `STRUCT` with `IEEE_DIVIDE(-1, 0)` read back (`TestBigQueryInfinityInsideCompositeValues`). **Not supported**, 400 from the engine, nothing written: an `ARRAY<FLOAT64>` query parameter holding an infinity in an `INSERT` ("Value has type ARRAY<STRING>", measured on the pinned emulator; not re-measured on CloudBurrow's build). Units `TestInfinityRecords`, `TestJSONLoadSendsInfinityRecordsAsAList`, `TestInfinityRows`. |
| `BYTES` query parameters, and `STRUCT` query parameters | **Verified** for a `BYTES` and an `ARRAY<BYTES>` parameter, named or positional, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`, and since #1082 for a `STRUCT` parameter and an `ARRAY` of them, with `BYTES` fields at any depth; **Not implemented** (501) for a `RANGE` inside a `STRUCT` parameter (#1111); **Not supported** for reading a whole `STRUCT` with a `TIMESTAMP` field (#1101) | `TestBigQueryBytesParameters` (#1078), official Go client (`Query.Parameters` with a `[]byte` and a `[][]byte`, `Client.JobFromID`). BigQuery takes a `BYTES` parameter's value as base64 and types the parameter `BYTES`. Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: `INSERT INTO ds.t (id, b) VALUES (1, @p)` with `@p` the bytes `ff 00 61` failed 400 "Value has type STRING which cannot be inserted into column b, which has type BYTES", `TO_HEX(@p)` failed "No matching signature … STRING", an `ARRAY<BYTES>` read back as its base64 texts, and `@p.x` of a `STRUCT` with a `BYTES` field failed "Cannot access field x on a value with type STRING": the emulator hands its engine the value as a string (its source, `queryParameterValueToGoValue`), which the engine types `STRING`. `FROM_BASE64` of the same text as a `STRING` parameter read back `ff0061` (measured). So the front sends a `BYTES` parameter as a `STRING` of its standard base64 and each reference to it as `FROM_BASE64(@p)`, and an `ARRAY<BYTES>` as an `ARRAY<STRING>` referred to as the array of its elements' bytes in order (NULL when it is NULL); positional parameters are sent as named ones (`@cloudburrow_p1` …), so that one can be referred to twice. The references are found by the front's lexer, so a string literal or comment is not changed. Read back: `SELECT @p` returns the bytes `ff 00 61` exactly, `TO_HEX(@p)` `ff0061`, `'@p'` the text `@p`, a positional `?` and a NULL the same, an `INSERT` of `@p` and of an `ARRAY<BYTES>` stores `ff0061`, `WHERE b = @p` finds it, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text and parameters. A value that is not base64 is **400** `invalidQuery`. **`STRUCT` parameters** (#1082): the emulator types a `STRUCT` parameter, and an `ARRAY` of them, as one `STRING`, the JSON of its value, whatever its fields (measured: `SELECT @p` of a `STRUCT<X STRING, Y INT64>` read back the `STRING` `{"X":"/wBh","Y":"2"}`, and `@p.X` failed "Cannot access field X on a value with type STRING"), while its engine types a `STRUCT` built in the query as it is (measured: `STRUCT<X BYTES, Y INT64>(FROM_BASE64(@a), @b)`, `ARRAY<STRUCT<…>>[…]`, `CAST(NULL AS STRUCT<…>)`, nested, read back and inserted into a `RECORD` so). So the front sends such a parameter as that expression, each reference to it replaced by it: each scalar of its value a parameter of its own (`@cloudburrow_s1_1` …), in a typed `STRUCT<…>(…)` or `ARRAY<…>[…]` in the value's order, a NULL `STRUCT` or field a typed NULL, an `ARRAY` given no values empty; a scalar is sent as its own type where the emulator types a scalar parameter so (`INT64`, `FLOAT64`, `BOOL`, `STRING`, `TIMESTAMP`, measured), `BYTES` in `FROM_BASE64`, `NUMERIC`, `BIGNUMERIC`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME` and `INTERVAL` (which the emulator types `STRING`, a `DATETIME` `TIMESTAMP`, measured) as a `CAST` of their text, `GEOGRAPHY` in `ST_GEOGFROMTEXT` and `JSON` in `PARSE_JSON`. Read back through the Go client: a `STRUCT` of `BYTES` (`ff 00 61`), `STRING`, `FLOAT64` 0.1, `BOOL`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME`, `TIMESTAMP`, `NUMERIC` 123.45, a nested `STRUCT` with `BYTES` and the `INT64` 9007199254740993, an `ARRAY` of `STRUCT`s and an `ARRAY<BYTES>` reads each field back exactly (`@p.X`, `TO_HEX(@p.X)`, `@p.I.Z`, `@p.A[OFFSET(1)].Z` …), `SELECT @p` of a `STRUCT` of `BYTES`, `STRING`, a nested `STRUCT` and an `ARRAY` of them reads the whole `RECORD`; an `ARRAY` of `STRUCT`s, positional `STRUCT`s and a NULL one (`@p IS NULL`) read back; an `INSERT` of a `STRUCT` and an `ARRAY` of them into a `RECORD` and a `REPEATED RECORD` with `BYTES` fields stores `ff0061` and the rest in order, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text and parameters; a `BYTES` field that is not base64 is **400**. **501**, naming it, nothing run: a `RANGE` field (or a type the front does not know) inside a `STRUCT`. **Not supported**: `SELECT @p` of a whole `STRUCT` with a `TIMESTAMP` field fails in the Go client, as any `TIMESTAMP` inside a `RECORD` reads back as the engine's text (#1101); `@p.TS` reads back. The other scalar types at the top level are the emulator's (#1108). **Not covered**: a Go client `[]byte{}` is sent with no value, which the emulator reads as NULL, as the Go client does not send an empty base64 text. Units `TestBytesParameters`, `TestBytesParameterJobShowsTheClients`. |
| The `-` operator on one operand (`-a` of a column or an expression) | **Verified** for `INT64`, `FLOAT64` and `NUMERIC` | `TestBigQueryUnaryMinus` (#1061), official Go client: `SELECT -a, -b, CAST(-c AS STRING)` of 3, 1.5 and `NUMERIC '2.25'` reads -3, -1.5 and -2.25, and the negation of the least `INT64` is an error. Measured first on the pinned emulator's engine: anything but a literal, which the analyzer folds, failed "no such function: googlesqlite_unary_minus", which goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1 does not register; CloudBurrow's build registers it (googlesqlite patch 0005, `TestUnaryMinus`). |
| Top-level `NUMERIC`, `BIGNUMERIC`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME`, `INTERVAL`, `GEOGRAPHY` and `JSON` query parameters, and `ARRAY`s of them | **Verified** named and positional, NULL, in an `ARRAY` (with a NULL element), selected, inserted into a column of the type and compared with one, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`, and since #1120 and #1119 `=` of `INTERVAL`s and a `GEOGRAPHY` read back as BigQuery writes it (`POINT(1 2)`) | `TestBigQueryScalarParameterTypes` (#1108), official Go client (`Query.Parameters` with a `*big.Rat`, `civil.Date`, `civil.DateTime`, `civil.Time`, `*bigquery.IntervalValue` and `*bigquery.QueryParameterValue` of `BIGNUMERIC`, `GEOGRAPHY` and `JSON`, `Client.JobFromID`). In BigQuery a parameter has the type its `parameterType` names. Measured first through the front with the Go client, on the pinned emulator: `SELECT @p` read back a `STRING` of the text for `NUMERIC` 123.45, `BIGNUMERIC`, `DATE`, `TIME`, `GEOGRAPHY`, `JSON` and `INTERVAL`, and a `TIMESTAMP` for a `DATETIME`, and a positional `NUMERIC` failed "strconv.ParseInt: parsing … invalid syntax"; `CAST` of the same text as a `STRING` read back each as sent, `ST_GEOGFROMTEXT` a `GEOGRAPHY` and `PARSE_JSON` a `JSON` (a `CAST` to either is refused). So the front sends such a parameter as the `STRING` of its value, each reference to it as its conversion (`CAST(@p AS NUMERIC)` …), as it sends a `BYTES` one (previous row), an `ARRAY` of them as an `ARRAY<STRING>` read back element by element in order, and one with a NULL element (which the emulator reads as `''` in an `ARRAY<STRING>`, measured) as a typed `ARRAY<…>[…]` of its elements; positional parameters as named ones. Read back through the Go client, for each type: `SELECT @p` of the type's column type and the value exactly (`NUMERIC` 123.45, `BIGNUMERIC` 1.25000000001, `DATE` 2020-01-02, `DATETIME` and `TIME` to the microsecond, `INTERVAL` 1-2 3 4:5:6, `JSON` `{"a":1}`, `GEOGRAPHY` `POINT(1 2)`), `@p IS NULL` false, a positional `?` the same, a NULL of the type NULL of the type, an `ARRAY` of three with a NULL in the middle its length, elements and NULL; an `INSERT` into a column of the type stores it, `WHERE v = @p` (`ST_EQUALS` for `GEOGRAPHY`, `TO_JSON_STRING` for `JSON`) finds it, a NULL of the type is inserted as NULL, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text and parameters. `TIMESTAMP`, `INT64`, `FLOAT64`, `BOOL` and `STRING` are sent as they are (the emulator types them so, measured, #1082). Units `TestBytesParameters`. |
| `NULL` arguments of `ARRAY_TO_STRING`, `ARRAY_CONCAT`, `ARRAY_REVERSE` and the `NET.` functions; an emulator 500 to a query | **Verified** NULL as in BigQuery, and a prompt 400 for a query the emulator answers 500 | `TestBigQueryNullArgumentsOfArrayFunctions` (#1109), official Go client (`Query.Read`, `Query.Run`, `Job.Read`). In BigQuery "If an operand is NULL, the function result is NULL" unless a function says otherwise ([function call rules](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/functions-reference)), and `ARRAY_CONCAT` "returns NULL if any input argument is NULL" ([array functions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/array_functions)). Measured first through the front: `SELECT ARRAY_TO_STRING(CAST(NULL AS ARRAY<STRING>), ',')` was answered 500 internalError "runtime error: invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference", which the Go client retried until its deadline (60 s): the engine (goccy/googlesqlite v0.3.1) passes a NULL to a function as a nil value, which these functions called a method on. Fixed in the engine CloudBurrow builds (googlesqlite patch 0011, next row); the front no longer rewrites these calls (it sent each as `IF(arg IS NULL …, NULL, call)` since #1109). Read back through `jobs.query` and a query job: `ARRAY_TO_STRING` of a NULL array, delimiter or `null_text`, with `SAFE.`, nested in `ARRAY_REVERSE` and of a NULL parameter, reads NULL; `ARRAY_REVERSE` and `ARRAY_CONCAT` of a NULL array and `NET.IPV4_TO_INT64`, `NET.IP_TO_STRING`, `NET.REG_DOMAIN` of a NULL are NULL; `ARRAY_TO_STRING(['a', NULL, 'b'], '-')` reads `a-b`, with `'n'` `a-n-b`, of an `ARRAY_AGG` subquery `a+b`. **An emulator 500 to a query**: the emulator answers 500 only for a panic (a query it cannot run is 400, its source), so the front answers a 500 the emulator gave a query (`jobs.query`, or `jobs.insert` of a query job) **400** `invalidQuery` with the emulator's text, which the client does not retry. A 500 to anything else the front sends first (a `datasets.insert`) is left as it is. Units `TestNoRetry`. |
| `NULL` arguments of the other functions the engine failed on, and `IN` with a NULL | **Verified** for those listed | `TestBigQueryNullArgumentsOfOtherFunctions` (#1121), official Go client, through `jobs.query` and a query job. In BigQuery a NULL operand makes a function's result NULL (function call rules, previous row), `GENERATE_DATE_ARRAY` and `GENERATE_TIMESTAMP_ARRAY` return "a NULL array" if any argument is NULL ([array functions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/array_functions)), "If json_key is NULL, an error is produced" (`JSON_OBJECT`, [JSON functions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/json_functions)), and `IN` "Returns FALSE if value_set is empty. Returns TRUE if value_set contains a value equal to search_value. Returns NULL if the equality comparison between search_value and any value in value_set produces NULL. Returns FALSE." ([operators](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/operators)). Found by calling each of the engine's functions with a NULL argument (in its own module, googlesqlite v0.3.1): a panic (the emulator's 500) for the step of `GENERATE_DATE_ARRAY` and `GENERATE_TIMESTAMP_ARRAY`, `MAKE_INTERVAL`, `INTERVAL <NULL> DAY`, `ST_BUFFER`, `ST_BUFFERWITHTOLERANCE`, `ST_INTERSECTSBOX`, `NULLIF(x, NULL)`, `JSON_OBJECT`'s key and `JSON_SET`'s path and value, beside the previous row's; an error for `ST_DISTANCE`, `ST_HAUSDORFFDWITHIN`, `JUSTIFY_DAYS`, `JUSTIFY_HOURS`, `JUSTIFY_INTERVAL` and `JSON_TYPE`; FALSE for `NULL IN UNNEST([1, 2])`, `1 IN UNNEST([2, NULL])` and `1 IN (2, NULL)`. Fixed in the engine (googlesqlite patch 0011: one guard where every scalar function is called returns NULL for a NULL argument of those functions, and returns any function's panic as an error naming it; `JSON_OBJECT`, `JSON_SET`, `NULLIF`, `IN` and `IN UNNEST` fixed where they are). Read back: each of those calls with a NULL reads NULL, promptly; `ARRAY_LENGTH(GENERATE_DATE_ARRAY('2020-01-01', '2020-01-03'))` 3, `MAKE_INTERVAL(hour => 10)` `0-0 0 10:0:0`, `ST_INTERSECTSBOX(p, 0, 0, 10, 10)` true, `NULLIF(1, NULL)` 1, `1 IN UNNEST([NULL, 1])` true, `NULL IN UNNEST([])` false, `JSON_SET(JSON '{"a":1}', '$.a', NULL)` `{"a":null}`; `JSON_OBJECT(CAST(NULL AS STRING), 1)` fails 400 "JSON_OBJECT: a key is NULL". |
| `=`, `<` and the other comparisons of `INTERVAL`s; a negative `INTERVAL` of less than an hour; `INTERVAL n MILLISECOND` and `MICROSECOND` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryIntervalComparison` (#1120, #1126), official Go client (`Query.Parameters` with a `*bigquery.IntervalValue`). In BigQuery "Values of the same comparable data type can be compared to each other. All data types are supported except for: GEOGRAPHY JSON ARRAY" ([comparable data types](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-types#comparable_data_types)); the page does not say how two intervals compare, and GoogleSQL's own implementation compares their length, a month counting 30 days and a day 24 hours ([interval_value.h](https://github.com/google/googlesql/blob/master/googlesql/public/interval_value.h), `GetAsMicros` and the comparison operators). The canonical format is `[sign]Y-M [sign]D [sign]H:M:S[.F]`, and `INTERVAL` takes `MILLISECOND` and `MICROSECOND` ([interval type](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-types#interval_type)). Measured first through the front: `SELECT INTERVAL 1 DAY = INTERVAL 1 DAY` failed 400 "unsupported eq operator for interval value", and so did `WHERE v = @p`; read in the engine: `INTERVAL -1 SECOND` was written `0-0 0 0:0:1` (and stored so, positive), and `INTERVAL 1500 MILLISECOND` failed "unexpected interval part". Fixed in the engine (googlesqlite patches 0009 and 0010). Read back: `INTERVAL 1 DAY = INTERVAL 1 DAY`, `INTERVAL 1 MONTH = INTERVAL 30 DAY`, `INTERVAL 1 DAY = INTERVAL 24 HOUR`, `INTERVAL 1 DAY < INTERVAL 25 HOUR`, `INTERVAL -1 SECOND < INTERVAL 0 SECOND` true; `CAST(INTERVAL -1 SECOND AS STRING)` `0-0 0 -0:0:1`, `INTERVAL -1 MONTH` `-0-1 0 0:0:0`, `INTERVAL 1500 MILLISECOND` `0-0 0 0:0:1.5`; a table's `WHERE v = @p` of `@p` 1 day finds `INTERVAL 1 DAY` and `INTERVAL 24 HOUR`, `v > @p` `INTERVAL 2 DAY`, `v < INTERVAL 0 SECOND` the stored `INTERVAL -1 SECOND`. **Not supported**: `ORDER BY`, `MIN` and `MAX` of an `INTERVAL` order its text (#1138). |
| A `REPEATED` column a streamed row or a load's record leaves out | **Verified** for `tabledata.insertAll` and a JSON load | `TestBigQueryRepeatedFieldLeftOutIsAnEmptyArray` (#1124), official Go client (`Inserter.Put` of a `ValuesSaver` without the field, `ReaderSource`, `Table.Read`). In BigQuery a stored array is never NULL: "each array is written as an empty array" ([array type](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/data-types#array_type)). Measured first through the front: a row streamed without `tags` read `tags IS NULL` true, `ARRAY_LENGTH(tags)` NULL and `ARRAY_TO_STRING(tags, ',')` NULL. Fixed in the emulator CloudBurrow builds (bigquery-emulator patch 0002: an empty array for such a column where every write of rows is bound, and for a `REPEATED` field given as null or left out of a `RECORD`). Read back: the streamed row and the loaded record read false, 0 and `''`, and `[]` in `tabledata.list`; a row with `["a", "b"]` 2 and `a,b`. |
| A `GEOGRAPHY`'s text | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryGeographyWKT` (#1119), official Go client (`Query.Read`, a `GEOGRAPHY` `QueryParameterValue`, `Inserter.Put`, `Table.Read`).
