BigQuery writes a geography's WKT with no space before a parenthesis and an empty one as `GEOMETRYCOLLECTION EMPTY`: every example in the [geography functions](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/geography_functions) reference (`POINT(0 0)`, `LINESTRING(1 2, 2 1)`, `GEOMETRYCOLLECTION(POINT(0 0), LINESTRING(1 2, 2 1))`, and `GEOMETRYCOLLECTION EMPTY` for `ST_GEOGFROMTEXT('POINT EMPTY')`). Measured first through the front: `SELECT ST_GEOGPOINT(1, 2)` read `POINT (1 2)`, and so did a `GEOGRAPHY` parameter `POINT(1 2)`. Fixed in the engine (googlesqlite patch 0012). Read back: `ST_GEOGPOINT(1, 2)` `POINT(1 2)`, `ST_GEOGFROMTEXT('POINT (1 2)')` `POINT(1 2)`, a `LINESTRING` and a `GEOMETRYCOLLECTION` as above, `ST_GEOGFROMTEXT('POINT EMPTY')` `GEOMETRYCOLLECTION EMPTY`, the parameter `POINT(1 2)`, and in `tabledata.list` a streamed `POINT (3 4)` `POINT(3 4)` and an inserted `ST_GEOGPOINT(5, 6)` `POINT(5 6)`. **Not supported**: WKT text inside a `RECORD` or `REPEATED` column is stored as given, and the numbers of given text as written (#1138). |
| `TO_JSON_STRING`, `TO_JSON` and `SAFE_TO_JSON` | **Verified** for every type of GoogleSQL's [JSON encodings](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/standard-sql/json_functions#json_encodings) table but `GEOGRAPHY` and the graph types, of a value read from a table (written by DML, `tabledata.insertAll` or a load) and of a literal; **Not verified** for `GEOGRAPHY`, which the table does not list (written as its WKT text, as before), and for the number of sub-second digits of a `TIME`, `DATETIME` or `TIMESTAMP` beyond the table's six-digit examples; **wrong**, the same way as before, in `JSON_OBJECT`, `JSON_ARRAY`, `JSON_SET`, `JSON_ARRAY_APPEND` and `JSON_ARRAY_INSERT`, which use the same encoding (#1127) | `TestBigQueryToJSONStringOfStoredRows` (#1116), official Go client: rows of `INT64` (one past 2^53), `FLOAT64`, `NUMERIC` (123.56), `BIGNUMERIC`, `STRING`, `BYTES`, `BOOL`, `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME`, `TIMESTAMP`, a `STRUCT` and an `ARRAY<BOOL>`, each written by DML, the `Inserter` and an NDJSON load, give the table's encodings (`true`, a quoted ISO 8601 date and time, a wide `INT64` and a fractional `NUMERIC` as strings), and `TO_JSON` of the row the same with the wide numbers as numbers; an `INTERVAL` is an ISO 8601 duration (`"PT10H20M30.52S"`), +inf `"Infinity"`, a `RANGE` `{"start":…,"end":…}`, `stringify_wide_numbers => TRUE` a string. **Measured first**: a `BOOL` read from a table was `1` and a `DATE`, `DATETIME`, `TIME` or `TIMESTAMP` was written without quotes, not JSON. Fixed in the engine CloudBurrow builds ([googlesqlite 0013](../third_party/bigquery-emulator/patches/googlesqlite/0013-typed-json-encoding.patch), whose `TestJSONEncodingOfEveryType` fails without it): the function is given its argument's type. An `INTERVAL` of a negative number of months fails in the engine before the function is called (#1128). |
| `BYTES` query parameters | **Verified** for a `BYTES` and an `ARRAY<BYTES>` parameter, named or positional, through `jobs.query` and `jobs.insert`; **Not implemented** (501) for a `STRUCT` parameter with a `BYTES` field | `TestBigQueryBytesParameters` (#1078), official Go client (`Query.Parameters` with a `[]byte` and a `[][]byte`, `Client.JobFromID`). BigQuery takes a `BYTES` parameter's value as base64 and types the parameter `BYTES`. Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: `INSERT INTO ds.t (id, b) VALUES (1, @p)` with `@p` the bytes `ff 00 61` failed 400 "Value has type STRING which cannot be inserted into column b, which has type BYTES", `TO_HEX(@p)` failed "No matching signature … STRING", an `ARRAY<BYTES>` read back as its base64 texts, and `@p.x` of a `STRUCT` with a `BYTES` field failed "Cannot access field x on a value with type STRING": the emulator hands its engine the value as a string (its source, `queryParameterValueToGoValue`), which the engine types `STRING`. `FROM_BASE64` of the same text as a `STRING` parameter read back `ff0061` (measured). So the front sends a `BYTES` parameter as a `STRING` of its standard base64 and each reference to it as `FROM_BASE64(@p)`, and an `ARRAY<BYTES>` as an `ARRAY<STRING>` referred to as the array of its elements' bytes in order (NULL when it is NULL); positional parameters are sent as named ones (`@cloudburrow_p1` …), so that one can be referred to twice. The references are found by the front's lexer, so a string literal or comment is not changed. Read back: `SELECT @p` returns the bytes `ff 00 61` exactly, `TO_HEX(@p)` `ff0061`, `'@p'` the text `@p`, a positional `?` and a NULL the same, an `INSERT` of `@p` and of an `ARRAY<BYTES>` stores `ff0061`, `WHERE b = @p` finds it, and `jobs.get` shows the client's text and parameters. A value that is not base64 is **400** `invalidQuery`. **Not covered**: a Go client `[]byte{}` is sent with no value, which the emulator reads as NULL, as the Go client does not send an empty base64 text. Units `TestBytesParameters`, `TestBytesParameterJobShowsTheClients`. |
| A load of several objects from Cloud Storage | **Verified** all or nothing for JSON, CSV and Parquet loads, with `WRITE_TRUNCATE` and `WRITE_APPEND`; **Not implemented** (501) with no Cloud Storage given to the front | `TestBigQueryLoadOfSeveralObjectsIsOneJob` (#1079), official Go client (`GCSReference` with a wildcard and with two URIs, `Loader.WriteDisposition`). BigQuery's load is one job: it loads every object or none. Measured first through the front, on the pinned emulator: the emulator reads a load from Cloud Storage itself one object at a time, each in a transaction of its own, giving only the first the job's `writeDisposition` (its source, `importFromGCS`), so a `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON` load of two objects whose second failed left the table with the first object's rows, its own gone under `WRITE_TRUNCATE` and `WRITE_TRUNCATE_DATA` and kept under `WRITE_APPEND`; a CSV load with columns, read by the front since #944, left the table as it was. Now the front reads every JSON and CSV load from Cloud Storage itself (its `--storage`, the instance's Cloud Storage) and sends the objects to the emulator as one upload, which it loads in one transaction; a Parquet load the front carries out itself, its rows in a scratch table first. Read back: after JSON (with a schema, without one, two URIs), CSV and Parquet loads whose second object fails, with `WRITE_TRUNCATE` and with `WRITE_APPEND`, the table holds its row as before; loads of two good objects load every row. A CSV load with no schema and no autodetect into a table that does not exist is **400** `invalid`, "No schema specified on job or table" (measured before: the emulator answered 500 "nil pointer dereference", which the Go client retried until its deadline). An `AVRO` or `ORC` load is refused by the emulator before anything is written (400 "not support sourceFormat", measured). **501** `notImplemented`: a load of several objects (more than one URI, or a wildcard) with no Cloud Storage given to the front; one of a single object is sent on. Units `TestCloudStorageLoadReportsCounts`, `TestCSVLoadFromCloudStorage`. |
| Loads through a resumable upload | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryAutodetectHeaderAndResumableLoads` (#919): the Go client with `googleapi.ChunkSize(googleapi.MinUploadChunkSize)` (256 KiB chunks; it uses a resumable upload above its chunk size, 16 MiB by default) loads every row of a CSV of over 768 KiB, and a header BigQuery refuses fails that job, which `jobs.list` reports. The emulator does not serve the protocol: it answered the first request with a `Location` of its own listen address, `http://0.0.0.0:9051/…`, which the Go client retried until it gave up (measured), and it takes a chunk only by `PUT`, where the Go client sends `POST` (from its source). Upstream: [goccy/bigquery-emulator#160](https://github.com/goccy/bigquery-emulator/issues/160) (closed) and [#393](https://github.com/goccy/bigquery-emulator/pull/393) (closed unmerged). The front serves the upload itself: it keeps the job and the chunks, in a file in its container, answers each chunk but the last 308 with its `Range` (or 200 with `X-Http-Status-Code-Override: 308`, as the Go client asks), and on the last sends the job and the data to the emulator as one multipart upload through the same checks as any load. The first request is checked for the table ID and schema; an unfinished upload is dropped after 24 hours. Unit `TestResumableUploads`. |
| Loads from Cloud Storage (`gs://` URIs) | **Verified** from the instance's Cloud Storage | `TestBigQueryAutodetectLoadFromCloudStorage` (#919), official Go client for both services: a CSV written to a bucket loads its rows with `autodetect`, and one whose header BigQuery refuses fails the job and leaves no table. Measured first: the emulator, given no `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST`, dialled `storage.googleapis.com` for a `gs://` load. CloudBurrow now starts it with `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` set to the in-cluster Cloud Storage server, `http://storage.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local:4443`, so a load reads the instance's own buckets; without Cloud Storage enabled the name does not resolve and the job fails, still offline. A CSV load's objects are read by the front, from the same server (#944, the CSV loads row); other formats by the emulator. Extract jobs to `gs://` write to the same server: the Extract jobs to Cloud Storage row (`TestBigQueryExtractToCloudStorage`, #939). |
| `jobs.list` with `projection=full`: each job's configuration | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryJobListGivesConfigurations` (#958), official Go client (`Client.Jobs`, which asks for `projection=full`, and `Job.Config`). Measured against the pinned emulator over REST: its `jobs.list` gave each job only `jobReference`, `kind`, `statistics` and `status`, whatever the projection, so `Job.Config()` of a listed job was nil; BigQuery's `full` projection "Includes all job data" (the enum's description in the API's discovery document; [jobs.list](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/jobs/list)). The front keeps each job's configuration from the emulator's answer to `jobs.insert`, and reads a job it has not seen (a `jobs.query`'s, whose answer has none, or one made before the front started) once with `jobs.get`; the last 5,000 are kept, and an older one is read again. A query job's and a `jobs.query` script's text read back as the client wrote them (#939). Without `projection=full` no configuration is given, as BigQuery's `minimal` "Does not include the job configuration". The emulator's `jobs.list` also lists every job whatever the request's `maxResults`; the front pages and filters it (#972, row below). Unit `TestJobListGivesConfigurations`. |
| `jobs.list`: order, `maxResults` and `pageToken`, `stateFilter`, `minCreationTime`, `maxCreationTime`, `parentJobId`, `allUsers` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryJobListPagesAndFilters` (#972), official Go client (`Client.Jobs` with `State`, `MinCreationTime`, `MaxCreationTime`, `ParentJobID`, `AllUsers`, and `iterator.NewPager` pages of two). Measured first against the pinned emulator over REST: `maxResults=2` listed nine jobs, oldest first, with no `nextPageToken`; its handler (`jobsListHandler`) reads none of the request's parameters. BigQuery's list "is sorted in reverse chronological order, by job creation time", pages by `maxResults` and `pageToken`, and filters by `stateFilter`, `minCreationTime` and `maxCreationTime` ("after or at", "before or at", in milliseconds) and `parentJobId` ("If set, show only child jobs of the specified parent. Otherwise, show all top-level jobs") ([jobs.list](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/jobs/list)). The front does each over the whole list, after its own jobs and the job times (#971) are in it: newest first (jobs of one millisecond, newest made first); a page token names the page's last job, so a job made between pages does not move the next page; a job with a `parentJobId` is listed only under it (the emulator makes no child jobs, so such a list is empty). The queries the front runs itself for its checks (reading a `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT`'s columns, the resync statements of #955, and the rest) are left out: they are no client's jobs; the last 5,000 are remembered. `allUsers` lists the same jobs: the emulator has one user, so every job is the caller's. A bad `pageToken`, `stateFilter` or creation time is 400 `invalid`. Unit `TestJobListPagesAndFilters`. |
| Job times: `statistics.creationTime`, `startTime`, `endTime` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryJobTimes` (#971), official Go client (`Job.LastStatus`, `Job.Status`, `Client.Jobs`, `JobStatistics.CreationTime`, `StartTime`, `EndTime`) and the generated client (`google.golang.org/api/bigquery/v2`) for the raw values: an upload, a load from Cloud Storage and a query job. BigQuery gives them "in milliseconds since the epoch" ([JobStatistics](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job#JobStatistics)). Measured first: the emulator gives every job it runs its times in seconds (its source: `startTime.Unix()`), so a load from Cloud Storage and a query job read back 1970-01-21, and an upload's job none, the zero time. The front times each `jobs.insert` and `jobs.query` it sends on: creation and start when it received the job, end when the emulator answered; a job answered with no times, or times in seconds, is given those in the `jobs.insert` answer, `jobs.get`, `jobs.cancel` and `jobs.list`. A job the front did not time (made before the front started, or one of more than 5,000 since) has its seconds read as milliseconds. The jobs the front writes itself (#957) keep their own. Times are the cluster's clock (measured 6 ms behind the host's). Unit `TestJobTimesInMilliseconds`. |
| `jobs.insert` with no `jobReference` | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryJobWithoutReference` (#973), the generated client (`Jobs.Insert` of a `Job` with no `JobReference`, which the official Go client always sends): a query job that succeeds and one that fails, a load from Cloud Storage and an upload each come back with a job ID in the request's project, which `jobs.get` finds, and the loads load. Measured first against the pinned emulator: the query jobs and the load from Cloud Storage were answered 500 "runtime error: invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference", which the client retries. BigQuery's `jobReference` is optional ([Job](https://cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/reference/rest/v2/Job)); the front gives such a job one before anything else reads it: the path's project, a job ID of its own, and the reference's `location` when it gave one (the emulator gives no job a location otherwise). A copy job, with or without a reference, is the emulator's 400 "unspecified job configuration query" (#987). Unit `TestJobWithoutReferenceIsGivenOne`. |
| `jobs.list` of a job the front failed | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryAutodetectHeaderAndResumableLoads` and `TestBigQueryExceptionHandlersRaiseAndCreateOrReplace` (#919, #918): a load, or a script, the front failed after the emulator ran it is listed with the front's `errorResult`; the emulator lists it as it recorded it, `DONE` and succeeded (measured). A job the emulator itself failed is the next row (#934). Unit `TestFailedScriptWithAHandlerIs501`. |
| `jobs.get` and `jobs.list` of a job the emulator failed | **Verified** | `TestBigQueryFailedJobsReadBackFailed` (#934), official Go client (`Job.Status`, `Client.JobFromID`, `Client.Jobs`). Measured against the pinned emulator: it answered a failing query job's `jobs.insert` with `status.errorResult` ("Table not found") and then `jobs.get` of it with `DONE` and no error; and it answered a failing load 400, then `jobs.get` of it the same way, and listed it with no status. BigQuery keeps a job's `errorResult`. The front notes the failure in each `jobs.insert` answer, an `errorResult` or a 400 or 404 (not a 409, which is about an ID another job holds), and `jobs.get`, `jobs.getQueryResults` and `jobs.list` report it; a job that succeeds still reads back succeeded. The failures are kept in the front's memory, the last 1,000; the emulator keeps no state across a restart either. `jobs.query`, which answers a failure with an error and lists no job for it, is unchanged. Unit `TestFailedJobsReadBackFailed`. |
| Where the checks run | **Verified** from the host, from Cloud Run, and from a pod without Cloud Run | The front runs **in the emulator's pod** (#902), a second container from the locally built storage image (`cloudburrow-storage bigquery-front`, the image the in-cluster Cloud Storage server runs, so enabling BigQuery builds it too), and serves the `bigquery` Service's REST port, 9050; the emulator listens on 9051, which the Service does not publish. Every client reaches the emulator through it: the host's tunnel, which the SDKs, `bq` and the console use through `CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_ENDPOINT`, and any pod that dials `bigquery.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local:9050`, the address `env --format kubernetes` prints and Cloud Run revisions and job tasks are given, with or without Cloud Run; that traffic stays in the cluster, so it does not depend on pods reaching the host (#575). `TestAPodDialingTheBigQueryServiceGetsTheFrontWithoutCloudRun`, on an instance without Cloud Run (emulators shard): a one-off pod running the official Go client at that address gets 409 for a dataset that exists, 400 for a hyphenated dataset ID and for a column named twice, and an `invalid` insertErrors entry for a row missing its REQUIRED value, none of them stored, and port 9060 answers; the Service selects the pod, which runs the front. `TestCloudRunRevisionGetsBigQueryRefusalsThroughTheFront` and `TestCloudRunRevisionDialingTheBigQueryServiceGetsTheFront` (run shard): a revision at the injected endpoint, and one that dials the Service itself, get the same four refusals. Until #902 the front was in the host tunnel's guard, and with Cloud Run the Service was routed to it through `cloudburrow-host` (#874, #881); neither is needed now, and nothing BigQuery's is published to pods through the host. The front serves the Storage Read API's port (gRPC, 9060) too since #1032, and the emulator's moves to 9061, which the Service does not publish either (row *Storage Read API of a table whose ID another dataset has*; unit `TestBigQueryManifestPutsTheFrontOnTheServicePort`). **The emulator's own ports refuse every other pod** (#1114): it listens on its pod's loopback alone (`--host=127.0.0.1`), and Kubernetes asks its container's readiness of the front, which dials 9051 and 9061 on loopback (`/cloudburrow/backend-ready`, `internal/frontready`). `TestEmulatorsBehindAFrontRefuseOtherPods`: from another pod, 9051 and 9061 at the BigQuery pod's IP refuse the connection, and 9050 and 9060 accept it. Measured first with the emulator on `0.0.0.0` (its default): from another pod, `CreateReadSession` of a table in a project the instance does not have, sent to 9061, which the front refuses `NOT_FOUND`, crashed the emulator, and every dataset was gone. DML is not checked. |
| Dry run statement type | **Not reliable** | Measured (#698), over REST: a dry run reports `statementType` `SELECT` for a `DELETE` and for a `CREATE TABLE` alike, so it cannot tell a read from a write. |
| Queries through `jobs.insert` | **Leave a dataset behind** | Measured (#698), over REST: each inserted query job creates a dataset named after its job ID to hold the result, and it is listed with the user's own. `jobs.query`, which the Go client's `Query.Read` sends when nothing forces a job, leaves none (`TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetsSchemaAndQuery` checks the dataset list is unchanged after a console query). |
| Creation and modification times | **Wrong unit** | Measured (#698): the emulator writes them in seconds where the API specifies milliseconds, so through the official client a table created today reads back as January 1970. The console shows no times. |
| Emulator environment variable | **None exists** | No official client reads one. `cloudburrow env` exports `CLOUDBURROW_BIGQUERY_ENDPOINT`, which code passes to `option.WithEndpoint`, and `CLOUDSDK_API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDES_BIGQUERY`, which only bq reads, with the flags in the `bq` row; see configuration.md. |

### State snapshots

`cloudburrow state save <file>` and `state load <file>`, over `POST /admin/state/export` and
`/admin/state/import` on the loopback control port, with the instance's admin token (#553). The archive is a gzipped tar whose first entry
is a versioned `manifest.json`, naming every enabled service as captured or not, with the reason.
A service is captured **record for record from the store it keeps its state in**, so it comes back
exactly: a queue's retry configuration and state, a task's schedule and dispatch count, a disabled
secret version. This holds whichever backend the instance uses: memory, a durable file, or
Kubernetes Secrets for Secret Manager.

| Service | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Cloud Tasks | **Verified** | `TestStateSaveResetLoadRestoresEverything` (CLI, official SDKs, CI instance) and `TestStateRoundTripsTasksSecretsAndProjects`, record-identical in ephemeral and persistent modes |
| Secret Manager | **Verified** | Same tests: every version and payload restored. In CI the backend is Kubernetes Secrets. **The archive holds secret values in plain form**: the CLI warns, and writes it `0600`. |
| Project registry | **Verified** | Same tests: a project created through the console is restored |
| Cloud Scheduler | **Verified** | `TestStateRestoresSchedulerJobs` (CLI, `cloud.google.com/go/scheduler/apiv1`, CI instance): a paused job is saved, reset away and listed back with its schedule, zone, target and next run (#600). `TestStateSaveCapturesSchedulerJobs` checks the store is record-identical and a job created after the save is gone. A job whose next run passed while it was away fires once on the next tick, as after a restart. |
| Cloud Storage | **Verified** | `TestStateRestoresStorage` and `TestStateRestoresVersionsAndHolds`. The state is exported from the storage server's store directly (#511, `builtinStorageSnapshotter`), so a restore fires no notifications: every bucket with all its fields, soft-deleted buckets, every object version (live, noncurrent and soft-deleted) with its generation, metageneration, holds, retention and customTime, notification configurations and undelivered events, IAM policies, sessions and multipart uploads, and the bytes, streamed. A load verifies that every blob hashes to its content address and that every object version's CRC32C matches, before it replaces anything, in one transaction. **HMAC key secrets are excluded**, and the archive says so (`secretsExcluded`): a restored key keeps its metadata and cannot sign. `TestSnapshotRoundTripKeepsVersionsAndHolds`, `TestSnapshotExcludesHMACSecrets`, `TestSnapshotImportVerifiesBytes`, `TestBuiltinStorageSnapshotRoundTrip` (10 MiB, streamed, never held whole in memory). |
| Pub/Sub | **Verified: resources, not messages** | `TestPubSubStateSaveAndLoad` (CLI, official `pubsub/v2` clients, CI instance, #880) and unit `TestPubSubStateRoundTrip` (through the real front, against `pstest`). Google's emulator has no export, so `pubsubSnapshotter` reads through the front with the official client, for every project CloudBurrow knows and every project a call through the front has named: **every schema with every revision** in order, **every topic** as the front returns it (labels, retention, schema settings), **every subscription with every setting** (ack deadline, retention, retained acknowledged messages, filter, ordering, exactly-once, retry and dead-letter policies, expiration policy, labels, push endpoint and attributes; the real endpoint, never the relay's; the expiration policy and labels an update set, which the front keeps and the emulator refuses, #949: unit `TestPubSubStateKeepsUpdatedLabels`), **how long each subscription had been idle**, its expiration clock, and **every snapshot's name and topic**. A load clears those projects' subscriptions, snapshots, topics and schemas and creates the archive's; each subscription reads back as `ListSubscriptions` read it before the save, and one saved 30 hours into a 48-hour ttl is kept 17 hours after the load and gone 2 hours later. **Not kept:** messages (published and unacknowledged, retained after acknowledgement, or in a snapshot's backlog), ack IDs and leases: a loaded subscription starts empty. A loaded snapshot is taken again from a subscription of its topic, so it marks the moment of the load, with a new expire time. A schema revision gets a new revision ID, and a topic's schema settings are pointed at the new IDs. A subscription whose topic was deleted cannot be created again and is left out. The time between a save and a load does not count towards expiration. |
| Cloud Run | **Not captured** | Services are Knative objects in the cluster; redeploy them from their images |
| Cloud KMS | **Not captured** | Key material is not exported. Keys recreated from their definitions get new material, so ciphertext produced before a `state load` cannot be decrypted after it (`TestStateManifestSaysKMSIsNotCaptured`, #388) |
| Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) | **Verified** | `TestStateRestoresCloudSQL` (CLI, `pgx`, CI instance): a table's rows come back exactly after they were changed, and a database created since the save is gone. Saved with `pg_dump -Fc` for each application database and loaded with `pg_restore --exit-on-error`, both run **inside the server's pod** through the instance's kubeconfig (#311). Nothing is exposed beyond the existing tunnel. The archive holds the dumps and no kubeconfig or cluster credential (`TestPostgresSnapshotArchiveHoldsNoClusterCredential`, and checked on the real archive). Roles are not captured, because the server has only the one it was initialised with. `reset` does not touch PostgreSQL; a load drops and recreates every application database. |
| Cloud SQL for MySQL | **Not captured** | A real MySQL: use `mysqldump` |
| Memorystore | **Not captured** | A real Valkey: use its own `BGSAVE`, or the append-only file on its volume |
| Firestore, Datastore, Bigtable, Spanner, BigQuery | **Not captured** | In-memory emulators with no export |

**Load replaces.** Loading resets each captured service to exactly the archive's contents, in one
atomic commit per service, so nothing created since the save survives. **Refused before anything
is touched:** an archive whose manifest version is unknown (`TestARefusedArchiveLoadsNothing`),
one in another format, one that is not an archive, and one holding a service this instance does
not run.

**From the console** (#801): the **Instance** page's **Save state** downloads the archive `state
save` writes, through the same code (`exportStateArchive`), and **Load state** uploads one through
`state load`'s (`importStateArchive`), bounded by the console's upload limit in Settings. The
entries are byte-identical to what `state save` writes from the same instance, only the
manifest's `created` differing (`TestConsoleInstanceActsThroughTheAdminAPI`); against the CI
instance, a bucket, a secret and a queue saved, reset and loaded from the console are read back
by the official clients (`TestConsoleStateSaveResetLoadRestores`). See the Console table.

### Python client libraries

The Go column is the rest of this document. The Python column is **Verified only where a pytest
case in `test/compat-python` exists**, run against a live instance in compat CI with the official
clients pinned in `requirements.lock`. Anything not listed is not claimed for Python.

| Operation | Go | Python | Python evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Storage: bucket create / get / list / delete | Verified | **Verified** | `test_bucket_and_object_crud_with_a_resumable_upload` |
| Storage: object upload, download, list, delete | Verified | **Verified** | same |
| Storage: resumable upload | Verified | **Verified** | same (a 256 KiB chunk size forces the resumable protocol) |
| Pub/Sub: topic and subscription create / delete | Verified | **Verified** | `test_publish_and_pull_with_ack` |
| Pub/Sub: publish, pull, acknowledge, no redelivery after ack | Verified | **Verified** | same |
| Cloud Tasks: queue and task create / get / delete | Verified | **Verified** | `test_queue_and_task_create_get_delete` |
| Secret Manager: secret create, version add, access by number and `latest` | Verified | **Verified** | `test_secret_version_add_and_access` |
| Cloud KMS: key ring, key, encrypt and decrypt with CRC32C checks | Verified | **Verified** | `test_key_ring_key_encrypt_and_decrypt` (#592) |
| Firestore: document CRUD, a query and a transaction | Verified | **Verified** | `test_document_crud_query_and_transaction` (#592) |
| Datastore: entity CRUD, a query and a transaction | Verified | **Verified** | `test_entity_crud_query_and_transaction` (#592) |
| Bigtable: table, rows and a filtered read | Verified | **Verified** | `test_table_and_rows_with_a_filtered_read` (#592) |
| Spanner: instance, database, DDL, write and query | Verified | **Verified** | `test_instance_database_ddl_write_and_query` (#592) |
| BigQuery: dataset, table, insert and query | Verified | **Verified** | `test_dataset_table_insert_and_query` (#592) |
| Impersonated credentials driving the Storage client | Verified | **Verified** | `test_impersonated_token_drives_the_storage_client` (#592): the token comes from the local IAM Credentials endpoint; google-auth's Regional Access Boundary lookup ignores `iam_endpoint_override`, so the test pre-empts it and asserts the URL, to notice a release that honours the override |
| Every snippet in [examples/python.md](examples/python.md) | — | **Verified** | `test_examples.py` runs each block as written |

**Configuration.** Storage and Pub/Sub need nothing but `cloudburrow env`. The Python Storage
client needs the scheme in `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST`, which the Go client does not, and `env` exports
the form both accept. Firestore, Datastore, Bigtable and Spanner read their emulator variables,
which the loopback guard checks at session start; each of these tests skips where the instance
does not enable its service. Cloud KMS and BigQuery, like Cloud Tasks and Secret Manager, need an
explicit channel or endpoint. Cloud Tasks and Secret Manager need an explicit plaintext channel (see
[credentials.md](credentials.md#python-clients-for-cloud-tasks-and-secret-manager)).

**Guards.** The suite refuses to run unless `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` is the generated
fixture and default credentials resolve to it. Every connection through Python sockets must be
loopback, which covers the HTTP clients, and so must every gRPC channel target. A violation
fails the whole session even if the test that caused it caught the error. `test_guard.py` proves
both guards by running sessions that break them. gRPC connects from C, which a Python guard
cannot see, so for gRPC it is the channel target that is checked, not the socket.

### Node.js client libraries

Every row below is **Verified** by compat CI (#589): on 2026-09-27 the merge-group run of the #759
train (run 36329620881) passed all 16 Node tests in the `storage` shard and all 3 in the
`emulators` shard, with none skipped, against the same instance the Go and Python suites use. The
suite drives the official `@google-cloud/*` clients at the exact versions in `package-lock.json`
(storage 8.2.0, pubsub 6.1.0, secret-manager 7.1.0, tasks 7.2.0, firestore 9.2.0), installed with
integrity hashes by `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, and `make compat-node` runs it. Compat CI runs it
on Node 24.21.0: Storage, Pub/Sub, Cloud Tasks, Secret Manager, the guard tests and the examples in
the `storage` shard, Firestore in the `emulators` shard, and a shard fails if any test it runs
skips. Anything not listed is not claimed for Node.

| Operation | Go | Node | Node evidence (`test/compat-node`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Storage: bucket create / get / list / delete | Verified | **Verified** | `bucket and object CRUD with a resumable upload` |
| Storage: object upload, download, list, delete | Verified | **Verified** | same |
| Storage: resumable upload | Verified | **Verified** | same (a 256 KiB `chunkSize` forces the resumable protocol) |
| Storage: `mediaLink` names the emulator's host | Verified | **Verified** | `mediaLink names the emulator host` |
| Storage: `ifGenerationMatch: 0`, ranged download | Verified | **Verified** | `ifGenerationMatch 0 creates once, and a ranged read is inclusive` |
| Storage: V4 signed URL, tampered signature refused 403 | Verified | **Verified** | `a V4 signed URL reads the object, and a tampered one is 403` |
| Pub/Sub: topic and subscription create / delete | Verified | **Verified** | both Pub/Sub tests |
| Pub/Sub: publish, then streaming pull and ack | Verified | **Verified** | `publish, then receive and ack by streaming pull` |
| Pub/Sub: publish, unary pull, acknowledge, no redelivery after ack | Verified | **Verified** | `publish, pull and acknowledge, with no redelivery after the ack` |
| Cloud Tasks: queue and task create / get / delete, `NOT_FOUND` after delete | Verified | **Verified** | `queue and task create, get and delete` |
| Secret Manager: secret create, version add, access by number and `latest` | Verified | **Verified** | `secret create, version add, access by number and latest` |
| Firestore: document set / get / delete, a `where` query, a transaction | Verified | **Verified** | `Firestore document CRUD, a query and a transaction` |
| Firestore: batched write, `onSnapshot` listener (the Listen stream) | — | **Verified** | `Firestore batched write, then a snapshot listener sees it` |
| [examples/node.md](examples/node.md): every block, run as written | — | **Verified** | `examples.test.mjs` (`example: Cloud Storage` … `example: Firestore`) |
| Storage: `save()` / `createWriteStream()` with the client's default single-request resumable upload | — | **Not supported** | The client sends `Content-Range: bytes 0-*/*` and the builtin server answers 400 `Invalid Content-Range`. Found by the `example: Cloud Storage` block before it passed `resumable: false`; no test pins it. Pass `resumable: false`, or a `chunkSize` |

**Configuration.** Pub/Sub and Firestore need nothing but `cloudburrow env`: the clients read
`PUBSUB_EMULATOR_HOST` and `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` and open a plaintext channel, Firestore's
with the emulator's `owner` token as its only credential. **Storage does not work
with `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` as exported.** The Node client uses the variable verbatim as the
JSON API's base URL, so it would need `/storage/v1`. It also uses it as the upload endpoint, which
must not have that path. So unset the variable and pass its value as `apiEndpoint`, as the client's
own source advises: `new Storage({ apiEndpoint: 'http://127.0.0.1:<port>' })`. With a custom
endpoint the client sends no credentials. Cloud Tasks and Secret Manager read no emulator
variable. Pass the endpoint from `CLOUDBURROW_TASKS_ENDPOINT` or
`CLOUDBURROW_SECRETMANAGER_ENDPOINT` as `apiEndpoint` and `port`, with `sslCreds:
grpc.credentials.createInsecure()` (`grpc` from `google-gax`).

**Guards.** `guard.mjs` is loaded with `--import` into every process of the run. It refuses to
start unless `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` is unset or is the generated fixture, and it checks
that application default credentials resolve to that fixture (exit 3). Every TCP connection and
every DNS lookup must be loopback. Node's gRPC (`@grpc/grpc-js`) is JavaScript and connects
through `net` like the HTTP clients do, so, unlike the Python guard, this one sees the gRPC
sockets as well as the HTTP ones. A violation makes the process exit 4, and so fails the run, even
if the test that caused it caught the error. `guard.test.mjs` proves both guards by running
sessions that break them. Each service's tests skip when `cloudburrow env` exports no variable for
that service.

### Cloud SQL is not an emulator

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A real SQL server, locally | **Verified** | PostgreSQL 17.11 in the cluster, digest-pinned, reached with an ordinary driver. A plain `psql` client created a table and inserted rows with nothing of CloudBurrow's involved. |
| Durability | **Verified, measured** | The only opt-in backend with a volume, because it is the only one that is a real database rather than an in-memory emulator. CI's restart probe (#701): `TestCloudSQLRestartSetup` runs alone just before `stop` and leaves a table with a row. After `stop` and `up` in persistent mode, `TestCloudSQLAcrossRestart` reads the row back; brought up again with `--mode ephemeral`, the server has no volume and the table does not exist (`42P01`). |
| Connecting from `cloudburrow env` alone | **Verified** | `TestCloudSQLFromTheExportedPGVariables` (#584): pgx, given only the `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGDATABASE` and `PGSSLMODE` that `env` exported (every other `PG*` variable cleared), and again given `CLOUDBURROW_CLOUDSQL_URL`, connects and runs `SELECT 1`. The host port is fixed at `9019` (`--port-cloudsql`, moved by `--port-base`). |
| MySQL, locally (`cloudsql-mysql`) | **Verified** | `TestCloudSQLMySQLDataPlane` (#297): MySQL 8.4.11, digest-pinned, reached with `go-sql-driver/mysql` from the host and with the `mysql` client from a pod through `cloudsql-mysql.cloudburrow.svc.cluster.local:3306`; `cloudburrow reset` drops every non-system database. A generated per-instance password, exported by `env` and never printed by `status`. |
| MySQL durability | **Verified, measured** | CI reads back a row written before `stop` after `up` in persistent mode, and finds none after `up --mode ephemeral`. |
| MySQL in the console | **Verified** | Cloud SQL's MySQL page (#868): databases, tables, columns and indexes, an SQL editor that reads by default and writes as the application's user in an opt-in, confirmed Read-write mode (#995), and create and drop database, through the console API and in headless Chrome. See "Cloud SQL for MySQL from the console" under Console. |
| **The Cloud SQL Admin API** | **Not implemented** | No instances, connection names, IAM database authentication, backups, replicas or Auth Proxy path. There is no `sqladmin` endpoint. |
| Google-published component | **No** | The only one here that is not. Google publishes no Cloud SQL emulator — `gcloud emulators` ships firestore and spanner, and the sole Cloud SQL component is `cloud-sql-proxy`, which connects to a real instance in GCP. |

An application that talks SQL works. An application that calls the Cloud SQL Admin API does
not, and nothing here implies otherwise. See [cloudsql.md](cloudsql.md) and
[#121](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/issues/121).

### Memorystore is not an emulator

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A real Redis-compatible server, locally | **Verified** | `TestMemorystoreDataPlane` (#296), with `github.com/redis/go-redis/v9` and nothing of CloudBurrow's: SET/GET, MULTI/EXEC, PUBLISH/SUBSCRIBE and Lua EVAL against the host endpoint, and GET from a pod through `memorystore.cloudburrow.svc.cluster.local:6379`. Valkey 8.1.10, digest-pinned. The host address comes from `REDIS_HOST`/`REDIS_PORT` as `cloudburrow env` exports them. |
| Durability | **Verified, measured** | CI stops and starts the persistent compat instance and `TestMemorystoreAcrossRestart` reads back a key written before the stop; brought up again in `--mode ephemeral`, the same test finds no key. This evidence tolerates one retry: `TestMemorystoreAcrossRestart` is on CI's [retry-once list](#retried-once). Persistent mode is an append-only file on a PVC, fsynced every second, so up to a second of writes can be lost on a crash; ephemeral mode writes nothing to disk. |
| **The Memorystore admin API (`redis.googleapis.com`)** | **Not implemented** | No instances, no `gcloud redis instances create`, no AUTH strings, TLS, maintenance, replicas, export/import or IAM. There is no admin endpoint. |
| Console screen | **Not supported** | No screen, by decision (#698): there is no admin API to read, and a keyspace or `INFO` view would be the console's own Redis client rather than a Memorystore screen. Recorded in [console-parity.md](console-parity.md) §3; `TestEveryServiceHasAParityRow` requires the row. |
| Google-published component | **No** | Google publishes no Memorystore emulator. Valkey is the server Memorystore for Valkey runs; Memorystore for Redis runs Redis, with which Valkey is protocol-compatible. |
| Authentication | **None** | No password, as for Cloud SQL: nothing in CloudBurrow authenticates a request, and the host endpoint is loopback only. An application that sends `AUTH` will get an error. |

An application that talks RESP works. An application that calls the Memorystore admin API does
not, and nothing here implies otherwise. See [memorystore.md](memorystore.md) and
[#296](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/issues/296).

**Bigtable starts without the network.** Its emulator is in the pinned Cloud SDK emulators image
and is the container's command; nothing is installed when the container starts (#611, #604).

Not covered, and not claimed: instance and cluster administration for Bigtable, Firestore
indexes and security rules, Datastore composite indexes, Spanner dialects other than
GoogleSQL, and backup/restore for any of them.

### Host endpoints survive a backend restart

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Advertised host port after a pod restart | **Verified** | `TestHostEndpointSurvivesABackendRestart` restarts the pod and requires the printed address to both accept a connection **and carry a request**; this evidence tolerates one retry, since the test is on CI's [retry-once list](#retried-once). Each tunnel binds a pod the forwarder chooses itself: the newest one that is Ready and **not being deleted**. `kubectl port-forward svc/…` could bind a pod that was terminating but still Ready during a rollout, such as `up --mode ephemeral` replacing a persistent pod, and connections through it hung (#381). |

This was broken until the restart criterion exposed it: the forwarder started `kubectl
port-forward` once and watched nothing, so a crash, an OOM kill, an eviction or a rollout left
the advertised endpoint dead for the life of the instance, while the pod was `Running`, the
service existed, and the startup banner still printed the address. Worse than a refusal: the
port still **accepted connections** and carried nothing, so a client's `connect()` succeeded
and every request timed out.

The tunnel is supervised and re-established on the **same** host port — the address has
already been printed, may be in an application's configuration, and for storage is baked into
the backend's advertised download URL, so reconnecting elsewhere would be a different kind of
broken. `Restarts()` counts re-establishments, because surviving a restart and never noticing
one are different states.

**Why it still flaked in CI (#526), measured:** `kubectl port-forward` does **not** exit when
its pod is deleted while the tunnel is idle. It keeps listening, fails the *next* connection's
stream, and exits only then. A supervisor that watched only for the process exiting therefore
kept a dead tunnel until a client tripped over it, and on a loaded runner the relaunch that
followed could bind the Service, resolving to whichever pod kubectl found, Ready or not.
Three failed CI runs showed the shape: the emulator up within a second of the restart, every
forwarder reporting its process alive, and clients timing out for minutes reading a server
preface that never came. Now the supervisor **watches the bound pod** and replaces the tunnel
within seconds of the pod going, **binds only a Ready, non-terminating pod** when it
re-establishes (never the Service), and counts a tunnel as up only once a probe connection
through it is **carried**, not merely accepted, using kubectl's own stream errors as the
signal. Each exit and re-establishment is logged, so a diagnose bundle says what the tunnel
did. In-process: `TestATunnelWhosePodIsGoneIsReplacedWithoutAClient`,
`TestRelaunchWaitsForAReadyPodInsteadOfTheService`, `TestALaunchWhoseStreamFailsIsRetried`,
against a fake kubectl.

Optional, and neither implies any management-API parity. See
[functions-and-builds.md](functions-and-builds.md).

| Capability | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Functions Framework, `http` signature | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` asserts `POST /` returns the handler's JSON and a body-less request still returns 200, through the official Cloud Run v2 client and the Knative ingress. Verified by a dated CI run (2026-09-27, [run 36357529139](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/actions/runs/36357529139) of `functions.yml` on main, `ubuntu-latest` amd64 runner, pack 0.40.9). Gated by `CLOUDBURROW_TEST_FUNCTIONS`; no merge runs it ([What CI does not run](#what-ci-does-not-run-and-why)). |
| Functions Framework, `cloudevent` signature | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` sends a binary-mode Google-schema CloudEvent and asserts the handler logged its type, subject and data. Verified by the same dated CI run (2026-09-27, run 36357529139, amd64). Gated; no merge runs it. |
| CloudEvent error handling | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` asserts a request without `ce-*` headers returns HTTP 400. Verified by the same dated CI run (2026-09-27, run 36357529139, amd64). Gated; no merge runs it. |
| Google Buildpacks source build | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` builds `testdata/function` with the pinned builder through `internal/buildpacks` into a local registry, and the image is what the rows above deploy. Verified by the same dated CI run (2026-09-27, run 36357529139, amd64). Gated; no merge runs it. |
| Build cache reuse | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsRebuildReusesLayers` builds `testdata/function` twice to the same image in a local registry and asserts the first build reports no `Reusing layer '…'` line and the second, from unchanged source, at least one (the lifecycle's wording in the pinned builder's lifecycle 0.21.18). Verified by a dated run (2026-09-27, macOS/arm64 Docker Desktop, emulated amd64 builder, pack 0.40.9): the rebuild reused 6 layers (`google.go.functions-framework:functions-framework`, `google.go.build:bin`, and four `buildpacksio/lifecycle` layers). Passed again natively in a dated CI run (2026-09-27, [run 36357529139](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/actions/runs/36357529139) of `functions.yml` on main, `ubuntu-latest` amd64 runner, pack 0.40.9), the rebuild again reusing 6 layers. Gated; no CI shard runs it ([What CI does not run](#what-ci-does-not-run-and-why)). |
| Failed-build diagnostics | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsBuildBrokenModulePathFails` builds a copy of the fixture whose `go.mod` declares `brokenmodule/function` and asserts the error `errors.Is` `buildpacks.ErrBuildFailed`, starts `build failed: exit status`, and carries the Go Functions Framework buildpack's message: "the module path in the function's go.mod must contain a dot in the first path element…, found: brokenmodule/function". Verified by a dated run (2026-09-27, macOS/arm64 Docker Desktop, emulated amd64 builder, pack 0.40.9), and passed again in the dated CI run (2026-09-27, run 36357529139, amd64). Gated; no CI shard runs it ([What CI does not run](#what-ci-does-not-run-and-why)). |
| **Cloud Functions management API** | **Not supported** | No `projects.locations.functions` surface exists. A working handler does not imply one. |
| **Eventarc trigger management** | **Not supported** | Nothing creates or routes triggers. |
| Languages other than Go | Planned | Google publishes other runtimes; untested is untested. |
| Native amd64 build host | **Verified** | `TestFunctionsFrameworkBuiltWithBuildpacks` runs only when the cluster nodes are amd64, since the builder emits amd64 images; Verified by the dated CI run (2026-09-27, run 36357529139, `ubuntu-latest` amd64). Gated; no merge runs it. The emulated arm64 build was only tried by hand. |

**The builder is `linux/amd64` only.** On arm64 it runs under emulation and emits an amd64
image, which needs an amd64-capable node. CloudBurrow reports this rather than letting it
surface as a scheduling failure.

## Local AI

**Text generation works**, on a runtime CloudBurrow builds from Google's source. See
[local-ai.md](local-ai.md), including §4 — which corrects this page's previous conclusion.

**Not re-run in CI.** The model download and CPU inference are too heavy for per-PR CI, so the
Verified rows in this section and in Vertex AI generation come from runs on a developer machine
on 2026-09-21 (see [What CI does not run](#what-ci-does-not-run-and-why)).

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Model catalogue with verified provenance | **Verified** | Publisher recorded per artifact; a community conversion is never reported as Google-published. |
| Gated-artifact handling | **Verified** | Refused without credentials, with an actionable message. |
| Disk preflight, checksum verification, atomic cache, recovery | **Verified** | `internal/localai` |
| **Linux generation runtime** | **Verified** | `//runtime/engine:litert_lm_main` built from source at commit `02e5030` and run **from the shipped image**: **78.43 tok/s prefill, 31.98 tok/s decode, 0.39 s to first token** over a 144-token generation, CPU backend, warm cache. No LiteRT-LM release publishes a Linux artifact; Linux is nevertheless a documented, supported build target, and the gap is packaging rather than capability. |
| **Linux embedding runtime** | **Runs; no model to run** | `//runtime/engine:embedding_litert_lm_main` builds, ships in the runtime image, loads models and initialises its engine. Every obtainable model is then rejected — see [embeddings.md](embeddings.md). |
| **Text generation** | **Verified** | On `litert-community/gemma-4-E2B-it.litertlm` (2.59 GB, ungated). |
| **A Google-published runnable model** | **Not available** | Every one is `gated: manual`. The model that works is a **community** conversion and is labelled as one everywhere it appears. |
| **Embeddings** | **Blocked on the model export, not the runtime and not access** | The earlier statement — *every embedding artifact is gated* — was **wrong**: three are ungated. They fail for another reason. One ships with no tokenizer at all; rebuilt correctly with Google's own `litert-lm-builder` it reaches `Input tensor bytes must be 4 but got 2048`, as does an independent bundle from a different publisher. The conversions export a fixed `[1,512]` encoder signature and the engine requires a dynamic one. No flag bridges it. See [embeddings.md](embeddings.md). |
| **Embeddings (community ONNX int8 EmbeddingGemma)** | **Implemented; not measured** | Maintainer decision 2026-09-28 (#41): `embeddinggemma-300m-onnx-community`, onnx-community's ONNX int8 conversion of EmbeddingGemma — **NOT an official Google artifact**, and labelled so in the API response (`modelDisplayName`, `X-CloudBurrow-Model-Provenance`), the console and the catalogue. Pinned to `onnx-community/embeddinggemma-300m-ONNX` revision `5090578d`, each file by SHA-256, licence `gemma`, fetched over HTTPS by `up -local-ai-embeddings`. Runs on ONNX Runtime only in a `-tags onnx` build; the default build answers every `:predict` 501. No run against the real model is recorded yet: no dimensions, finite-value or retrieval-quality check has been measured. The official LiteRT artifacts remain blocked as described in [embeddings.md](embeddings.md). |
| Output quality | **Not claimed** | The measured run answered a factual question wrongly. That is a small quantised model's quality; CloudBurrow does not present model output as correct. |
| Benchmarks | **Measured, single sample** | One run on one machine (Apple M4 Max, arm64, CPU, container). Not a benchmark, and no comparison is drawn from them. Two conditions change them substantially: a **cold** XNNPACK cache raises init from 0.31 s to 3.27 s, and a **short** answer collapses decode speed (6.89 tok/s over two tokens on the same binary) because the first token's fixed cost is averaged over the generation. |
| Pinned artifact checksums | **Absent** | The Google artifacts are gated, so they could not be downloaded and hashed. The code supports pinning; the status reports the absence rather than implying verification. |
| Runtime distribution | **Published per release; not yet exercised by one** | Each release builds `deploy/litert-lm/Dockerfile` natively for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64, pushes `ghcr.io/cloudburrow/litert-lm:<tag>`, attests the index, and records its digest in the release notes. A release CLI defaults `--local-ai-image` to that digest and pulls it on first use; a checkout build defaults to `cloudburrow/litert-lm:local` from `make litert-lm`, never pulled (#602). No release has run this job yet, so no published digest exists. Locally, the Dockerfile builds it: **6m17s measured** from a cold cache on an M4 Max with 16 CPUs, once, producing a 274 MB image. Debian 13 is the base because Abseil needs C++20 `<source_location>`, which Debian 12's default clang 14 lacks. |
| Runtime image completeness | **Verified** | The image is run, not merely built. `litert_lm_main` links `libGemmaModelConstraintProvider.so` — shipped prebuilt upstream, found via a `RUNPATH` into Bazel's output tree — so a multi-stage build that copies only the binary produces an image that builds cleanly and fails on first run. The prebuilt libraries are staged and registered with `ldconfig`. |
| Catalogue artifact filenames | **Verified against the live API** | `TestCatalogArtifactsExistUpstream` (tag `upstream`) lists each repository and asserts the named file is in it, and HEADs the resolve URL for entries marked ungated. It was written after the community entry was found naming `gemma-4-E2B-it-int4.litertlm`, a filename inferred from the `google/` repositories' convention, which returns **404**. |

## Vertex AI generation (local)

A **subset** of `generateContent`, served by the runtime CloudBurrow builds. Not a Vertex
emulator and not a Gemini replica. See [generation.md](generation.md) for the exact surface.

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `generateContent` through the official SDK | **Verified** | `TestRealGenerationThroughTheOfficialSDK`, `google.golang.org/genai` v1.71.0 pointed at a local endpoint, real runtime, real model. |
| `streamGenerateContent` (SSE) | **Verified** | 12 events over 741 ms through the SDK's own stream iterator. A unit test additionally asserts the first event arrives *while generation is still running*, so streaming cannot regress into buffering. |
| Both SDK path shapes | **Verified** | Vertex `/v1beta1/projects/{p}/locations/{l}/publishers/google/models/{m}:verb` and Gemini-API `/v1beta/models/{m}:verb`, taken from what the SDK actually sends. |
| No silent model substitution | **Verified** | A request for another model returns 404 naming what is served; `modelVersion` reports what ran; aliases must be configured by hand. |
| Generation options | **None supported** | Every one is refused with a message naming it. `maxOutputTokens` is refused on measurement: the runtime accepts it and ignores it — 309 decoded tokens at limits of 8, 40 and unset. |
| Tools, safety settings, multimodal input, multi-turn, `countTokens` | **Refused** | Each returns an error rather than being ignored. `safetySettings` in particular: **no filtering happens here**, so accepting the field would imply it does. |
| Token accounting | **Absent** | `usageMetadata` is never emitted. Nothing here measures tokens. |
| Prompt/output separation | **Verified** | The runtime echoes the prompt before generating, and echoes a multi-line prompt across lines, so forwarding from the marker returned the caller's own prompt as generated text. The echo is now skipped by matching rather than counting, which stays correct if a future runtime stops echoing. Covered against a simulated runtime in both directions and against the real one by `TestRealMultiLinePromptIsNotEchoedBack`. |
| Cancellation | **Verified** | A cancelled request kills the runtime container by name. Found by testing: killing the `docker run` client left the container generating, because it is a client for work in the daemon and the runtime ignores `SIGTERM`. |
| Output quality, safety, or equivalence to Gemini | **Not claimed** | No test asserts what the model says. |
| Embeddings | **Blocked on the model export** | Not served. See [embeddings.md](embeddings.md): ungated artifacts do exist, and the runtime rejects them. |
| Text-embedding `:predict` subset | **Implemented; not measured** | `POST /v1` (and `/v1beta1`) `/projects/{p}/locations/{l}/publishers/google/models/embeddinggemma-300m-onnx-community:predict` only: `instances[].content`, `task_type` (EmbeddingGemma's prompt prefixes), `title`; `parameters.autoTruncate`, `outputDimensionality` (768/512/256/128, truncated and renormalised). Any other model is 404, never substituted. Response `predictions[].embeddings.values` and `statistics`. Unit-tested with a fake runtime (`TestPredictServesOnlyTheCommunityIdentity`); **not** run against the real model or an official SDK. A **community conversion, not an official Google artifact**. Console: Vertex AI → Embeddings. |

### Console playground

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Real inference from the browser | **Verified** | Driven through Chromium against the real runtime; 12 lines generated, streamed. |
| Streaming in the UI | **Verified** | Output grew in **6 separate steps over 1628–2209 ms**, so it is not one late write. |
| Uses the same API as the SDK | **Verified** | A relay to the identical Vertex path and body; asserted by test, including that no generation option is added. |
| Readiness | **Verified** | A live probe requiring a 404 for an unserved model — proves answering *and* routing without loading a model. |
| Model identity and provenance | **Verified** | Model, publisher and a notice that a community conversion is not a Gemini result, shown where the output appears. |
| Refused options listed | **Verified** | All thirteen, held in step with the API by `TestPlaygroundRefusalsMatchTheAPI`. |
| Cancellation | **Verified** | Stops the stream and the container; recorded as **Cancelled**, not Failed — a distinction only browser use exposed. |
| History | **In memory, bounded** | 20 entries, never written to storage, never leaves the browser. |
| Off by default | **Verified** | Not advertised, not navigable, and the rest of the console unaffected. |
| Model download/load/unload, embedding view | **Not implemented** | See [playground.md](playground.md) §7. Custom prediction has its own page; see [Vertex AI custom prediction](#vertex-ai-custom-prediction). |
| Pixel parity with Vertex reference screens | **Not claimed** | Structural only; no reference screenshots exist. |

## Vertex AI custom prediction

Google's **serving contract**, on CloudBurrow's **own runtime**. See
[prediction.md](prediction.md) for the decision and its reasoning.

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `AIP_HTTP_PORT` / `AIP_HEALTH_ROUTE` / `AIP_PREDICT_ROUTE` honoured | **Verified** | `TestPredictionEndpointServesTheVertexContract` deploys with **non-default** routes and asserts the defaults return 404, so the environment is proven plumbed through rather than coincidentally matching. Names checked against `google.cloud.aiplatform.constants.prediction` 2.1.3. |
| Health route reports readiness | **Verified** | Same test. Any 2xx is healthy, as Vertex defines it; a redirect is not. |
| `{"instances":[...]}` → `{"predictions":[...]}` | **Verified** | Same test, decoded through `internal/prediction` types rather than string matching. |
| One prediction per instance enforced | **Verified** | `prediction.ValidateResponse`; a mismatched count is a 500, not a mis-attributed result. |
| `parameters` passed to the container | **Verified** | `TestPredictionDeadlineBoundsASlowPrediction` drives the fixture's delay through it. |
| Malformed input refused | **Verified** | Empty instances, wrong instance type, unknown field and invalid JSON all return 400. |
| Caller deadline bounds a slow prediction | **Verified** | A 10s prediction fails a 2s caller, and the endpoint remains usable afterwards. |
| Endpoint deletion is complete | **Verified** | `TestPredictionEndpointDeletionIsComplete`; `GetService` then returns `NotFound`. |
| Startup failure reported with the container's output | **Verified** | `TestPredictionStartupFailureIsReported`. The adapter prefers Knative's `ConfigurationsReady` message over the top-level `does not have any ready Revision`. |
| **Startup-failure latency** | **Verified** | Knative declares a revision failed only after its `progress-deadline`, and Cloud Run v2 exposes no field that shortens it; with Knative's default `600s` the failure was reported after **602s, measured** (#568). `up` sets the deadline to `240s`, Cloud Run's default startup-probe budget, on install and on an already-installed cluster (`TestKnativeInstallSetsTheRevisionProgressDeadline`, `TestAnAlreadyInstalledKnativeGetsTheProgressDeadline`). Until then the endpoint reports `PENDING`. |
| `AIP_STORAGE_URI` artifact download | **Not supported** | Recognised as a contract variable; nothing fetches from GCS. The offline path downloads no artifacts and loads no credentials. |
| `LocalModel.deploy_to_local_endpoint` | **Declined by design** | It runs `containers.run(detach=True)` outside the cluster — unowned by readiness, reset or delete. Execution goes through the owned runtime instead. |
| `LocalModel.build_cpr_model` | Available, not invoked | CloudBurrow accepts any image honouring the contract; it does not build one for you. |
| Vertex model registry, `Endpoint`, `DeployedModel`, `PredictionService` | **Not supported** | No Vertex management surface is served at all, so a client fails to connect rather than receiving a stub. |
| Model Garden, tuning, batch prediction | **Not supported** | Out of scope. |
| Console: Online prediction | **Verified** | Vertex AI → Online prediction lists the Cloud Run services that set an `AIP_*` variable, read through `ListServices`, and sends `{"instances","parameters"}` to one's predict route through the cluster ingress; the container's status and body are shown verbatim, its own 400s included, and the console's refusals (not a JSON array, not deployed) are kept apart from them (`TestConsoleOnlinePredictionThroughTheConsoleAPI`, `TestCloudRunPredictorAnswersOnlinePredictionInTheBrowser`). Needs the ingress published on a host port; see [playground.md](playground.md) §8. |
| **GPU / accelerators** | **Not supported** | CPU only, on every platform. NVIDIA is untested and nothing configures it. On Apple Silicon a Linux container has no Metal device, so no configuration achieves it — **NVIDIA container flags do not establish Metal acceleration**. |

The host-advertised URI is the cluster ingress address and the Knative gateway is not
published on a host port, so a host-side caller reaches an endpoint through a port-forward
with the service in the `Host` header. In-cluster callers use the cluster-local name
directly. Both are documented in [prediction.md](prediction.md#reaching-the-endpoint).

## Credentials, metadata and external tooling

**CloudBurrow authenticates nothing.** These exist so that tools which insist on credentials
can run offline. See [credentials.md](credentials.md).

| Capability | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| IAM Credentials `generateAccessToken` for impersonation | **Verified** | `TestImpersonatedTokenUsedWithACloudBurrowClient` (#303): a `google.golang.org/api/impersonate` token source, served by the metadata server, drives an official Storage client against CloudBurrow. A transport guard fails any request not bound for loopback. **The token is local and not validated; no permission is checked.** Go's impersonate package takes no endpoint override, so its client needs a transport that routes `iamcredentials.googleapis.com` locally ([credentials.md](credentials.md#service-account-impersonation-iam-credentials)). |
| IAM Credentials `generateIdToken` | **Verified** | Unit-tested through the official `impersonate.IDTokenSource`: a JWT with the requested audience and the impersonated account's email, verified against the local `/certs`. It will **not** verify against Google's certificates. |
| IAM Credentials `signJwt` | **Implemented** | Signs the payload's claims with the instance key. Unit-tested over REST. |
| IAM Credentials `signBlob` | **Unimplemented** | UNIMPLEMENTED, tested: a local signature would make signed URLs that no Google service accepts. |
| `cloudburrow env` shell/JSON/plain export | **Verified** | `TestEnvExportsEveryClientVariable`, `TestEnvPrintsARunningInstancesLivePorts`; an instance that is not running is refused with nothing on stdout, `TestEnvRefusesAnInstanceThatIsNotRunning` (#630). Only the running instance's services are exported, `TestEnvExportsOnlyTheServicesARunningInstanceServes` (#652). Exports the client-library variables *and* the `gcloud` endpoint overrides, because `gcloud` ignores the former. |
| ADC fixture usable by Google's auth library | **Verified** | `TestOfficialAuthLibraryUsesTheLocalFixture`: `google.FindDefaultCredentials` mints a token through the fixture. The test fails if a `ya29.` token appears, which would mean it reached Google with the developer's real credentials. |
| Token exchange stays local | **Verified** | Same test. The fixture's `token_uri`, `auth_uri` and cert URLs all point at the instance. |
| Metadata server contract (`/`, project, zone, email, token, identity, scopes, aliases) | **Verified** | `TestMetadataServerAnswersTheContract` and `internal/metadata`. |
| `Metadata-Flavor: Google` required and echoed | **Verified** | Enforced even though the tokens grant nothing: a local server accepting what a real one rejects teaches code to fail in production. |
| ID token is a valid RS256 JWT | **Verified** | Signed with the instance key and verifiable against `/certs`. |
| **ID token verifies against Google's certificates** | **Not supported** | Google did not issue it. Tokens are structurally valid and self-consistent; signature verification against Google's public certs is **not** claimed. |
| **Token validation of any kind** | **Not supported** | No signature, expiry, audience, scope or assertion is ever checked. The token endpoint checks only that an assertion was *sent*, so a client silently sending none finds out. |
| **IAM, scopes, per-resource permissions** | **Not supported** | Every caller can do everything. |
| `cloudburrow gcloud-setup` / `gcloud-teardown` | **Verified where gcloud is installed** | `TestGcloudSetupConfiguration` (#305): through the configuration alone, `gcloud storage ls` and `gcloud pubsub topics list` reach CloudBurrow. The default configuration is unchanged, and teardown removes the configuration and is safe to repeat. Overrides written: `storage`, `pubsub`, `cloudkms`, `secretmanager`, `cloudtasks`, `cloudscheduler`, `logging` and `cloudresourcemanager` (`cloudscheduler` and `logging` since #591; `cloudresourcemanager` since #682, written whatever `--services` selects); `gcloud secrets`, `gcloud tasks` (queues and tasks), `gcloud scheduler` and `gcloud logging` are driven by `TestGcloudSecrets`, `TestGcloudTasks`, `TestGcloudScheduler` and `TestGcloudLogging`; `gcloud projects` by `TestGcloudProjectsThroughGcloudSetup`. |
| `gcloud storage ls` | **Verified** | With `CLOUDSDK_API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDES_STORAGE` and a local token. |
| `gcloud pubsub topics create` / `list` | **Verified** | With `CLOUDSDK_API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDES_PUBSUB`. |
| **`gcloud` honouring `*_EMULATOR_HOST`** | **Not supported (upstream)** | `gcloud` ignores them and goes to the real service, which **leaves the machine**. Inherited limitation, recorded rather than worked around; `cloudburrow env` exports the overrides too. |
| Terraform `google` provider create / read / destroy | **Verified** | `TestTerraformAppliesAndDestroysThroughTheWrapper`: `google_storage_bucket`, `google_pubsub_topic`, `google_secret_manager_secret`, `google_secret_manager_secret_iam_member` (#365), `google_cloud_tasks_queue` and `google_cloud_tasks_queue_iam_member` (#366; IAM stored, not enforced) applied through `cloudburrow terraform` with `hashicorp/google` 8.4.0 on Terraform 1.16.4 (the exact version the modules require, installed only if it matches the hashes in the committed `test/compat/testdata/terraform/.terraform.lock.hcl`, #694), read back through the official SDKs, destroyed, and confirmed gone. A second `plan` after the apply is clean for the Secret Manager and Cloud Tasks resources. **The queue is then changed in place** (#692): adding `retry_config { max_attempts = 7 }` and `rate_limits { max_concurrent_dispatches = 3 }` applies as a PATCH, the official client reads both back, and the next plan is clean. **A bucket plans clean too** (#515), with no `-target`. (Before the cut-over, #519, fake-gcs-server forced a replacement by reporting `location` as `US-CENTRAL1` for a bucket created in `US`, measured on #515; that was #374.) Also verified, apply, clean plan, and destroy: `TestTerraformStorageResources` covers a bucket with versioning, labels, a lifecycle rule, CORS, `retention_policy` and `soft_delete_policy`; a `google_storage_bucket_object` with `temporary_hold` and `event_based_hold` on a bucket with `enable_object_retention` (a held object refuses deletion; releasing the holds is an update that restates the object's `storageClass`, which is accepted when unchanged); `google_storage_bucket_iam_member` (stored, not enforced); `google_storage_hmac_key`; and `google_storage_notification` to a `google_pubsub_topic`. `TestTerraformForceDestroyVersionedBucket` shows `force_destroy` removing a bucket with noncurrent versions. `TestTerraformRun` (#591) applies, plans clean, updates and destroys a `google_cloud_run_v2_service` and a `google_cloud_run_v2_job` (see Cloud Run). `TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription` (#591) applies a `google_pubsub_topic`, a `google_pubsub_subscription` and a `google_cloud_scheduler_job`, plans clean, updates the job, plans clean and destroys them (see Cloud Scheduler). **A topic's and a subscription's labels are changed in place** (#949), together with the subscription's ack deadline, read back by the official client, and the next plan is clean: the emulator refuses a `labels` mask, and CloudBurrow's front applies it. Since #928 the front reads the provider's URL mask (`updateMask=ackDeadlineSeconds,labels,bigqueryConfig`, camelCase), keeps the labels, and sends the emulator the other paths in the body, snake_case, so every other change applies too: `TestTerraformPubSubPushAndExpiration` applies a subscription with `push_config` and `expiration_policy`, plans clean, and changes the ttl and the push endpoint in place (see Pub/Sub, CloudBurrow's front). **OpenTofu 1.12.6 too**, through `cloudburrow terraform --binary tofu` (#719): `TestTofuAppliesAndDestroysThroughTheWrapper` applies the first test's module, reads it back, plans clean and destroys it, and `TestTofuSchedulerAndSubscription` runs the scheduler module, both in CI's storage shard, which installs the release checked against its pinned SHA-256. OpenTofu resolves `hashicorp/google` from `registry.opentofu.org`; `tofu init` downloads it as `terraform init` does, and apply, plan and destroy run with egress blocked. The wrapper sets `storage_custom_endpoint`, `pubsub_custom_endpoint`, `secret_manager_custom_endpoint`, `cloud_tasks_custom_endpoint`, `cloud_run_v2_custom_endpoint`, `kms_custom_endpoint`, `cloud_scheduler_custom_endpoint`, the Resource Manager and Billing endpoints, `project` and a fixture `access_token`. `terraform init` still downloads the provider once. |
| Terraform, other services' endpoints | **Not set** | `cloudburrow terraform` sets an endpoint only for a service with a Verified row here, and names the enabled services it left out: their resources would otherwise be sent to real Google. Cloud KMS is wired (#425): `kms_custom_endpoint` is set, and `TestTerraformKMS` applies, plans clean, updates and destroys a key ring, a key and their IAM members over the JSON API. |
| Terraform resources beyond Storage, Pub/Sub topics and subscriptions, Secret Manager, Cloud Tasks queues, Cloud KMS (`TestTerraformKMS`), Cloud Run services and jobs (`TestTerraformRun`) and Cloud Scheduler jobs (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`) | Planned | Untested is untested. |
| Metadata server and the CLI-hosted APIs reachable from inside the cluster | **Verified** | With Cloud Run enabled, `up` publishes them as `cloudburrow-host.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local:<port>` (#575). On Docker Desktop that is through `host.docker.internal`; on Docker Engine on Linux it is through a relay on the kind gateway. Admin is never published. `TestAPodReachesTheCLIHostedServices` covers Secret Manager and Cloud Tasks from a pod through the official clients on CI's Docker Engine. `TestCloudRunRevisionUsesInjectedMetadataADCAndServedEndpoints` (#681) covers the metadata server (a token through the official auth library), KMS, Scheduler and Logging from a Cloud Run revision, at the addresses the adapter injected. Unit tests cover both runtimes' discovery and the relay. See [networking.md](networking.md#reaching-your-machine-from-a-pod). |

## Cloud Scheduler — `google.cloud.scheduler.v1`

**Built, not reused** (#302). Google publishes no Cloud Scheduler emulator. It runs in the CLI
process, as Cloud Tasks does, and is opt-in: `--services scheduler`, on `--port-scheduler`
(default `9008`). `env` exports `CLOUDBURROW_SCHEDULER_ENDPOINT`. No client library reads it, so
pass it to `option.WithEndpoint`. Per-method status is generated in
[coverage/scheduler.md](coverage/scheduler.md).

| Claim | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `CreateJob`, `GetJob`, `ListJobs`, `PauseJob`, `ResumeJob`, `RunJob`, `DeleteJob` | **Verified** | `TestSchedulerHTTPAndPubSubJobs`, with `cloud.google.com/go/scheduler/apiv1` against the CI instance. |
| `RunJob` delivers at once to an HTTP target | **Verified** | The same test, against an `httptest` server. Deliveries carry `X-CloudScheduler`, `X-CloudScheduler-JobName` and `X-CloudScheduler-ScheduleTime`, and `User-Agent: Google-Cloud-Scheduler`. A 2xx response is success. HTTP targets are called from the host, so they must be reachable from it. |
| Pub/Sub targets | **Verified** | The same test: the job's data and attributes reach a subscription on the local emulator, published through the official client. A job whose topic does not exist fails its attempt, as on the real service. |
| Cron schedules in IANA time zones | **Verified** | Unit-tested through the official client with a fake clock. Unix-cron with five fields, ranges, steps and names. Zones come from Go's embedded tzdata, so they work on hosts with no zoneinfo. Invalid expressions and unknown zones are INVALID_ARGUMENT. |
| Paused jobs do not fire; resumed ones do | **Verified** | Unit-tested through the official client with a fake clock. Resuming restarts from the next slot; runs missed while paused are not made up. |
| Retries | **Verified** | Unit-tested: `retry_count` retries on the Cloud Tasks backoff schedule, fed from `min_backoff_duration`, `max_backoff_duration` and `max_doublings`, and bounded by `max_retry_duration`. The last attempt's outcome is in `status` and `last_attempt_time`. |
| `UpdateJob` | **Verified** | Unit-tested, and applied by Terraform's provider (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`) and gcloud (`TestGcloudScheduler`) over JSON. An empty mask replaces every settable field; the mask paths are `description`, `schedule`, `time_zone`, the target, `retry_config` and `attempt_deadline`. Over JSON the mask is lowerCamelCase (`timeZone`), converted by the transcoder. **Refused as the contract refuses** (#795), on `CreateJob` and `UpdateJob`, each `INVALID_ARGUMENT`: a `time_zone` of `Local` (Go's name for the host's zone, not a tz database name), a `schedule` with a `TZ=` or `CRON_TZ=` prefix (the cron parser's extension, not unix-cron, which overrode `time_zone`; one with no space after it panicked in the parser), and an `http_target.body` on a method other than POST, PUT or PATCH ("It is an error to set body on a job with an incompatible HttpMethod"). Before, each was accepted (unit `TestSchedulerRefusesWhatTheContractRefuses`). An invalid cron expression and an unknown time zone were refused already. From the console: `TestConsoleSchedulerEditJob`. |
| `ResumeJob` on an enabled job | **Verified** | Succeeds and changes nothing (#591): the Terraform provider resumes every job it creates unpaused, and `TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription` reads such a job back ENABLED after apply. It was FAILED_PRECONDITION here, which failed every such apply. That Google accepts it is inferred from the provider, not measured against Google. |
| `retry_config` of a job given none | **Implemented** | Not returned, though the job retries by the defaults (#591): the provider plans clean against Google for such a job, which it could not if the defaults came back, and here they did, so every plan wanted to remove them. Inferred from the provider, not measured against Google. A job given a `retry_config` returns it, with the defaults filled in. |
| JSON API (REST) | **Verified** | **One port serves gRPC and JSON** (#591), as `cloudscheduler.googleapis.com` does: the shared `Transcoder` serves Google's `google.api.http` bindings of `google.cloud.scheduler.v1`, and the Locations bindings of `cloudscheduler_v1.yaml`, with the same `GRPCServer` gRPC registers. Every CloudScheduler method is transcoded; Locations answer **501 UNIMPLEMENTED**, and a path Google does not bind 404. The query policy is KMS's: an unknown parameter is INVALID_ARGUMENT. The official client's REST transport drives a job's lifecycle (unit `TestRESTJobLifecycleWithTheOfficialRESTClient`, `TestRESTWire`). Terraform (`TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`) and gcloud (`TestGcloudScheduler`) use it. JSON requests appear in `/admin/events`; fault injection and the gRPC request log are not interposed on them. |
| Terraform `google_cloud_scheduler_job` | **Verified** | `TestTerraformSchedulerAndSubscription`, with egress blocked: a Pub/Sub job applies (a create, then the provider's `:resume`), plans clean, takes a change to its description and schedule (a PATCH) and `paused = true` (a `:pause`), plans clean and is destroyed, each step read back with the official gRPC client. The wrapper sets `cloud_scheduler_custom_endpoint`. |
| `gcloud scheduler jobs` | **Verified where gcloud is installed** | `TestGcloudScheduler`, through `gcloud-setup`'s `cloudscheduler` override: `create http`, `create pubsub`, `list`, `describe`, `update http`, `pause`, `resume`, `run` and `delete`, read back with the official gRPC client. **Pass `--location`**: without it gcloud looks for an App Engine app's region, which is not served, and fails. |
| App Engine targets | **Unimplemented** | UNIMPLEMENTED, tested: there is no App Engine locally. |
| OAuth and OIDC tokens on HTTP targets | **Unimplemented** | UNIMPLEMENTED, tested. CloudBurrow mints no tokens, and a target that checked one would receive nothing it could verify. |
| Durability | **Verified, measured** | Jobs follow `--mode`: a store under the state directory in persistent mode, memory in ephemeral mode. `/admin/reset` clears them.
