An ACTIVE key's row offers **Deactivate** and no **Delete**, and a delete sent to the route anyway is refused and leaves it ACTIVE; **Deactivate** (`hmacKeys.update`) makes the client read INACTIVE, the row then offers **Activate** and **Delete**, and **Delete** (`hmacKeys.delete`) makes it read DELETED. In headless Chrome, `TestStorageHMACKeySecretShownOnce` finds the service account on the page and Settings beside the bucket browser in the product's pages; **Cancel** on Create key sends nothing; the create shows the secret once, with **Copy** and the note that it will not be shown again, in a dialog a stray backdrop click does not close; after **Done**, and again after a reload, neither the page (the table, the Activity panel, the notifications), the URL nor the browser's storage holds it, and the ACTIVE key's menu offers Deactivate and not Delete. Unit: `TestStorageSettingsHMACKeysThroughTheConsole` (against the in-process storage server, with the Request Log fed by its calls), `TestOneTimeValueIsInTheCreateResponseOnly`. |
| Bucket settings, retention lock, soft-deleted restore and managed folders | **Verified** | `TestConsoleStorageBucketSettings` (#789), in the storage shard, drives each through the console's routes as its forms and buttons submit them and reads the result back through the official storage client. **Edit bucket** (`buckets.patch` on the JSON API, against the metageneration just read): labels, default storage class NEARLINE, versioning, a 60-second retention period, a lifecycle Delete rule at age 30 and a CORS configuration are what `BucketHandle.Attrs` reads; a label removed in the form is gone; a soft delete retention of 5 seconds is refused with the API's "between 604800 (7 days) and 7776000 (90 days)" message. **Lock retention policy** (`LockRetentionPolicy` with the metageneration the API requires) reads `IsLocked` through the client; the page then no longer offers it and the route refuses a second one as not available, and removing the locked policy is refused with the API's "cannot be removed". The server's `lockRetentionPolicy` does not check for a lock already in place, and Google's answer to a repeat lock is not recorded (#829), so the refusal of a second lock is the console's, not the API's. A soft-deleted object listed on its bucket's **Deleted objects** tab and restored there (`ObjectHandle.Restore`) is readable again with its bytes; a deleted bucket listed on the **Deleted buckets** page and restored there (`buckets.restore`, through Google's generated JSON API client) is readable again. Managed folders are listed from `managedFolders.list` and shown typed Managed folder (`TestStorageBrowserMarksManagedFolders`, against a server answering as the API documents); their create and delete are the next row. In headless Chrome, `TestStorageBucketSettingsThroughTheForm` opens Edit bucket prefilled with STANDARD and 604800 and the location disabled, cancels a change with nothing sent, sees 5 seconds refused on the form with the API's message, saves a label, a retention period and a lifecycle rule that the console API then reads, and locks the policy through a confirmation that says it is permanent and asks for the bucket's name (Cancel there sends nothing). Unit: `TestStorageBucketSettingsThroughTheAPI`, `TestStorageSoftDeletedRestoreThroughTheAPI`, `TestStorageBrowserListsManagedFoldersFromTheServer`. |
| Managed folders: create and delete | **Verified** | `TestConsoleStorageManagedFolders` (#828), in the storage shard: a managed folder made through Google's generated JSON API client appears in the bucket browser typed Managed folder, with **Delete managed folder** on its row; **Create managed folder**, submitted on a folder's page as its form submits it, makes one under that prefix that `managedFolders.get` reads; the row's delete (`managedFolders.delete` with `allowNonEmpty`) removes it and keeps the object under it, and the folder is then plain again; a second delete is refused as not offered; in a bucket without uniform bucket-level access the create is refused with the API's message. Edit bucket's **Uniform bucket-level access** checkbox turns the setting on, sent only when it changes. In headless Chrome, `TestStorageManagedFoldersThroughTheBrowser` turns it on, creates a managed folder from the bucket's page, sees it listed, cancels the delete's confirmation (which says the objects under it are kept) with nothing sent, and deletes it by typing its name. Unit: `TestStorageBrowserCreatesAndDeletesManagedFolders`. |
| Bucket Pub/Sub notifications | **Verified** | `TestConsoleStorageNotifications` (#791), in the storage shard: **Create notification** on a bucket's page, submitted through the console's route with the values its form sends (the topic picked from the project's topics, `OBJECT_FINALIZE`, prefix `watched/`, a custom attribute), is the one configuration the official Storage client's `BucketHandle.Notifications` lists, with that topic, event type, prefix, attribute and `JSON_API_V1`. The bucket's Notifications tab lists it, and its page reads it back from `notifications.get`. An object uploaded afterwards with the official Storage client under the prefix produces a message the official Pub/Sub client pulls from a subscription on the topic, with the event type, bucket, object, payload format, custom attribute and configuration named; the first message pulled is that upload's, not the one made just before it outside the prefix. **Delete** from its row leaves the client's list empty. The topic is a picker of the project's topics, and Create notification is offered only where it can succeed: against a storage server with no Pub/Sub, the tab carries the server's own 501 message and the route refuses the create (`TestStorageNotificationsWithoutPubSub`); a project with no topic is told to create one, and a create against a server that refuses it shows the server's 501 on the form (`TestStorageNotificationRefusalsAreTheServers`). In headless Chrome, `TestStorageNotificationsThroughTheForm` opens the form with the project's one topic picked and `JSON_API_V1`, cancels a change with nothing sent, saves a notification that the console API then lists, and deletes it from its row after typing its name. Unit: `TestStorageNotificationsThroughTheAPI`, against the in-process storage server delivering to a `pstest` Pub/Sub. |
| Object versions, holds, object retention and Run lifecycle now | **Verified** | `TestConsoleStorageObjectVersionsHoldsAndRetention` (#853), in the storage shard, on a bucket the official client made with versioning and object retention enabled (`SetObjectRetention`), each driven through the console's routes as its toggle, forms and buttons submit and read back through the official storage client. **Show versions** (`GET /api/objects/storage/versions`, `objects.list` with `versions=true`) lists both generations of an object written twice, with the generations the client reports, one Live and one Noncurrent; a generation's page, `_details/<bucket>/<generation>/<object>`, says which. **Restore as live version** (`objects.rewrite` through the client's `Copier`, the generation as its source, against the live generation just read) makes the noncurrent bytes live as a new generation and keeps the one it replaced; **Delete version** (`objects.delete` with the generation) removes that generation alone. **Edit holds and custom time** (`objects.patch` against the metageneration just read) sets a temporary hold, after which the client's delete is refused, and a custom time the client reads; emptying the custom time is refused with the API's message (it cannot be removed). **Edit object retention** sets an Unlocked configuration the client reads; shortening it is refused 403 naming `overrideUnlockedRetention`, and made with **Override unlocked retention** checked, which also removes it. **Run lifecycle now** is labelled a CloudBurrow extension: it is `POST /_cloudburrow/lifecycle`, which runs every bucket's rules, and its result names the versions deleted; the object a Delete rule matched is no longer live and the others are untouched. In process against the built-in server, `TestStorageObjectVersionsListOpenRestoreDelete`, `TestStorageObjectHoldsAndRetention` and `TestStorageRunLifecycleNow`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageObjectVersionsThroughTheBrowser` (every surface but Edit object retention, since the console's Create form cannot make a bucket with object retention). |
| Pub/Sub actions: create subscription, publish, pull | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubActions` (#294), on a topic's page, each through the official `pubsub/v2` client against the emulator. **Create subscription** takes an ID, a push endpoint (empty for pull) and an ack deadline, and the SDK reads back what was set. **Publish** takes a body, attributes and an ordering key, and an SDK subscriber receives the same data and attributes. **Pull and ack** leaves nothing for the next SDK pull. **Pull without ack** sets the ack deadline to 0, so the SDK is redelivered the message; its copy says so and that delivery attempts go up, because Pub/Sub has no peek. Pulled messages are shown in the action's dialog and are never written to the operations ledger or the logs; the ledger records that each action ran. Push subscriptions cannot be pulled and say where their messages go. A delivery attempt count is shown only where Pub/Sub keeps one, on a subscription with a dead-letter policy. |
| Pub/Sub action: delete a subscription from its topic's page | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubDeleteSubscription` (#595): each subscription row on a topic's page offers **Delete**, confirmed by typing the subscription's name, and performed at the path `[topic, subscription]` through the official `pubsub/v2` client's `DeleteSubscription`. After a console delete of a subscription the SDK created, the SDK's `GetSubscription` answers `NOT_FOUND` and the topic page no longer lists it; the ledger records the delete against the row's path. A subscription of another topic or another project is refused through the page. |
| Pub/Sub Subscriptions screen: list, detail, delete | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubSubscriptionsScreen` (#595), at `/pubsub/subscriptions`, each through the official `pubsub/v2` client against the emulator. A subscription the SDK created with a push endpoint and a dead-letter policy is listed at `/api/resources/pubsub-subscriptions` by `ListSubscriptions`, with its topic, delivery type (pull, push, BigQuery, Cloud Storage or Bigtable, from the configuration the emulator returns) and ack deadline; its page shows the push endpoint and the dead-letter topic and maximum delivery attempts; deleting it from the screen, confirmed by typing its name, makes the SDK's `GetSubscription` answer `NOT_FOUND`, and the ledger records the delete. A subscription whose topic was deleted is listed as such and can be deleted; another project's subscription cannot be opened or deleted (unit `TestTheSubscriptionsScreenListsOpensAndDeletes`, against `pstest`). There is no create form on this screen: a subscription is created from its topic's page. **Idle time and expiration** (#996): a subscription's page has an Expiration group with its period, how long it has been idle, and when expiration deletes it, read from CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front (its activity records, the Subscription expiration row), not from any Pub/Sub API: `TestConsolePubSubIdleTimeAndPushAttributes` advances the front's clock two hours and the page shows a subscription idle for at least that, with deletion its period after its last activity by the front's clock, because the page's `GetSubscription` is not activity to the front (#1039), which its record then shows (unit `TestGetSubscriptionIsNotActivity`; unit `TestPubSubSubscriptionPageShowsIdleTimeAndExpiry`, through the real front: an advanced clock is named, a policy without a period never deletes it, and a Pub/Sub without the front says the idle time is not known). In headless Chrome, `TestPubSubIdleTimeAndPushAttributesThroughTheForms` sees the group on the page. In headless Chrome, `TestSubscriptionsDeleteConfirmedByName` (#700) deletes one from its row's menu: focus moves into the confirmation's name field, the short name is refused on the form with nothing sent, and the full name sends one DELETE, after which the row is gone, the console API no longer lists the subscription and its topic is still listed. |
| Pub/Sub: snapshots and Seek from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubSnapshotsAndSeek` (#787), through the official `pubsub/v2` client against the emulator, read back by it. **Create snapshot** on a subscription's page makes a snapshot the client's `ListSnapshots` returns, of the subscription's topic, and it is listed on the Snapshots screen (`/pubsub/snapshots`, from `ListSnapshots`) and in its topic page's Snapshots section (`ListTopicSnapshots`). **Seek to snapshot** from the console, after the messages were acknowledged, makes the client's pull receive them again; **Seek to time**, to a time after them, leaves the client's pull empty. A Seek to another topic's snapshot is refused with exactly the client's own `FAILED_PRECONDITION` message. A snapshot deleted from the Snapshots screen is `NOT_FOUND` through the client's `GetSnapshot`. **Not offered:** labels on Create snapshot (the emulator drops them, asserted by the same test) and any snapshot edit (`UpdateSnapshot` is `UNIMPLEMENTED`, asserted by the same test and quoted on the snapshot's page). Unit `TestASubscriptionPageCreatesSnapshotsAndSeeks` and `TestTheSnapshotsScreenListsOpensAndDeletes`, against `pstest` with snapshots added, since `pstest` serves none. In headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSnapshotSeekAndDeleteThroughTheForms` (`test/browser`, storage shard): Cancel on Create snapshot sends nothing; a snapshot created through the form makes Seek to snapshot appear prefilled; the seek asks for the subscription's name back, refuses the short name and a cancelled confirmation with nothing sent, and sends one seek for the full name; the snapshot's row deletes it, confirmed by its name. |
| Pub/Sub: edit a topic and a subscription from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription` (#786), through the official `pubsub/v2` client against the emulator, read back by it. **Edit topic** on a topic's page changes its message retention, the one field the emulator's `UpdateTopic` applies. **Edit subscription** on a subscription's page changes its ack deadline, message retention, retain acknowledged messages, retry policy and dead-letter policy, and switches it from pull to push and back through `push_config`; `GetSubscription` reads each, and only the changed fields are in the mask (unit `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionThroughUpdateSubscription`, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionSendsOnlyChangesAndKeepsWhatItDoesNotShow`, `TestPubSubEditTopicThroughUpdateTopic`, against `pstest`). A value the emulator refuses, a topic retention of 5m or an ack deadline of 601, is refused with exactly the message the official client's own call receives, and changes nothing. **Labels are edited on both forms** (#949), applied by CloudBurrow's front, and held to Google's label rules on the form (#962, the Label rules row): unit `TestPubSubFormsHoldLabelsToGoogleRules` refuses an uppercase key before `UpdateTopic` or `UpdateSubscription` is sent, naming it, and in headless Chrome `TestPubSubLabelsAreCheckedOnTheForms` sees a value with a space refused on the open Edit topic form, naming the line and the value, with nothing sent. The filter is shown disabled with the emulator's refusal of a change to it; the topic and message ordering are shown disabled too. **The expiration period is editable** (#891), applied by CloudBurrow's front: the same test changes it to 3d from the form, read back by `GetSubscription`, and a period of 12h is refused with exactly the message the official client's own update receives; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionExpirationThroughTheForm` sees it prefilled with 31d, refused at 12h on the open form, and saved as 10d and as never. **Exactly-once delivery is editable** (#880): the same test turns it on and off from the form, read back by `GetSubscription`, and a change that would give an exactly-once subscription a push endpoint is refused on the form, since Google does not offer the pair; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionExactlyOnceThroughTheForm` does the same through the checkbox. A topic's retention, once set, is not emptied, since the emulator saves that as 31 days. `ModifyPushConfig` is not what the console calls. In headless Chrome, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionThroughTheForm` (`test/browser`, storage shard) opens the dialog prefilled and inside the window, finds that Cancel discards a change without asking or sending, sees an ack deadline of 601 refused on the form with the emulator's message, and saves a push endpoint that the console API then reads. |
| Pub/Sub Schemas screen: create, commit, roll back, delete, test a message | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubSchemas` (#788), at `/pubsub/schemas`, through the official `apiv1.SchemaClient` against the emulator, read back by it. A schema created on the screen is returned by the client's `GetSchema` and `ListSchemas`; **Commit revision** makes the client's latest revision the committed definition; **Roll back to this revision** makes the chosen revision's definition the latest, as a new revision; **Delete revision** removes it from `ListSchemaRevisions`; **Delete schema**, on its page or its row, leaves `GetSchema` `NOT_FOUND`. A malformed definition, on create or commit, is refused with exactly the message the client's own `ValidateSchema` receives, and nothing is created or committed; **Test message** answers Valid for a conforming message and refuses a non-conforming one with exactly the client's `ValidateMessage` message. A topic created with schema settings shows its schema and encoding on its page, and after the schema's delete, the `_deleted-schema_` the client's `GetTopic` reports. **Not offered:** Protocol Buffer schemas (`UNIMPLEMENTED`), Rollback on the latest revision, and Delete revision on the only one (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`), each asserted by the same test. Unit `TestTheSchemasScreenCreatesCommitsRollsBackAndDeletes` and `TestSchemaFormsShowTheAPIsRefusal`, against `pstest`. In headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSchemaCreateShowsTheValidationError` (`test/browser`, storage shard): Cancel sends nothing, an invalid definition is refused on the open form with `ValidateSchema`'s message, the corrected form creates the schema, and its row's Delete is confirmed by its name. |
| Pub/Sub: create options from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions` (#852), through the official client against the emulator: **Create topic** with a message retention and a schema (an ID of the project's, sent as its full name, with JSON or BINARY encoding), read back by `GetTopic`, and the schema refuses a non-conforming publish; a missing schema is refused with the emulator's `NOT_FOUND`. **Create subscription** on a topic's page with an ack deadline, retention, retained acknowledged messages, message ordering, a filter, retry and dead-letter policies, each read back by `GetSubscription`, the filter delivering only matching messages; **push attributes** with the push endpoint (#996, `TestConsolePubSubIdleTimeAndPushAttributes`), read back by `GetSubscription`, and refused without an endpoint; a value out of range is refused with the emulator's message, and a malformed filter is refused at once (the console sends the create without the client's retry of `UNKNOWN`) with the emulator's answer and the console's explanation (unit `TestPubSubCreateSubscriptionDoesNotRetryAFilterRefusal`). **Exactly-once delivery and the expiration period** (#873): a 12h period is refused with the API's reason ("expiration_policy.ttl must be at least 1 day"), exactly-once with a push endpoint is refused by the console, and by the API through the front since #880 (the emulator accepts the pair; Google does not support it), and a subscription with a 1-day period and exactly-once reads both back. That they act is the Subscription expiration and Exactly-once delivery rows. Create topic is its own page. **Labels** (#962): both forms offer them, one `key=value` per line, and hold them to Google's label rules (the Label rules row) in the browser, line by line (the key and value patterns and the 64-label limit the field carries), and again before the create is sent; a label Google takes, international characters included, is created and read back by the official client (unit `TestPubSubFormsHoldLabelsToGoogleRules`). In headless Chrome, `TestPubSubCreateOptionsThroughTheForms` (`test/browser`, storage shard), `TestPubSubIdleTimeAndPushAttributesThroughTheForms` (a push subscription created with an attribute, read back by the console API), and `TestPubSubLabelsAreCheckedOnTheForms`: an uppercase key is refused on Create topic's labels field, naming the line, the key and the rule, with nothing sent, and `team=a`, `env=dev` and `日本=東京` create the topic, read back by the console API. |
| Pub/Sub: Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension) from a subscription's page | **Verified** as a CloudBurrow test extension; Google's Pub/Sub has no such call | `TestConsolePubSubAdvanceClock` (#1040), storage shard, through the console API, read back with the official `pubsub/v2` client. A subscription's page offers **Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension)**, which takes a duration (days, hours, minutes, seconds) and asks for the subscription's name back, saying the clock is the whole instance's, ages every subscription of every project and never moves back. It calls the Pub/Sub front's `cloudburrow.pubsub.v1.EmulatorClock/Advance` (#873), which sweeps before it answers, and reports the front's new clock and the subscriptions that expired, read around the call from the front's activity records and `ListSubscriptions` (neither is activity on a subscription). In the test an advance of 1h expires a subscription with a one-day period idle for 23h30m, which `GetSubscription` then reads `NOT_FOUND`, and keeps one idle as long with a two-day period and the page's own subscription (the page's read is not activity, #1039); an advance of 0s is refused. Without the front it is refused, saying so (unit `TestPubSubAdvanceClockReportsWhatExpired`, `TestParseAdvance`). In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, storage shard), `TestPubSubAdvanceClockThroughTheForm` sees the extension named on the form and the confirmation, Cancel send nothing, and the confirmed advance answer with the new clock. |
| Cloud KMS: create a key with its options from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleKMSCreateKeyOptions` (#852, compat, served shard), through the in-process service's own `CreateCryptoKey`, read back with the official client: **Create key** on a ring's page sends the destroy scheduled duration (30d by default, which cannot be changed later) and the rotation period and next rotation time, and `GetCryptoKey` reads each. 12 hours is refused with exactly the message the official client's own `CreateCryptoKey` receives, and no key is made (unit `TestKMSCreateKeyWithDestroyDurationAndRotation`). In headless Chrome, `TestKMSCreateKeyOptionsThroughTheForm` (`test/browser`, run shard). |
| Cloud Storage: create a bucket with its options from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleStorageCreateBucketOptions` (#852), storage shard: **Create** sends `buckets.insert` with labels, the default storage class, uniform bucket-level access, versioning, the soft delete retention and, with `enableObjectRetention=true`, object retention; the official client's `Attrs` reads each back. A 5-second soft delete retention is refused with the API's message and makes no bucket (unit `TestStorageCreateBucketOptionsThroughTheAPI`). The form is its own page. In headless Chrome, `TestStorageCreateBucketOptionsThroughTheForm` (`test/browser`, storage shard). |
| Cloud Run: labels, variables from Secret Manager and Execute with overrides from the console | **Verified** | #852, run shard, through the adapter's API as the official clients read it. `TestConsoleRunServiceLabelsAndSecrets`: a service deployed with labels and `TOKEN=secret:1` is read back by `GetService` with the labels and the `secretKeyRef`, and its edit form is prefilled with both. `TestConsoleRunJobLabelsSecretsAndOverrides`: a job created with labels and a secret-backed variable is read back by `GetJob` with both; **Execute with overrides** (arguments, a variable, 2 tasks, a 90-second timeout) runs an execution that succeeds, which the job's own arguments would not, with the secret's payload and the overridden variable in the task, and the job is unchanged. Edit and deploy new revision and Edit job replace the labels and variables from Secret Manager with the form's (unit `TestRunEditSetsLabelsAndSecretBackedVariables`, `TestRunJobLabelsSecretsAndExecuteWithOverrides`). In headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunJobExecuteWithOverridesThroughTheForm` (`test/browser`, run shard). |
| Bigtable: create and delete a table from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigtableCreateAndDeleteTable` (#699), in the emulators shard. A table created on the Bigtable screen with two column families is listed by the official `bigtable` admin client, in the console's one fixed instance (`cloudburrow`), and `TableInfo` returns exactly those families; a console delete removes it, so `Tables` no longer lists it and `TableInfo` answers `NOT_FOUND`. **Not checked in a browser.** |
| Bigtable: column families and row writes from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigtableFamiliesAndRowWrites` (#797), in the emulators shard, through the official `bigtable` admin and data clients against the emulator, read back with them. **Add column family** with a maximum of 2 versions or 7 days is returned by `TableInfo` with the policy `(versions() > 2 || age() > 7d)`, and adding it again is refused with the emulator's own message; **Edit GC policy**, from the family's row in the Schema tab, to 1 version and 1 hour only when both are exceeded, is returned as `(versions() > 1 && age() > 1h)`; **Delete column family** leaves `TableInfo` without it. **Write cell**, from the table's page with a millisecond timestamp and from a row's page with none, is read by `ReadRow` with its value and that timestamp, and a write to a family that does not exist is refused with the emulator's message; **Delete cells** removes that column's cells and nothing else; **Delete row** leaves `ReadRow` empty. Every delete is offered as one the client confirms by typing the name back, and Delete row returns to the table. The GC form holds a maximum number of versions, a maximum age in whole days, hours or minutes, and either or both; a stored policy it cannot express is shown with no edit (`TestGCPolicyFormRoundTripsWhatItCanHold`). **Not checked in a browser:** no browser test drives it yet (the emulators shard has run a browser step since #854); the actions are covered by this API test and the unit tests `TestBigtableFamilyAndRowActionsThroughTheOfficialClients` and `TestBigtableActionsAreOfferedWhereTheyApply`, against `bttest`. |
| Spanner: create a database, drop it, delete its instance from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleSpannerCreateAndDropDatabase` (#699), in the emulators shard. The console's create makes the instance it names when absent, then the database with its first table: the official instance admin client returns the instance and the database admin client returns the database and its DDL. **Drop database**, on the database's page, leaves `GetDatabase` `NOT_FOUND`; **Delete instance**, on the instance's page, leaves `GetInstance` `NOT_FOUND`. **Not checked in a browser.** |
| Spanner: INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE from the query editor | **Verified** | `TestConsoleSpannerDML` (#798), in the emulators shard. The editor on a database's page (and its tables' pages) reads by default; switching it to **Read-write (writes data)** and confirming a dialog that names the database runs one `INSERT`, `UPDATE` or `DELETE` in a read-write transaction through the official `spanner` client, which commits it, and reports the rows it changed. Each of an INSERT of two rows, an UPDATE and a DELETE reports its count and is read back by the official client's `ReadRow`. In read-only mode a DML statement is refused before it is sent, with the reason, and the row is still there; a duplicate key is refused with Spanner's `ALREADY_EXISTS` message; DDL is refused in either mode (a new database's first table is set in **Create database**), and two statements at once are refused, because `ExecuteBatchDml` is not verified here: nothing changes, as `GetDatabaseDdl` and `ReadRow` confirm. A `SELECT` is refused in read-write mode, so it is never wrapped in a write transaction, and still runs read-only. A read-write statement is at most 16 KiB and stops after 15 s. Unit: `TestSpannerStatementClassifier` (comments, quoted text, statement hints and several statements), `TestSpannerEditorRefusesBeforeSending`, `TestQueryModeChoosesTheCallNotTheText`, `TestReadWriteIsOfferedOnlyWhereItCanSucceed`. **Not checked in a browser:** no browser test drives it yet (the emulators shard has run a browser step since #854), so the mode switch and the confirmation are covered by these tests of the route they call. |
| Cloud SQL: create and drop a database from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleCloudSQLCreateAndDropDatabase` (#699), in the emulators shard, against the instance's PostgreSQL. A database created on the Cloud SQL screen is in `pg_database` and accepts a `pgx` connection as itself; a console drop removes it. Dropping the database the server was initialised with is refused with 400 and the provider's message, and that database is still there. **Not checked in a browser.** |
| Cloud SQL: the SQL editors' Read-write mode | **Verified** | `TestConsoleCloudSQLReadWriteEditor` and `TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLReadWriteEditor` (#995), in the emulators shard, through the console API, read back with `pgx` and `go-sql-driver/mysql`. Both editors read by default, as before, and a database's page (and its tables') offers **Read-write (writes data)**, naming the database in its confirmation, as Spanner's (#798) and BigQuery's (#994) do; the statement runs as `cloudburrow`, the application's user, and the answer is the database's own. **PostgreSQL:** the text runs in one transaction, committed when it succeeds and rolled back when it fails; `CREATE TABLE` answers `Committed. PostgreSQL answered CREATE TABLE.`, an `INSERT` of two rows `INSERT 0 2: 2 rows affected`, an `UPDATE` and a `DELETE` one row each, and `pgx` reads the table as each left it; a duplicate key is PostgreSQL's `SQLSTATE 23505`, a two-statement write whose second statement fails leaves nothing of the first, the default mode still refuses the `INSERT` with PostgreSQL's read-only-transaction error, and `DROP TABLE` removes the table from `pg_tables`. A statement PostgreSQL does not run in a transaction block (`CREATE DATABASE`, `VACUUM`) is refused with its error; that is not tested. **MySQL:** one statement per run as `cloudburrow`, with MySQL's autocommit (DDL commits as it runs, in MySQL itself); `CREATE TABLE`, an `INSERT` of two rows (answered `2 rows affected`, and `CURRENT_USER()` in it reads `cloudburrow@…`), an `UPDATE`, a `DELETE` and `DROP TABLE` commit and the driver reads each; a duplicate key is MySQL's error 1062; afterwards the read-only mode still refuses an `INSERT` and a `CREATE TABLE` with 1142 for `cloudburrow_console`, which is not widened. In headless Chrome, `TestCloudSQLReadWriteEditorsThroughThePage` drives both engines: the confirmation names the database and shows the statement, Cancel sends nothing, and a confirmed `CREATE TABLE`, `INSERT` and `DROP TABLE` answer in the database's words. |
| BigQuery: datasets, tables, schema, preview and a read-only query | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetsSchemaAndQuery` (#698), in the emulators shard. A dataset and table the official `bigquery` client created, with four streamed rows, are listed on the BigQuery screen with their table count; the table's page shows the schema as created and the four rows (`tabledata.list`); the query editor runs a `SELECT` against the dataset on the page and returns the inserted rows with the result's own columns, and the dataset list is unchanged afterwards. A `DELETE` is refused with 400 before it is sent, and the table still has four rows: the editor runs one `SELECT` or `WITH … SELECT` only, checked lexically because BigQuery has no read-only transaction and the emulator's dry run calls every statement `SELECT` (unit `TestTheBigQueryEditorRunsOnlyOneSelect`). At most 200 rows are shown; a read stops after 15 s. **Another project** shows a prompt naming the one project the emulator serves, not an error and not an empty table (same test; unit `TestBigQueryOtherProjectsArePromptsNotErrors`). Read-only is the default; DDL and scripts run in the opt-in Read-write mode (a later row), DML nowhere yet. |
| BigQuery: create and delete datasets and tables, and insert rows, from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetTableAndRowWrites` (#854, #874), in the emulators shard, each read back through the official `bigquery` client. **Create dataset** makes the dataset with its location, description and labels; a second of the same ID is refused at once with the API's "Already Exists", and `bad-name` with the API's "Invalid dataset ID". **Create table** on a dataset's page makes the table with the schema the editor sent, each field's name, type and mode as read back by `Table.Metadata`, **RECORD columns with nested fields included** (#874): a RECORD holding a REQUIRED field and a RECORD two levels down, and a REPEATED RECORD, read back field by field; two names differing only in case are refused ("Field X already exists in schema"); a RECORD inside a REPEATED RECORD is created (#881), and **Insert rows** with a value in it is written and read back (#900, #1061; it was refused as not implemented against the pinned v0.8.1). **Insert rows** streams rows with `tabledata.insertAll`, which `Table.Read` returns with their types, RECORD objects included; a batch holding one bad row, a wrong-type value, a row missing a REQUIRED value (a nested one too, named `addr.city`) and a row naming no such field are refused, naming the row and field, and the table's rows are unchanged afterwards. **Delete table**, and **Delete dataset** from the list with a table still in it (deleted with its contents), leave `Metadata` NOT_FOUND. **The refusals are the API's**: since the validating front (#861) stands in the port the console uses, the console no longer checks IDs, field names, duplicates or row values itself (#874); it shows the API's message without the `googleapi: Error 400:` envelope, and of a refused batch lists only the invalid rows, not those BigQuery reports as `stopped` (unit `TestBigQueryConsoleShowsTheAPIsRefusals`, against the front). Every write in another project is refused with the one-project prompt (unit `TestBigQueryWritesStayInTheServedProject`); the nested schema and the shaping of each value for `insertAll` are unit-tested in `TestBigQuerySchemaEditorBuildsNestedSchemas` and `TestBigQueryRowsAreShapedForInsertAll`. In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard), `TestBigQueryCreateTableInsertRowsAndDeleteThroughTheForms` builds a schema in the editor, sees a case-only duplicate refused on the form with nothing sent, inserts rows after a refused one, and deletes the table by typing its name; `TestBigQueryRecordColumnsThroughTheSchemaEditor` chooses RECORD, sees a RECORD with no nested field refused on the form, builds a RECORD with a REQUIRED field and a RECORD inside it, reads the dotted fields back on the schema tab, and inserts and previews a row after one missing its nested REQUIRED value is refused. GEOGRAPHY values are not checked. |
| BigQuery: load from and export to Cloud Storage, and Job history, from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory` (#993), in the emulators shard, each outcome read back through the official `bigquery` client, and a load's `badRecords`, which that client's `LoadStatistics` lacks, through the generated one. **Load from Cloud Storage**, on a dataset's page (with the table to load into) and on a table's page, runs `jobs.insert` with the form's `gs://` URIs, file format (CSV, `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON`, Parquet), write preference (`WRITE_APPEND`, `WRITE_TRUNCATE`), a schema built in the schema editor or auto-detect, and, for CSV, the options the front serves (#944, #945, #952): header rows to skip, field delimiter, quote character (empty: none), null marker and null markers, encoding (UTF-8, ISO-8859-1), source column match, jagged rows, quoted newlines, ASCII control characters, number of errors allowed and ignore unknown values. An option left at BigQuery's default is not sent. A CSV of a header, two rows and one too wide loads the two with the bad record left out; a pipe-delimited file with a `\N` null marker is appended as a NULL; the dialog answers with the job, linked to its page, its output rows and files. A URI that does not exist, and every combination the front does not serve (a load with `maxBadRecords` the emulator then fails, a JSON auto-detect load into a new table, ...), are refused **in the API's words**, and nothing is made; a CSV option changed on a load of another format is refused before anything is sent. Not offered: `timeZone` and the date and time formats (501), `WRITE_EMPTY`, Avro and ORC, which nothing tests; a load of a file uploaded in the browser is #999. **Export to Cloud Storage**, on a table's page, runs an extract to one URI as CSV or `NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON`, `GZIP` or not, with a one-character delimiter and the header row (#939, #957): a `|`-delimited CSV with its header is read back from the bucket; a JSON export of a NULL is the front's 501, in its words. The export form also offers `AVRO`, which the front writes (#957), with `DEFLATE`, `SNAPPY` or no compression; `GZIP` with Avro, and `DEFLATE` or `SNAPPY` with CSV or JSON, are refused on the form (unit `TestBigQueryExportFormOffersAvro`; the Avro export is not yet run from the console in a compat test). Parquet and several URIs are 501 and not offered. **Copy table** (#782) on a table's page runs a copy job (`Table.CopierFrom`, the Copy jobs row) to a destination dataset and table with `WRITE_EMPTY`, `WRITE_TRUNCATE` or `WRITE_APPEND`, answered with its job; a copy onto itself is refused on the form (unit `TestBigQueryCopyTableFormRunsACopyJob`; not yet run against the emulator in a compat test). **Job history** (`/bigquery-jobs`, BigQuery's second page) lists the served project's jobs from `jobs.list` with the full projection, newest first, each with its type, state (Succeeded or Failed), creation time and error; a job's page reads `jobs.get` and shows its reference, state, times and duration, its `errorResult`, what it was asked to do (the query, the load's source and destination and options, the export's source and destination), the load statistics the job reports (output rows, bad records, input files and bytes, each only when reported, #960, #966), the files an export wrote, the bad records of `status.errors` on an Errors tab, and the configuration as the API gave it. **Delete job** (`jobs.delete`), on the page and the list's row, asks for the job's ID back; after it, `jobs.get` of an emulator job and of one the front ran is 404. **Cancel job** (`jobs.cancel`) is offered only on a job that is not `DONE`: every job the emulator and the front run is done when `jobs.insert` answers, and `jobs.cancel` of a done job leaves it done (same test, through the client), so no page offers it here (unit `TestBigQueryJobActionsFollowTheJobsState`). A CSV load from Cloud Storage reads back without its `sourceUris` (#998). Units `TestBigQueryLoadFormBecomesTheLoadsSource`, `TestBigQueryJobPageShowsWhatTheJobReported`, `TestBigQueryWritesStayInTheServedProject`. In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard), `TestBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistoryThroughTheForms` sees a load of a missing object refused on the form, loads a CSV the console uploaded with a schema built in the editor, follows the job's link, exports with a `|` delimiter and downloads the object, finds both jobs in Job history, and deletes the load job by typing its ID. |
| BigQuery: load a file uploaded from the browser, from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadFromAFile` (#999), in the emulators shard, through the console API, each outcome read back with the official `bigquery` client. **Load from a file**, on a dataset's page (with the table to load into) and on a table's page, offers Load from Cloud Storage's form with a file control where the URIs are: the same formats, write preferences, schema or auto-detect and CSV options. The form posts the action and the file together to `POST /api/actions/bigquery/upload`, a route of the console's own beside the object upload route, under the same upload limit (Settings, 32 MiB by default) and behind the same Host and same-origin guards; the action must be one the page offers. The console streams the file to the front through the official Go client's `ReaderSource`, in 4 MiB chunks: a smaller file as one multipart upload, a larger one as a resumable upload (#919, #931). In the test a CSV with the form's schema and one header row makes a new table with its two rows, a newline-delimited JSON file is appended, and a CSV of more than 5 MiB, sent resumable, replaces the rows with `WRITE_TRUNCATE`; each answers with its job, linked to Job history, whose page shows `LOAD` and its output rows, and `JobFromID` reads it back `DONE` into that table. A CSV option on a JSON load is refused before anything is sent, a CSV row the table cannot hold is refused in the API's words, and neither changes the table; another project is refused. Units `TestActionUploadStreamsTheFileToAnOfferedAction`, `TestActionUploadIsHeldToTheUploadLimit`, `TestActionUploadRefusesACrossSitePost`, `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileOffersLoadFromCloudStoragesOptions`, `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileSendsTheFileAsTheLoadsData`. In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard), `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileThroughTheForm` sees the form refuse a submit with no file and send nothing, then loads a chosen CSV with a schema built in the editor and follows the job's link. |
| BigQuery: a query job's statement type and DML row counts on its Job history page | **Verified** | `TestConsoleBigQueryJobPageShowsDMLRows` (#1041), in the emulators shard: for an `INSERT`, an `UPDATE`, a `MERGE`, a `DELETE` and a `DELETE` that matched nothing, each run through the official Go client (`Query.Run`, `Job.Wait`), the job's page shows the statement type, affected rows (`numDmlAffectedRows`) and the inserted, updated and deleted rows of `dmlStats` that the client reads from `jobs.get` (#1008), each only when the job reported it: a count `dmlStats` leaves out is not shown as 0. A `SELECT` job's page shows its statement type and no row counts. Unit `TestBigQueryJobPageShowsAQueryJobsDMLRows`. |
| BigQuery: the query editor's Read-write mode, Edit table, views, Insert rows' options and flexible field names, from the console | **Verified**, with the limits named | `TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDDLAndScripts` and `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions` (#994), in the emulators shard, each change read back through the official `bigquery` client. **Read-write mode** is offered on a dataset's and a table's page, naming the project and the default dataset; a run asks first, then sends the statement with `jobs.query` (GoogleSQL, the page's dataset as the default): `CREATE SCHEMA` with a description, `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT` of the dataset's table by its unqualified name (columns and rows read back), `CREATE VIEW` (its rows read back; its query text is #1014), `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` of a table that exists (unchanged), a script whose `DECLARE`d variable a `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT` reads, and `DROP TABLE`, and no job-named dataset is left behind. **A DML statement of its own** (#1024, `TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDML`) runs too, answered from the rows the front reports in `jobs.query`'s answer (`numDmlAffectedRows`, and `dmlStats` for a `MERGE`; the DML row): an `INSERT` of two rows "This statement added 2 rows.", an `UPDATE` "modified 2 rows", a `DELETE` "removed 1 row", a `MERGE` from a subquery "modified 2 rows: 1 inserted, 1 updated, 0 deleted.", a `DELETE` of nothing "removed 0 rows" and `TRUNCATE TABLE` "removed 6 rows", each change read back through the official client; the wording is CloudBurrow's. DML inside a script or a `BEGIN` block is refused before it is sent, naming #1028 (the front does not count its rows), and `UPDATE … FROM` with the emulator's reason, "Update with joins not supported" (#1027), and the table keeps its rows; a lone `SELECT` is sent back to Read-only; `ALTER TABLE` is the front's 501 in its words, without the client's envelope. The console answers a DDL statement with a sentence naming its job (unit `TestAWriteReporterAnswersWithItsSentence`). **Create table** takes BigQuery's flexible field names (`first name`), and with the **VIEW** type makes a view from its query (`tables.insert`), whose query reads back as written; a view with a schema, or a table with a view query, is refused. A view's page offers Edit table and Delete table. **Edit table** (`tables.patch`) changes the description and labels, on a table and on a view, keeping labels it does not change, and since #1025 removes a label and clears the description; a form with no change says so, and no field is added (#1013). **Insert rows** with **Skip invalid rows** inserts the valid rows and answers with the skipped one, numbered, with the API's reason; with **Ignore unknown values** a field the table lacks is dropped. Units `TestTheBigQueryReadWriteEditorScreensStatements`, `TestBigQueryDMLAnswerSaysWhatChanged`, `TestBigQueryWriteReportSendsJobsQuery`, `TestBigQueryCreateViewAndEditTable`, `TestBigQueryInsertRowsOptions`, `TestBigQueryFieldNamesAreFlexible`. In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard), `TestBigQueryReadWriteEditorThroughTheForms` sees a `CREATE TABLE` refused in Read-only, switches to Read-write, cancels the confirmation with nothing sent, confirms a `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT` and sees the table, confirms an `INSERT` of two rows and reads "This statement added 2 rows.", and sees a `DELETE` inside a script refused naming #1028; `TestBigQueryFlexibleNamesInsertOptionsEditTableAndViewThroughTheForms` makes a table with a field `first name`, inserts with Skip invalid rows and reads the skipped row in the result, edits the description and a label, then clears the description and removes the label, and makes a view from Create table. |
| Memorystore in the console | **Not supported** | No screen: there is no Memorystore admin API here, and every screen reads through a service's own API. See console-parity.md §3. |
| Cloud SQL for MySQL from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLSchemaAndReadOnlyQuery` and `TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLCreateAndDropDatabase` (#868), in the emulators shard, against the instance's MySQL 8.4 with `go-sql-driver/mysql`. The page at `/cloudsql-mysql` (Cloud SQL's **MySQL** page, beside **PostgreSQL**) lists the one server, its version in the list's note, and its databases without MySQL's system ones; a table the driver created is listed with its columns (type, nullability, key) and its primary key as `information_schema` holds them. **Read-only editor:** a `SELECT` returns the driver's rows, NULL as an em dash. MySQL's `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY` is used, but on its own it does not stop DDL — MySQL commits implicitly before `CREATE TABLE`, `DROP TABLE` or `DROP DATABASE`, which then run (measured against the pinned 8.4.11) — so each statement runs as `cloudburrow_console`, an account the screen creates and grants only `SELECT` and `SHOW VIEW` on the database queried, with a password generated per console process. `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `DROP TABLE`, `CREATE TABLE`, `TRUNCATE` and a read of `mysql.user` are each refused by MySQL with error 1142, and the table still holds its rows; a query of a system database is refused by the console. The account is visible on the Users tab and in `mysql.user`, and is not removed by `reset`. **Create database** runs `CREATE DATABASE` as root and grants the application's user `cloudburrow` all privileges on it, so the driver opens it as that user and creates a table; **Delete** runs `DROP DATABASE` and it is gone from `information_schema.SCHEMATA`. Dropping `cloudburrow` or a system database is refused with 400 and the provider's message. In headless Chrome: `TestCloudSQLMySQLCreateQueryAndDropThroughThePage`. The editor's Read-write mode, which writes as the application's user and leaves `cloudburrow_console` as it is, is the row "Cloud SQL: the SQL editors' Read-write mode" (#995). The Cloud SQL Admin API is still not served: no instances, flags or user administration. |
| Every selectable service has a screen or a documented exclusion | **Verified** | `TestEveryServiceHasAParityRow` (#698): every service in `config.KnownServices()` is built by the console's registry, shown on a non-provider screen (Cloud Logging, in the Logs Explorer), or excluded with a reason (Memorystore), and console-parity.md §3 has a row under the dashboard's title for each, beginning **No screen** exactly for the excluded ones. |
| Cloud Tasks: edit a queue and create a task from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAndCreateTask` (#784), through the in-process service's own `UpdateQueue` and `CreateTask`, read back with the official client. **Edit queue** on a queue's page is prefilled from the queue and sends its rate limits (max dispatches per second, max concurrent dispatches) and retry parameters (max attempts, min and max backoff, max doublings, max retry duration) with an update mask naming each; `GetQueue` reads back what was saved. Max burst size is shown disabled and never sent: it is output only. A max attempts of -2 is refused with `UpdateQueue`'s own `INVALID_ARGUMENT` message and changes nothing, and a queue deleted meanwhile is `NOT_FOUND` rather than created again. **Create task** on the queue's page takes a URL, HTTP method, headers, body, optional schedule time and optional task ID: a task scheduled an hour out is returned by `ListTasks` with the URL, method, headers and body given, one due now is dispatched to a test HTTP target with them, and a GET with a body is refused with `CreateTask`'s message. App Engine targets, OIDC or OAuth tokens, App Engine routing and Cloud Logging are not offered, because the API refuses them. In headless Chrome, `TestTasksEditQueueThroughTheForm` (`test/browser`, storage shard) saves a max attempts through the dialog, sees -2 refused on the form with the API's message, and finds that Cancel discards a change without asking. |
| Instance page: state save and load, reset, seed | **Verified** | `/instance` (#801), under Management tools and in Settings and utilities. Each action is the admin API's (`POST /admin/state/export`, `/admin/state/import`, `/admin/reset`, `/admin/seed`), called in process with the admin token the page sends, and what the page offers is what `GET /admin/instance` says those routes accept: the reset components, with those that cannot be scoped to a project disabled while one is named; **Reseed** only when `up` had a seed file and no project is named; the seedable components; the manifest's captured and not-captured services, with the reasons. `TestConsoleStateSaveResetLoadRestores` (compat, storage shard): a bucket with an object, a secret with a version and a queue are saved from the console, reset from the console for their project only, which leaves another project's queue, and loaded from the console, and the official Storage, Secret Manager and Cloud Tasks clients read them back as they were; a seed uploaded twice with **If not exists** creates its queue once, and without it the second is refused with the admin API's 409. Unit, `TestConsoleInstanceActsThroughTheAdminAPI`: the archive is `state save`'s, a refusal (a project reset naming a component that cannot be scoped, reseed without a seed file, a file that is not an archive, a seed conflict) is relayed with the admin API's status and message and changes nothing, and every action is in the operations ledger with its outcome and never its payload. The console adds no admin token of its own: without it every endpoint answers the admin API's 401, also to a request shaped like a workload's, and records nothing; a cross-origin page is refused even with it (`TestConsoleInstanceRefusesARequestWithoutTheAdminToken`, `TestConsoleInstanceRefusesACrossOriginRequest`; see [networking.md](networking.md#the-console-and-the-admin-token)). In headless Chrome, `TestInstanceResetConfirmedByTypingTheScope` (`test/browser`, storage shard) resets one project's Cloud Tasks state through the form: Reset is confirmed by typing the project, Cancel sends nothing, another word is refused on the form, and the reset removes that project's queue and leaves another's. **Save, Load and Seed are not driven in a browser**; their requests are the ones the compat test sends. |
| Cloud Logging: delete a log from the Logs Explorer | **Verified** | `TestConsoleDeleteLogIsSeenByTheOfficialClient` (#799, served shard): entries written to two logs with `cloud.google.com/go/logging`, one deleted through the console's `DELETE /api/logs`, and `logadmin` then reads no entries for it and no longer lists it, while the other log keeps its entry; the Explorer stops showing the deleted entries. The console calls `DeleteLog` on the in-process service, after the instance's fault rules as its interceptor applies them. **Delete log** is on the row menu of a `logging/<log>` entry of the selected project only, confirmed by typing the log ID (Cancel discards without asking); pod logs and CloudBurrow's own request log have no API that deletes them, so it is not offered there and the page says so. A log of another project is refused by the route before the API is called, and a failure shows the API's own message (a second delete is its `NotFound`). Unit: `TestConsoleDeleteLogThroughDeleteLog`, `TestNoDeleteLogWithoutLogging`. No headless-browser test: the Logs Explorer's Cloud Logging entries are served only in the served shard, which runs no browser step. |
| Firestore and Datastore: create, edit and delete documents and entities from the console | **Verified** | `TestConsoleFirestoreDocumentCreateEditDelete` and `TestConsoleDatastoreEntityCreateEditDelete` (#796), in the emulators shard, each write through the official `firestore` or `datastore` client against the emulator and read back with it. **Firestore:** Start collection and Add document (a named ID, or empty for an auto ID) create with `Create`, so a taken ID is the emulator's `ALREADY_EXISTS`; Add field adds one field of each type — string, number (an integer without a decimal point, a double with one), boolean, null, timestamp, geopoint, reference, map, array, and since #995 bytes, as base64 — and `DocumentRef.Get` returns each as that Go type, a map's and an array's nested values included; Edit field, saved as prefilled, leaves the double 2.0 a double, and a change of type is stored as the new type; a latitude of 200 is refused with the emulator's `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; Delete field removes the field and Delete document leaves `Get` `NOT_FOUND`, and a second delete is the emulator's `NOT_FOUND`. A field edit is an update of that one field with the document's update time as its precondition. **Datastore:** Create entity inserts, so a taken key is the emulator's `ALREADY_EXISTS`, and with no key Datastore allocates a numeric ID; Add property adds one of each type — string, integer, float, boolean, timestamp, key (with an ancestor), geopoint, array, embedded entity, null — one excluded from indexes, and `Get` returns each with its type and flag; Edit property saved as prefilled keeps the float a float and the exclusion; a key value is shown and prefilled as `Kind/name=…` or `Kind/id=…` pairs, ancestors first, with a slash or percent sign in a kind or name escaped and `__namespace__/{namespace}/` first in a namespace other than the default, so Edit property saved unchanged writes back the same key — a key to the name `id=7`, which the older `Order/id=7` form rewrote to the numeric ID 7, a name holding `/`, `%` or `=`, an ancestor, a namespace, and from an entity in a namespace a key in the default one (#887, `TestConsoleDatastoreKeyValuesRoundTripThroughEditProperty`; unit `TestDatastoreKeyValuesRoundTripThroughEditProperty`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreKeyValueEditPropertyIsANoOp`); a key value naming an entity in another project or database, which the official Go client cannot express and reads as this project's, is shown with where it is — `Order/id=7 (project another-project)`, `(database another-db)` — and offered no edit, since the form writes a key in this project's default database, and a key inside an array or an embedded entity is shown as `key(Order/name=id=7)` rather than the client's `/Order,id=7`, as are a nested timestamp, geopoint and blob by their type (#893, #894; `TestConsoleDatastoreKeepsAKeyValuesProjectAndDatabase`, the entity written through the Datastore v1 API; unit `TestDatastoreKeyValuesKeepTheirProjectAndDatabase`, `TestDatastoreFormValuesAreWrittenAsTheClientWritesThem`, `TestNestedDatastoreValuesRenderAsTheirType`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreNestedAndForeignKeysThroughTheBrowser`, which the emulators shard's browser step runs with the Datastore and Firestore emulators' addresses exported, #895); a kind's entities and the query builder's results are read with the v1 API's `RunQuery`, not the client, so such a key, and an embedded entity's own key in another project or database, are shown with where they are in the listing's Properties cell as on the entity's page, and paging still uses the query cursor in the client's encoding (#904, `TestConsoleDatastoreListingAndQueryShowWhereAKeyIs`; unit `TestDatastoreQueriesAreWrittenAsTheClientWritesThem`, `TestDatastoreEmbeddedEntityKeyKeepsItsPartition`); an array or embedded entity property's **Elements** tab lists every value inside it, and **Edit value** on one — a key, timestamp, geopoint, string, number, boolean or null, prefilled — writes back that value only, through `Lookup` and `Commit` in a transaction, keeping its index flag: saved unchanged on every value of an entity the official client wrote, the entity reads back proto-equal through `Lookup`, and changing a key, a timestamp, a geopoint, a key in a nested array and an embedded entity's own key leaves every other value as it was and the official client reads the new ones as their types; a key in another project or database is offered no Edit value and the action is refused (#905, `TestConsoleDatastoreEditValueInsideArraysAndEntities`; unit `TestDatastoreElementsAreListedAndAddressed`, `TestDatastoreEditValueWritesOnlyThatValue`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreEditValueInsideAnArrayThroughTheBrowser`); the Elements tab lists every array and embedded entity inside a property as well, and **Add value** — on the property's page and on each of them — inserts into an array at an index or appends, or adds a property to an embedded entity, or its key (`__key__`) when it has none, while **Remove value**, confirmed by typing the value's name back, removes an array's value (the rest close up), an embedded entity's property or its key: on an entity the official client wrote, beside a key to another project added through the v1 API, each writes that change only, through `Lookup` and `Commit` in a transaction, every other value reads back proto-equal through `Lookup` and the client reads the new values as their types; an index past the end, an array inside an array and a property the entity already has are refused, and a reserved name with the emulator's own message; the emulator stores an array's values excluded from indexes after its indexed ones, measured at the top level and inside an embedded entity, so a change that would put an excluded value before an indexed one is refused rather than moved (#911, `TestConsoleDatastoreAddAndRemoveValues`; unit `TestDatastoreAddAndRemoveValueChangeOnlyThatValue`, `TestDatastoreValueActionsAreOfferedWhereTheyApply`); a blob is shown and edited as base64, standard encoding with padding as the v1 REST API's `blobValue`, by Edit property, Add property and Edit value — whose Exclude from indexes is prefilled with the value's own flag — and saved unchanged writes the same bytes back (proto-equal through `Lookup`), a change is read back by the official client as those bytes, and a blob over 1,500 bytes is refused indexed with the client's "too long to index" and written excluded, which the emulator enforces too (#912, `TestConsoleDatastoreBlobValuesAsBase64`; unit `TestDatastoreBlobValuesRoundTripAsBase64`; in headless Chrome, with Add value and Remove value, `TestDatastoreAddRemoveAndBlobValuesThroughTheBrowser`); Edit value, Add value, Remove value and Exclude from indexes address a value by its place in the property, so each carries a digest of the property as its page read it, in a hidden form field, and is refused inside the same `Lookup`/`Commit` transaction, before anything is written, when the property is not that any more, saying it changed since the page was loaded and to reload the page: after the official client inserted a value at the front of the array, and after the v1 API changed one value's index flag, all four drawn before the change are refused and the property reads back through `Lookup` as the other writer left it, while a change to another property of the entity refuses nothing and Remove value then removes the value its row showed; an action with no digest is refused (#923, `TestConsoleDatastoreValueActionsRefuseAConcurrentChange`; unit `TestDatastoreValueActionsRefuseAChangedProperty`, `TestHiddenFieldsAreSentBackAsTheyCame`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreStaleValueActionIsRefusedAndWholeArrayExcludedThroughTheBrowser`); **Exclude from indexes**, on an array or embedded entity property's page and on every array or embedded entity inside one, sets or clears the flag on every value inside it, at any depth, an embedded entity's own included, in one write: on an array the official client wrote, every value reads back excluded through `Lookup` in the same order and otherwise proto-equal, the client reads the property excluded, a 2,000-byte blob can then be added excluded at the front (which the ordering rule refused before), indexing it again is refused naming the blob and the 1,500-byte rule, and once it is removed indexing gives back the array proto-equal to what the client stored; on an embedded entity it excludes the entity and every value inside, and on the array inside it indexes that array alone; an embedded entity inside an array excluded before an indexed neighbour is refused as Add value refuses it (#924, `TestConsoleDatastoreExcludeWholeArrayFromIndexes`; unit `TestDatastoreExcludeFromIndexesSetsEveryValue`). Only the emulator's ordering was measured; whether production Firestore in Datastore mode reorders an array's excluded values the same way is not known; an indexed 1,600-byte string is refused with the client's "too long to index"; Delete property and Delete entity remove them, `Get` answering `datastore: no such entity`. Changes to an entity are a read and an update in one transaction, through the Datastore v1 API (`Lookup` and `Commit`) rather than the client: the client's read kept only a key value's namespace, so Add, Edit and Delete property had rewritten a key value naming another project or database to this project's default database, reproduced against the emulator; now every value the change does not touch is written back exactly as `Lookup` returned it, and `Lookup` reads it back so (#893, `TestConsoleDatastoreKeepsAKeyValuesProjectAndDatabase`). Maps, arrays and embedded entities are written as JSON, so a value holding bytes, a timestamp, a geopoint, a reference or a key inside one is shown and offered no Edit field or Edit property as a whole (`TestValuesTheFormCannotHoldOfferNoEdit`); in Datastore a value inside one is edited with Edit value (#905) and added or removed with Add value and Remove value (#911), and in Firestore likewise since #995 (the row "Firestore: values inside maps and arrays, and bytes"); subcollections, namespaces and child entities are the next row. Every delete is confirmed by typing the name back. The forms and actions are covered by these API tests and the unit tests `TestEditFormsRoundTripTheStoredType`, `TestTypedValuesAreReadAsTheirTypeNeverGuessed` and `TestDocumentAndEntityActionsAreOfferedWhereTheyApply`. |
| Firestore: values inside maps and arrays, and bytes | **Verified** | `TestConsoleFirestoreEditValuesInsideMapsAndArrays` (#995), in the emulators shard, through the console API, read back with the official `firestore` client. A map or array field's page has an **Elements** tab listing every value inside it, at any depth, as `meta.items[2]` or `meta.items[2]["odd name"]`, each with its own page; **Edit value** (string, number, boolean, null, timestamp, geopoint, reference or bytes, prefilled), **Add value** on a map (a new key) or an array (at an index, or appended), on the field's page and on each map or array inside it, and **Remove value**, confirmed by typing the value's name back. A document the client wrote, holding an array with a double and a map of a timestamp with microseconds, bytes, a geopoint, a reference in this project and one in another project, a boolean and a null: Edit value saved as prefilled on every value it is offered for leaves `DocumentRef.Get` equal to what the client wrote; the other project's reference, and a map or array, are offered no Edit value. Then a timestamp inside the array's map, the bytes and the geopoint are changed, a reference is inserted at index 1, a key added and the array's first value removed, and the client reads each new value as its type and every other value and field as written. Each change is read with `BatchGetDocuments` and written with one `Commit` masked to the changed value's field path as far as it runs through maps (an array is written whole: a field path cannot name an index), preconditioned on the document's update time; each action carries a digest of the field as drawn, and one drawn before another writer changed the field is refused with "changed since the page was loaded" and changes nothing. **Bytes** are shown and edited as base64 (standard encoding, padded, as the REST API's `bytesValue`): Add field `bytes` `AQID` is read back as the bytes 1, 2, 3, and its Edit field is prefilled `bytes` `AQID`. Unit: `TestFirestoreElementsAreListedAndAddressed`, `TestFirestoreEditValueSavedUnchangedWritesTheSameValue`, `TestFirestoreAddAndRemoveValueChangeOnlyThatValue`, `TestFirestoreValueWritesAreMaskedToWhatChanged`, `TestFirestoreValueActionsRefuseAChangedField`, `TestFirestoreBytesRoundTripAsBase64`; in headless Chrome `TestFirestoreEditAddRemoveAndBytesValuesThroughTheBrowser`. A Firestore vector value is not given an Elements tab. |
| Firestore: Edit field refuses a reference into another project or database | **Verified** | `TestConsoleFirestoreEditFieldRefusesAForeignReference` (#1050), in the emulators shard: a top-level field holding a reference to a document of another project, written with the official `firestore` client, is offered no **Edit field** on its page (#995), and a `PATCH /api/resources/firestore` posted by hand is refused 400 with the note the page shows, since the form writes a reference into this project's default database, which would change the document it names; the client reads the field back unchanged. A reference into this project's default database is still edited through the same route. Unit `TestFirestoreEditFieldRefusesAForeignReference` (another project, and another database of this one). Datastore refuses a key in another project or database the same way (#893). |
| Firestore subcollections, Datastore namespaces and child entities in the console | **Verified** | #854, in the emulators shard, read back through the official clients. **Firestore** (`TestConsoleFirestoreSubcollections`): a document's Collections tab lists its subcollections as `DocumentRef.Collections` (`ListCollectionIds`) does, each opening its own page by its path; **Start collection** on a document's page makes a subcollection with its first document, read back at `users/alice/orders/o1`; the subcollection's page names its parent document and lists its documents; Add document, Add field and Delete document work in it as at the top level; a nested document's breadcrumb leads back through each collection and document above it. A subcollection is not deleted with its document. A document that does not exist but holds subcollections is listed since #875 (next row). **Datastore** (`TestConsoleDatastoreNamespacesAndChildren`): Create entity with a namespace writes the entity in it and not in the default; a reserved namespace (`__…__`) is refused; the Datastore screen lists every namespace's kinds with a Namespace column, and a row in another namespace opens its kind there; a Namespaces page lists them and each one's page its kinds. **Create child entity** on an entity's page writes an entity whose parent is that one, in its namespace, with a name or an allocated ID; the parent's Children tab (a kindless ancestor query, bounded at 1,000 descendants) lists it by its key path and opens it; its page shows the key path and parent, and Add property and Delete entity act on it; a child of an entity that does not exist is refused with `no such entity`. A child is listed by its key path, with `/` and `%` in a name escaped (unit `TestDatastorePathsAddressNamespacesAndAncestors`); since #875 an entity's page is addressed by its encoded key (next row). In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard): `TestFirestoreStartASubcollectionOnADocumentPage` and `TestDatastoreNamespaceAndChildEntityThroughTheBrowser`. |
| Firestore documents that do not exist, and Datastore entity addresses, in the console | **Verified** | #875, in the emulators shard, read back through the official clients. **Firestore** (`TestConsoleFirestoreListsMissingDocumentsWithSubcollections`): a collection's page is read with `ListDocuments` and `show_missing` — the client's `CollectionRef.DocumentRefs` — rather than a query, so a document that does not exist but has a subcollection is listed as `DocumentRefs` lists it, in italics with *no fields — has subcollections*, and paging continues with the API's page token (a page token can be issued for a page that ends the collection, so the next page can be empty). Its page says it does not exist and its Collections tab opens its subcollections; **Add field** on it creates it (`Create`, so one written meanwhile is `ALREADY_EXISTS`), which the client reads back; **Delete document** on it says there is nothing to delete. The documents that exist are then read in one `BatchGetDocuments` for their fields. The query builder still runs a query, which, as in Firestore, does not return a missing document. **Datastore** (`TestConsoleDatastoreOpensARootEntityNamedLikeAKeyPath`, unit `TestDatastoreEntityAddressIsUnambiguous`): an entity's page is addressed by its key encoded as Google's console encodes it — the URL-safe base64 of the key's protocol buffer, the client's `Key.Encode` — so a root entity named `Customer/alice/Order/x` in kind `Order` and the child that path names each open their own page, and Add property on each changes that entity only; so do a root named `id=7` and the numeric id 7. The page is headed by the key as the listing names it, and Delete entity asks for that back. A link in the older form (a name, `id=N` or a key path) still opens what it did. The precedence when a segment is both (#882, unit `TestDatastoreEncodedKeyPrecedence`): it is an encoded key only when it is the canonical encoding — what `Key.Encode` writes, or that with the page's own project named — of a complete key with the page's namespace and kind and the default database; otherwise it is the older form, so another project's, namespace's or kind's key, a non-canonical encoding and an incomplete key are names here. The only older link this changes is a root entity whose name is itself such an encoding, and its row opens it by its own encoded key. The listing named a root entity by its name, so that root and that child shared a Key cell; since #882 a **Parent** column on a kind's entities and the query builder's results — *none (root entity)*, or the parent's key path, as Google's console lists an entity's parent — tells the rows apart (`TestConsoleDatastoreRowsNameTheirParent`, which also opens the child by its key with this project named and reads another project's key as a name no entity has; unit `TestDatastoreEntityRowsTellARootFromTheChild`). Since #885 entities are listed in a **Name/ID** column rendered as Google's console renders it — `name=…` for a name, `id=…` for a numeric ID — so a root named `id=7` (`name=id=7`) and the numeric ID 7 (`id=7`), which both have no parent and read the same before, differ in the list, in the query builder's results and in their pages' headings and breadcrumbs; key paths (the Parent column, Key path, a child's heading) use the same rendering, `Customer/name=alice/Order/id=7`, and Delete entity asks for the heading back. Create entity's Key identifier reads that rendering too, so `name=id=7` makes the name `id=7`. An older `/datastore/Order/id=7` link still opens the numeric ID 7 (`TestConsoleDatastoreNameIDTellsANameFromAnID`, unit `TestDatastoreNameIDTellsANameFromAnID`; in headless Chrome, `TestDatastoreNameIDColumnTellsANameFromAnID`). The Firestore screen's Documents count, and a Collections tab's, count the same `ListDocuments` list as the collection's page, so a document that does not exist but has subcollections is counted, and the lists' notes say so (`TestConsoleFirestoreCountsMissingDocuments`, #882). In headless Chrome (`test/browser`, emulators shard): `TestFirestoreMissingDocumentIsListedInItalics`, `TestDatastoreRootEntityNamedLikeAKeyPathOpens`, `TestFirestoreCollectionCountIncludesMissingDocuments` and `TestDatastoreKindListShowsEachRowsParent`. |
| **Cloud Tasks attempt history on a task's page** | **Not supported** | A task's page shows its attempt and response counts and last response code, not a history of attempts. |
| Deploy a Cloud Run service from the console | **Verified** | Through the **Cloud Run v2 adapter**, not by applying a Knative manifest — applying directly would let the console accept a configuration the API refuses, which is the console inventing support. The deploy **waits for readiness**: a service reported created and never ready is the failure the console exists to make visible. Verified end to end, including invoking the deployed service through the ingress. |
| Delete a Cloud Run service from the console | **Verified** | Through the same adapter, waiting for the operation. |
| Kubernetes pods, services, jobs and events | **Verified** | Scoped, **read-only**, across namespaces, each row showing whether CloudBurrow owns it. Events are what turn "the pod is Pending" into a reason. |
| Ownership shown per object | **Verified** | A developer can tell what CloudBurrow created from what they created. |
| **GKE cluster metadata** | **Not supported** | This is a kind cluster. No node pools, autopilot or release channels are presented, because presenting them would be fabricating exactly what the issue forbids. |
| **Creating Kubernetes workloads from manifests** | **Not supported** | Read-only by design: a console that could apply arbitrary manifests would create workloads CloudBurrow does not track and cannot clean up. |
| Logs Explorer, live | **Verified** | Real pod logs followed from the cluster, streamed over **Server-Sent Events** (#88). Verified in a real browser: 88 live rows, `live` state, pause control, severity filter. |
| Pause / resume with buffering | **Verified** | Resume shows what happened while paused rather than skipping it — the lines you paused to read are usually next to the ones you need. |
| Reconnect and resume | **Verified** | Every event carries an id; a reconnecting client sends `Last-Event-ID` and the **gap** is replayed rather than everything, which would make a reconnect look like a flood of new activity. |
| Filters: severity, source, resource, operation, text | **Verified** | Applied to both the backlog and the live stream. |
| **Credential redaction** | **Verified** | On the way **in**, not on the way out: an entry stored with a token in it has already been written somewhere a later change might expose. Covers `ya29.` and `cbl_` tokens, `Authorization`, api-key/password/secret/token assignments, PEM private keys and JWTs. Operation failure causes are redacted too. |
| Message truncation | **Verified** | 2 KiB, marked. An application logging a whole request body would otherwise push out everything that explains it. |
| Bounded retention | **Verified** | 2000 entries in memory. Nothing is persisted: keeping logs would make CloudBurrow responsible for data it never promised to keep. |
| **System namespaces are not followed** | **By design** | Measured: the control plane, kourier and Knative's own components were **90% of the buffer**, pushing out the lines that explain a developer's failure. Only `default` and the managed namespace are followed; the Events screen and `kubectl logs` still reach the rest. |
| Cloud Tasks attempt history | **Verified** | Each dispatch attempt is logged with its outcome. A queue that says "1 task" tells a developer nothing; the attempt says why it is still there. |
| Activity: operations with states and causes | **Verified** | PENDING / SUCCEEDED / FAILED from the **backend's verdict**, never from the console's optimism. A failed operation links to its own logs. |
| **Severity of container logs is inferred** | Partial | Container logs carry no structured severity, so it is a heuristic over the words applications conventionally use. Used for filtering, never to claim an application said something it did not. |
| **Billing, quota, SLA or cost metrics** | **Not supported** | Nothing of the kind is displayed. There is no billing here to report. |
| **Request-rate, latency and resource metrics** | **Partial** | **Cloud Tasks, Secret Manager, the Cloud Run v2 adapter, Cloud KMS, Cloud Scheduler, Cloud Logging, Resource Manager and Cloud Storage**, the services CloudBurrow serves itself: every call is counted by service, method and canonical code, with a latency histogram, at `GET /metrics` on the loopback control port in the Prometheus text format (`TestCreateTaskCallsAreCounted`, which parses it with Prometheus's own parser). `/monitoring` charts request rate, error rate and p50/p95 latency per service. **Pub/Sub and the opt-in emulators are not measured**, since their traffic goes over a raw port-forward, and are labelled so, never charted at zero. That set is derived from the services that registered a call observer, not kept by hand, so a measured service is never also reported unmeasured (`TestMetricsSchedulerAndLoggingAreMeasured`, #600).
